# Changelog

## [Unreleased]
- Release v276 (base master `3ba486b08` = v275's master `30bc780ce` + web-only merges + #2214 images on interface session messages: migration `20260930865000_surface_message_images`, new native host call `qorpera_native_surface_session_images`, images in surface turns + `coding_view_attachment`, object-gateway `surface-upload`/`surface-input` routes, member-company-gateway routes): runtime bundle 276 `cfae66f2acddf87c86f2b8445279158dde2ab74a216561eb294e35fa75f9da05` (activation `20260929027699_runtime_bundle_activation_v276.sql`, requiring `20260930865000_surface_message_images`); Linux x64 guest manifest re-pinned (`856067200f2f4b6636e0ac978bb83d380a6339d487574040dfe371907f28ddbb`) for `qorpera-native-provider-host.mjs` (`bed29ca1492b3240f39a3c7b1218654a29f0c3bc7a3f9a580eec7df7411d3c66`, 6,011,183 B), `qorpera-ephemeral-runner.mjs` (`7b1ed940`, 163,464 B), `qorpera-lxd-runner-bootstrap.mjs` (`095184ae`, 86,779 B) and `qorpera-lxd-native-login-bootstrap.mjs` (`ca90cd73`, 63,320 B); orchestrator `cfdbc913`, provider `27721d27`, shared vault `eadbed6c4c81`, login helper `c7caf6d4d62a` and coding executor stay as live (their #2214 rebuilds differ only by an unused contracts constant and the observation name list). Rollout: rolls (additive migration `20260930865000` + LXD runner image v276 + node release 163 + `qorpera-exec-node02`; then the Edge Function `member-company-gateway` and node 1's object gateway from `3ba486b08`). Way back: emergency revert to bundle 275 (`7587f80b`) and release 162.
- Interface session images (migration `20260930865000`): a surface turn reads the member's images through `qorpera_native_surface_session_images` (a new host call in the path control, `NATIVE_HOST_CALLS` and `qorpera_native_host_calls`). A folder or native_ops turn gets their paths under "Images the member attached" to open with `company_read`; a code_repo turn lists them in its brief and gets `coding_view_attachment {clientInputId, sha256}`, which reads the folder file, checks its hash and shows it with `folderImageView`. The coding context-view check takes its images; observations and the action review (read) name it. PLATFORM_UPDATES `surface-session-images`.
- Release v275 (base master `a5d09e5bf` incl. #2184 Gemini 4 Neon `includeServerSideToolInvocations`/observability/`GEMINI_API:*` error classification + key health/check + ChatGPT/Claude subscription credits + split member default models, #2178/#2183 surface figures + `surface-figures` platform update, #2170 surface runtime hardening, #2153 LXD provider client hardening, #2167 ephemeral runner guest & unit hardening, #2165 `qorpera-folder` `4d07acb9`, #2142 secret-broker/login-helper shutdown & refresh hardening; 1 unapplied schema migration `20260930858000_member_default_models_and_gemini_health.sql`; `856000` & `859000` already live): runtime bundle 275 `7587f80b896c520e234d93404bfbc9ba09f932f26055de194b52907ec8a025c1` (activation `20260929027599_runtime_bundle_activation_v275.sql`, requiring `20260930858000_member_default_models_and_gemini_health`); Linux x64 guest manifest re-pinned (`3f6ef92216ff7e38e5d01e0af6fe73db7a4fb52b97fc400fc84fe06943af778e`) for `qorpera-native-provider-host.mjs` (`285637df30ff381ffb37ac34df368e01c4d682cb2da5b0ace552998c34565060`, 6,002,977 B), `qorpera-lxd-runner-bootstrap.mjs` (`5e9bb408`, 86,630 B), `qorpera-lxd-native-login-bootstrap.mjs` (`3a901307`, 63,171 B), guest service units (`79116c3b`, `4b943dfd`, `5375306d`), `install-image-contract.sh` (`e015461d`), and `run-job` (`8cf02010`); provider `27721d27` and `qorpera-folder` `4d07acb9` already pinned; orchestrator `cfdbc913`, shared-execution `8169eb45`, patched Codex 0.170.0 `9d6a86ec`, and Claude Code 2.1.325 unchanged. Shared vault (`qorpera-cloud-secret-broker-service.mjs`, `eadbed6c4c81`), login helper (`c7caf6d4d62a`), and Node 1 `:10000` nginx `^/v1/gemini-keys/<uuid>/check$` updated before bundle activation. Rollout: rolls (additive migration `20260930858000` + shared vault/helper + `:10000` nginx `/check` route + LXD runner image v275 + node release 162 + `qorpera-exec-node02`). Way back: emergency revert to bundle 274 (`39dfbcaf`) and release 161.
- Fixed Gemini 4 Neon run failures and added key health diagnostics, subscription API credits parsing, and member default-model routing (migration `20260930858000_member_default_models_and_gemini_health.sql`): `cloud-gemini-tool-session.ts` now sends `toolConfig.includeServerSideToolInvocations: true` when combining `functionDeclarations` with `google_search`, emits `model_request` observability spans, classifies Google HTTP/RPC errors into sanitized `GEMINI_API:*` failure codes, and records/clears key health (`qorpera_record_gemini_key_error`); the shared vault (`cloud-gemini-key-vault.ts`, `cloud-secret-broker.ts`, `cloud-secret-broker-http.ts`) adds a zero-token `POST /v1/gemini-keys/:ticketId/check` key check; `native-provider-usage.ts` and `cloud-claude-login-usage.ts` parse optional ChatGPT and Claude API/extra credits (`credits`); and SQL/host dispatch respects `qorpera_member_default_models` across conversations, in-house workers, and coding sessions.
- `propose_surface` takes `figure` (one of the surface figures in `@qorpera/contracts` `SURFACE_FIGURES`, saved as icon `figure:<name>`) in place of `icon` and `accentHex`; a surface's colour follows its kind (Jonas, 22 Nov 2026: "just not the colors"). Platform update `surface-figures`.
- Release v274 (base master `659c5a904` incl. #2182 `code_repo` Surface coding-session execution & auto-composed previews, #2144 hosted app vault secrets & code/ops routines, #2138 coding-worker webHosting auth & streams, #2140/#2154 folder SQL perf & sharded storage usage, #2150 guest stage/plumb hardening, #2163 object-gateway scan-stream, #2176/#2181 Studio & Surface preview/publish perf, plus 4 unapplied schema migrations `20260930850000`, `20260930851000`, `20260930852000`, `20260930857000`; `20260930855000` already live): runtime bundle 274 `39dfbcaf263f21a6fe0b0c3a5da63ec43010bdb745de64631cf1b798cfc98bb7` (activation `20260929027499_runtime_bundle_activation_v274.sql`, requiring `20260930850000_app_vault_secrets_and_code_routines` and `20260930857000_surface_code_repo_coding_sessions`); Linux x64 guest manifest re-pinned (`351b64c6dcb234be129389f7ef0e0b3ab407283fa9a2964de4556735d5871857`) for `qorpera-native-provider-host.mjs` (`9afff07b6cfa38b227d514ee79835e4b9abe3c7cb7767661e1b625c7301a50ac`, 5,987,072 B), `qorpera-lxd-runner-bootstrap.mjs` (`cb98175d`, 86,579 B), `qorpera-lxd-native-login-bootstrap.mjs` (`e1d4f373`, 63,120 B), and `qorpera-claude-box-stage.sh` (`e414a21d`, 2,798 B); orchestrator `cfdbc913` already pinned; provider `82257d34`, shared-execution `8169eb45`, patched Codex 0.170.0 `9d6a86ec`, and Claude Code 2.1.325 unchanged. Rollout: rolls (additive migrations + edge functions `runner-orchestrator` + LXD runner image v274 + node release 161 + `qorpera-exec-node02` + coding executor `e9ca23d7` on both nodes + object gateway `6b47dc85` `b36` on node 1, preserving all `QORPERA_SURFACE_*` flags). Way back: emergency revert to bundle 273 (`b04f15eb`) and release 160.
- Release v273 (base master `1a4d0a2c1` incl. #2095 Surface-First Company Harness, #2126 Surface host wiring, #2168 `QORPERA_SURFACE_FIRST` provider/shared-execution forwarding and LXD guest bootstrap validation, plus 14 unapplied schema migrations `20260930828000`–`831000`, `836000`–`838000`, `840000`–`846000`): runtime bundle 273 `b04f15eb5ff3da03f707ff6febaae3e1dc8d7f0f1494ad241f39f878d85c6883` (activation `20260929027399_runtime_bundle_activation_v273.sql`, requiring `20260930843000_surface_platform_live` and `20260930844000_surface_host_wiring`); Linux x64 guest manifest re-pinned (`4c56dda28f69dcd2e47403fe2af733fc611acc2ce5d7ea9725fa24d1597b6cd7`) for `qorpera-native-provider-host.mjs` (`0b8934f2b6b28adfd8a3462d8f03e8a2b34008e64dc71756f2d753ebfd3575dd`, 5,954,607 B), `qorpera-ephemeral-runner.mjs` (`e4f0bc5f`, 163,372 B), `qorpera-lxd-runner-bootstrap.mjs` (`bf0dd2b8`, 86,522 B), `qorpera-lxd-native-login-bootstrap.mjs` (`1286a04a`, 63,063 B), and `qorpera-identity-recovery-restore.mjs` (`42c982ff`, 30,242 B); `install-lxd-provider-host.sh` (`82257d34`) and `install-shared-execution-guest.sh` (`8169eb45`) re-pinned; patched Codex 0.170.0 `9d6a86ec` and Claude Code 2.1.325 unchanged. Rollout: rolls (additive migrations + unused legacy snapshot-v1 drops in `831000`; edge functions incl. `_shared/` deployed, node release 160 + `qorpera-exec-node02` + object gateway `b35` + coding executor rolled, then `QORPERA_SURFACE_*` flags and `qorpera_surface_settings.enabled = true`). Way back: emergency revert to bundle 272 (`04b3381f`) and release 159.
- Enriched `data_describe` (`cloud-data-tools.ts`) description and output trimming ladder to pass through PostgreSQL DDL inspection metadata (`nullable`, `default`, `constraints`, `indexes`, `viewDefinition`, `enums`, `extensions`, `ddlTruncated`) from the company-data gateway and strip DDL extras before falling back to column-names-only output when the payload exceeds tool output limits.
- Documented native static, hybrid, and full-stack dynamic webapp `.qorpera/checks.json` deployment (`kind`, `port`, `healthPath`, `dockerfile`, `spaFallback`, `_redirects`, `_headers`, `*.qorpera.site` -> custom domain) in `CloudNativeCodingRuntime` instructions and added the `native-web-hosting` capability entry (`2026-11-22`) in `cloud-platform-updates.ts`.
- Isolated each coding session's FUSE folder cache directory in `cloud-coding-guest-agent.ts` (`/var/lib/qorpera-folder/cache/s-<worker32>`, 64 bytes `<= 80`-byte `qorpera-folder` `--cache-dir` limit, mode `0700`, disjoint from `folder-local`) instead of sharing `/var/lib/qorpera-folder/cache/machine` across sibling coding sessions, eliminating cross-session `<cache-dir>/names` `flock` contention and `O(N^2)` FUSE peer cache-invalidation broadcasts while keeping caches warm across turns, view replacements, and `quiesce`/sleep/wake, reclaiming each session's cache directory on `stop-session` teardown, and pruning orphaned `cache/s-*` directories and legacy `cache/machine` once per boot at the first mount; raised `deploy/coding-worker/coding-gateway-nginx.conf` with `worker_rlimit_nofile 4096` and `worker_connections 2048` and documented `LimitNOFILE=4096` and `--connections-max=512` for `qorpera-coding-forward` in `deploy/coding-worker/README.md`.
- Added in-session visual screenshot inspection for coding sessions (zero SQL migrations):
  - `coding_run_command` (`cloud-coding-command-tools.ts`, `cloud-coding-image-inspect.ts`) sets `images: true` and adds `inspectImage` mode (mutually exclusive with `command`/`timeoutMs`/`runChecks`), strictly validating a relative workspace path to a `.png`, `.jpg`, `.jpeg`, or `.webp` regular file inside the workspace root (rejecting path traversal, absolute paths, symlinks escaping workspace, non-regular files, unsupported extensions, corrupt images, and raw files over 8 MiB), reading bounded base64 output through the existing receipted command gateway, sanitizing/resampling to `<= 1920x1200` PNG via `folderImageView` (`oneImageAtATime`), rechecking authority before returning or refusing, and attaching the multimodal PNG directly to the tool result.
  - `CloudNativeToolBoundary` (`cloud-native-tool-boundary.ts`) admits verified shown images from `images: true` `"worker"` tools matching `imageSha256` in their JSON result, and `CloudNativeCodingRuntime` (`cloud-native-coding-runtime.ts`) verifies shown coding screenshot provenance in `recordContextView` (still refusing `capture`, `companyReadId`, and unverified/mismatched `imageSha256`) and instructs coding agents to visually inspect screenshots before `coding_finish`.
  - Added platform update `coding-visual-screenshot-inspection` (`2026-11-21`) in `cloud-platform-updates.ts`.
- Added sibling-agent repository coordination and pre-flight check execution for Qorpera-hosted repositories (zero SQL migrations):
  - `manage_code` (`cloud-code-tools.ts`) adds read-only `action: "status"` (classified as `"read"` in `cloudActionEffect`, no `operationKey` required), returning active/blocked sibling coding sessions targeting `code/<owner>/<name>/`, active worktree directories annotated with their owning session, recent landed changes and notices from `changes/index.md`, restoring flag, and parsed `.qorpera/checks.json` configuration summary (including `mode` and per-check `needs`), with bounded output fitting and strict folder read-provenance release (`qorpera_folder_record_release`) over the redacted text.
  - `coding_run_command` (`cloud-coding-command-tools.ts`, `cloud-code-checks.ts`) adds `runChecks: true` mode (with optional `checkNames` and `failFast`) inside coding sessions using the existing command gateway and operation receipts, reading and strictly validating `.qorpera/checks.json` in exact parity with `runner-orchestrator/src/code-checks.ts` (including BOM, strict UTF-8, and `needs` DAG dependencies), running sequential or parallel DAG waves (up to 4 concurrent ready checks) with `CI=true QORPERA_CHECKS=1` and closed stdin, enforcing per-check timeouts and receipted `coding_cancel_command` cancellation on fail-fast, timeout, or turn abort, and returning structured per-check pass/fail results with redacted output tails without landing on `main`.
  - Added platform update `code-status-and-preflight-checks` (`2026-11-21`) in `cloud-platform-updates.ts`.
- Documented optional `needs` dependencies for parallel `.qorpera/checks.json` execution in `CloudNativeCodingRuntime` instructions and added the `parallel-code-checks` entry to `PLATFORM_UPDATES`.
- Enabled the Company Databases `data` CLI (`data describe|apply|query|change`) inside coding sessions (migration `20260930829000`, `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-COMPANY-DATABASES.md` §9a): `cloud-coding-guest-agent.ts` atomically ensures `/usr/local/bin/data` (`0555`, delegating to `/opt/qorpera-folder/qorpera-folder data` via `sudo -n` when non-root; also baked into `deploy/coding-machine/build-coding-image.sh`) and bind-mounts each session's `/run/qorpera-folder/views/<worker32>.json` read-only at `/run/qorpera-folder/view.json` in `user@<uid>.service` and every command unit so each session gets its own default view with zero DB credentials on disk (the security boundary remains the CID-authenticated coding machine, since sessions on one coding machine share root); `cloud-native-coding-runtime.ts` documents the `data` CLI for coding agents; `cloud-platform-updates.ts` adds the `data-in-coding-sessions` capability entry dated `2026-11-21`.
- `CloudCompanyFolderClient.stage()` (`cloud-company-folder-client.ts`) pre-hashes and deduplicates unique `(hash, mediaType, executable)` files across `write` and `new_folder` units and stages them with bounded concurrency (`STAGE_CONCURRENCY = 4`, sibling abort on first failure) while preserving deterministic change-set ordering; `HttpCloudCompanyFolderBytes` (`cloud-company-folder-bytes.ts`) eliminates redundant hashing in `manifestOf()` (single hash on `<= 4 MiB` files, incremental whole-file hash on multi-chunk files), avoids `Uint8Array.from` copies on `ArrayBuffer`-backed PUTs and single-chunk responses, skips re-hashing/copying on 1-chunk `get()`, and fetches deduplicated multi-chunk `get()`/`range()` reads with bounded concurrency (`CHUNK_CONCURRENCY = 4`).
- Release v272 (#2030, accept Google AI Studio `AQ.` Auth keys alongside `AIza` keys in Gemini API key capture/open and web Settings, plus #2023, #2024, #2025, #2027; master `963844dda` since v271's release merge `2bfb399ab`): no database migrations. Runtime bundle 272 `04b3381f2c24f3011cfd8dbc57bf11b0dcf0d1e206ce6626ea054047d15ff4b9` (activation `20260929027299` over v271 `65fdee24c81b0a24a84aaf0359a68decf04e840d7668c3b35d26cf3ed0a6ee47`, model policy `c41c1599df9fce1578594311451f519525d7fcf7a9cc2d958a3a3452b8835db4` unchanged), guest re-pin for `qorpera-native-provider-host.mjs` (`73a89afa3a2f242c5ededaf16d1b8cf88e7e8d3424f65377a6fd1ba6a3b67da8`, 5,676,702 B; guest manifest `46dbf6b12c5e55b8f2634975aff4caccefea5bea0d70888b439501ead386d443`); all other guest artifacts, patched Codex 0.170.0 `9d6a86ec`, Claude Code 2.1.325, and login helper/child unchanged. Shared vault (`qorpera-cloud-secret-broker-service.mjs`, `be0c0a3a8daf`) updated on both nodes for `AQ.` key capture/verify/open; Node 1 `:10000` nginx routes unchanged. Way back: bundle 271 by the release lane's emergency path.
- `folderImageView` (`cloud-folder-images.ts`) bypasses the 36.8 MiB `Float32Array` resampling pass on 1:1 images (`<= 1920x1200`) while preserving exact transparent-pixel RGB zeroing, replaces ~41.5M per-byte `predicted()`/`paeth()` calls in `encodePng()` with inline scratch-buffered filter scoring (eliminating the redundant re-prediction pass), inlines PNG `unfilter()` Paeth/Average loops, and adds a direct 24-bit RGB row-reader fast path with bit-for-bit identical output PNGs.
- Release v271 (#2019, Gemini 4 Neon in conversations, plus #2007, #2009–#2018, #2020, #2021; master `313cea318` since v270's release merge `730b00906`): one migration, `20260930823000_gemini_4_neon` (additive: RLS-protected `qorpera_gemini_keys`, `qorpera_gemini_key_tickets`, and `qorpera_gemini_run_usage` keyed by `(run_id, lease_id)`, nullable `gemini_model`/`gemini_effort`/`gemini_key_id` on `qorpera_execution_runs`, widened model and notification checks, member settings RPCs, shared-vault capture/open RPCs, and binding-scoped usage/cap RPCs; safe alongside running v270 nodes). Runtime bundle 271 `65fdee24c81b0a24a84aaf0359a68decf04e840d7668c3b35d26cf3ed0a6ee47` (model policy `c41c1599df9fce1578594311451f519525d7fcf7a9cc2d958a3a3452b8835db4` adding `geminiChoice: "thread"`, activation `20260929027199` requiring `20260930823000_gemini_4_neon`), guest re-pin for `qorpera-native-provider-host.mjs` (`b8befb538f4d30e9db8f12f408d96e171d9156f945c29087b72e18e39278009e`, 5,671,195 B), `qorpera-lxd-runner-bootstrap.mjs` (`06f479c0854dce729f1d9a876fd129cb18fd9514871e65a63f51b7c13311cad1`, 85,499 B), and `qorpera-lxd-native-login-bootstrap.mjs` (`12022ce4a24c7007f32aaf9455126b5752a312f94b152bf958546b7bcc474f93`, 62,279 B); patched Codex 0.170.0 `9d6a86ec` and Claude Code 2.1.325 unchanged. Shared vault (`qorpera-cloud-secret-broker-service.mjs`, adding `POST /v1/gemini-keys/:ticketId` and `POST /v1/gemini-keys/open`) and Node 1's shared-vault `:10000` nginx block are updated in their own quiet window before Gemini key save/open is active (compatible with both v270 and v271). Way back: bundle 270 by the release lane's emergency path; migration `20260930823000_gemini_4_neon` stays safely inactive under bundle 270 (`qorpera_active_bundle_gemini_choice()` returns `false`).
- `CloudRunLog` (`cloud-run-log.ts`) and `CloudRunLogRpcProtection` (`cloud-run-log-protection.ts`) batch step classification (`narrowBatch` deduplicating unioned paths up to 1,024/RPC) and deduplicated keyed-hash requests (`hashes` up to 128/RPC) across all candidate steps in a commit batch, cutting a 16-step flush or precommit from 16–32 sequential protection RPCs down to 2 RPCs while preserving ascending `step.j` `#narrowFrom` propagation, fail-closed validation, and custom `narrow()` mock fallback.
- Support for Gemini 4 Neon (`gemini-4-neon`) in attended conversations backed by a member's own Gemini API key (e.g. bundled Google AI Ultra API credits), with optional default-for-chats toggle and monthly USD spend cap. Migration `20260930823000_gemini_4_neon` adds RLS-protected `qorpera_gemini_keys`, `qorpera_gemini_key_tickets`, and `qorpera_gemini_run_usage`, widens `qorpera_session_models_model_id_check` and `qorpera_member_notifications_kind` (`gemini_cap_reached`), pins `gemini_model`/`gemini_effort`/`gemini_key_id` on `qorpera_execution_runs`, and exposes member settings, shared-vault capture/open, and binding-scoped usage/cap RPCs. The shared vault (`cloud-gemini-key-vault.ts`, `POST /v1/gemini-keys/:ticketId`, `POST /v1/gemini-keys/open`) verifies keys with Google before sealing under the member key and refuses runs when revoked or capped. Attended runs (`cloud-gemini-tool-session.ts`) execute directly against the Gemini REST API with Qorpera's tool boundary, action-review gate, `google_search`, opaque `thoughtSignature` preservation, multimodal PNG forwarding, and `thinkingConfig.thinkingLevel` (`low`, `medium`, `high`, `xhigh`); background workers, routines, and coding sessions stay on Codex or Claude Code, and hitting a cap pauses Gemini turns and notifies the member without switching logins or tainting Codex usage signals.
- Release v270 (#2005, thread decisions step 5, the fold, and #2006, the system prompt overhaul; master since v269's
  release merge bd48f5e9e holds them alone): one migration, `20260930822000_thread_run_wakes` (one anchored text patch of
  `qorpera_read_session_events`, refusing unless its anchor stands exactly once: each event a run wrote carries
  `run_wake`, the kind of wake that started that run, through the unique index on `qorpera_wake_occurrences.run_id`; the
  tail read goes through it; no table, trigger, grant or new function; an event no run wrote, or of a run with no wake,
  reads as before). Runtime bundle 270
  `91df0bffb97a021554a0520013daeb21930ca38242a93cf8acc8e2c6829db72c` (activation `20260929027099`, which requires
  822000; #2006's words and prompt assembly: the staff and worker constitutions, the role overlays
  `qorpera.hire-overlay/v21`, the tool-result rules, `send_message`, `ask_member` and `send_coworker_message`'s
  descriptions, the Claude Code worker's brief, the Co-Founder's seeded instructions, the platform update
  `clearer-agent-voice`; no tool name, input schema or handler changes), the runner image of node release 157 with the
  native provider host `33ee585ba3fe8faf9eb5157d8ec40f5e2c052ac725645ab55a740ccbc19f15bc` (it carries the runtime; the
  patched Codex 0.170.0 `9d6a86ec` as in v269). The shared vault (784784b9), the model login helper (cedd6a37) and its
  isolation child stay as v269 left them, and the coding executor stays f8db2998: their builds at this release are
  v269's byte for byte (the executor's manifest names the new bundle only). The company machine image, the folder
  service, the shared execution package and the test machines do not move. Node 1's object gateway does not move either,
  by the lead's decision, although this release builds it differently: #2006's new seeded Co-Founder instructions
  (`COMPANY_STATE_COFOUNDER_INSTRUCTIONS`, written into a new company's Co-Founder agent.md) are NOT live with this
  release; a company created before the gateway's own later step is seeded with the old text, as every existing company
  was. Way back: bundle 269 by the release lane's emergency path; the migration stays (bundle 269 never reads
  `run_wake`, and the web draws an event without it as before).
- System prompt overhaul (`qorpera.hire-overlay/v21`): reordered staff instructions so conversational warmth and plain-language guidance open immediately after company identity and `# Trust and authority` + `# Tool results` sit right before untrusted context and head data; rewrote constitution, role overlays, wake protocols, and communication tool descriptions (`send_message`, `ask_member`, `send_coworker_message`) for clearer, more natural prose with less duplication; listed all 12 `save_app` kits in `# Apps`; gated `# Registration and bank` to Co-Founder and finance/money/bank roles; upgraded `CLAUDE_WORKER_SYSTEM_NOTE` with full-ambition, visual-inspection, WebP, plain-language, and security guidance; added platform update `clearer-agent-voice`.
- `hashArtifact()` in `cloud-codex-tool-session.ts` raises its `createReadStream` `highWaterMark` from 64 KiB to 1 MiB (`ARTIFACT_HASH_HIGH_WATER_MARK`) to reduce syscall overhead when hashing the ~319 MB Codex binary while keeping exact full-file SHA-256 verification unchanged.
- The thread read says what woke each run (thread decisions step 5, the fold; Jonas, 20 Nov 2026: "Fold them").
  Migration `20260930822000_thread_run_wakes`: one text patch of `qorpera_read_session_events` (anchor required exactly
  once; the tail read goes through it): each event a run wrote gets `run_wake`, the wake's kind, by the unique index
  on `qorpera_wake_occurrences.run_id`. No table, no trigger, no grant change, no new function; who may read a thread
  is unchanged, and the field tells a reader nothing the agent's activity does not already show. Runtime code is
  untouched.
- Release v269 (#2001, thread decisions step 4, and #2003, its words; master since v268's release merge afaae0474 holds
  them and the web-only #2000 and #2002): one migration, `20260930821000_decision_rounds` (two internal functions nobody
  may call, `qorpera_assert_asks_again` and `qorpera_decision_round`; three anchored text patches, each refusing unless
  its anchor stands exactly once: `qorpera_interaction_request_valid` lets a question's payload carry `asksAgain`,
  `qorpera_folder_append_interaction_request` checks such a card under the thread's lock, and
  `qorpera_list_company_open_decisions` gives it `round`; no table, trigger or grant changes; a card without `asksAgain`
  is written, listed and answered as before). Runtime bundle 269

  question `round` (its number, and the card it asks again with the answer that led to it). Two internal functions
  nobody may call (`qorpera_assert_asks_again`, `qorpera_decision_round`); no table, no trigger, no grant changes.
  A card without `asksAgain` is appended, listed and answered exactly as before. When the kernel refuses a card that
  carries `asksAgain`, `ask_member` answers the agent with the kernel's words (`REFUSED: NOT_AVAILABLE: asksAgain must
  name ...; leave it out otherwise`) instead of an unconfirmed outcome, so it raises the card without the link; a retry
  whose first attempt ended without an answer is never called refused. A card without `asksAgain` is unchanged here
  too: the kernel's refusal of it is still answered as an unconfirmed outcome, not in words.

- A Claude Code worker wakes its agent's machine instead of going to Codex (the founder, 17 Nov 2026, to "Wake the agent's machine for Claude work instead of falling back to Codex?": "sure"; migration `20260930811000`, SQL test 1294). A defaulted write worker whose member can use Claude Code but whose agent has no machine running for that member is resolved to Claude Code; the worker drain wakes the agent's newest machine for that member (one made from an image without the home is passed over) or starts one new `<agent>-home` machine (2 CPUs, 4 GiB, 40 GiB), one per agent and member however many workers ask, waits up to 5 minutes for it, and hands the worker to Codex at once when the machine is refused, stopped, fails to start or does not run in time. A going Claude Code run with a home session keeps its machine from sleeping; once it ends the idle rule decides as before. SQL only: no bundle, image or node change.
- Web search on a member's Codex login (the founder, 16 Nov 2026: "Yes, turn it on"), paid by that member's own ChatGPT plan: the agent's thread starts with `web_search="cached"` (OpenAI's index, no live fetch of a site), and the process with `features.standalone_web_search=true`, which `verifyConfiguration` requires on a Codex login and refuses on a plan run. Every model then searches through Codex's own `web.run` (`<provider>/alpha/search`, the same sign-in), as the Responses Lite models (every one in the provider's catalog) already did; its result is a function output the result fence issues as one of Codex's own (`unconfirmed`, untrusted data, at most 32 KiB), and the run's work log keeps it as a `web_search` step with its query. The provider's hosted search is never offered: its `web_search_call` item is one the pinned fence refuses, so the next request would fail. Only where Codex has its own web.run (its OpenAI provider, which a Codex login uses); the reviewer's, compactor's, voice and picture threads keep `web_search="disabled"`; a ChatGPT plan run has none. No Codex rebuild: the native Codex stays `9c299f88`, runtime bundle only. Every run's text (the constitution's "Not a local coding session") names web.run beside the qorpera tools, for a run that has it; the Co-Founder learns of it from the platform update `web-search`. Proof: `patched-codex-web-search.test.ts` on the pinned artifact with a loopback provider. - Many Claude Code runs per member at once, the vault as the one renewer (the founder's "Yes, build it", 16 Nov 2026; migration `20260930810000`, after `20260930804000`, SQL test 1287; `lanes/agent-box-20261113/claude-parallel/DESIGN.md`). A Claude Code worker's box reaches `api.anthropic.com` alone, so its Claude Code cannot renew; the run seals nothing back and asks the vault every 60 s for the version it holds, putting a newer `.credentials.json` in place (Claude Code reads it again before its next call). The shared vault renews: at a run's open and polls, and once a day for an idle login, its helper runs the pinned Claude Code's `auth status --json` on the login (`/v1/claude/check`), and a changed file is sealed as the next version under the login's renewal lock (compare-and-swap); two refusals of one version two minutes apart sign the login out. The member's lease becomes a cap (`qorpera_claude_parallel_runs`, 1 until the release sets 4), per member across all of the member's Claude logins. Under several logins (`20260930804000`) everything is per login: a lease records the worker's login when it is taken and the run opens, polls and is refreshed from that login to its end (a follow-up or the member's In-use switch moves only later runs; the run host never puts another login's files in place), a login is checked at a run's open and poll only while a live lease holds it, the idle pass keeps every active login alive, and the second refusal of the login In use moves In use to the member's next active login (one not used up first). The staff instructions and the worker tool say "up to four at once"; platform update `claude-code-workers-at-once`. Staff instruction pins `83618e1bb237` → `b2a4cebaa9c4610e79652da7d31a5bf3d922e7235cf55dd5c8e0225f78bb5e12` (conversation and private), the company run's tool catalog `457027590ec4` → `fb26aca8dfc8a0e94259d65b04576ddeb916fd01973d806958835208dc7e93f3` (manage_worker's harness line), from master since v259, measured on node 1's test VM (run 20261119T101331-61483-976bfb8b). A run's first open that the vault answers busy (its renewal for the run still running) is asked again, up to 6 times 5 s apart. A guard test keeps every `cloud-claude-*` source off Anthropic's sign-in hosts, so a Claude Code reviewer beside a run (#1897, which shares the run's login folder) cannot renew either once both land. - Claude plan usage and limit resets for the Claude login card (the founder, 16-17 Nov 2026: "could we add that? I would also like to add the resets here", then "Yes, build it"; migration `20260930793000`, SQL test 1290). Only the unmodified, pinned Claude Code talks to Anthropic with the member's sign-in: a Claude Code worker's run keeps Claude Code's last `rate_limit_event` and its host records it (at most once a minute and at the end) through the new native host call `qorpera_record_claude_run_usage` (path control, `NATIVE_HOST_CALLS` and its SQL row); the account refresh's vault route also reads the member's Claude login when the member's read asks (`qorpera_begin_claude_login_read`): the vault opens it under the member's key and hands the login helper its `.credentials.json` only, and the helper runs Claude Code in its isolation with Anthropic's API host alone (it cannot renew; nothing is sealed back; the folder is wiped): `-p` with the `get_usage` control request (no model call) for the 5-hour, weekly and per-model weekly windows, and Claude Code's own `/limit-reset` screen in a terminal (`script`), read with a small terminal emulator, for the resets the account holds. A member's click uses one reset: the vault claims the request once (`qorpera_claim_claude_login_reset`), the helper opens `/limit-reset <kind>`, presses Enter only on the confirm screen naming that kind with "Yes" under the cursor, and records Claude Code's answer (reset, nothing used, or not confirmed). Any screen it cannot read shows no reset and presses nothing. Proof on the test VMs against a stub of api.anthropic.com: `deploy/claude-box/test/usage-probe.sh`. On a machine set up as a vault host (the helper's package, unit and pinned binaries; `deploy/claude-box/test/vault-proof.sh`) three things stopped every read in the helper's sandbox, now fixed: the relay's socket path was too long under `/run/qorpera-model-login-helper` (each launch's folder is now a short `mkdtemp`); Ubuntu 26.04's `stty` is a symlink into the uutils binary (`script` and `stty` are now checked and run as the root-owned files their names lead to); and the relay and the namespace's entry let 8 connections through at once, while Claude Code opens about a dozen as it starts, so its own reset check was refused about half the time (a Claude read's relay now holds 32, the entry 32). A `/limit-reset` that still answers "Couldn't check for a reset right now" starts Claude Code again, three times at most, pressing no key. A `get_usage` answer whose `five_hour` is null (no Claude use in the last 5 hours, seen on a real account) reads as the 5-hour window at 0 % used with no reset time, so the card keeps its 5-hour bar.

- The review reads the script a machine command starts with (2026-11-17 amendment "the review reads the script it
  judges", brief A of the mywave review; its refusal and retry parts came first in #1942). Only a plain command is
  looked at (plain words, plain quoted strings, `;`, newline, `&&`, `|`, output redirections; nothing bash would
  expand). It names at most one file, by grammar: after zero or more leading `cd <path> &&` (literal, no `..`, no
  options, no redirection), the first command must be an allow-listed runner form (the prefixes `time`, `nohup`, `nice`,
  `timeout`, `xvfb-run`, an interpreter with its allowed options and the script path, the path as the command word, or
  `source f`), with no variable set before it, no redirection but `2>&1` or to `/dev/null`, and, in a pipeline, only
  `head`, `tail`, `grep` (one pattern), `cat`, `wc`, `sort` or `uniq` beside it, with no operand and only options from
  an exact list each; its file is named when its path has no `..` segment. A later script is listed as not shown, and a
  command that starts with anything else names no file. The file is read on the machine before the review and shown
  whole (64 KiB); a card's approval covers its digest; the machine runs the model's command byte for byte. The file is
  evidence, not proof of what runs: the agent's own login shell and profile decide what a program name runs, and the
  reviewer is told so. A file not shown is never on its own a reason to deny or ask. Known limits: a script after
  another command, a second script, a script after `source`, one with a variable set before it and one whose output goes
  to a file are not shown; the agent's home reads no files; a file changed between the read and the start is not
  detected. Runtime bundle only: no migration, host call, executor or image moves.

- The first team in one step (the founder, 17 November 2026: "on 5, I agree with batch hiring, but we should allow for
  continuous hiring as the company evolves"; contract 14.1, the 2026-11-17 amendment; migration `20260930799000`, SQL
  test 1296; lanes/mywave-fixes-20261117/DESIGN-B.md). A new runtime tool, `hire_team`, hires 2 to 12 coworkers in one
  call, each with `hire_agent`'s fields and checks: one automatic review of the call (delegation; one card naming every
  hire), recorded for all of its new agent folders, and one folder commit, which indexes every new agent in its own
  transaction, so each first run lists the whole team as coworkers. One `operationKey` per call; each hire's ids come
  from the run, the key and its place in the list, in namespaces of their own. Names repeated in the call are refused
  before the review, and handles are numbered past the folders taken and the call's own. A hire whose folder was made
  meanwhile is that hire's conflict alone, and the answer names each hire's outcome (`partly_saved`). Its arguments are
  bounded at 90 KiB (the action review row keeps them). `hire_agent` says it is for one hire at any time as the company
  evolves; the Co-Founder carries both tools in full, an employee opens `hire_team` by more_tools. Catalog: the
  Co-Founder's grows by `hire_team` (2,506 bytes: description 1,215, schema 1,237) and `hire_agent`'s description (927 to
  1,040 bytes); the pins moved. A platform update (`hire-team`) tells the Co-Founder. Deploy order: the migration before
  the bundle's activation (the release's `migrate` runs first). The migration only adds the name where `hire_agent` is
  named (the runtime observation list, the review recorder's tools and contract 14.1's two checks), so an older runtime
  is unchanged by it; a runtime with `hire_team` on a database without it would have every team refused at the review
  recorder, saving nothing.
- Release v254 (company mail handles and companies with no name yet, #1915 and #1921; a member's message waits at a
  rate limit, #1916; each merged in the release branch at its approved head; and master since v253: pending spectators,
  #1911; long company names in the invitation email, #1901; the kits' pages without a header, #1899; coworker messages
  wake only on request, #1920; coworker messages close together fold into one wake, #1930): runtime bundle 254
  `73401553e00450485082a7b3af00ada3b4aad05b26b37b42fdf47a4fe324ba1e` (activation `20260929025499`, previous 253
  `ec5969910e64`). Its activation requires `20260930796000` (the kernel's question input `mail_address` and the company
  mail handles). The roll's `migrate` runs `20260930794000` (pending spectators), `20260930796000`, `20260930798000` (the
  coworker wake fold) and `20260930801000` (the rate-limit wait; #1902, its web half, is live before it) before the switch. The guest moves: the native provider
  host (`5eb2561c`); the ephemeral runner, the two LXD bootstraps and the native Codex (`9c299f88`) stay.
- A member's own message waits at a ChatGPT rate limit instead of failing (contract 17, the 2026-11-17 amendment "a
  member's message waits at a rate limit"; migration `20260930801000`, SQL test 1289). On 17 Nov 2026 the founder's
  "retry" in Produkt's thread (run `e5391a06`, `room_delivery`) failed in 2 s with `rate_limited` and only a failure card
  showed. Now a started `session_turn` or `room_delivery` run turned away with `rate_limited` before any effect pauses on
  the same login (`run_paused`, `pausedFor: "rate_limited"`, no attempt spent) for 1, 2, 4, 8, 15 minutes with jitter,
  at most `rate_limit_waits` (5, `qorpera_member_rate_limit_settings`; 0 restores the old failure) times, then fails as
  before. The recovery read says `rate_limited`; a waiting run's activity says `paused_for` and is never movable; a
  usage reading does not release it. No account cool-down, turn cap, claim, routine or file-wake change. Deploy: the web
  first (it reads both fields when present), then this migration on its own; no runtime bundle, runner, host, executor
  or vault change.
- A coworker message wakes the coworker only when the sender says so (the founder, 17 November 2026: "Build, no cap", after
  213 of about 520 runs in two days were started by coworker messages, 91 of them in one company's first hour, most of
  them news with a file link; lanes/agent-messaging-volume-20261117). `send_coworker_message` requires `priority`: a call
  without it is refused with the reason, and the tool's text says true is for a request, a question, a handover the
  coworker waits for or a member's decision that changes its work, false (a note for its next turn) for other news, and
  no message for what the files and todos show. An answer the asker waits for is sent with `priority` true (contract
  14.6, the 2026-11-17 amendment). The coworker paragraph and the coworker wake's protocol stop replies that
  say received, agreed or done, and passing news on to a third coworker who need not act. `PLATFORM_UPDATES` tells the
  Co-Founder (`coworker-messages-wake-on-request`). The runtime bundle only; no migration.
- Coworker messages waiting for one agent are delivered in one run (the founder, 17 November 2026: "Build, no cap";
  migration `20260930798000`, SQL test 1295; lanes/agent-messaging-volume-20261117/DESIGN-FOLD.md). An agent's coworker
  wakes all go to its own thread, which runs one run at a time, so messages that arrive while it is busy used to start
  one run each, one after another (prod, 16-17 Nov: 78 of 227). Now, when the run of a coworker wake reads its wake
  (`qorpera_folder_read_wake` → `qorpera_coworker_take_waiting`), it takes over the other coworker wakes still queued in
  that thread with the same payer, made no later than the run, oldest first: each passes its own run's checks under its
  own row lock (`qorpera_wake_authorized`, its chain not stopped under the chain lock taken shared, its pair conversation
  KEY SHARE SKIP LOCKED), its sender's folder reads are copied into the run under the taken wake's id (so the reads check
  and erasure cover them), its message becomes a delivered row of `qorpera_pending_peer_messages` (new column `wake_id`)
  that holds the wake: it stays queued under the run. The run sees them in
  `wake.payload.heldMessages` in full, with their senders, oldest first, up to 12 lines and 19200 bytes of shown text;
  the rest wait for the next run, which takes them the same way. When the taking run's job completes, the
  materializer cancels a held wake as delivered ("Delivered with another coworker message in run …"). When the job
  fails or is cancelled (a Stop of the run's chain included), the held wake becomes a run of its own, through the same
  job-status wake-up and due scan that start any wake that waited behind a live run. It does not if its own chain is
  stopped. The drain never offers a hold as a held note, so a message reaches one live run at a time. A taken message
  is answered under the taking run's chain: what the agent sends from that run carries that run's causal root and hop
  count. A Stop of the taken message's own chain after the take cancels its held wake but does not reach the taking run
  or its answer; a Stop of the taking run's chain ends both (contract 14.6, the 2026-11-17 amendment). A lone message,
  the send and its receipt are unchanged; no cap on chain depth or message count. Held notes are now cut at 1600 bytes, not 400
  characters, and a cut one ends ` … [cut: the full message is in your transcript with this coworker]`; a note of the
  pair whose transcript the wake shows is left out only when that transcript (400 characters a line) shows it whole. The context
  message's heldMessages line and the coworker wake's protocol say the list also holds messages that waited for this
  run, to be treated like the message that woke it; the note form of `send_coworker_message` no longer names 400
  characters; contract 14.6 gains the amendment (the staff instruction pins and the tool catalog pins move). Deploy order: either; the
  payload keeps the shape every live bundle parses (`heldMessages`), so the migration works with the bundles live today
  and the runtime text change needs no migration.

  LXD bootstraps; the native Codex stays `9c299f88`.
- Agents show the product first (hire overlay v19; the founder, 17 Nov 2026, on the lead's review of the test company

  the outcome its own. The board kit's choose guide says the poster carries the direction; ask_member no longer names an

  (ask_member): `a1ff85fb723361253d3f9c9c6ab309d613fd56c5cf8d940983654367c7c1265f` and

- The Claude Code box offers every built-in tool of 2.1.325's print mode but ten (the founder, 16 Nov 2026: "make sure
  they are running with bypass permissions, and all permissions turned ON"; lanes/agent-box-20261113/harness-permissions/
  AUDIT.md; the runtime bundle only). `CLAUDE_BOX_TOOLS` goes from 8 to 18: Task (sub-agents), WebSearch, Skill,
  ToolSearch, TaskCreate/TaskGet/TaskList/TaskUpdate, ReportFindings and Monitor join Bash, Read, Edit, Write,
  NotebookEdit, Grep, Glob and TaskStop. Kept out, with their reasons in `CLAUDE_BOX_TOOLS_KEPT_OUT`: WebFetch and
  Workflow (the sign-in), EnterWorktree and ExitWorktree (git inside the box, beside the sign-in, on a repository config
  the agent controls; `git worktree` works in the agent's own shell), ListAgents and SendMessage (the member's own
  Claude sessions outside the run), ScheduleWakeup (it keeps Claude Code running after its report) and the three cron
  tools (a durable job lands in the company folder). Task and WebSearch run on the member's own Claude plan, as the run
  does, and draw more of it. The run's steps say what Monitor, Task, WebSearch and Skill were asked. Codex's start is
  unchanged.
- Extra high on GPT (contract 17, the second 2026-11-16 amendment; migration `20260930788000`, SQL test 1285). The model policy declares `threadEfforts: ["xhigh"]`; `allowed` stays low, medium, high (`CLOUD_RUNTIME_ROUTINE_EFFORTS`, the routine tools' set), and a run's effort may be `xhigh` (`CLOUD_RUNTIME_REASONING_EFFORTS`, the validators of a run's effort). Under a bundle that declares it, the setter takes `xhigh` on a GPT model and a member's turn pins it, so Codex runs with `model_reasoning_effort="xhigh"`; under one without it, a conversation saved at `xhigh` runs at high. The answers name `efforts` per listed model. Deploy order: (1) apply `20260930788000` (alone it changes nothing a member sees: the setter still refuses `xhigh` on GPT, `efforts` names GPT low to high); (2) the release cut regenerates the bundle (model policy hash changes) and guards its activation on `to_regprocedure('public.qorpera_model_policy_thread_efforts(jsonb)')`, since an older kernel refuses a manifest with `threadEfforts`; the native provider host (runner image) and the coding executor roll with it, both before the switch: each checks a run's effort (the provider host its admission and Codex's configuration, the executor every coding and machine command's tool authority, `parseCloudNativeToolAuthority`), and the live builds refuse `xhigh`, so an older executor would refuse every command of an extra-high turn; the shared vault's bytes move too (the policy constants) but it reads no effort, so it may stay; (3) the web (`efforts` in the answer) is safe before and after: it offers Extra high on GPT only when `efforts` names it.
- The agent's home for every company (the founder, 16 November 2026, to "Switch it on for every company in the next release, with the node 1 fix?": "Yes, next release"; migration `20260930789000`, SQL test 1286; contract, the 2026-11-16 amendment "the agent's home for every company"). `qorpera_home_settings` gains `every_company` (boolean, not null, false by default), and `qorpera_company_home_allowed` is the platform switch AND (`every_company` OR the company's row in `qorpera_home_companies`) AND the sharing rule `qorpera_folder_home_shared`, as before; its signature, grants and security are unchanged. The migration turns `every_company` on only where the platform switch's row is on, so a database whose switch is off or absent stays off; the listed companies (Brooks) keep their rows. Everything that asks the gate follows it: a run's home (`qorpera_company_home_available`), the Claude Code worker's machine (`qorpera_claude_worker_home`) and the Claude models in a conversation (`qorpera_member_may_pick_claude`, still behind the Claude sign-in list). A machine made from an image without the pinned Codex or `sftp-server` answers its home `HOME_OUTDATED`: a Codex run's home calls get those words and the run goes on with the company's own tools; a Claude Code worker ends blocked with them and its agent is told. It rides the next release with #1880, never before it. The way back, one statement, leaves the listed companies on: `update public.qorpera_home_settings set every_company = false where singleton;`
- Company mail handles and companies with no name yet (Jonas, 17 November 2026: "go with your picks, build it"; lanes/company-mail-handles-20261117, PR B; the database is PR A, migration `20260930796000`, the web PR C). A company named `No name yet` has no name: the constitution tells every run (a worker's too) to call it "the company" and never invent or use a name in messages, files or email. `ask_member`'s question takes an optional `input: {kind: "mail_address"}`, the address card, only with `allowCustom: true` (refused otherwise, with the reason, new card or stored); a new address card's options are handles (`^[a-z0-9]([a-z0-9-]{0,22}[a-z0-9])$`), each value the same as its label or left out; the member's answer is the address they made, as a custom answer. To keep `ask_member`'s schema under its 4600-byte bound (Codex drops field descriptions near 5000), an option's `preview` and `allowCustom` descriptions are shorter, saying the same, and `input` has no description of its own: the tool description's new line says what an address card is. The founding texts never name `ask_member`, since a group chat's instructions must not. The Co-Founder never opens the company's first address: once the company has a name and owns none, it asks the founder on an address card with one to three handles made from the name, and not while the company has no name; the hello is that card's text when the card goes out in the same run. Founding offers up to three names once on a question card with a Not yet option, asks again only when something waits for the name, can be agreed with no name (the first line stays `# No name yet`), and the Brand owner's first identity is then colours and a mark without lettering. After the founding, the Co-Founder's role says: when the company has a name and owns no email address, never open one itself but raise the same address card in its next run with the founders (runs with `manage_hook`), and while it has no name, offer names only when something waits for one. `manage_hook`'s mailbox `localPart` is `<box>.<handle>` under a handle the company owns (box 1 to 39, handle 2 to 24), its description and schema say so, and the database's two new refusals reach the model. The action reviewer's instructions no longer call the Co-Founder opening the company's main mailbox requested: the first address is a founder's, on the address card, so a first address under a new handle is not requested, and another under a handle the company owns is judged like any mailbox. `PLATFORM_UPDATES` entry `no-name-and-mail-handles` (a change in behaviour, 17 November 2026). The founding and constitution pins move.
- Release v252 (conversation models GPT-6.1 Sol, Fable 5.2 and Opus 5.5, #1877; the move of all of a member's chats
  waiting on a used-up login, #1876, merged in the release branch; and master since v251): runtime bundle 252
  `ac0e54611d0dbf9ca095972482ebe4ed36ff93c428f019e5a29a513040ceb206` (activation `20260929025299`, previous 251
  `27a9118662d8`). Its activation requires the release's two migrations, which the roll's `migrate` runs before the switch,
  in this order: `20260930784000` (bundle 252 declares `modelPolicy.claudeChoice`, which an older kernel refuses) and
  `20260930785000` (#1876's web calls its two functions). The guest moves: the native provider host (`e6f30e63`) alone; the
  native Codex stays `9c299f88`.
- Fixed: `fill_company_account`'s helper found no browser on a real machine (`BROWSER_NOT_OPEN`), so every machine image
  build from master stopped at its fill check (16 Nov 2026, the 8a image). The desktop's Chromium runs sandboxed, and its
  browser process then rewrites its command line into one string, its arguments joined by spaces (no NUL between them);
  `machineChromiumPort` read only NUL-separated arguments and so never saw the profile. A command line of one string is
  now read word by word; the profile, the `--type=` refusal, the pinned binary, the desktop's account and the loopback
  socket are checked as before. #1644's own proof ran Chromium with `--no-sandbox`, which keeps the arguments apart.
  Proof on a test machine, the image's desktop in a nested VM with the AppArmor profile loaded, the build's fill check
  word for word: `deploy/machine/test/prove-account-fill.sh`.
- A job guest's runner takes no job before its native company host listens (16 Nov 2026: Room reconstruction run `009ad432` and Brand run `0fb578d3` failed with `fetch failed` 0.1 s after their runner started; the member saw an internal error). The host unit is `Type=exec` and makes its cloud calls before it listens, so the runner activated, a queued job arrived at once, and its execute POST to `127.0.0.1` was refused. The runner now waits for the bridge's `GET /healthz` (no credential, at most 5 min) before it activates. A refused connection to the bridge is `Native provider bridge is not listening [code=NATIVE_BRIDGE_CONNECTION:TRANSPORT_UNAVAILABLE]`, a pre-turn cut-off retried on attempts 1 and 2 (nothing reached the host, so the database requeues the job pristine); a third failure reads as a lost connection on the web. Any other lost connection stays as before. No SQL, no web. The guest moves: the ephemeral runner (`qorpera-ephemeral-runner.mjs`), so it takes effect with the next job guest image.
- Claude first for coding and design work (the founder's rule of 16 Nov 2026; migration `20260930787000`, SQL test 1284). A plain worker whose brief names no harness is resolved by the dispatch, in the index's transaction: it runs on Claude Code (`harness_default`, the member's Claude login recorded as for an asked one) when it has access write and its member can use Claude Code now (`qorpera_claude_worker_problem` answers nothing: a member's word, on the Claude list, sign-in open, an active Claude login, the agent's own machine running for that member's work), else on Codex; each follow-up resolves it again. A brief may name `harness: codex` when a member asks for Codex, and an asked harness wins. A Claude Code run of such a worker that ends blocked for a Claude-side reason (`claudeSide`: signed out, not open, plan limit, could not start, or cut before any step) calls `qorpera_claude_worker_fall_back` (scope binding, in the path control and NATIVE_HOST_CALLS) instead of reporting: the same worker, same sequence, goes back to the drain without its Claude harness or login, its dispatch's context names the files Claude Code changed, an event `fell_back` keeps the reason, and the drain starts it on Codex under `worker-dispatch:<id>:<sequence>:codex` (the wake bus admits that key for a plain Codex worker only). A node without the Claude Code worker's composition asks the same before it refuses the run. An asked Claude Code worker still ends blocked in words. Defaulted workers take the member's one-at-a-time Claude Code lease, as asked ones do. The staff instructions say to leave harness out unless a member asks; platform update `claude-first-for-workers`. Staff instruction pins `beda60c7d3a9` → `9cccae895f75480c500c7f6f2f488ccf14e463496feabdd59f2ac772c95edce2` (conversation and private); the worker's and the prompts' unchanged.
- A Claude Code worker's run is claimed only by a shared execution host's orchestrator (migration `20260930786000`, SQL test 1283; 16 Nov 2026: node 1 has no shared execution host, and a Claude Code worker there fails in stage `claude-worker`: its one-VM guest has no `/dev/fuse` and no box group). `qorpera_runtime_claim_turn` answers no, before any dispatch role is read, when the asking orchestrator has no bound execution host (`qorpera_execution_host_principals`) and the run's worker (`qorpera_run_worker`) names `harness = 'claude-code'`; every other run, and every role and wait of the claim turn, is as before. So such a run waits in the queue while the shared host is full (its refusal's 15 s, then it claims again), says it is not its work, is silent or drains for a roll, and nothing else takes it; a day in the queue still ends it ("No computer could start the run within a day"). A Claude Code worker's run that the shared host can never take (a sponsor other than the Codex subscription harness, or a login homed only on another adapter) therefore waits the whole day, holding its member's Claude lease; no production path to that is known today. deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-IDENTICAL-NODES.md's claim turn says the same. Test 1268's fixture binds the base orchestrator as a shared host, so the Claude Code tests built on it (1269, 1270, 1281) still start their runs. SQL only: no runtime bundle, no orchestrator or node program.
- A Claude Code worker's report no longer names Claude Code's own temporary file (16 Nov 2026: run 7a174bc9's file list named `product/backup-review.md` and `product/backup-review.md.tmp.9.58b93b9e96db`). The pinned 2.1.325 writes a file atomically through a sibling `<file>.tmp.<pid>.<12 hex>` (its pid, six random bytes in lowercase hex) and renames it; a machine view's boundary that came while it stood committed it, and `qorpera_claude_worker_run_facts` lists every file the run's commits wrote. The report now leaves out a name of exactly that shape when the run also wrote `<file>` (the results file included; the pair compared by folder key, as the folder compares names); any other name, a member's own included, stays (`claudeWorkerReportedOutputs`). Only Claude Code's own name is covered: the run facts still list every path the run's view wrote, also one gone again since. The runtime bundle only; no SQL, no node program.
- Claude in a conversation (contract 17, the 2026-11-16 amendment; migration `20260930784000`, SQL test 1281). The Model menu's models are `gpt-6-astra`, `gpt-6.1-sol`, `claude-fable-5-2` and `claude-opus-5-5`; xhigh and max only with Claude; the answers name `models`. The model policy declares `claudeChoice: "thread"`. Under a bundle that declares it, a member's turn by a member who may run Claude Code pins the conversation's Claude pick on its run, a run carrying on its chain keeps it, the run's context names `claude: {model, effort}`, and the turn instructions hand real work to a Claude Code worker; the GPT front runs on its default model and effort. A Claude Code worker records the pick, its dispatch wake carries `claude`, and the box starts Claude Code with `--model` and `--effort`. Deploy order: apply `20260930784000` before any bundle that declares `claudeChoice` activates (an older kernel refuses that manifest, so no run of it could start); the activation guards on `qorpera_execution_runs.claude_model` or `qorpera_pin_execution_claude_choice()`. The migration alone offers GPT-6.1 Sol and no Claude.
- Release v251 (the Claude Code box's scratch root beside the run's root, #1873; and master since v250: the Data list,
  company databases slice 10, and the folder service's data role and the folder program's `data` command, slice 8a):
  runtime bundle 251 `27a9118662d860eecae26f6b94168adaea1c75e5279a62c0e5c06e23a83bae72` (activation `20260929025199`,
  previous 250 `78618f12bdb6`). Its activation requires the release's two migrations, which the roll's `migrate` runs
  before the switch: `20260930779000` and `20260930782000`. The guest moves: the native provider host (`2c4b7f47`) alone;
  the native Codex stays `9c299f88`.
- A Claude Code worker's box starts in a shared run slot (16 Nov 2026: the founder's first worker on v250 ended "Claude Code could not start on Qorpera's side"). The box's scratch root was `<run root>/claude-box`, under the slot's `/var/lib/qorpera-shared-execution/slots/<n>/host/qorpera-native-run-<run>-XXXXXX` (109-110 bytes), so its shell socket's path was 131 bytes and its listen failed (Linux holds 107). The scratch root is now `qorpera-claude-box-XXXXXX` beside the run's root, in the host's temporary root (the slot's host folder: the unit may write there, the slot's wipe empties it, a one-VM host's `/var/lib/qorpera-native-provider/tmp` too), at most 85 bytes with its sockets; the worker removes it with the run as before. Every socket path of the box (the shell's, the egress relay's) is checked against one bound (100 bytes, the relay's) before anything of the home opens, and the shell's listen checks it too. A box that did not start keeps the member's words; the run's provider session names the step that failed and a fixed error code, `claude-code/<pin>/none/none/box-<step>[:<code>]` (for example `box-socket-path`, `box-shell-socket:EINVAL`), and the host's journal says the same. Moves: the native provider host and the runtime bundle; no SQL, no web.
- Agents' words about company databases say how to answer a question a member asked on the Data page's list (slice 10 part g; words only, no SQL, the runtime bundle only; staff runs, not workers): a member's message whose first line is `> page:data#<database>.<table> · <about>` (or `#<database>:<question>`) was asked on that list; every member may read that conversation, so the agent answers only from rows every member may read (the number or the few rows first, never the SQL; for a database only some members may read, or one whose questions name `:viewer`, it says in one line that the list itself shows them) and offers Keep as a list on a question card, and on yes writes the question as `data/<database>/queries/<name>.sql` (at most 60 characters of `a-z` and `0-9`, a `-` only between them) with `--` lines in the member's words, so it shows beside the tables. The sentence stands after the paragraph's last one, apart from part d's (#1868). Pins: the staff instructions (a conversation and a private conversation) `b83cea836544` (part d's) → `beda60c7d3a9bd03bd8dd945ac5c2292e9a6062bcbdc5747d35e7e8cf7885102`, measured on a test VM on the tree merged with master after parts d, e and f; the worker's and the three prompts do not move.
- Agents' words about company databases say who sees a database's whole tables (the Data list, slice 10 part d; words only, no SQL, the runtime bundle only): members see whole tables when they may read the folder and change rows when they may write it, and a database whose rows are private to each member (its questions name `:viewer`) says `members = "writers"` under `[list]` in its database.toml, so only those who may write the folder see whole tables. The gateway serves that rule from part b (#1865) and the writing from part c (#1866); this goes out only once they are live. Pins: the staff instructions (a conversation and a private conversation) `122b203975a8` → `b83cea836544021b3c4e8b04fb01603a28839fbdb4de4ec2f44e9980623e5474`, the worker's `1b0960ecac63` → `4ecdde26333cfc305d263663ad41c07578885ea56214b993c8c1b9d9e1b1989b`, measured on node 2's test VM on the tree merged with v250's master; the three prompts do not move.
