- Agents' words about sites say what is true since site hosts went on (own code storage, step 5.1, D1b; site hosts are on since release v247; words only, no SQL, the runtime bundle only). `publish_site` publishes to a private address of its own, `https://<slug>.qorpera.com/`; its files may link each other from `/` or relatively; each site is its own origin (no sandbox: browser storage, cookies and frames of its own pages work; other sites cannot frame it or load its files; no service worker; never a cookie for all of qorpera.com), as site-serve answers on a site host. The slug field of `publish_site` and `share_app` says the address and the kernel's name rule (`SITE_NAME_RULE`; Codex sends no pattern). After a publish or a share the agent puts the address in its message as a link, where the member sees the site's row and owners and admins copy the password (web #1848), and never writes a password. The coding session reads that a site is served at the top of its own address, so links from `/` work (the collect lets them through while hosts are on), and its checks file's `name` follows the name rule. Going live: a repository's website shows under the agent's thread and in What's new, where an owner or admin may look at it first, the agent's one line says what changes for customers, and an urgent fix or Go back is asked of an owner or admin (only they can; the kernel answers anyone else 42501). The publish and share answers' `password` words, `publish_site`'s refusal of a source under code/ and the platform update `site-addresses` (`cloud-platform-updates.ts`; its id and date stay) name owners and admins too. Pins: the staff instructions (a conversation and a private conversation) `e5fb4abd8824` → `df04c4e4ec9f04528ed39273af9b2a20771e5a038343ea4b77b28c8b01f85e6d`, measured on node 2's test VM on master's tree; the worker's instructions, the three prompts and both catalog pins do not move (the site tools are opened on demand through `more_tools`, outside the catalog as sent).
- An agent signs in to a website from its company machine with a company account, and never sees the password (Jonas, 10 Nov 2026: "Build machine sign-ins", "they should be able to use the vault too"; migration `20260930781000`, SQL test 1279; deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-COMPANY-ACCOUNTS.md "Machines"). New machine tool `fill_company_account {operationKey, machine, account, field: login|password|code}` (toolVersion `qorpera.machine-tools/v1`, receipt key `fill_company_account:<operationKey>`, review external), offered to a company agent's own run, never a worker's, on a host with the vault's company accounts and a machine gateway that has `accountFill`: it types the account's value into the field that has the focus in the machine's browser, and answers `{filled: {machine, account, field}}` or `{filled: false, refused: {reason, message}}`; no value is in its input, output or receipt. The gateway's `/v1/machine-commands/account-fill {commandId, envelope, idempotencyKey}` (`CloudMachineAccountFillRefusal`, 409 with a code and, for `VAULT_REFUSED`, the database's words) runs the image's new fill helper (`cloud-machine-account-fill.ts`, program `cloud-machine-account-fill-main.ts`): it reads the executor's target on stdin, checks the focused tab through the DevTools port `desktop open` gives the desktop's Chromium (taken only when the desktop's own pinned Chromium holds it on 127.0.0.1), makes an X25519 key pair in memory and prints only its public half; the executor's box comes second on stdin, sealed by the vault to that key and bound to the machine, its placement and its boot (`CompanyAccountFillIdentity`'s machine kind); the helper opens it, checks the tab again against the hosts sealed in it (https, the account's site or a sign-in host, the page's own document, an input box of the field's kind, no CAPTCHA, check or block page), sets the value on the checked box from inside its document and prints one code. Both checks and the typing are bound to the document the tab showed at the first look (its main frame's loader, the helper's own isolated world made in it and never again elsewhere, and the tab's Page-domain navigation events): any navigation, reload, closed tab or lost world before typing refuses with `FILL_PAGE_CHANGED`, and the address and document are checked again right before and right after the value goes in (review round 1 of #1644: a redirect between the address read and the typing could otherwise put the value into an autofocused box of another site). The value is never typed at the tab (`Input.insertText` reaches whatever document the tab shows when it lands; review round 2): one `Runtime.callFunctionOn` on the checked box, in the helper's world of the bound document (refused by the browser once that document is gone), checks the world's stamp, the address and the box's focus and sets the value with the native value setter, then fires `input` and `change`. `FILLED` means that same box, still with the focus, holds the value exactly and a complete scan (iterative, no depth limit, the document, every open shadow root and every frame of its own origin, within 250 000 elements) finds no copy in any other input or text area, at two looks 200 ms apart; once the value may have reached the page anything else is `FILL_LOST` (review round 3: there is no `FILL_UNCONFIRMED`; the box and copies found are emptied, but a page can copy the value again at any time), whose words say the value may still be on the page, to close the tab, and to set the account `needs_person` if unsure. No result suggests a screenshot: every one (`filled`'s `next` and each refusal) ends by saying to take no screenshot of the tab until it is closed or has gone to another page, and the description says a `desktop shot` of a page only before the vault types into it. Proof against the image's pinned Chromium: `scripts/test-machine-account-fill-browser.mjs` (Docker on a test machine; probe `packages/runtime/test/fixtures/machine-account-fill-browser-probe.ts`). The vault: `CompanyAccountVault.machineFill`, the broker's `companyAccountMachineFill` and `readCompanyAccountMachineFill` (`qorpera_fill_company_account_on_machine`), and `/v1/machine-account-fills` on the coding executor's token only (`parseCompanyAccountMachineFillRequest`). `company_account`'s description and "Accounts on websites" say the vault types a value on a machine with `fill_company_account`; `fill_company_account` joins the observation vocabulary. The company run's tool catalog pin moved (company_account's description).
- The Claude Code box runs Claude Code with its commit and pull-request attribution off (`attribution: {commit: "", pr: ""}` in the box's read-only settings file): the pinned 2.1.325 otherwise tells itself, in a reminder beside the prompt, to end commit messages with a Co-Authored-By line and pull-request descriptions with a "Generated with Claude Code" line, and a worker's commits and pull requests look the same whichever harness ran it.
- A worker that runs on Claude Code (agent-box slice C4c, the composition; migration `20260930772000`, SQL test 1270). Agents hear of it as the platform update `claude-code-workers` (a capability, 15 November 2026), and `manage_worker`'s harness field says to give such a worker access write; the tool catalog's text pin moves with it. A plain worker the agent handed to Claude Code (C4a) is no longer refused at the divert on a host that carries the vault's Claude sign-in route and the home channel: its run starts no Codex, is admitted with Claude Code's own eight tools as its catalog and its brief as its context, opens its member's sealed sign-in for this run (C4b) into a private login folder (0700, files 0600, overwritten and removed at the end, also on a stop or a failure), starts the Claude Code box on the agent's own machine (C2, with a short system note through the new `--append-system-prompt-file` option of the box), keeps each tool call as one step of the run's own work log, seals `.credentials.json` back early when Claude Code renewed it and again at the end when it changed again, and writes Claude Code's last message as the worker's results through `report_work`'s own path (succeeded whenever Claude Code's result is a success, or blocked with words the agent can act on: the sign-in could not be opened, the sign-in ended or Claude Code not open, the plan's limit in Claude Code's own words, read only from its own error marks and an error result, the home's own refusals, the box that did not start, other tools than the eight or a tool before its first line, a run cut short). A follow-up's prompt says where the worker's earlier work is and what its last report said; the report's file list is read once the machine view's last boundary is in (read again until it stands still, bounded). The run's provider session names Claude Code's build and the model its init line named. SQL: a Claude Code worker with write access shares its agent's machine with the agent's own rights (`qorpera_folder_home_shared`), a read one is refused at the dispatch and at a follow-up, the dispatch's home check asks what the worker's run will ask, and `qorpera_claude_worker_run_facts` gives the run its member's name and what its home changed.
- A plain worker that Claude Code does, on a member's word and that member's own Claude sign-in (agent-box slice C4a, lanes/agent-box-20261113/C4-WORKER-DESIGN.md as the lead ruled it; migration `20260930770000`, SQL test 1268; nothing runs Claude Code yet). `manage_worker` takes `harness: "claude-code"` on a worker without species coding (its harness field says when; codex stays the unsaid default and is dropped), its brief names it, and the database checks, in words the agent can act on, a member behind the ask, the Claude list and sign-in, an active Claude login and the agent's machine running for that member's work. The worker records the member's Claude login as its payer; its wake names the harness, and one Claude Code run per member goes at a time (the drain's lease; a second worker waits, not due until the lease ends, so waiting workers never fill a drain's pass). Until the composition exists (C4c), the company runtime refuses such a wake at its divert in stage `claude-worker` (`NATIVE_CLAUDE_WORKER:UNKNOWN`, not retried), so it never runs on Codex. One line in a staff run's instructions; the conversation and private instruction pins and the tool catalog pin moved.
- A Claude Code worker's run gets its member's Claude sign-in from the shared vault for that run only, and the credentials Claude Code renewed go back (agent-box slice C4b; migration `20260930771000`, SQL test 1269; wired into no run yet, C4c calls it). The vault (`CloudClaudeLoginVault`, `cloud-claude-login-vault.ts`; `POST /v1/claude-logins/open` and `/seal` under the runtime token and the run's lease proof, shared vault only) opens the sealed `qorpera.claude-login-files/v1` files only when the database says the run is the worker run of a plain worker with harness claude-code at work, the login is the one its dispatch recorded and still active with its member on the Claude list, and the member's Claude Code lease is this run's; a signed-out login answers `SIGNED_OUT`, a member off the list or a sign-in switched off `NOT_OPEN`. Its files are checked for shape only, never read. The seal-back takes `.credentials.json` (and `.claude.json` in #1428's shape), keeps a file the run did not send as the run opened it, seals the next version under the member's key and swaps it in only if the version and envelope the run names are still the login's (`stale` otherwise: the newer one stays); the `sealed` answer names the new version and envelope digest. A seal-back is also taken after the worker reported or was cancelled, while the member's lease is this run's and live: the vault re-opens the login for it through its own entry (`qorpera_open_claude_login_for_seal_back`, every check of the open but the worker at work), never through the open route's, which gives such a worker nothing. A retry after a lost answer answers `stale` with the version named plus one: the caller treats that as possibly its own seal. The run host's side is `openClaudeLoginForRun` and `sealClaudeLoginBack` (`cloud-secret-broker-http.ts`: nothing is sent when no file changed; each `unchanged` or `sealed` answer carries the base the run seals from next, so a run may seal right after Claude Code's start and again at the end). `checkClaudeLoginFiles` is exported for it.
- The Claude Code box on the run host (agent-box slice C2, lanes/agent-box-20261113/CLAUDE-HOME-BUILD.md; not wired to any run yet, the runners' gates stay closed until C4). `launchClaudeCodeBox` (`cloud-claude-box.ts`) starts the unmodified, pinned Claude Code 2.1.325 (`deploy/first-node/native-claude/linux-x64-artifact.json`) in a bwrap box with P4's set-up A5: the sign-in on descriptor 3 or Claude Code's own login folder at `/slot/config`, deny rules on `/slot` and `/opt/claude` only, `--settings` read-only with `--setting-sources user --strict-mcp-config`, HOME and the config folder never on the home, egress to `api.anthropic.com` (plus `platform.claude.com` for a login folder, where Claude Code renews its token). Bash runs on the home through `CLAUDE_CODE_SHELL_PREFIX` and one exec-server channel per run (`cloud-claude-home-shell.ts`, the prefix `cloud-claude-home-shell-client.ts`); Read, Edit, Write and NotebookEdit reach the home's `/company`, agent home folder and `/tmp` through sshfs over the session's sftp channel, mounted in a user, mount and PID namespace of the run's own (`deploy/claude-box/`), never in the run host's. The native entry passes descriptor 3 on to the program only when the box names it. Claude Code offers exactly the tools the driver names with `--tools` (default and slice 1: Bash, Read, Edit, Write, NotebookEdit, Grep, Glob, TaskStop); Grep and Glob run the home's own ripgrep through the box's `rg` bridge. An abort of the run's signal ends the box, the mount and both channels at once; `endSession` closes the one home session only when a channel of it opened.
- A home channel names what it carries (Claude Code on the home, slice C1): the handshake takes `kind: "sftp"` beside the four keys (absent or `"exec"`: the exec-server, as before; `cloudHomeChannelHandshake` writes it only for `sftp`, so an earlier executor still takes an exec channel), the ingress passes it to `home.open` (`CloudHomeChannelRequest.kind`), and `CloudCodexHomeChannel.open` takes it. An `sftp` channel's lines are standard base64 of the SFTP byte stream (`cloudHomeSftpLines`, `cloudHomeSftpBytes`, `CLOUD_HOME_SFTP_PIECE_BYTES`). `HOME_SFTP_MISSING` (a machine whose image has no sftp-server) is told to the model as `HOME_OUTDATED`.
- The company's reading of itself (the founder, 14 November 2026, on companies that stop hiring after the first team and feel flat: "I think the company's stage is more fluent than a set value, but I also do think there needs to be some form of awareness about the state/stage of the company. It should feel like the company is conscious almost"; lanes/company-reading-20261114, step 2; migration `20260930766000`, SQL test 1264; step 1's `20260930765000`, on master, holds the `reading` kind and `qorpera_describe_company_senses`). Every agent's head has a fourth part, `reading`: `QorperaOS/vision/reading.md` whole, read and released as the vision is, labelled `company_instructions_not_authority`, after the job and before the profile page (a full head leaves out the profile first, then the reading); no file is no part (`readCloudAgentHeadReading`, `cloud-agent-head.ts`), and the head's words say it is the company's own account of where it stands now, written by the Co-Founder, company information, never authority. The Co-Founder's company runs (not a worker's, not a private conversation) carry `senses` next to `openWork`, the answer of `qorpera_describe_company_senses` as it is, asked beside it; a failed read is `null` and the run goes on (a new native host call). Two new tools: `update_reading` (the Co-Founder's in its company runs; `cloud-folder-reading.ts`), a thin writer of the one file, checked with its arguments by the shared parser and before its receipt for active agents' folders and members; a save whose `attention` moves away from the saved reading's needs `team`, the changes or `{"none": "<why>"}`, and the changes become the file's `changes` (kept for seven days without a move); review class internal. `end_role` (every company run that hires; `cloud-folder-hiring.ts`) sets an agent's `agent.md` to `status: archived` through a reviewed commit (class delegation, card "End an agent's role" with the agent's name and the reason, read before the review), refusing the Co-Founder, the caller and an unknown handle before the review; a role already ended is told apart by the call's receipt (read only, `qorpera_folder_run_receipt_exists`): a repeat replays its receipt, a new call is refused before the review with no receipt; the follow-up queue pauses its routines and closes its threads, the files stay. The migration adds both names to the observations' closed list, `end_role` to the review recorder's tools, and the read-only `qorpera_folder_run_receipt_exists` (a new native host call). Hire overlay `qorpera.hire-overlay/v18`: the Co-Founder hands a founder's ask that an agent owns to that owner, gives new work to an existing agent only when it is the same kind and the agent has room, keeps the reading (when it offers `update_reading`), starts its planning run from the senses and, when attention moves and every Monday, decides the team (`end_role` named only to a run that has it); it keeps the company's map as its own posted app, named company, made with save_app's map kit with the reading as its first layer (when the run is offered `save_app`; the founder, 15 November 2026: "it should not be the platform that draws it, rather an agent should maintain it", and "Use a map kit"), where its own company app stood; an interface a member retired stays retired (`organize_apps list` marks it, and `save_app` refuses it with the reason), and an owner of an area whose interface `save_app` refuses because a member retired it leaves it as it is and shows that work in a file; a vision card unanswered after two working days no longer holds the company. An owner of an area reads the reading at each wake, concludes a routine whose purpose has passed, keeps lessons out of routines' instructions, and tells the Co-Founder when the reading is wrong about its area. `hire_agent`'s description says the same as the overlay about an existing coworker. Under tools on demand (T3-6) `update_reading` is carried in full in the Co-Founder's catalog (`CLOUD_DIRECT_TOOLS`: it is used in every planning run and whenever the reading stops being true), and `end_role` opens through `more_tools` ("end an agent's role when its work is over") in every run that has it, as seldom used as the other team tools there; both descriptions are contracts in T3-5's form. Its entry in `PLATFORM_UPDATES` (`company-reading`, a change in behaviour, 15 November 2026) tells the Co-Founder in its next planning run: keep the reading with update_reading, owe a team decision when attention moves (end_role among its answers), and make the company's map from the reading with the map kit. The instruction pins, the tool catalog pins and the native host call list move.
- Release v249 (the agent's home, switched off: #1761, #1760 and #1817; the agents' instruction stack #1811; and master since
  v248): runtime bundle 249 `049019cdd704d376c49d9c09a1c38d219d74c936f9d412a0b8d847fc44c4eb0b` (activation `20260929024999`,
  previous 248 `cc7b46c6bab4`). Its activation requires the release's five migrations, which the roll's `migrate` runs
  before the coding executors and the switch: `20260930752000`, `20260930754000`, `20260930776000`, `20260930777000` and
  `20260930778000`. The guest moves in three files: the native provider host (`a4d73f4d`), the LXD runner bootstrap

- The Co-Founder reflects on the platform's updates (the founder, 15 November 2026: "I would like to develop something entirely different - sort of a reflection mechanic that the co-founder runs on an update hook. not any update, but when a newer better model is released, or an update to behavior or platform capability drops, it should reflect. if it's a model-drop theres a lot to go over and review since we now have smarter brains. if it's a platform capability that's been added, the co-founder may want to use it. like the interface kit, or if it was added support for banking or other stuff. I hope such a hook is possible"; shown the lead's design, he said "continue"; QORPERA-RUNTIME-CONTRACT.md, the 2026-11-15 amendment; migration `20260930780000`, SQL test 1278). `PLATFORM_UPDATES` (`cloud-platform-updates.ts`) is the bundle's list of what changed for agents: an id, a kind (`model`, `behaviour`, `capability`), the day its PR was written (the seeds: the day they went live), a title (60 bytes) and a paragraph to the Co-Founder (700 bytes). Seeded with what is live by bundle 248: `site-addresses` (v247), `company-databases`, `shape-kits` and `map-kit` (v248). The company's record is `QorperaOS/vision/reflections.md`, plain markdown, one line `- <id> | <day> | <use|later|not|reviewed> | <line>` per reflection, newest last, read tolerantly (every line, up to 256 KiB; the newest line for an id wins). An update is pending when it is dated no more than 30 days (`UPDATE_MARGIN_DAYS`) before the day the company's `QorperaOS/vision` folder was made (seeded at creation, never moved), so a company created between a PR's day and its release still gets it, and has no line, or its newest is `later` and older than 28 days. A record the run cannot read gives `updates` only a `problem`: keep only the newest line for each update (no list, so nothing already decided comes back). A Co-Founder's planning run (its routine runs in the company, not a worker's) reads them beside `openWork` and gets the context key `updates` (`{pending: [{id, kind, date, title, text}], more?}`, at most 6, oldest first) when its run has `record_reflection`; the two reads are released with the context; a failed read leaves the key out and logs "Platform updates were not read (<category>)". Its planning run opens with the lead's sentence group (`PLANNING_REFLECT`): a capability is taken up now, later or not; a new model makes the run a review of the work made before; a change in behaviour adjusts routines and job descriptions; each is recorded and the founders are told in one message. `record_reflection {operationKey, update, decision, line}` (the Co-Founder's company runs only, opened through `more_tools`; review class internal, no card) refuses an unknown id, `reviewed` for a capability and `use`, `later` or `not` for a model before writing, answers `unchanged` for the same decision on the same day, and writes the record under its receipt (`saved`, `conflict`, refused; past 64 KiB it is refused with the advice to keep only the newest line for each update). The closed list of tool operations takes `record_reflection` (20260930780000). AGENTS.md: a PR that gives agents a capability, a model or a behaviour they should take up adds its entry, dated the day the PR is written; a test fails when a model of `AGENT_MODELS` is neither one of today's three nor named by a `model` update. `scripts/check-comments.mjs` takes a SQL test's `-- @fixture <file> until "<marker>"` line as a directive, as the test runner needs it first. No wake of its own: a company without a planning routine reflects once it has one again.
- Two more shape kits for interface apps, `ring` and `flow` (company views plan step 5, lanes/company-views-20261114/PLAN.md; Jonas liked the sketches "duty ring", "week ring" and "money river"; no migration). `ring` draws a year, a month or a week as one ring: its months or days round it, the part gone by a shade darker, a hand at now (the page's clock, or `now`), marks for points in time and wedges for stretches, wedges with amounts reaching out as far as their amount; the middle holds the next thing (the next mark, today's included, or the next wedge to begin, else one going on) and how many days off it is, or `centre`'s number, and follows a finger, the pointer or the arrow keys round the ring. `flow` draws money or people as wide soft bands from where they come, through the company's bar in the middle or straight from source to end (`links`), to where they go, each band as wide as its amount and what is kept last; past six ends on a side the smallest join one band, Other; the big number, all that came in or `headline`, becomes a band's amount and share as a member points at it. A mark's, a wedge's, the centre's, an end's, a link's and the headline's amount may be live (`KIT_LIVE_VALUES`). Each has its engine and guide (`packages/runtime/kits/{ring,flow}/`, `app_guide ring` and `app_guide flow`). save_app refuses what the page cannot draw (`parseKitData`: a ring's dates that are no `YYYY-MM-DD`, its span, more than 12 marks or 8 wedges, none at all; a flow's end without a name or with an id twice, more than 24 ends a side or 24 links, a link to an end it lacks), and its look names a ring's marks and wedges outside its span and a flow's side whose smallest ends join Other (`kitNotes`, which takes the day it counts from). app_guide's description names both (922 bytes), and `kit`'s schema description gives each a clause (save_app's schema 4,212 bytes; its description, 3,826 bytes since the instruction stack, names no kit).
- A company machine's `/company` belongs to the image's own agent account (agent-box slice 3; `deploy/machine/qorpera-machine-folder.service`: `--owner-uid 2000 --owner-gid 2000`, the ids `build-machine-image.sh` gives `qorpera-agent`). The model's own file tools on an agent's home run as that account, and the mount is checked by the kernel (`default_permissions`), so a root-owned mount let them read the company folder and write nothing. Root writes through it as before, so `machine_run` is unchanged. Rides the machine image.
- Every agent keeps every tool, and a run's first request describes in full only the tools its kind of run uses most (agents' instructions, track 3, T3-6; no SQL, no Codex change, the runtime bundle only). `cloudToolsOnDemand` (`cloud-native-tools-on-demand.ts`) splits a run's tools by name: its kind's direct list (`CLOUD_DIRECT_TOOLS`: the file reads and writes, `read_context`, `recall_memory`, `read_skill`, the sends, `manage_todo`, `manage_worker`, the four everyday machine tools, `save_app`, `post_app`, `update_memory`, and the Co-Founder's `hire_agent`; a worker's reads, writes, machine tools, apps and `report_work`), the tools a run has only when it needs them (`decide_routine`, `company_search`, `zoom`, the proposals' tools) and every connector tool go to Codex in full; every other tool stays the run's and is listed, one line each, in the one tool added, `more_tools`. Its `describe` answers tools' descriptions and whole forms (every bound and field description); its `use` is not a call of its own: the boundary (`CloudNativeToolBoundary.invoke`) runs it as the direct call of the tool it names, with that tool's parse, review and approval card, work-log step, operation key, receipt, envelope and observation, and a use that names no tool of the run, or has no arguments, is refused by `more_tools` without running anything. A routed call whose arguments do not fit is refused with the tool's form. A tool's own name, which Codex then answers itself ("unsupported call"), is answered by the result fence with how to open it. Codex fixes a thread's tools at its start and its own tool search is refused by the patched result fence, so the tools open inside one ordinary tool, whose answers come at the end of the history like any other: the cached prefix stays whole. The run's instructions are compiled for every tool it has and for `more_tools`, and a staff run that has it reads where those tools are: How work gets done's line on which tools exist says "the ones you see, and the ones more_tools lists, which you open there; a tool these texts name that you do not see is one of those" (in the shared part, the same for every wake; a run without `more_tools` reads the old line). `update_memory` is carried in full for every staff run whatever its count (1 % of runs: agents barely write their notebooks, the fault to cure); a worker keeps it on demand. Its admission records the catalog Codex is sent. `describe` is observed as `unknown_tool` (a named operation would need a migration). The catalog pins move; `scripts/prove-first-requests.mjs` (S0) offers each kind's tools this way, and `scripts/prove-tools-on-demand.mjs` proves a use through the patched Codex against a direct call.
- Every tool's description is its contract (agents' instructions, track 3, T3-5; words and where they stand, no tool does anything new; no SQL, the runtime bundle only). A description says what the tool does and when to reach for it, then one short line per action, in lines instead of one block; what a field means is on the field, which Codex sends (it drops a schema's bounds, patterns and formats, and every field description of a schema of 5,000 bytes or more as it sends it; save_app's kits' guides never reached the model on its fields, so since #1804 they are app_guide's answers and save_app's fields carry a line each). The rules every tool repeated (operation keys, results as data, paging with nextCursor, not waiting for results, plain words, links, a stale version or a missing folder) leave the tools for the instructions, which say each of them once in the one-voice core this stands on (the list: lanes/agent-instructions-20261113/t3-5/MOVED-TO-CORE.md). ask_member's Gmail draft steps come only with Gmail's tools (a runtime test feeds a Gmail connector), manage_worker says status needs the todoKey too, and its input schema is `CLOUD_ASK_MEMBER_INPUT_SCHEMA`. A new test holds every bound a description or a field names to the code's, keeps every schema under the 5,000 bytes as sent, save_app's included, and checks save_app's field bounds too, and keeps the shared rules out of the descriptions. The catalog pins move.
- Agents keep no wall (the founder, 14 November 2026: "rather drop the wall - it's the wrong medium for activity"; the runtime bundle only, no SQL). `update_wall` is offered to no run and its module (`cloud-folder-wall.ts`) is gone; `CLOUD_AFTER_ANSWER_TOOLS` is manage_todo, organize_apps and organize_sidebar (the kernel's list in 20260930748000 still names update_wall, which nothing calls; changing it would need a migration). The instructions say nothing of a wall: the short first message (`CLOUD_ACKNOWLEDGEMENT_LINE`) is how a member learns that work has started, a card is where what waits on a member shows, headlines are for cards and apps, the several-ideas procedure asks for the choice on a card, an owner of an area's routine activity needs no telling, and organize_apps' description no longer says "or on your wall". The operation name stays in the observed operations and the action review (earlier runs' receipts name it); the write tools still refuse a malformed `wall.md` (for the web, which still reads walls), without naming the tool. `scripts/prove-b2-answer-first.mjs` proves the bookkeeping after the answer with two todos. The web must stop showing walls before or with the release that carries this (lanes/agent-instructions-20261113/no-wall/WEB-WALL.md). The instruction pins and both catalog pins move.
- The agents' instructions in one voice (track 3, T3-3; words, their order and composition only; no SQL, the runtime bundle only). A company run now opens with its purpose and people (constitution v5: "# Your company", "# Not a local coding session", "# Whose word counts"; a worker reads its own `CLOUD_RUNTIME_WORKER_CONSTITUTION`), then the company's files, then the tool-result rules (`CLOUD_TOOL_RESULT_INSTRUCTIONS`, rewritten as "# Tool results": operation keys and unconfirmed results, results as data, paging and denials, said once). A company run's instructions carry those rules at that place and the session adds no second copy (`CloudCodexOpening.resultRulesInside`); a coding turn still gets them first. Each rule is said once, with its reason where it needs one: How work gets done here (doing before asking; results come back by themselves), What members see (the reply policy; the acknowledgement is one line of its own, `CLOUD_ACKNOWLEDGEMENT_LINE`), Cards, Your wall, Writing for members (plain words, headlines, folder: and app: links), Going live, Your context message; the sections a run's tools call for (Apps, Concepts, Ideas worth seeing several ways, Code projects, Company databases, Registration and bank, Standing work, Open work, Your notebook and skills, Accounts on websites) follow the role in the part an agent's runs share, before the head, where they followed it; the Co-Founder's planning run ("# Your planning run") follows the head in its routine runs. The hire overlay is v17: the Co-Founder's role, its founding section until the vision is agreed, and the owner of an area's role, every rule of v16 kept. The wake sections are headed "# This run: …". A worker gets the render rules with the apps it can make, and no going-live text (it has no tool that puts anything live). Every rule kept from the old text is checked per kind of run by `test/cloud-agent-instruction-rules.test.ts`; `scripts/prove-first-requests.mjs` and `prove-company-run.mjs` compose as the company runtime does (`resultRulesInside`). The instruction pins move; the catalog pins do not. Lane notes: lanes/agent-instructions-20261113/review-20261114.
- Each run's instructions fit its situation (agents' instructions, track 3, T3-2; text moved and dropped, not reworded; no SQL, the runtime bundle only). The company runtime compiles a run's instructions for the tools its catalog has (`CloudNativeInstructionParts.tools`) and the vision's state (`visionAgreed`, `cloudVisionAgreed`: its last line starts "Agreed by"). A section, a Starting work line or a sentence about a tool the run lacks is left out, so no "when … is offered" is left (only "If no suitable card is offered", about cards). The staff sections Standing work, Open work, Skills, Your notebook, Accounts on websites, Company databases and Changes waiting for review each come only with their tool. A worker gets no role section, no text on the company's registration and bank, and no lines on its voice, cards or held messages. The Co-Founder's founding and vision-draft work comes only until the vision is agreed (its first-conversation, team-members and later-founder sentences stay); once it is agreed, the hand-off's steps (vision, owners, the first todo closed, the founders told) and what to do when one failed stay in its planning run, which finishes them; and its planning run (`cloudCoFounderPlanningRun`, under "# Your daily plan") only in its routine runs. What an agent's runs share (`cloudNativeStableInstructions`) reads only the tools those runs share whatever their wake (`CLOUD_WAKE_TOOLS` left out), its kind, whether it is a worker and the vision's state, so it stays the same across an agent's runs in one conversation (RUNTIME-AGENT-MEMORY-TREE.md §6.1); a test compiles six wakes of one agent and finds one shared part. The instruction pins move; the catalog pins do not.
- Agents no longer read about the Company Computer, and two laws say what is true today (agents' instructions, track 3, T3-1; words only, no SQL, the runtime bundle only). No company has had an office since 3 October 2026 and no code makes one, so no run is offered `computer_*` tools; every text a run without one reads stops naming them: the dynamic routine's and `decide_routine`'s "wait for the Company Computer", the machine check-in's "your computer" and `computer_shell action list`, the office-start continuation's schema, "the company's computer" in Starting work and in the file tools' and `save_app`'s refusals (now "a machine"), `openWork.computerStopped`, the proposals' "its desktop, a shell command", the tool-result rules' desktop folder reports, `list_hooks`' and `manage_hook`'s `computer_browser vault_fill` and `vault_capture` (they now say no tool can type or read a signed hook's secret), `ask_member`'s coding value via `vault_capture`, the action reviewer's "permanently deleting on the company computer", and `company_search`'s "work on files … on the company's computer" (a machine now; the tool is offered wherever the folder's search is on, office or not). The Company Computer's own tools and its wake keep their words: only a run with an office reads them. One text is left on purpose: the Accounts section's "type one only with vault_fill" sits inside a "when company_account is offered" clause, which T3-2 takes out with the other hedged clauses. Law 3 says members see messages, cards, the wall, apps and the company's files; law 6 names a machine where the company's browser and desktop were, and drops the office's screen leases (constitution v4). The bootstrap binding's ids and versions leave the instructions: the run's row, its admission and its bound context keep them. The instruction and catalog pins move; `scripts/prove-first-requests.mjs` saves the first request of four kinds of run as the patched Codex sends it (S0).
- Release v248 (the desktop-computers lead's plain runtime release: a member opens an app or a file on an agent's computer
  #1781 and #1808, a member-woken machine sleeps 10 minutes after the member is gone #1782 and #1783, and master since v247):

  previous 247 `e92d0a54a98a`). Its activation requires the release's nine migrations, which the roll's `migrate` runs
  before the coding executors and the switch: `20260930758000`, `20260930759000`, `20260930763000`, `20260930764000`,

- A member opens one of the company's apps on an agent's desktop computer (migration `20260930758000`, MEMBER-OPEN-BRIEF; Jonas, 14 Nov 2026: "Build it now"). The member listener's start route takes `{"open": "<path>"}` beside `{}` (`cloudMemberMachineOpenPathValid`: 1 to 500 bytes, no empty piece, `.` or `..`, no control character, lone surrogate or backslash; anything else is still `member_start_invalid`) and answers `opened`; the start's refusals add `DESKTOP_OPEN_PATH_INVALID`, `DESKTOP_OPEN_NOT_FOUND`, `DESKTOP_MEMBER_IN_CONTROL`, `DESKTOP_OPEN_NO_FOLDER` and `DESKTOP_DID_NOT_OPEN` (the last two logged by their codes). The peer route carries only the start's id, as before; its answer may say `opened`.
- Agents learn the views the company is owed (company views step 4, slice O2a: lanes/company-views-20261114/DESIGN-4.md; deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-INTERFACE-APPS.md §3d; no migration, no web change): the `look` guide says that `apps/products`, `apps/money` and `apps/ads` are views members find first, to make one when asked at exactly that folder, and that the platform draws the website itself; a `save_app` of one of those three (not a concept, not icons alone) adds "This is the company's <name> view: members find it first in the apps drawer." to its answer's `look`, a line that asks for no fix. No tool description or schema changes, so the tool catalog's pins stand.
- An agent stops keeping an interface a member retired (the founder, 15 Nov 2026: "users should also get a "retire interface" config when rightclicking an interface to stop maintaining it, and hide it"; deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-INTERFACE-APPS.md §3e). The runtime reads the members' list, `apps/retired.md` (cloud-client `app-retired.ts`), as the run's own read, released with a tool's other reads (`cloud-folder-app-retired.ts`). `save_app` on a retired name (the app, or a new one made under that name), `post_app` on a retired app either way and `make_picture` into one are refused before their review and again before they write; `company_write`, `company_edit`, `company_mkdir`, `company_delete` and either end of `company_move` are refused in a retired app's folder, the folder itself included: `APP_RETIRED: a member retired the interface <name> on <day>. Leave it: …`. The file itself is closed to every agent change, as is a move of `apps/` while anything stands at its `retired.md`, and a move of a folder holding a `retired.md` onto `apps` itself (`APP_RETIRED_MEMBERS_ONLY`). `organize_apps list` ends a retired app's line with ` (retired by a member)` and counts it in no group or loose number; moving it stays allowed. No tool description, role text or context prose changes, so no instruction pin moves; no migration, no tool name.
- A member's yes applies a database step that destroys data: `data_apply {database, allow}` (company databases, slice 7 part (b); deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-COMPANY-DATABASES.md §3 "A step" point 4 and §4; the founder's decision of 13 Nov 2026, "only what destroys data waits for a member", and of 14 Nov, "Yes, build them"; migration `20260930764000`, SQL test 1262). Until now a step that would drop a table or a column, empty a table or delete rows was held for good. The data gateway's held answer now carries `allow`: the step, the hash of its text and the hash of what it would destroy. `data_apply` called again with that `allow` is a destructive action (`cloudActionEffect`) that is always asked on a card (`dataDestroyCheck`): whatever the reviewer said short of a denial becomes an ask, a company Allow rule it cited is dropped (an Ask first rule stays), and no Always allow is offered. The card's facts are not the agent's words: the tool's `prepare` has the gateway run the step, keep nothing and say what it would destroy (`apply` with `check`), and refuses before any card when the step is no longer held as the `allow` says. On the member's yes the tool asks for its data pass with the approval's id (`qorpera_run_data_pass_approved`, through `CloudCompanyFolderClient.dataPass(database, signal, reviewId)`): the platform keeps the `allow` in the pass only for a member's Allow on this agent's `data_apply` card that this very run used, and the gateway applies the step only for a pass that carries its `allow`, while its text and what it destroys (counted rows included) are still what the member was shown; else it is held again with a new `allow`. A reviewer's own allow, a rule, another run's approval and the run's own word never apply it. `data_apply`'s description and the instructions' "Company databases" paragraph say how a member is asked, so the tool catalog's and the instructions' pins move. Needs the gateway of this PR on the store's machines before a runtime bundle carries it.
- Agents write rows of the company's databases: `data_change {database, change, params?}` (Jonas, 14 Nov 2026, asked whether agents have the database tools they would have elsewhere and shown that they could not write rows: "Yes, build them"; company databases, slice 7 part (a); deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-COMPANY-DATABASES.md §8; migration `20260930763000`, SQL test 1261). A change is a file of the database's folder, `data/<database>/actions/<change>.sql`: one insert, update, delete or merge with `:name` for each value, which an agent writes with the file tools. `data_change` (`cloud-data-tools.ts`) asks for a data pass as the three data tools do (the pass already keeps `actions/`), and the data gateway's run door runs the statement as the database folder's write login in a transaction of its own, 5 s at most (`POST /v1/run-data/change`; the same call for members is `/v1/company-data/<company>/change`, which the web's `qorpera.act` will use). The answer is `changed` (the rows the statement wrote) and what it returns (`columns`, `rows`, `more`), cut at an agent's 200 rows and 24 KiB. A change may delete rows (its own defined act); one that would drop or empty a table or drop a column, which the write login can only do through a function a step made, is rolled back by the gateway, as is a statement that is no write of rows and one that sends back more than 1 MiB. A call that ends without an answer says the change may or may not have run and to read before running it again, never that it failed. Not reviewed (`cloudActionEffect`: internal, as `company_write`). Offered where `data_apply` is: a worker's run only reads. `data_describe` lists a database's changes with their words and parameters. The instructions' "Company databases" paragraph names `actions/` and `data_change`, so the tool catalog's and the instructions' pins move. Needs the gateway of this PR on the store's machines (a later gateway) before a runtime bundle offers it.
- Three shape kits for interface apps (Jonas, 14 Nov 2026, "Yes, start building" on lanes/company-views-20261114/PLAN.md step 2; no migration): `curve` draws one value over time big (the past as an area, what is expected as a dotted line in a band, now, a safe level, marks; the big number follows a finger or the arrow keys), `stack` the things that want a member's yes one card at a time (a card's buttons are the options of the open question that points at the app and answer it through `qorpera.answer`; save_app adds `questions` to a stack's reads and `answers` to its writes, and when the writes are full without `answers` its look says one must give way; a card holds the open question of its whole title, also one longer than the card shows, and a card already answered holds none, so the same ask again is a card of its own), `shelf` the company's things side by side with one level behind each (a low one warm, a note with the agent's figure, a gallery without levels; a tapped thing opens with a note to the app's owner). Each has its engine and guide (`packages/runtime/kits/<kit>/`, `app_guide` answers it), and every kit's live values bind in them. A kit's page carries the agents' figures its data names (`figure`), each a small WebP as a data: URL (`kitFigures`, `KIT_FIGURES` from `packages/runtime/kits/figures/`, written in by `npm run generate:app-kits`).
- The map kit for interface apps (Jonas, 15 Nov 2026, on the company's page: "it should not be the platform that draws it, rather an agent should maintain it. users should also get a "retire interface" config when rightclicking an interface to stop maintaining it, and hide it. then adding layers and complexity makes sense, as it let's the user dive into what's being made"; asked how the agent should make it: "Use a map kit"; lanes/company-reading-20261114/BRIEF-MAP.md; no migration): `map` draws the company, or one owner's area, as one picture: three to seven things, each one of the reading's 25 shapes, pale and filled from the bottom as far as it is built or known (`full`), the attended one biggest in the middle and the others bigger the more agents stand at them, the agents' figures at a thing's corners with a mark on those at work, a member's mark where a thing waits; a thing opens into a layer of what is made inside it (`layers`, 1 to 12 things, four layers deep, 80 in all), zoomed into with a spring, marked beside the layer's title by its own small shape, and left by the path at the top or Escape (no entry in the browser's history); a thing with an interface of its own links to it (`opens`); one not looked at for 14 days (`seen`) is pale with its age; `unknown` and `assumed` are their outline alone, dashed (a "?" inside, or pale inside). Its first layer may be the company's reading, live (`top: "reading"`): the company's sentence as the title, a quiet line of what changed with "Not right?" (a note to the app's owner, sent from the member's bar), the team in a row before the first reading, grey and still with since when and why while the company cannot run. `save_app` adds `reading` to a map's reads when it draws the reading or names an agent, whose figure the page finds there; a map's page carries all twelve figures. `parseKitData` names a map's problems (no first layer, 3 to 7 things in it, a layer of 1 to 12, too deep, too many). Registered beside the other kits (`DATA_KITS`, `APP_GUIDES`, `APP_KIT_CHOICE`, the data kits' shared guide, `KIT_FILES`, `app_guide`'s description, the look notes, `scripts/generate-app-kits.mjs`), so the tool catalog's pins move; save_app's description takes `reading` and `map` from `APP_READS` and `APP_KITS`.
- Agents read how an app should look before their first one: `app_guide look` (company views plan step 1, "Taste"; Jonas, 14 Nov 2026, after 44 sketched screens: "it would still be important that the agents knew to diverge from them and create whatever the company needed"; deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-INTERFACE-APPS.md §3d; no migration, no web change). The guide gives his taste in a few lines (Qorpera's own plain look, the company's real things, one big shape a screen, no grids of small controls, chip rows, scattered dots or text cards, and copy the quality, not the layout) and tells seven screens he liked and three he did not, a line each. `app_guide`'s description and `save_app`'s `kit` say to read it before the first app; no save waits on it. save_app's schema grows 44 bytes (3,532, under the 5,000 past which Codex drops field descriptions), app_guide's description 117 (927). The screens' pictures reach no company's folder yet: the guide tells them in words.
- The vault's bank sync also records the company's money in and out each day, by kind (company views step 3, slice M2: lanes/company-views-20261114/DESIGN-3.md; Jonas, 14 Nov 2026: "Yes, start step 3"; migration `20260930769000`, SQL test 1267). The reduction (`bank-summary-reduce.ts`) gains `reduceBankDays`: from the same accounts and transactions as the summary (only the accounts the member shares now, which only the vault knows), each day from the first booked one (at most 400 days back) to today, money in and money out summed by a kind word with how many payments. Kinds out are the summary's categories as words (`salaries`, `taxes`, `rent`, `utilities`, `subscriptions`, `fees`, `other`), read over the whole history, so this month's days add up to the summary's categories; kinds in say what paid, never who: `sales` (payouts from Stripe, MobilePay, SumUp, Zettle, Shopify, Klarna, Nets/Nexi), `tax` (Skattestyrelsen, SKAT), `other`. No party, text, account or single payment is in them. The sync calls the new broker function `qorpera_record_bank_days` (broker only, the company's authority, the sync's live lease) after keeping the transactions and before `finish`; it replaces the company's rows of the range in one call and keeps a cover of the counted days and a digest of the accounts they were counted from (`qorpera_bank_days_cover.accounts_sha256`: sha-256 of the distinct account keys the vault shares now, sorted; the vault passes the keys and SQL makes the digest), so a day inside it with no row had no money moving; a write from another set of accounts (another bank, also under a rotated secret, or an account added or no longer shared) clears the company's days first, and a consent renewed over the same accounts keeps them. A days write that fails is logged as a fixed phrase and never holds the summary back. "Today" and every day in both reductions are dates in the company's own time zone (`qorpera_profiles.time_zone`, 20260930739000), Europe/Copenhagen when it has none or one the database or the runtime does not know (`qorpera_bank_days_time_zone`, the rule the company's numbers read by too); before, it was the UTC date, a day behind in Copenhagen from midnight to 01:00, 02:00 in summer. The vault learns the zone from `qorpera_record_bank_transactions`' answer, which gains `timeZone` (a vault from before reads only the keys it knows). The summary's shape is unchanged (the Bank page parses it strictly). A vault from before this never calls the function, so days stay empty until the bundle with it runs: the migration goes first.
- A render's final frames go into the company folder as they finish, and no person is built from plain shapes (Jonas, 14 Nov 2026, after the first real job on the machine image with Blender and the starter set: "are they implemented in the best possible way, or are we still using the wrong processes/machine sizes?", and to these two sentences: "Yes, build it"; words only: no migration, no tool, no machine image change). That job, a company's storyboard as real photos, was done as told (an 8-processor machine, the set's light and materials, a small frame first), and showed two gaps. Its six final frames lay on the machine's disk when the run ended before it had copied them, so the look step in every run's instructions now ends: save each final frame into the company folder as it is finished (on a machine, under `/company`), not only on the machine's disk. And the hand that reaches for the product was a ribbed tube, because the starter set has a room and no people: the instructions now say that a person, a hand or a face put together from plain shapes gives a render away at once, and to frame the shot so that no person is in it, or, where the person is the point of the shot, to make that picture with `make_picture` when the run has it, describing the product as the render shows it. Both sentences are in what every run reads, a worker's too. The pinned instruction hashes moved (cloud-native-company-context.test.ts: the conversation's and the private one, and the worker's). The guide in the machine image (`/opt/qorpera-assets/README.md`) does not say either yet: it changes with the next machine image.
- Agents receive the guides for making an interface app: `app_guide {about}` (found 14 Nov 2026 by the instructions lane; deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-INTERFACE-APPS.md §3d; migration `20260930762000`, SQL test 1260). The guides (what a kit's one file holds, the explainer's calls, a page's base style, its live data and changes, an app's icons) stood on the fields of `save_app`'s schema, about 40 KB, and **no model ever received them**: the patched Codex sends no field description of a tool whose schema is 5,000 bytes or more as it sends it, so `save_app`'s schema arrived as 1,291 bytes of bare shape and the kits, live since v243, were filled from their names (on production one model app held `{"groups":[]}`). `app_guide` (`cloud-app-guides.ts`, offered wherever `save_app` is; review class `read`; it reads nothing of the company) answers one guide's text, `about` one of model, board, path, object, explainer, style, reads, writes, icons. `save_app` saves a kit's own file (`<kit>.json`, `scenes.js`) only in a run that has read the kit's guide, else `APP_GUIDE_UNREAD` before anything is written; a save that leaves the kit's file alone needs none. `save_app`'s fields say a line each (which kit draws what stays on `kit`), and a test holds its schema under 4,600 bytes; its description points at `app_guide` where it pointed at the schema and no longer says twice that a kit's pictures must be in the folder at the save. A model whose groups hold no input is refused (`APP_DATA_INVALID`), as one without groups is. A worker that may not write is offered neither `save_app` nor `app_guide`. The company context says where a kit's file is described ("save_app says what each kit draws, and app_guide what the kit's file holds"). Tool and instruction text move (`save_app`'s description and schema, a new tool, that sentence), so the pinned catalog and instruction hashes move; no node change, no web change.
- Release v247 (the own-code-storage lane's cut: site hosts #1774 with the agent-box riders #1786, #1792, #1788, light asks
  #1796 and `app_guide` #1804): runtime bundle 247 `e92d0a54a98a4f08d78b386d63f0f91f3678141c5e940288c25b7673e0640762`
  (activation `20260929024799`, previous 246 `4addaeac561c`). Its activation requires the release's four migrations,
  which the roll's `migrate` runs before the coding executors and the switch: `20260930756000` (site hosts),
  `20260930760000` (a sleeping machine keeps its disk across an image change), `20260930761000` (light asks) and
  `20260930762000` (`app_guide`). The guest moves in three files: the native provider host (`c572cee7`), the LXD runner
  bootstrap (`e032570d`) and the LXD native login bootstrap (`c64a65a4`); the native Codex stays `9c299f88`.
- Light asks: one small question a member's own action raises, answered by one call to the small model with no tools, with nothing shown in a conversation (Jonas, 14 Nov 2026, on a group just made in the apps drawer: "when a new group is created, I would like to run a light call, having an agent name it"; asked how, he chose "Build the light call"; deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-LIGHT-ASK.md; the contract's 2026-11-14 amendment; migration `20260930761000`, SQL test 1258). The first kind, `name-app-group`, names a new group of apps. A member asks with a closed kind and its input, never a prompt (`qorpera_request_light_ask`, `qorpera_read_light_ask`). The ask is an `agent` wake with the new source kind `light-ask`, no parent run and no conversation, to the Co-Founder, paid by the asker's own Codex login with the most room (a ChatGPT plan too: the ask is its owner's own request, made now; never a company account). The run (`cloud-native-company-runtime.ts`, `#lightAsk`) is admitted with an empty tool catalog, which the cloud takes for a light ask's run and for no other, binds its one question as its context, asks once on a tool-less side thread with the compactor's model and effort (`runCloudCodexToolSession`'s new `light`, in place of the agent's thread, which never starts), stores the answer through the new host call `qorpera_native_light_ask_answer`, and ends silent. The kind's instructions are the bundle's (`cloud-light-ask.ts`); the input goes to the model as data between tags; a reply that is no name, an ask past its ten minutes, or a usage limit stores no answer and still ends the run. No tool's text or schema changes, so the tool catalog's pins stand.
- A sleeping machine keeps its disk when the machine image changes (agent-box, 14 Nov 2026; migration `20260930760000`, SQL test 1257): its placement is stale only in another place (another project, storage pool, network or provider), so a new image, bootstrap, guest helper or largest machine no longer wakes it on a new disk. The machine tools' words follow: `machine_start` and `machine_stop` no longer name a new image among the ways a disk is lost, `machine_start` says newer preinstalled tools come only on new machines, and the screen's and the keys' refusals on an earlier image say to start a new machine instead of promising the tool after a sleep and wake. `scripts/prove-disk-kept.mjs` proves a wake across an image switch. A kept machine whose guest helper the executor no longer accepts is not stuck: the executor's record of `COMPANY_MACHINE_HELPER_DIFFERS` retires a placement of an earlier policy (review r1 F2 of #1788), its commands end lost, it is told (`diskFreed`) and it starts on a new disk when next wanted. Release order: this migration with the coding executor of #1786 (an earlier helper takes `/company`), then the runtime bundle for the words.
- An agent reads which rule a site's name broke, and that a site Qorpera took down takes nothing (own code storage,
  step 5.1, the lead's calls on the cross-check's seams 2 and 7; migration `20260930756000`). `publish_site`,
  `share_app` and `unpublish_site` refuse a slug of the wrong shape with the kernel's own sentence, `SITE_NAME_INVALID:
  A site's name is 3 to 48 characters of a to z, 0 to 9 and single hyphens, starts and ends with a letter or digit,
  does not end in -preview, does not contain qorpera, and is not a reserved name (such as app, api, mail, shop,
  login).` (`SITE_NAME_RULE`, byte for byte `qorpera_site_name_words()`; a test reads the migration), and relay the
  kernel's refusal of any other name in the same words. A site taken down: the kernel's refusal (`SITE_TAKEN_DOWN: This
  site was taken down by Qorpera (<reason>) and cannot be published until Qorpera restores it. Tell a member of the
  company.`) is relayed as it is at the check and the begin, and now also when it comes during an upload (before, a
  refusal after the begin was told as `not_live` with the site's link). `unpublish_site` relays the same refusal as it
  is: a takedown sticks until Qorpera restores it (the lead's call, 13 Nov 2026). The tools' descriptions and the pins
  do not change.

  `d831f085fcae6d1ecb58aec2ebba8dd23a67af80929e740b740ca5c2e9996540` once master's starter-set look step met it (v247's candidate, 14 November 2026),
  and `80d863024aee068befff119c6665150b25d8511dee39341ea1f28d5846623af2` once master's `app_guide` (20260930762000) met it (release v247, measured on the
  merged tree; the worker's is master's, `1d31664cbdb78bd48da2f14d3e4e553bf3dba87bb555423d9f21c8e42def3daa`); the

- A company website is served at an address of its own, `https://<name>.qorpera.com/`, and its next version at
  `https://<name>--next.qorpera.com/` (own code storage, step 5, release 5.1, part S1; `supabase/functions/site-serve`;
  `lanes/own-code-storage/STEP5-BUILD.md`). Inert until the operator turns site hosts on: the host is read by the one
  host rule SQL and the web share, and the database decides the rest (`qorpera_site_serve_host`,
  `qorpera_site_unlock_host`, `qorpera_site_enter`); before migration `20260930756000` and while hosts are off,
  `sites.qorpera.com` answers as before but for one header: every answer of site-serve now carries an explicit
  `Cache-Control` with `no-transform` (D23; the old host's pages and redirects `no-store, no-transform`, its files
  `private, no-cache, no-transform`, its CORS preflight `no-store, no-transform`, where it had none). While on, old
  links move by 308 (303 for the password form), a site Qorpera took down answers 410 on every host, a version's own
  `404.html` answers a missing page, and Look first trades a one-time ticket for a member's session that shows the
  ready version with a "Not live yet" bar. A site host is its own origin with no sandbox; the session is the host-only
  `__Host-qorpera_site` cookie (Lax); every answer carries `Cross-Origin-Resource-Policy: same-origin`,
  `frame-ancestors 'self'`, HSTS without subdomains, `Cache-Control` with `no-transform` and
  `CDN-Cache-Control: no-store`; requests from another origin's pages are refused 403 by Fetch Metadata, service worker
  scripts 404, and the password form and the ticket refuse another Origin. Between the front's change and
  `hosts_enabled` every first-level name answers the password page (401) and its next host "Not live yet" (401):
  `deploy/sites/SITE-HOSTS.md`. Moves at release: `site-serve.ts` into the kit's `functions/src/site-serve/` and
  `functions.sh install`, after the migration and before the front.
- Company websites' own addresses reach the site-serve function (own code storage, step 5, release 5.1, part R1;
  `deploy/sites/SITE-HOSTS.md`). api-front's virtual host `others`, which answered 404 for every other name under
  qorpera.com, becomes `site-hosts`: every path of `<slug>.qorpera.com` and `<slug>--next.qorpera.com` goes to
  site-serve with `X-Forwarded-Host` the name the request asked for (a visitor's dropped), WebSocket upgrades are
  refused (403), and nothing else of the stack is reachable on those names; exact names and `*-preview` app hosts still
  win. No new listener, tunnel, DNS record or certificate. `deploy/sites/sites-route.mjs --check` now also reads the
  site hosts (a made-up name's own host, next host and `/rest/v1/` answer site-serve's locked page; the API name answers
  404 for site-serve with that name forged), robots.txt's last group, the web's host-form marker and the relay's 403 for
  a forged change from another site (P1); a token that may not read Cache Rules gives a warning, not a failure. `SITE-HOSTS.md` is the operator's document: rollout, takedown,
  the name rule and reserved list, the standing rule for new platform names, the cookie invariant, reports. Moves at
  release: the front's `site-hosts` block (production's `/opt/dbhome/front/envoy-front.yaml` and node 2's kit, then a
  recreate of `api-front`), after site-serve and before `hosts_enabled`.
- The app relay refuses a change sent from another site (own code storage, step 5, release 5.1, part P1): any request
  but GET, HEAD and OPTIONS whose `Sec-Fetch-Site` is `same-site` or `cross-site`, or whose `Origin` is present and not
  the app's own host (`null` only with `Sec-Fetch-Site: same-origin`), answers 403 with `no-store` before the gate, the
  database or the app see it. From 5.1 every company website is a same-site neighbour of every app link and could
  otherwise make a visitor's browser post into an app on its Lax gate cookie. Requests with neither header pass as
  before. Moves at release: `relay.mjs` into the DB primary's `front/app-relay/` and a recreate of `app-relay`, before
  `hosts_enabled`.
- A kit page's live value that does not answer no longer stops its page (company databases, found by v246's release prep before any release held the script; no migration, no web change, no tool's text moves). The live values' script in a kit's page (`KIT_LIVE_VALUES`, `cloud-app-kits.ts`) started a watch the bridge refused again at EVERY load, and the engines load at every live value's change, so one binding to a question that does not exist (no such database yet, a wrong name) beside one value that moves made a refused call per change: the app's frame stops a page after 300 refused calls (`MAX_REFUSED`, the web's `app-frame.ts`) and the member reads "This app reloaded itself". Now a refused start waits before its next try: 10 seconds, twice as long after each refusal in a row, 10 minutes at most (an answer ends the row). No load starts it inside the wait; at the wait's end the script asks the engine for a load itself (after a load that is running), so a database made during the wait shows without anything else changing, and a question the data no longer names is left alone. A page makes at most 100 refused starts, counting the ones that are out together: a start takes one from the page's count and its own first answer gives it back. A start still out when the data drops its question stays counted, and its watch is stopped only when that start answers (a refusal keeps it counted), so the frame is never refused a start the page's count did not hold, however often a question is named and dropped: at most 100 of the frame's 300; after them it starts no watch that is not running, and waits for none (a value is then not known until the page is opened again). `save_app` writes the script into the page, so a page saved before this keeps the old one until it is saved again: none exists, since no release held #1744's script. The page's script is 3,816 bytes for a model (the test's ceiling moves from 3,584 to 4,096).
- Every machine has Blender, a starter set of real light, materials and furniture, and picture tools (Jonas, 13 Nov 2026, on agents' machines: they "should have the proper stuff downloaded though which im not confident they do"; machine image only, no SQL; `deploy/machine/README.md`, "Renders"). The image gains Blender's official build at a pinned version and sha-256 (4.3.2: Cycles on the processors, OpenImageDenoise) with the six X and GL libraries it asks for; a starter set at `/opt/qorpera-assets` (Poly Haven, CC0: 10 HDRIs, 14 texture sets, 10 models at 2k, 219 MiB in 141 files, each fetched from the address and checked against the sha-256 that `deploy/machine/render-assets.tsv` pins) with a guide for agents and a picture of everything in it; ImageMagick, the WebP tools, Pillow and numpy; and what agents installed onto their machines again and again besides (the heads of production's machine commands, 4 to 13 Nov): fonttools with brotli, poppler-utils and pypdf. The build renders one small frame of the set with Cycles and OpenImageDenoise and refuses an image whose Blender is another version, whose set does not load, or that leaves the smallest machine (10 GiB) under 1.5 GiB free. `machine_start`'s description says Blender is installed and where the set's guide is (3,956 of 4,096 bytes, a worker's 4,077), and the look step for renders points at the set. The pinned instruction hashes move again (measured on node 2's test machine). The new image is a release step: until it is rolled, nothing changes on a machine, and the words reach agents with the bundle cut after it.
- A member's live picture of an agent's desktop in the large view (migration `20260930745000`, POINTER-BRIEF.md step 2, the lead's B1: node 1's own Cloudflare tunnel). The executor's new live-picture listener (`CloudMemberMachineStreamListener`, `cloud-member-machine-stream-http.ts`; loopback only, behind node 1's own tunnel `screens.qorpera.com`) serves ONE route, `GET /v1/member-machine-streams/<ticket>`, a WebSocket upgrade with the subprotocol `qorpera.machine-stream.v1` and the Origin the ticket was bought for; anything else, an unknown, used, expired or other-Origin ticket, or one over the limits, is a bare 404. A ticket is bought on the pointer route as the member, on a live pointer pass of the same hold: `{"stream": {"pass": p, "hold": {"generation": g} | {"epoch": e, "seat": s}}}` answers `{schema, ticket, seconds: 30}` (32 random bytes, kept in memory, used once; the member bridge passes the browser's Origin on, and only that Origin can open it). The stream only shows: a browser's data message ends it (1008); it runs while the member holds a live pass of its hold on that executor (checked every second; the window renews it), at most 2 streams a member, 8 a machine, 64 on the listener, 8 hours each, a ping every 30 s; the listener holds at most 256 connections at once, upgraded or not, and one that has not sent its whole upgrade within 5 s is gone within about 6 s (the deadline checked every second), and its close cuts every connection that has not upgraded; a close says why (4001 the pass ended or the stream is old, 4003 a release cycle refused, 4004 the desktop ended, 1001 the executor stops). A desktop on another node is relayed through that node's peer listener (`POST /v1/machine-commands/member-stream`, which reads the newest pass itself; frames of a kind byte and a length, only from the machine's node to the browser's). Pointer begins, the one pointer request that calls out (PostgREST, as the member), are bounded on each executor: one per token at a time, 16 at once, 40 a second; past it `503` before any call. The executor's config takes `"memberStreams": {"port": 8793}` (needs `memberScreens`). Node 1's door of that tunnel is `deploy/screens-door/` (the connector is the cloudflared our own database stack already runs, taken from its pinned image, not downloaded). The member bridge's pointer route takes only a member's own unexpired token, read locally with no call out, and checks its issuer when the deployment names one (`QORPERA_MEMBER_TOKEN_ISSUER`, the string the auth service itself puts in `iss`, copied unchanged from the stack's environment at the release — on the pinned self-hosted configuration `API_EXTERNAL_URL` as it stands — never the address the functions reach the stack at); unset, that check alone is off and every other one holds. A pointer pass's time in the executor's memory counts from when SQL was asked (the monotonic clock), not from its answer's arrival, and a pass with nothing left is refused `POINTER_PASS_ENDED` before its first move, at the begin and on the machine's node (moves and streams); the begin answers what is left of it now (v242 release review r1 F2 of the member's pointer). Node 1 joins a peer's frame from its pieces once (never a growing buffer per piece), and the listener and the peer listener write a picture's frame head and payload as two writes, never joined (the v245 release review). Both ways a member's stream goes out (the listener's WebSocket, and the peer route's reply to node 1) write a message through `writeMemberStreamMessage`: its head and its bytes corked, and `send` resolves only while the way holds at most `MEMBER_STREAM_HIGH_WATER_BYTES` (2 MiB) and `MEMBER_STREAM_UNFLUSHED_MESSAGES` (16) messages not yet flushed, counted by the writes' callbacks; a member that stops reading then holds at most that, never hundreds of thousands of small messages. Every frame head is a buffer of its own, never from Node's shared pool (review r12 of #1746). The listener bounds the client's side as one invariant (the v245 release review F1): whatever a client sends on an upgraded stream, the listener spends a bounded amount of work on it and holds a bounded number of bytes for it, and once a stream is ended nothing more of its input is read. It keeps at most one control frame's bytes (131) of a client's input, checks a frame's first two bytes before copying any of it and parses a read where it is (no joining of what came before); past 32 frames or 256 reads in a 10 s window the stream is ended (1008); a pong is written only while at most 4 wait unflushed, our ping only once the last was answered, and a pong answers it only once that ping has left the process (review r14: unasked pongs keep nothing alive); ended, the socket is paused, its data handler taken off and its carry let go, the close written and the socket destroyed once that is flushed or after 2 s; its windows on a monotonic clock.
- A sleeping machine keeps its disk unless its server is really short of disk, and never loses it silently (agent-box slice 5, "an agent's home is kept"; migration `20260930757000`, SQL test 1253; scripts/prove-disk-kept.mjs). Read on production 13 Nov: node 2's executor gave away 25 sleeping machines' disks in 7 days (9 on the 13th), across companies, because its paper budget counted every placement's whole disk while the pool held a fraction of it. The room for a new disk (`qorpera_machine_executor_disk_room`, used by the executor's pass, the candidates and the placement) is now, on the executor's fresh report, the pool's real free space less a fifth of the pool (what else shares it, db-standby among it, and ZFS's own room) and less what its machines could still grow into (only one asleep since 30 s before the report counts just what it holds); the paper budget only without a fresh report. A sleeping disk goes only when a machine in line fits nowhere now (no node takes it as it is) and what sleeping disks really hold would make it fit (never one whose use is not known, never one used in the last 24 hours while an older one can go, the asking company's own before another's, the longest asleep first, only as many as needed; one shortage takes one round of disks). The machine's row keeps when (`disk_freed_at`; a wake moved to a new disk after it slept, an executor given up and an operator's release count too), its view says `diskFreed` in plain words while it matters, the agent's own free machine taken again is first one that kept its disk, and `reused` says when the one given starts empty. `machine_start` and `machine_stop` say when a machine comes back with an empty disk and that the result then says diskFreed.
- An interface app may read the company's databases: `data` joins `APP_READS` (cloud-client `interface-apps.ts`; the web's bridge checks it before a page's `qorpera.query` or `qorpera.watch`), so `save_app`'s description and its `reads` enum list it and the tool catalog's pins move. `save_app` adds `data` to a kit app's reads when its data names a question (`kitBindings` in `cloud-app-kits.ts`, the same test as the page's script): on a new app, and on a change whose data (from the call or the folder) names one, then rewriting the stored manifest with its version; it only adds, and its answer's `look` says so in one line. `kitNotes` tells the maker when the data names more than 8 different questions (a question with its params once): a page keeps 8 live, and the rest show as not known. A watch the bridge refuses (`failed`) is started again once per load, however many values share it. The page stops the watches its data no longer names before it starts new ones (a bridge keeps 8), keys a question by its params in the order of their names (as `kitBindings` does), and a model page that reads while the member drags keeps the result and shows it when they let go.
- A model or path kit's data can name a live number from a company database where it takes a number (company databases; no migration, no web change; the bridge's `qorpera.watch` is built apart). A model input's `value`, a path step's `value` and a path figure's `value` may be `{"query": "<database>/<question>", "params": {...}, "column": "...", "example": n}`. The page `save_app` writes (`kitPage`) runs a small script before the engine (`KIT_LIVE_VALUES`) that gives `QORPERA_KIT` `load()`, `onLive(fn)`, `live` and `changed`: `load()` reads the data file, puts each value's current number in its place with `"state": "known"` beside it (null and `"unknown"` while a question has not answered within 3 seconds), watches each question with its params once, keeps the last good answer when a later one fails, and stops watching what the data no longer names; `onLive` runs at most once a frame when a watched value changed. Where `qorpera.watch` is missing (outside Qorpera, an older web app) a value is its `example`, drawn as assumed, or unknown with none. The model and path engines read through `QORPERA_KIT.load` and read again at once on `onLive`; the ten seconds of folder change notices stay. The model engine keeps every input the member dragged or set with a scenario where they put it on any reload until they reset it (before, a scenario's value went back when the file changed that input); a value set only by "Try an example" gives way once the data knows that input. A reload keeps a note the member opened, waits for a slider being dragged, and `load()` calls run one after another. Both guides say it in two lines, so the tool catalog's pinned hash moves.
- `machine_start` waits for its machine by default, and each wait is kept (agent-box B1 follow-up, the coordinator's word after v244's first live start; migration `20260930755000`, SQL test 1251). The first live start (13 Nov 16:41Z) answered `waiting` (NODE_CAPACITY_EXHAUSTED): node 2's machine disk budget was full, so a new machine is first in line until the executor frees a sleeping machine's disk, about 2 s (22 of 36 new agent machines in 7 days); the runtime waited only on `starting`, and only when the model asked. Now the call waits up to 60 s (`CLOUD_MACHINE_START_WAIT_SECONDS.byDefault`) without being asked, through `waiting` too (not for computer hours: only a member adds them); `waitSeconds` asks for less, 0 for none, or in a worker's run up to 300. While it waits it asks the executor for a word up to three times (placed, then booted). When the wait ends it reports it to the kernel (`qorpera_native_machine_manage`, action `waited`): whether the model asked, the limit, the state the start answered and why it waited, how long, how it ended (running, limit, hours, not_asked, stopped, gone, work_stopped). A kernel without the action refuses it and the answer goes on. `machine_start`'s description says it in its wait sentence, for agents and workers.
