- Agents size a machine for the job, and light and look at a render like a photograph (Jonas, 13 Nov 2026, on a company's Blender renders that looked drawn: "how come we seem to not get very realistic animations for anything, even the things we want to make realistic?", and on the cause: custom machines are "built, just not used well enough"; no migration, no new tool). `machine_start`'s description says what a size buys and costs: rendering, encoding and big builds use every cpu, a full HD frame that took 22 minutes on 2 cpus took 5 on 8 (measured: `deploy/machine/README.md`, "Sizing a machine for the job"), computer hours count size times time awake so the larger machine costs the same, and it is stopped with `machine_stop` when done (`MACHINE_SIZE_FOR_THE_JOB`; the description is 3,856 bytes, the worker's 3,977, of 4,096). No agent had started a machine of more than 4 cpus. The company instructions tell every run, a worker's too, to light a render meant to look real with an HDRI and textured materials instead of plain colours in a dark, empty world, and to render one small frame into the company folder, open it with `company_read`, name what gives it away, fix the weakest thing and render again before the final frames. The staff's "Starting work" gains a line for work that keeps processors busy, with what to do when a large machine is refused (a smaller one) or waits in line (delete it, then a smaller one, so the large one does not start later unused). The three pinned instruction hashes move (measured on node 2's test machine); the prompts do not.
- Agents make pictures: `make_picture {operationKey, path, prompt, expectedVersion?}` (Jonas, 13 Nov 2026: "I would also turn on agents generating images of course"; deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-INTERFACE-APPS.md §3c; migration `20260930746000`, SQL test 1240). One picture is made on a side thread of the run's own Codex, the one thread of the run started with a thread config, `{"features.image_generation": true, "features.omit_app_server_notification_media": true}`; the agent's thread and the process keep the feature off, and `verifyConfiguration` is unchanged. The bytes come from the file Codex saves (`<CODEX_HOME>/generated_images/<thread>/<item>.png`, checked to be exactly there, a regular file of one link, at most 12 MiB, a PNG), which is then removed; the picture is saved as one `image/png` unit under the call's receipt with company_write's rules (`expectedVersion`, protected paths, a missing folder), and shown to the model after its answer, fitted as company_read fits an image. Under `apps/<name>/` another agent's or a member's app makes the call delegation, as for save_app; a read-only worker and a run on a member's ChatGPT plan do not have it. Every failure is a refusal that saved nothing, in plain words (the member's picture limit with when it resets, the generator's refusal after the image tool was called, the image tool never called; none within 4 minutes, not a usable PNG, over 12 MiB, no picture login), and nothing a picture call does fails the run. A repeat of a call within the run is answered from the tool's memory of its answers (the newest 256, the picture for the newest 4) and makes no second picture, and notes a change among the company's pages again; the same operationKey with other arguments is refused (`OPERATION_KEY_USED`). The image tool never called is `NO_PICTURE_FREE_PLAN` when Codex named the plan Free (`account/updated` or `account/rateLimits/updated`), else `NO_PICTURE` (why not known: change the prompt, try once more). The picture model's revised prompt is held to 4096 bytes and left out (`revisedPromptCut`) when the answer would not reach the model whole. Under `apps/<name>/` the app's room is checked as save_app counts it (`appFileSizes`, `APP_LIST_LIMIT`, exported) before the picture is made, and again from a fresh listing with the picture's bytes before it is saved (another run may fill the app meanwhile). `CloudPictureChannel` (`cloud-picture-channel.ts`) is the lane's seam, `runCloudCodexToolSession` and `runCloudNativeRunToolSession` take it as `pictures`; the context view takes make_picture's picture as machine_screen's screen (`kind: "picture"`); the result fence allows one more side thread's connections; a Codex login's egress relay lets every connection be silent 5 minutes while a picture call lasts (`allowLongWaits`, `NATIVE_EGRESS_LONG_IDLE_MS`), widening no host. `mutationText` of the file tools is exported for the picture's answer.
- A delivered answer shows no failure under it (migration `20260930751000`, SQL test 1245; the lead's call inside the answer-first build). `qorpera_list_staff_execution_activity` called every failed run of an agent `needs_attention`, so a run that answered its member and then failed put a card under that answer ("{agent} did not finish this run"), with Try again while it had saved nothing durable, which runs the member's message again and can answer it twice. Such a run is now listed only while it has sent no final message of its own, or its work left an open proposal (a decision the member must still make, shown as one). A failure with no answer, a member's Stop, the superseded rule (`20260930542000`), the acknowledgement rules, the activity classes, the priorities and every key of an entry are unchanged, and the job's error, the run's row, the wake's status and the operator views keep the failure. The window this closes was 0.13 s before `20260930748000` and is the bookkeeping tail after it (2 to 8 s measured). No runtime change and no web change.
- The answer first, then the bookkeeping (the agent's home program, B2; the contract's 2026-11-13 amendment, proposed; migration `20260930748000`, SQL test 1242). A run's final send (or a card's) no longer ends its turn: after it the tool boundary runs only the run's own bookkeeping, `update_wall`, `manage_todo`, `organize_apps` and `organize_sidebar` (`CLOUD_AFTER_ANSWER_TOOLS`), and the answer's own call again (its key replays it); any other call runs nothing, is answered `TOOL_UNAVAILABLE`, and ends the turn at the next model request, as the final did before. The boundary marks a confirmed final's reply `answered` (host-only, stripped before Codex, as `completesTurn` is); the session keeps the run delivered when the provider fails a turn after its answer, records that turn as failed, and gives a usage limit, rate limit or outage it met to the company runtime, which records it against the run's login (`qorpera_record_provider_usage_signal`) as for a failed run (`CloudCodexToolSessionResult.usageSignal`). The kernel's receipt begin marks the four tools' company revisions so the wake's work gate admits them after the final; every other receipt and a second send are refused as before. The words: "How a turn works" says the final is the last message and the bookkeeping comes after it, so the member gets the answer without waiting for it; `send_message`'s, `ask_member`'s ("It is your final send") and `update_wall`'s descriptions, the member-message and room wake texts, the closing reminder and "Writing to people" no longer say the final is the last act. Measured on Part D's 45 chat turns: the answer about 5.7 s sooner on average (8.5 s in the 30 turns with bookkeeping); one closing model round more per run that sends a final. Rolls after the migration.
- Agents make, change and ask the company's databases (company databases, slice 5; deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-COMPANY-DATABASES.md §3 and §6; migration `20260930750000`, SQL test 1244). `data_describe`, `data_apply` and `data_query` (`cloud-data-tools.ts`) call the data gateway's run door with a credential of its own (the systemd credential `qorpera-company-data`, distinct from every other the host holds, so the store's machine holds nothing that opens another service) and a data pass the run's folder client asks for as the run (`CloudCompanyFolderClient.dataPass`, `qorpera_run_data_pass`, a new native host call of scope folder); the gateway redeems it once (`qorpera_data_pass_redeem`) and works only from the files it kept. When the gateway asks for a file's text, the tool reads it as the run and asks again with a new pass, the texts sent in bodies that fit the gateway's 1 MiB as JSON (escaping counted); the gateway keeps each verified text, also before the company has a database, so the next round asks only for the rest. An apply call starts no step once 45 s have passed and answers `more: true` with the steps still `waiting`; the tool waits 120 s for it (30 s for describe and query), says to call `data_apply` again when steps wait, and when the call ends without an answer says the steps may still be applying and `data_describe` shows what landed, never that they failed. A pass keeps at most 1,000 files, checked as each is kept; with no name it looks through at most 5,000 folders under data/, with its own words. The tools are offered only where the provider host names the door (`QORPERA_COMPANY_DATA_URL` with `QORPERA_COMPANY_DATA_CA_FILE`, the CA pinned, and that credential; a door named without them stops the host); a worker is not offered `data_apply`. None is reviewed: describe and query read, and a step that would destroy data is held until a member says yes. The company instructions say when a database is the right home and when a file is, the folder's shape, and never a secret in a step; the three names join the observations' tool operations.
- Every company machine image carries the pinned Codex (the agent's home): `build-machine-image.sh` requires it as its eighth argument, checks its sha-256 and size against `deploy/first-node/native-codex/linux-x64-artifact.json` before it builds, checks `/opt/qorpera-machine/codex` (root, `0555`, that sha-256) in the image, and records it as `codexSha256` in `image-build.json`. The agent account is made after the desktop's, so the desktop account keeps the uid earlier images gave it.
- The agent's home channel keeps itself open while it is quiet, and says when a machine's Codex is outdated (home-lxd, 14 Nov 2026). Each end of an open channel sends `CLOUD_HOME_CHANNEL_KEEPALIVE` (`{"qorpera":"keepalive"}`) every 20 s and drops the other's, so it never reaches the machine or Codex: the exec-server says nothing while the model thinks or a command runs quietly, and node 2's TLS front ends an upgraded connection that is quiet for its read timeout, which would end the exec-server and all it runs. A channel the executor refuses `HOME_CODEX_NOT_PINNED` (the machine's Codex is not the pinned build) is told `HOME_OUTDATED` (`CLOUD_HOME_OUTDATED`) instead of "could not be reached". The front's own template is `deploy/coding-worker/coding-gateway-nginx.conf` (`scripts/coding-gateway-nginx.mjs` renders it): only `/v1/home-session` passes the upgrade, unbuffered, with 90 s timeouts.
- The agent's home, the channel and Codex's own tools on it, **behind a switch that is off everywhere** (the founder, 13 November 2026, "Yes, all four"; `lanes/agent-box-20261113/HOME-PLAN.md` slices 1, 2 and 4, proven in `P1-CODEX-OWN-TOOLS.md`; migration `20260930752000`, SQL test 1246). With the switch off, which is how every company starts, nothing here runs and a run is exactly as before: the same tools, the same instructions, the same request to the provider, no environment at all. With it on for a company (`qorpera_home_settings` and `qorpera_home_companies`, both the operator's), a run whose own agent has a machine running gets a **home**: the run's sealed slot keeps the model's program and the member's sign-in, and Codex's own shell, stdin and `apply_patch` run on that machine. Its only new way out of the box is one Unix socket in the run's own scratch root, bound read-only as the result fence's is (`cloud-native-isolation.ts` `homeSocket`), which the host relays to the coding executor's one streaming route (`/v1/home-session`, an upgrade on the same ingress, the same transport token, the run's holder proven as every other route proves it, then `qorpera_authorize_home_session`), and from there to `codex exec-server --listen stdio` inside the machine, as the machine's own `qorpera-agent` account (uid 2000), which is not root by default so that what the model's tools write is owned by the agent, and which reaches root without a password (`/etc/sudoers.d/qorpera-agent`, the rule every coding session's user already has), so Codex's own shell can do on that machine everything `machine_run` does there as root: install packages, change system files, start services, use Docker (the lead's correction, 13 Nov 2026: a home whose shell is weaker than the tool it replaces kneecaps the agent). Codex's three features `shell_tool` and `unified_exec` are on and its sandbox is `danger-full-access` (the machine is the boundary); `view_image` stays off, so a tool whose output the result fence cannot admit is never offered at all. **Every command and every change to files is reviewed before it reaches the machine** (`CloudCodexHome`, slice 4), by the same gate, the same machine-work rule and the same "ask first" cards as `machine_run`, and a refusal is the gate's own words, which Codex gives the model as its tool's failure. **The run's work log keeps a step for each of Codex's own calls that acts on the machine** (slice 2), written from the request the host reviewed (a patch from Codex's own word of it), finished with the exact envelope the model was shown: without it a run that worked on its home kept no log at all and, in Shared context, the fold left its own work out. A run with a home starts its own Codex process and leaves no spare. Nothing of this shows a member a command, a step or a terminal. Order in a cut: migration `20260930752000`, then the coding executor on each node, then the machine image (the pinned Codex and the agent account), then the runtime bundle; until the image carries them the switch stays off, and a run whose channel cannot be opened is told in plain words and goes on with the company's own tools. Review round 1 (#1761) and an audit of the build closed these: **the review approves what the model chose, once per call**: a command (its whole text, its folder, whether on a terminal, and the machine; never Codex's per-process ids or environment, so the same command made again after a card is approved is the same action), what it types into a running shell (with the shell it goes into), and a patch once with every file and diff, however many files it touches (a lone file change no patch accounts for is reviewed with the text it writes); each call that changes something has its step committed **before** it happens and finished when its output is released, in the company folder's own paths, so erasure and zoom find it, and an action whose record cannot be kept does not happen (`WORK_LOG_UNAVAILABLE`); a parked card tells the model to do the same thing again itself, since there is no such tool of its own (`CloudActionCall.repeat`); a home that is not there is said in words to each request (`HOME_UNAVAILABLE`, `HOME_NOT_RUNNING`, `HOME_BUSY`), a machine lost mid-command answers what was out there `HOME_LOST`, and a machine that restarted under the run gets a new session on its new boot; either end of the channel ending ends the other; the run's session is closed in SQL when the run ends or is stopped, and a run that comes back supersedes its own; one deadline covers the channel's opening and ends with it; one streaming decoder per connection, shared by every reader; a run whose agent's machine is on another node reaches it through that node's peer listener, as a machine command does.
- The agent's home reaches SQL on a real run host (found 14 Nov 2026 by reading; migration `20260930752000`, SQL test 1246 section 6). The run's host asks for the run's home (`qorpera_company_home_available`) and closes its session (`qorpera_close_home_session`) through the path control and the native host's entry point, as every call of a run's host goes; neither was in the path control's list, in `NATIVE_HOST_CALLS` or in `qorpera_native_host_calls`, and the runtime took the refusal for "no home", so no run would ever have had one. Both are now in all three (scope `binding`, the standard budget), and the coding executor's control lets its `qorpera_authorize_home_session` through (it was not in `CODING_CONTROL_METHODS` either). A failed ask no longer looks like "no home": SQL's answer of no home stays quiet, an ask that cannot work (a call the host's lists or the entry point do not know, a refusal, an answer of another shape) fails the run in the `codex-home` stage before Codex starts, and so does any SQLSTATE PostgREST answers that is not transient (an internal error `XX000`, a missing function or relation, a violated key); only a lost answer (no answer or a late one, a 5xx that is not PostgREST's, or a transient SQLSTATE such as a lock or statement timeout, or `PGRST003`) leaves that attempt without a home, said in the host's journal (`The agent's home was not asked (<category>)`). For that, `CloudControlRpcEnded` keeps the SQLSTATE it was thrown for in `sqlstate`, which the path control fills and only the home's ask reads (never `code`, so what every other caller asks again is unchanged). The dispatch's refusal of a name it does not list is its own error (`NativeHostCallUnknown`), which the path control passes on instead of calling it a lost connection. A session close that fails is said in the host's journal too and still does not fail the run. Tests drive the ask and the close through the real path control and dispatch with a stand-in only below `fetch` (`cloud-home-call-gates.test.ts`, and the company runtime suite's own runs over the dispatch), and guard the class from the sources: every name the runtime passes to its path control is in the path control's list, and every name of `CloudCompanyPathRpc` the list takes is in `NATIVE_HOST_CALLS` or `NATIVE_HOST_IDENTITY_CALLS` and reaches an entry point through the real dispatch, except the three wake checks no runtime source sends (named, and held unsent), and every call the executor's sources make is in its control's list (one named, dated exception: the light ask's `qorpera_native_light_ask_answer`, fixed in its own lane).
- A machine a member woke does not sleep under a run's open home (migration `20260930778000`, SQL test 1276; found merging v248's member sleep, `20260930759000`, into the home). `qorpera_company_machine_member_only` now answers false while a home session that is not closed (`qorpera_company_home_sessions.closed_at` null) is on the machine and its run's job is queued or leased, the same test it makes of an agent's running command; a session whose run ended without closing it keeps nothing awake. Closing a session does not move the machine's last use, so a machine whose member left long ago is due again at once, as after a command's end. 759000's text is patched by exact anchor and nothing else of it changes; SQL test 1256 lists `qorpera_authorize_home_session` among the functions that move a machine's last use (an agent's use, not a member's sign).
- A member sends an app's owner a note about the thing open in a kit's page (Jonas, 13 Nov 2026: "you can't act very much inside of the interfaces"; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-INTERFACE-APPS.md` §3b; the web's `qorpera.tell` is PR #1739; no migration, no node change; reaches agents with the next runtime bundle). The four data kits draw one quiet button at the foot of the detail a tap opens (`board`'s sheet, world and lightbox, `path`'s step sheet, `object`'s part note, `model`'s note under an input's label), only where the page's bridge has `tell`, and a press asks `tell({about, at, text})` for that thing; a refusal shows its reason quietly beside the button for six seconds, moving nothing. The data's `words.tell` and `words.told` are the button's words, and a thing's `ask` (at most 40 characters) its draft. `save_app`'s kits paragraph says so once; the company instructions say that a member's message whose first line is `> app:<name>#<id> · <about>` was sent from that app's page about that thing.
- Interface apps are things to see and handle, drawn by kits (Jonas, 13 Nov 2026, on a new company whose seven apps were each an employee's document made short: "they are just long text documents... there is currently no real difference between the interfaces and just reading the company's files directly", and "they are supposed to be objects, models, apps and videos that are visual-first to let you see the company"; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-INTERFACE-APPS.md` §1 and §3b; no migration, no node change, no change to the web's own code; reaches agents with the next runtime bundle). Four data kits beside the explainer: `save_app` takes `manifest.kit` `model`, `board`, `path` or `object`, the agent writes one data file (`<kit>.json`) and the pictures it names, and `save_app` writes the kit's `engine.js` and an `index.html` that names those pictures (`cloud-app-kits.ts`; engines and their guides in `packages/runtime/kits/<kit>/`, their text in `cloud-app-kit-engines.generated.ts` by `npm run generate:app-kits`). A new kit app without its data is refused (`APP_DATA_MISSING`), data the kit cannot read too (`APP_DATA_INVALID`), and the kit's own two files are never the agent's (`APP_KIT_FILE`). The answer's `look` names the pictures the folder lacks and the texts a kit's page cuts. `save_app`'s description and its `kit` schema say what each kit draws and what its file holds; a page written by hand (the product's own prototype, a working tool) follows the kits, and its look notes start at 250 words and at 80 with nothing to look at (were 500 and 150). The agents' instructions: an app is an object, a model, an app or a video, one app a thing, never a document and never the state of someone's work (that is the line under the agent's name, its wall and its questions); real things are shown by their real pictures; what is not known is drawn as open, never written about; the concept brief is shown to founders as a storyboard, never as text. Hire overlay `qorpera.hire-overlay/v16`: an employee no longer keeps a posted app of its area's state, and the Co-Founder has an app that reads as a document remade with a kit.
- A member's pointer moves the desktop's pointer (migration `20260930744000`, POINTER-BRIEF.md step 1). The executor's member listener takes `POST /v1/member-machine-screens/<companyId>/<machineId>/pointer` (JSON, at most 256 bytes): `{"begin": {"generation": g}}` or `{"begin": {"epoch": e, "seat": s}}` asks for a pointer pass (200 `{schema, pass, milliseconds}`), `{"pass": p, "x": n, "y": n}` moves on it (200 `{schema, moved}`; positions within 3840 x 2160); anything else is 400, and no move or move failure is ever logged (`CloudMemberMachinePointerRequest`, `CloudMemberMachinePointerAnswer`). The peer listener takes `/v1/machine-commands/member-pointer` (`qorpera.member-machine-pointer-http/v1`: a pass id and a position; `createHttpMemberMachineScreenPeer().memberPointer`, a 3 s deadline). New refusals: `CLOUD_MEMBER_MACHINE_POINTER_REFUSALS` (the pass's own as SQL gives them, the screen off, a restart, no X, and `POINTER_PASS_ENDED`). No agent text and no tool changes.
- `company_list` answers one moment of the folder (owed from v240, 13 Nov 2026; no migration, no tool text): it joins the folder core's pages of 200, and a rename between two of them showed a path twice or not at all. A look whose pages answered at different folder clocks is now taken again, at most three looks a call, all within 4 s (twice a look's 2 s, so a look that took its whole 2 s is still taken again once); the answer is a look whose pages answered at one clock (the folder at that moment), and `asOfSeq` is its first page's. The clock is the whole company folder's, so a change elsewhere costs a look too; two looks that read the same are no proof (a file renamed back and forth in step with the pages is missed alike by both, review r1 of #1748). If no look was at one clock, the answer says so in one line before its last: "The folder changed while it was listed, so a path may show twice here or not at all; call again the same way for a clean listing." A one-page listing is one statement and is never looked at again.
- `company_search` says that a file changed since the last answer only when an answer showed part of it (the v240 release review's F2, round 2 of #1731; no migration, no tool text). A cursor inside a file is no proof the model saw any of it: the search can run out of time there before a hit, or the fitting can leave out the file's first hit. The run now keeps, with each cursor it seals, whether an answer showed part of the file the cursor is inside, at that version (still so while later cursors stay in that version), and the notice, at a call's start or between two of its pages, comes only then.
- Agents are named by the work they own, not like people (Jonas, 13 November 2026: "I don't know who does what, and their human-like names makes it difficult to know"; no migration). `hire_agent`'s description and its `name` schema say that the name is how everyone finds the agent in the app: one to three plain words for what it owns, in the founders' language (Customer research, Market, Strategy, Brand, Product, Engineering, Bookkeeping), never a person's name and never a name a coworker already has. Nothing else moves: the handle still comes from the name, and an agent hired earlier keeps the name it was given. The tool catalog pins moved (cloud-native-company-runtime.test.ts, both).
- An agent's machine is ready and used again (agent-box B1; the founder's 13 Nov 2026 decision on the agent's home; migration `20260930747000`, SQL test 1241). A conversation's run may wait in place while its machine starts, as a worker can: `machine_start` takes `waitSeconds` (1 to 60 in a conversation's run, `CLOUD_MACHINE_START_WAIT_SECONDS`; a worker's stays 1 to 300; `machine_read` still waits for workers only). The run stays on the machine's list of runs to tell, so a wait that ends first is woken when the machine runs, as before; the kernel's status look (the wait's own look) suppresses the run's own 'running' word once it sees the machine running, so it is not woken a second time. A new task machine is the agent's own free machine of the same size, woken if it sleeps (`qorpera_company_machine_reusable`: the same agent, member, kind of run, cpus and memory, at least the disk; free: not in line, not starting for someone, no command running, no busy program, no member at its desktop, no run that still goes used it); its name and starter stay, its purpose becomes the new one, and the answer says `reused`. `machine_start`'s description says both in two sentences; a stopped conversation's run is told "a member stopped it".
- A last-resort node is chosen for new work only when no other node has room (a smaller third node as emergency capacity; migration `20260930749000`, SQL test 1243; SQL only, no runtime bundle cut). `qorpera_runtime_nodes.last_resort` (false for every node, so nothing changes until an operator sets it) and `qorpera_set_runtime_node_last_resort(node, flag, expectedVersion, reason)`, modelled on the runs switch: the service role only, the policy version checked and not bumped, each change kept with its reason in `qorpera_runtime_node_last_resort_changes`; a new policy version keeps the flag. Every chooser of new work ranks a last-resort node below every other node that is eligible and fits, one key placed before the free-room keys: the run and sign-in candidates (which carry it as `lastResort`), the node chooser (`qorpera_runtime_node_placement_decide`: a healthy last-resort node still beats the node where the run's last start failed), the sign-in and account-refresh gateway route, run placement for orchestrator roles, a new Company Computer's fleet placement, agents' machine candidates, a member's new coding machine, and a company's coding executor enrollment (an executor on a last-resort node only when no other enabled one serves the harness). For agent runs there is no strict capacity guarantee today, by either route: the choosers rank a last-resort node last and that is all the flag claims, because the claim turn is not gated on it (yes at once with no dispatch role, after 1.5 s for a fast host, after 10 s for an overflow host). `dispatch_role = 'overflow'` on that node's orchestrator is a brake only while the regular orchestrators are declared `fast` and alive: set on the spare alone, with their roles unset as they are today, it has the reverse effect, because `qorpera_runtime_run_placement` then answers `NO_FAST_HOST` and the overflow branch reads that as permission to claim at once. Even with them `fast` it is bounded, since that branch returns true unconditionally once a run has waited 10 seconds. The honest ceiling for runs is "the last-resort node takes runs the fleet has left waiting ten seconds", and a strict rule needs a design pass over the claim turn and the reservation together. For every other kind of work it is a guarantee, because those paths choose the node themselves. A last-resort node's machine executor admits a waiting machine, or gives up a stopped machine's disk for one, only when no other node could fit it now (the candidates' own test). `qorpera_company_machine_candidates` now also asks what the node's observation reports about its hardware, as the run and sign-in candidates, the coding executor's pick and admission itself (`NODE_HARDWARE_CHANGED`) all do: a node reporting fewer CPUs or less memory than its policy counts on was a candidate that no machine could actually be admitted to. Sticky choices are unchanged: a warm conversation's node, a machine waking on its node, a member's coding machine or storage, a routed sign-in.
- A member's shared action carries the desktop's lease generation its input was made under (migration `20260930738000`): the executor's member listener takes `{"action": {...}, "generation": g}` beside `{"action": {...}}` (a safe whole number, 0 or more; never beside an epoch or a seat; anything else is 400) and passes it on as `CloudMemberMachineActRequest.generation`; `DESKTOP_HELD_SINCE` (someone held the desktop privately since the input was made) is a member action refusal (409). A page before it sends none and is served as before.
- machine_screen tells the agent that a member used the screen it shares with members (migration `20260930734000`): the answer's `memberTouched` (`CloudMachineScreenAnswer.memberTouched`, `{name, secondsAgo}`, never what was done; a name of up to 256 characters, counted as code points) becomes one line of the result, said until an answer that carries it is ready (a call refused after SQL's read, the desktop busy, says it at the next call), "{name} used this screen {n} seconds ago (members can use it with you): what you saw before may have changed, so go by this picture before you act" ("look again" when no picture came). `DESKTOP_BUSY` now says "another run or a member is acting on this desktop right now". The member listener takes a shared action, the action alone (`{"action": {...}}`; `CloudMemberMachineActRequest` with `shared: true`); one of an epoch and a seat without the other stays a bad request. `DESKTOP_ACTIONS_TOO_FAST` joins a member's action refusals. No tool description and no catalog pin moved.
- A member answers in a mail thread: `qorpera_send_member_mail` takes `replyTo` (Jonas, 12 Nov 2026: the Mailbox gets "similar answer/reply buttons as in the real gmail/outlook app, not the chatcomposer to tell other agents what to reply"; contract section 19, "Mail is an event source (M2)", step M2e+; migration `20260930740000`, SQL test 1234; SQL only, no runtime bundle cut). `replyTo` is `{kind, id}`, the message the email answers as `qorpera_member_mail_thread` names it: a received email of the company by its delivery id, or a sent one (an agent's or a member's) by its mail id. The email joins that message's thread and carries `In-Reply-To` and `References` (the answered message's id after the ids of its own References, the first and the newest that fit one header line, built by the new `qorpera_mail_reply_references`), so the other side's mail program threads it and the answer to it joins the same thread. A message with no usable id is answered in its thread without those headers. It stays the member's own act: no AI line, no automatic review, and unlike an agent's reply it answers automatic email and never carries `Auto-Submitted` (`in_reply_to_delivery_id` stays empty on a member's email, so the outbox's claim is unchanged). A message that is not the company's, or is account mail, is refused (42501); a `replyTo` of another shape is 22023. `qorpera_send_agent_mail` is not touched. Two reads follow it. A received email now joins the thread of a message its mailbox knows, sent or received, that it answers or references first (`qorpera_mail_received_note`; before, only its first reference was looked up, among sent email alone), so the answer to a member's reply that names only a received email comes back to the thread. And `qorpera_member_mail_thread` gives a received message's `replyTo`, the addresses its Reply-To header names, while its stored message is kept, so the Mailbox's Reply goes where the email asks.
- The import's follow-ups, part R (own code storage, step 6; `lanes/own-code-storage/STEP6-FOLLOW-UPS-PLAN.md` sections 1, 3, 7 and 8 with `STEP6-BUILD.md` "The import's follow-ups: the lead's calls"; reads the keys of migration `20260930741000` and of the coding executor's new look, and needs neither: every new key is optional, so with today's SQL and executors each answer reads as before). `manage_code` `bring_in`: the look's `lines` (the other branches with changes main lacks, at most ten shown) are read closed, and the look-only answer names them with how many changes each holds that main lacks and how far it is behind main, marks one whose files the folder cannot hold, and says how to bring one in as main instead; the brought-in answer names them and how a coding session merges one into main. `from.branch` is taken beside `github` too (the lead's decision 3: SQL and the executor's fetch already took it), by SQL's rule for a branch name, with its own words when the source has no such branch; the schema's `branch` says so, so the catalog text moves (names and descriptions do not). The import read takes an optional twelfth key `file`: once a file came in, the answer says whether the file there is still the bundle that was read, with the folder's version of it (a UUID), so the agent's `company_delete` under that version removes only that bundle, or says to leave it. `tidy` is no longer a step (no executor wrote it; C1 refuses it). `openWork` takes `codeBroughtIn` (1 to 8 items: the place, where it came from as a member reads it, who asked, when, the earlier changes and the other lines of work), read closed, and the staff instructions' "# Open work" paragraph ends with what it is and to read the repository's `changes/1.md` first. The coding session is told how to bring a branch under `refs/qorpera/imported/heads/` into the default branch: merge it into its own branch, run the checks, commit and finish. Pins: conversation and private instructions `2a84534c…` → `e99256e9…`, catalog text `ceef1a8c…` → `cad90139…` on its own base; with master's organize_apps colours and overlay v15 merged in (13 Nov 2026), measured on the merged tree: instructions `e14e3c2d…` (master's) → `931fa606…`, catalog text `12b5fae5…` (master's) → `5aed3950…`; worker instructions, prompts and the names and descriptions are master's.
- Open work carries `codeBroughtIn` (code a member or another agent brought into Qorpera, told once to every active staff agent but the asker, at its next run; no wake), and an import's read says `file` (null, or the path with the folder's version of the bundle that was read while it is still that bundle) (own code storage, the import's follow-ups, part A; migration `20260930741000`, SQL test 1235; table `qorpera_code_import_tellings`, function `qorpera_take_agent_code_imports`). **Order in its release: this runtime first, then the migration, then the coding executors.**
- A command's output fills one result (keyholes S1; the contract's 2026-11-12 amendment "a command's output fills one result"; Jonas, 12 Nov 2026: "Yes, build them"; no migration). `machine_read` and `coding_read_command` ask for one result's room by default (31,968 bytes, `CLOUD_COMMAND_PAGE_ASK`; `limit` 4 to 32,768 asks for less; it was 4,096 by default and 16,384 at most) and cut the page to what fits once, at an exact cursor (`fitCloudCommandPage`), instead of halving and asking again: plain output shows up to about 31 KiB in one call. A page is asked again only for a cut whose cursor it cannot tell (U+FFFD on both sides of it), for exactly the bytes that fit. `machine_run` reads its command once at once when its start shows less than a read can (a start on a machine with the company folder holds its output back; a command that already ended printed more than the start's first page), and `coding_run_command` when its command already ended with more output than its start shows; a refused or failed read keeps the start's answer, and nothing waits for the command. The executor's service says on every answer what a page may ask it for (`x-qorpera-command-page-bytes: 65536`, `cloud-command-pages.ts`); the coding and machine gateways learn it per executor (one memory per process, and per router for an executor's peers) and ask an executor that has not said so for 16,384 at most; an executor that stops saying it (one rolled back under a running runtime) refuses a larger page once, and that read is asked once more, in the same call, for 16,384 (`scripts/prove-s1-command-output.mjs` proves both orders end to end). The machine guest's `prepare` answer says `pageBytes`. The descriptions of `machine_run`, `machine_read`, `coding_run_command` and `coding_read_command` and the two read tools' `limit` maximum (32,768) changed (the company run's catalog pins do not cover the machine or coding tools, and did not move). The coding command guest's program keeps its size: the shared parse writes the new bound as a number of the old one's length. Order in a cut: the coding executor on each node first, then the runtime bundle; the machine image whenever it next rolls (until then machines' guests are asked for 16,384 at most).
- Agents give the apps drawer's groups the company's colours (Jonas, 12 Nov 2026: "have the co-founder pick colours for fjeldtur's groups"; the 2026-11-11 amendment "groups of apps", item 7; no migration: the cloud client's `app-groups.ts` reads and writes the colours line, `<!-- group colours: trips=teal, market=green -->`, and the web draws them). `organize_apps` keeps a group's `colour` through every change (rename, move of the group, move of an app into it, `save_app`'s `group`); `ungroup` takes it away with the group. `create_group` takes an optional `colour`, one of the agents' palette (`AGENT_COLOURS`; a group of that name already there gets it, and `null` is none), and the new action `colour_group {group, colour}` sets it, `null` taking it away. A name not in the palette is refused ("colour must be one of the palette's colours (charcoal, …, gray), or null for none"), a group the layout does not have too ("No group <id>"), and a colour already so saves nothing and says so. `list` adds each group's colour to its line, `group trips: Fjeldtur Trips (4 apps, teal)`, with its paging unchanged. The input schema's `colour` is `["string", "null"]` with the palette's enum. The description names `colour_group` and gets one more rule of a good layout (now seven, well inside 4096 bytes): every group gets a colour that sets it apart and fits its product or area, by the palette's names, a group inside a group usually takes its parent's (left unset), and members' own colours win in their drawer. The Co-Founder's role overlay is `qorpera.hire-overlay/v15`: its tidy pass also takes a top-level group without a colour as a sign. The tool catalog pins moved (cloud-native-company-runtime.test.ts, both, with the hire overlay's schema there), and so did the three folder-run instruction and prompt pins (cloud-native-company-context.test.ts), for overlay v15. Ships with the next runtime cut.
- `company_read` reads several files in one call (keyholes S3; the contract's 2026-11-12 amendment, item 2; Jonas, 12 Nov 2026: "Yes, build them"; no migration). It takes `paths` (1 to 8, each once) instead of `path`, and `offset` and `limit` may be left out with `path` (0, and 32 KiB). Each file is read from its start into one result, every text cut at the same number of characters, the largest that fits (small files whole, the rest sharing what is left alike), and each says its version, `bytesRead`, `totalBytes` and `nextOffset`, read on with `path`. Each path is answered on its own (absent, a folder, a link's target, `refused` with today's words, or not readable now: the folder's own failure, or a request that timed out or lost its connection); stopped work, a cancel, bytes that fail their check or a revoked read show nothing. The folder's byte transport reads an answer cut off after its headers as a lost answer (`transport_unavailable`), as a failed fetch already was. An image among several is named, not shown; alone it is shown as before. Each file shown is its own release under the hash of the whole answer, which cites the first. The paths' entries are read side by side and their bytes one file at a time, so a call never contends with itself at the object gateway, which serves two operations at a time and one on any one object, and answers 503 for a file just written when it cannot take it now; files of the same content share objects (the proof sweep of #1700, and its review r4). After #1721 (13 Nov 2026): a read of several files cuts a secret out of its answer before it hashes and releases it, as `released()` does, so its context-view citation holds and the run goes on; and no window starts or ends inside a secret (`cloudSecretSpans`, from the boundary's own patterns): a file read from any offset, files read together and cut alike, a link's target and a listed target's start each end before a secret their edge would cut, take a secret they start at whole (past `limit` if need be), and start at a secret's start when asked to start inside it; the read takes 1 KiB past and before the window to see them. A secret-shaped run longer than that is shown cut as far as read, and the answer says so (`secretRun`). Tests in cloud-folder-read-windows.test.ts; proof in scripts/prove-s3-multi-read.mjs. The tool catalog pins moved (cloud-native-company-runtime.test.ts, both).
- `company_list` shows a folder's tree in one call (keyholes S2; the contract's 2026-11-12 amendment "more of the company folder in one call", item 1; Jonas, 12 Nov 2026: "Yes, build them"; no migration). It takes `depth` (1 to 8; default 1, or 8 with a glob) and `glob` (company_search's syntax, not case-sensitive), and `after` and `limit` may be left out (limit 1 to 500, default 500; it was at most 50). The answer is one short line an entry (a file's path, a tab and its size; a folder's path and /; a link's path, a tab and `->` its target, cut at 512 bytes), with the folder, its version and `asOfSeq` first and where to go on last; entries no longer carry versions. Past depth 1 it filters the folder core's subtree listing in the runtime, looking through at most 5,000 entries or about 2 s of pages a call, and says where it got to. Everything shown is released, the cursor too. The tool catalog pins moved (cloud-native-company-runtime.test.ts, both).
- A machine's desktop shows the company's time (Jonas, 12 Nov 2026, asked whether each company gets a time zone taken from the founder's browser so the clock is right: "Yes, from the browser"; machine image only: `deploy/machine/desktop` and the build's checks; no SQL, no tool change, the guest helper and its pin untouched). The dock's clock showed UTC, an hour off for a Danish founder, unless `desktop start --tz` said otherwise. Now `desktop start` without `--tz` takes the company's time zone from `/etc/qorpera-machine/desktop-timezone`, one word in root's file that the machine's executor writes (its own change, with the company's zone in SQL): the programs' `TZ`, the dock's clocks and the backdrop's hour follow it. Only the start of a desktop reads it: a desktop that runs keeps the zone it has, and a word that changes meanwhile is taken at the next start; `--tz` still wins. The word came from a browser, so only a plain file of root's own is read (no link, no pipe), its bytes must be exactly the word or the word and one line end (compared byte for byte, since the shell's `read` skips a NUL: review r1 F1 of #1718), and the word has a zone name's shape (letters, digits, `_`, `+`, `-`, at most two `/`, 64 bytes at most) before it is put after `/usr/share/zoneinfo/`, where it must name a file; anything else, and a machine without the file, leaves the desktop on UTC as before. The machine's own time zone stays UTC. The image build checks it on the image with XFCE's own answers, and refuses an image that keeps such a file.
- Root follows no link for the desktop's account on a machine (review r2 of #1704, 12 Nov 2026; machine image only; no SQL, no tool change, the guest helper and its pin untouched). `desktop open`, `--copy` and `desktop shot` / `--shot` worked on paths with root's rights. The account `desktop`, where pages and an agent's programs on the desktop run, could make a name on such a path a link: `--copy` then handed it a file only root may read (the company folder's view with its pass, say), a screenshot was written over any file or into any folder, and root's link or copy landed in a folder of root's through the account's Desktop folder. A look at the path before its use closed none of it (a link in the middle of a way root owns; a part of the way planted after the look). Now `desktop` does nothing with a target's path itself. A new program, `/opt/qorpera-machine/desktop-files`, does the work in one of two ways: as the account, in a process of the account's own (its user and groups, no descriptor of root's), which is how everything in the account's Desktop folder is done; or as root inside folders it holds, opened part by part without following a link, on a way of folders that root alone can change (owner, group's and everyone's rights, ACLs). `--copy` of what lies on such a way is read by root and put on the desktop by the account's process; anything else the account copies with its own rights, or it is refused with the part of the way that stopped root. A picture is taken into root's own folder and written by root only where the whole way is root's alone (`/company`, `/root`); elsewhere the account saves it as its own file. A copy is made under a name the desktop does not show and gets its name in one step when it is whole; what is cut off (root's wait for the account's side has an end, and another may end that process) is taken away again by the account at root's asking, after everything that process had started is ended and collected, so no half copy and no half picture stays or comes back. For an agent: a picture under `/tmp` is now the account's file, a picture into a folder root made under `/tmp` is refused with the way out, and `--copy` of a file only root can read wants a way without a link. The image build proves it with both accounts, the review's two cases and the races in the Desktop folder (deploy/machine/README.md, "Root and the desktop's account").
- Root keeps its notes about a machine's desktop where only root writes (review r1 F1 of #1704, 12 Nov 2026; machine image only, with the look above; no SQL, no tool change, the guest helper and its pin untouched). Root's `desktop` kept its notes about the desktop (the language, the time zone, the idle clock, the folders it serves) in `/run/qorpera-desktop`, which belonged to the account `desktop`, where pages and an agent's programs on the desktop run. The account could make `lang` there a link to a file only root may read, such as the company folder's view with its pass, and the next program root started on the desktop was handed that file as its `LANGUAGE`: for an agent's own `desktop open` and `desktop run` in the image that was live, and at a press on the new dock. Now `/run/qorpera-desktop` is root's folder (the account may read it and can change nothing in it; the paths the screen helper and the executor touch stay) and the account's own runtime folder is `/run/qorpera-desktop/session`. What the display says of its size is taken only as digits (it went into the shell's arithmetic unchecked). The image build proves the boundary with both accounts and a file only root can read (deploy/machine/README.md, "Root and the desktop's account"). What `desktop open` does in the account's Desktop folder, `--copy` and `desktop shot`: the entry above.
- A machine's desktop looks like the office desktops did (Jonas, 12 Nov 2026, on the first desktop computers: "what happened to the agent wallpapers? the docker has 4 settings icons instead of file explorer and terminal? … why did we regress so much since the "office" computers"; machine image only: `deploy/machine/`, no SQL, no tool change, the guest helper and its pin untouched). When desktops moved onto company machines the office desktop's look was never carried over: the image had stock XFCE with its default bars, no backdrop and launchers without pictures. Now: one flat dark dock along the bottom with exactly three tools (browser, files, terminal) under Qorpera's own icons, the date at its left and the time at its right, a mark under an icon whose program is open, no top bar and no desktop icons; a backdrop for each hour of the day with the figure the machine names in `/etc/qorpera-machine/desktop-figure` (one word; nothing writes it yet, so every machine shows the neutral Qorpera backdrop until the next step hands the agent's figure in); the office's window and terminal settings. A press on a dock icon brings back the program's window or starts the program the way `desktop open URL` and `desktop run` do, as a unit of its own: the browser a member opens from the dock is the one an agent's `desktop open` finds. A press can steer nothing but which of the three programs starts: root's side works in `/`, takes from the request folder only which name is there, and starts the program through `desktop dock`, an entry for the dock alone whose launches look no word up as a file and put nothing on the desktop (review r3 F1 of #1704). The look is written into the desktop's account at every `desktop start` from pictures the image holds read-only (5.7 MiB), so it cannot drift or be lost. `desktop start` returns once the backdrop is drawn (1.2 to 1.6 s, 2.7 s before), so a first look never shows a bare screen; its commands, exit codes and messages are unchanged. Reaches a machine with the next machine image, at its next start from it (deploy/machine/README.md, "The desktop's look").
- One tool result holds up to 32 KiB, not 8 KiB (perf sprint round 2, T2, 8 Nov 2026; proposed in the contract's 2026-11-08 amendment, Jonas decides; migration `20260930733000`, SQL test 1226). The patched Codex's result fence, `MAX_CLOUD_TOOL_RESULT_BYTES`, the context view and the run log all move to 32 KiB; Codex's history keeps such an output whole under the session's `tool_output_token_limit=65536`, as it does today. `company_read` asks for up to 32 KiB a call and shows about 31 KiB of plain text, instead of about 7 KiB, so a run reads a long file in a quarter of the rounds. It needs the rebuilt Codex pin: the bundle that carries this constant must carry that binary. The run log's shared parser (`memory-run-files.ts`, which the runtime runs on every log block it stages, reads and resumes from) keeps a step's result to the same bound, from one constant (`CLOUD_TOOL_RESULT_MAX_BYTES`); `MAX_CLOUD_TOOL_RESULT_BYTES` and the run log's field bounds (`CLOUD_RUN_LOG_FIELD_BYTES`, `CLOUD_RUN_LOG_READS_BYTES`, `CLOUD_RUN_LOG_STEP_BYTES`) now come from the cloud client, values unchanged. `read_event`'s description says what one piece shows now (up to 16,000 characters of plain text, or about 23 KiB as base64; it said about 4,000 and about 3 KiB); the tool catalog pins moved again.
- The package caches' trim goes on while a file it counted stays on disk (a release review's finding on v236; own code storage, step 6, part W; no migration). A file hard-linked into two tool folders was counted only in the first folder walked, so emptying that folder took its bytes off although the other folder still held them, and the trim could stop with the caches still over the limit. `trimCodingPackageCaches` now counts each file and folder once and how many tool folders hold it; emptying a folder takes off only what no folder still holds. Where no file is linked between two tool folders (the image proof's trim case), it empties the same folders as before.
- Agents bring existing code into Qorpera with `manage_code`, action `bring_in` (own code storage, step 6, part D, commit 2; `lanes/own-code-storage/STEP6-IMPORT-BUILD.md` sections 4.2, 4.4 and 6 as `STEP6-BUILD.md` "The import: what the second check changed" corrects them; needs migration `20260930736000` (part A) and the coding executor's import (parts B, C1, C2, C3, Q) live first). `bring_in` takes `operationKey`, `owner`, `name` (the new repository's place, `code/<owner>/<name>/`), `from` (`{"github":"Owner/Name"}`, also read from a GitHub address, or `{"file":"code/_incoming/<folder>/<name>.bundle"}`, a machine's `/company/…` path read as the company path, with `branch` only for a file) and `look`; `change` is no longer required by the schema and stays required for undo and describe. The tool asks `qorpera_folder_request_code_import` (a run effect under the run's receipt, as undo) and then looks at `qorpera_folder_code_import` every 2 s for 30 s, as an undo's: its answers say what the look found (size, history, how many people other than the company's agents changed it in the last 30 days, files, whether it fits, and the strings that look like keys by kind and file, "still in the code" when main holds them), that the code is in (with change 1 and, for GitHub code, that the copy on GitHub stays and Qorpera no longer works on it there; for a file, that Qorpera no longer needs the bundle it read from that path and that the agent deletes the file only if it is still the bundle it made for this and leaves any other file there, since Qorpera leaves it where it is and another file may stand at the path by then; the answer carries no SHA-256 of the file), why nothing was brought in (one sentence for each of the kernel's seven refusals and the executor's codes, `[code=…]` only on a failure or a code with no words of its own), or where a running import is. The folder client's two calls read their answers closed (`qorpera.code-import/v1`, and the look as part C1's `codeImportLookOf` reads it). Both calls are on the native host's list (`NATIVE_HOST_CALLS`), the path control's folder calls (their errors kept as SQL gave them) and its permitted list, and the gateway's call names. An undo the kernel answers as a wall at change 1 now says that change 1 is the code as it came into Qorpera, not that a restore stands in the way. The company's agents are told that code on GitHub or in a file comes in with `bring_in`, with a look first and a member asked once when people other than the company's agents changed it in the last 30 days, and that GitHub is used only when a member asks for it by name; a coding session is told that a brought-in repository keeps its other branches under `refs/qorpera/imported/heads/`, to read and never change. The conversation and private instruction pins and both catalog pins move; the worker's does not.
- Every machine has compressed memory and a guard at the edge of its memory, and a desktop action on the spot the pointer is on is fast (Jonas, 12 Nov 2026, on whether 2 processors and 4 GiB are enough for a desktop computer: they are, and the size tests found three things that mattered more; machine image only: `deploy/machine/`, no SQL, the guest helper and its pin untouched). (1) `desktop click`, `double`, `move` and `drag` on the spot the pointer is already on took 15 s, because xdotool's `mousemove --sync` waits for a move that never comes; the move is now waited for only when the pointer will move (0.12 s). (2) A machine sets up compressed memory at every boot, before `basic.target`: a zram swap with zstd, as large as its memory, priority 100, `vm.swappiness=150`, `vm.page-cluster=0` (`machine-memory swap`, `qorpera-machine-memory.service`). A 4 GiB desktop that froze past 7 tabs beside heavy web apps and a 1.5 GB job carried 24 with it. It never fails or holds up a boot, says in one journal line when it cannot be set up, and the image build fails on a kernel without the zram module. (3) When memory and that reserve are both nearly gone, one process is ended and the machine keeps answering: earlyoom from our own unit in the control slice (`machine-memory guard`, `qorpera-machine-memory-guard.service`; it acts with about 300 MiB still available, which is 17 % in earlyoom's measure on 4 GiB and 29 % on 2 GiB, and a tenth of the reserve free; SIGKILL), by the kernel's own `oom_score`, so a browser tab goes first, then the biggest other program. The LXD agent and what the executor runs through it, the folder program, the watchdog and the desktop's X server and session are protected (`OOMScoreAdjust`), from the kernel's own killer too. An agent whose own job is ended sees its command fail with exit code 128 and no output when the program was the command itself (the guest helper reports a signal as 128), and `Killed` with 137 when a shell stays above it. Sizes, the least size of a desktop computer and billing are unchanged. Proven on throwaway machines from a candidate image (`scripts/prove-machine-memory.sh`, deploy/machine/README.md "Memory"). Needs a new machine image on node 2; machines asleep at the roll wake from it, running ones after their next sleep.
- Three follow-ups of own code storage's steps 4 and 6 in the words agents read (step 6, part D, commit 1; `lanes/own-code-storage/STEP6-IMPORT-BUILD.md` section 6 and `STEP6-BUILD.md` "Part D"; no migration, no tool description changes). The company's agents are told that when `changes/index.md` says a repository was put back to an earlier copy, the changes it names are gone and are made again if still wanted (the coding executor writes that line for 30 days after an operator's restore, part F), and that a change whose checks pass shows on What's new within a minute after it goes in (step 4's live proof: an agent read "nothing ready" seconds after a landing and spent a turn on it). A coding session is told that the checks file's `site` may give `title`, the name members see for the site (1 to 120 characters on one line, no space at either end; without one, What's new shows the site's address). No word in the company's or the coding session's instructions promised a look at a site before it is live, so none changed for that. The conversation and private instruction pins and the worker's move.
- A coding machine keeps the package downloads of its sessions and of Qorpera's checks (own code storage, step 6, part W; `lanes/own-code-storage/STEP6-BUILD.md`; no migration). The coding guest agent makes `/var/cache/qorpera-packages` (`CODING_PACKAGE_CACHES`) at the first mount of each boot, before it looks for the session's user: it writes `/etc/tmpfiles.d/qorpera-packages.conf` (the folder and one per tool, each root's in the group `qorpera-coding`, mode 2775, with the group's rwx as access and default ACL entries, so what one session or `sudo` makes there the next can add to whatever its umask; tmpfiles' age `30d`, which the image's `systemd-tmpfiles-clean.timer` applies) and runs `systemd-tmpfiles --create` on it, then empties whole tool folders, the least recently read, written or changed first, while together they hold more than 8 GiB (`trimCodingPackageCaches`; a folder stays, emptied). A record in `/run/qorpera-guest` keeps it to once a boot. A step that fails leaves no record and the mount goes on as before; the next mount tries again. Asked in v2 (`qorpera.coding-guest-mount/v2`), the mount answer says whether the caches are ready (`packageCaches`); asked in v1 it is exactly the answer from before. The tools' trees (browsers, npx packages, uv archives and environments, pnpm's links: `CODING_PACKAGE_CACHES.tools[].trees`) are `x` lines in the tmpfiles file, so tmpfiles never takes a file out of one, and the agent removes each whole at the first mount of a boot, before the trim, when nothing in it was read, written or changed for 30 days (`ageCodingPackageTrees`; a folder's own read time, which its listing moves, is not counted; the machine's root is `relatime`, so a browser launched in the last 30 days stays). yarn 1 is not among the tools (the next session could not add to its cache). The coding session's words about its machine gain one sentence: "Packages you download with npm, pnpm, pip, uv and Playwright are kept on this machine for later sessions and for Qorpera's checks." A release moves the guest agent's pin in each node's config (`folder.agent`) and the runtime bundle.
- A coding session is told that a repository's site is served under an address of its own, never at the top of a domain: its pages, styles and scripts link its files with relative links, never with links that start with `/`, with a bundler's base path set (Vite's `base: "./"`), and that Qorpera refuses a site whose pages or styles link from the top (own code storage, step 4; 12 Nov 2026; the coding executor refuses such a site with `SITE_LINKS_FROM_ROOT`). The first live site loaded `/src/style.css` from the top of the sites' domain and showed with no design. 336 bytes more on each coding turn; no pin moves (the coding session's instructions have none).
- An app's own icons, drawn by its agent (Jonas, 12 Nov 2026: "change the icons of the interfaces and have a generate icon button too for them to create a new custom one based on it's name or contents"; the 2026-11-11 amendment "groups of apps", item 6; no migration in this part: the web draws the icons and the member's pick, which the database keeps). `save_app` checks every file under an app's `icons/` before anything is read: it must be `icons/<id>.svg`, its id lowercase letters, digits and hyphens, and read as `parseAppIcon` (`@qorpera/cloud-client/app-icons`) reads it, or the call is refused naming the file and what to change, such as `icons/compass.svg: an icon is drawn on the 48 by 48 grid: viewBox="0 0 48 48"`. An app keeps at most 12: a save that adds icons past that is refused with `APP_ICONS_FULL`, which names the three oldest and says to give a new icon the id of one it replaces or to delete one with `company_delete` first (save_app removes nothing); a save that adds none goes through however many the app holds. An explainer may hold icons too. A save of icons alone answers "Tell the member in one line what you drew, and that they pick one under Icon and colour in the app's menu in the apps drawer." The schema says on `files` how to draw them: on a member's ask, 3 clearly different ones from the app's name and what its page shows, in one call with `manifest` null and only the icons, never changing `manifest.emblem`; the 48 by 48 grid, one bold black shape with its details cut out in white (white strokes 2.6 to 3.4 wide with round caps), only the seven shapes, a few large parts that read at 34 px; and that company_read shows an .svg as text, so the agent looks at them on a machine when machine_run is offered. `manifest.emblem` says on the schema what an emblem is and that members may pick another or one of the app's icons. Fixed with it: the 24 emblems of the cloud client (14 new) put save_app's description at 4177 bytes, past the tool boundary's 4096, so every company run would have failed to register its tools; the description no longer lists them ("emblem: one of the 24 pictures its schema lists"), the schema's enum does. The tool catalog pins moved (cloud-native-company-runtime.test.ts, both); the instruction pins did not. Ships with the next runtime cut.
- `organize_sidebar` says what members see now that each has their own sidebar (the 2026-11-11 amendment "each member's own sidebar", item 4; Jonas, 11 Nov 2026: "Yes, do it"; no migration). Its description no longer says everyone sees a change at once: it changes the company's sidebar, which every member sees except where a member placed things themself, and what a member placed is theirs, like pins and hidden agents. The tool catalog pins moved (cloud-native-company-runtime.test.ts, both). Ships with the next runtime cut.
- A repository's website goes live only from Qorpera's What's new page, on a member's yes (own code storage, step 4; 11 Nov 2026; migration `20260930729000`; the coding executor keeps each change's site and uploads it; the web draws the page). Every run's core, a worker's too: #1637's paragraph on changes going live is rewritten. Agents keep no `whats-new` app; a website made from a repository kept in Qorpera shows on What's new with its notice and picture and a member puts it live there, so the agent never publishes it, never asks about it in a question card, and says in one line that it waits there; anything else that goes live gets one question card (Put it live, Not yet); the urgent fix stays, and a repository's website still waits for a member's Go back. The company's agents (the code bullet): the checks file's `"site": {"folder": "dist"}`, `changes/index.md` says which version is live, an undo goes live with the next change that passes, and a broken live site is a member's Go back. A coding session: a website adds `site` to its checks file (index.html at its top, at most 500 files and 25 MB in at most 100 folders, none hidden, no secret value of the session), with an optional `name` used once. `publish_site` refuses a source whose first part is `code` (any letters) in its arguments, before the review and any card. `organize_apps` takes `whats-new`, the platform's page (titled What's new, present always as `sites` is). `publish_site`'s and `share_app`'s descriptions and answers say the password is on the Sites page in the apps drawer (Sites left Settings on 9 Nov). Bytes a run: a worker's instructions 405 fewer; a conversation's or a private conversation's 24 fewer, and its tool catalog 53 more; a coding turn's 556 more. All three instruction pins and both catalog pins moved. Order in the cut: after the coding executor (v233's checks parser refuses a checks file with `site`).
- What each agent's work waits on the member for, in one read (migration `20260930723000`, SQL test 1215; Jonas, 11 Nov 2026: "I would like the agents to be better at indicating when they need the users input and it's really stopped at you. it's hard to tell right now."). `qorpera_list_member_waiting_work(profile)` answers `{schema: "qorpera.member-waiting-work/v1", agents}`, newest agent first, each `{staffAgentId, sessionId, count, questions, approvals, latestAt, kind, what}`: the ask_member cards (question, secret, takeover, draft) raised for the caller in the agents' own threads that are still open, by `qorpera_list_member_open_questions`' rules (an active room bound to the agent they are an active member of, its conversation open and company-wide, not past its time, not answered or being answered; none for a viewer, at most 20 a thread), and the approval cards that wait for the caller's answer, by `qorpera_list_member_waiting_approvals`' rules (their own, and for an owner or admin the company's, in the agent's own open thread they may read). `kind` and `what` are the newest item's: its surface or `approval`, and its title or the approval's action title in one line of at most 120 characters, "…" where cut. Stable and read-only, no table or index; authenticated only (a non-member is refused, 42501), on the API allowlist and in CLIENT-CONTRACT.md. The web reads it for the rail and the tab's title; a web on a database without it reads the approval cards alone.
- Agents put what their work waits for on a card (no SQL; Jonas, 11 Nov 2026, as above). `ask_member`'s description and its question schema now say a question goes on a card for every answer the work cannot go on without, never only in chat, open questions too (the likely answers and `allowCustom`, which the card already shows as a field for the member's own answer), that the card's body says plainly what is stopped until they answer ("I've stopped the price list until you pick one"), and that optional ideas, follow-up offers and news never go on a card; it no longer sends open-ended blocking questions to chat. The company context's "Writing to people" says the same in a short paragraph, and the wall's waiting items mirror the open cards (each card goes on it in the same turn, an answered one comes off). Every run's instructions and the tool catalog change, so their pinned hashes moved.
- A coding session and the company's agents are told that Qorpera runs a repository's checks before a change goes into its main code (own code storage, step 3; 11 Nov 2026; migration `20260930721000`; the coding executor runs the checks; no tool description changes). The session's instructions say what `.qorpera/checks.json` holds (each check a name, a `run` command, and optional `timeoutSeconds` and `cwd`), that each runs from the top of a clean copy of what would go in, on the session's machine, with `CI=true` and stdin closed, and that only work whose every check passes goes in. They ask the session to add the file in its change when the repository has none (with one for a Node project), to keep checks free of its own servers and ports and of files git does not track, to run them itself the same way from a new `git clone --shared` copy in its home (`~/qorpera-check`, removed first) before `coding_finish`, never to remove or weaken a check or a test to pass, and never to commit test output or logs as proof. A merge with a default branch that moved is checked as merged, and half-done work is not handed in since it would not pass its checks. The company's agents are told that a change whose checks fail goes nowhere and its turn fails, so they follow up and, after three failures in a row, narrow the change or drop it; that checks that could not run are no fault of the code; that the record says which checks passed and whether the change changed them, and to read why when it did; that a repository with no checks file takes its changes unchecked until its next session adds one; to check what checks cannot by opening what a change built; and that Qorpera does not check an undo. A coding turn's instructions grow by 1,325 bytes, every company agent's run by 695; a worker's instructions and the tool catalog do not change. Cut it after the coding executor that runs the checks: before it, sessions write checks files nothing runs.
- `update_wall` takes `now`, the one line the sidebar shows under the agent's name: where its work stands, at most 60 characters, such as "Prices set, now choosing suppliers" (Jonas, 11 Nov 2026: the newest done headline shown there "sucks as a headline"). Every call gives it, or null to keep the one there; a wall without one yet is refused with `WALL_NOW_MISSING`, so each agent writes it at its next update, and a new one past 60 characters with `WALL_NOW_TOO_LONG`. The tool's description and the agent's context now say what a wall line says: what a member now has, what was decided or what is needed from them, in everyday words and the active voice; never the step taken ("Prices checked", "Requirements documented") and never the team's own housekeeping (logins, access, the team's own tools or rules it set for itself), which the sidebar showed as "Lokal adgangskode er afleveret" and "Gamle mandatgodkendelser må ikke genoplives". The tool catalog and the conversation instructions' bytes change. No migration; ships with the next runtime cut.
- Agents sort the apps drawer into groups (APP-GROUPS-SPEC §1 and §3; Jonas, 11 Nov 2026: "the same way we have categories and groups for agents, we should have so for the interfaces as well … the agents should be able to organize the interfaces for you natively and help group interfaces to products and work"; migration `20260930726000` only adds `organize_apps` to the operations a run's observations may name). The company's layout is the plain file `apps/groups.md` (the cloud client's `app-groups.ts`). `organize_apps` (new `cloud-folder-app-groups.ts`), the twin of `organize_sidebar`, offered to every company run that is not a worker's, writes it through the folder client under the call's receipt with the version read: `list` shows every drawer key the company has (`page:mailbox`, `page:members`, `page:bank`, `app:<name>` for each app folder with its manifest's title, and `(not posted)` after it when not posted, `concepts` while there is a concept, and `sites` when the file lists it) in its group, one line each in the file's own form (`group <id>: <name> (n apps)`, two spaces a level), the loose entries last in the drawer's order, with `groups`, `looseApps` and `notInCompany`, a page at a time within one tool result (`cursor`); a change answers only what it did, so its answer and receipt stay small however many apps there are; `create_group {name, parent?, items?}` answers the new id (a slug of the name, never one the file has or retired; a group of that name already there is that group), `rename_group`, `ungroup` (its entries stand where it stood, and its id is kept retired in the file) and `move {item, group|null, before?}` (a key or `group:<id>`; loose without `before` is last, after the entries the file does not list). It refuses a third group level, a group inside itself and a key the company does not have (a concept app names `concepts` instead); a change already made saves nothing and says so, an app whose folder is gone leaves the file with the next change, and a save that meets a newer file says refused. Its description carries the six rules of a good layout (group by product, customer or piece of work; one to three words; basics loose at the front; two levels, three to twelve apps; members' own changes are theirs; say a change in one line), and it is reviewed as internal. `save_app` takes an optional `group`, a group's id: an app the layout does not list yet goes last in that group in the same commit, one it lists stays where it is, and a group that does not exist (or a concept) is refused before anything is written; its answer says `group` and `groupPlaced`, from the layout write's own outcome. The company context says members find the apps grouped in the drawer and to place a new app in its group, and the Co-Founder's role overlay is `qorpera.hire-overlay/v14`: in its planning runs it looks at `organize_apps list` and sorts the drawer only on a sign (more than eight apps loose, a group of one or of more than twelve, a new product or area with no group). The tool list, both catalog pins and the three folder-run instruction pins move.
- A member reads several paths of their company's folder in one call (migration `20260930722000`, SQL test 1214; Jonas, 11 Nov 2026: "Yes, do it", to putting an app's per-file reads together). `qorpera_member_folder_read_many(environment, paths)` answers `{schema: "qorpera.folder-read-many/v1", reads}`, each path's answer what `qorpera_member_folder_read` answers for it, in the order asked, for 1 to 64 paths (an app's limit of files), with the member's actor checked once and every path read in one snapshot. It decides nothing the single read does not: a path the member may not read looks absent, an invalid path refuses the whole call (22023), a non-member, a snapshot-v1 company and a company being deleted are refused as before. Authenticated only, never anon; on the API allowlist and in CLIENT-CONTRACT.md. Nothing calls it until the web does, and the web reads each path alone where it is missing.
- A member turns an agent's desktop on (20260930714000). The member listener takes `POST /v1/member-machine-screens/<companyId>/<machineId>/start` with the member's token and `{}` (any key is a bad request: what to open is for later), begins the start as the member (`qorpera_member_machine_desktop_start_begin`; a member's hold of the desktop does not stop it), reads it as the executor (`qorpera_member_machine_desktop_start_authority`) and runs it locally or sends it once to the machine's executor (`/v1/machine-commands/member-start` on the peer listener). Answers `{schema: "qorpera.member-machine-start/v1", outcome: started|running}` or 409 with one of `CLOUD_MEMBER_MACHINE_START_REFUSALS`; `DESKTOP_DID_NOT_START` is also logged as `member-machine-screen-error` by its code. No agent text changes.
- save_coding_value after a member held the machine's desktop (v233 release review r1, F32 of #1661): when SQL answers `clear`, the coding executor of the machine's node clears the clipboard (`localMachineCodingValueClear`, the host's `clearClipboard`) and reads nothing; the agent hears `CLIPBOARD_EMPTY` (true then: it copies the key on the site and calls again; `DESKTOP_MEMBER_IN_CONTROL` stays for a member who holds the desktop). No agent text changes.
- The coding executor carries save_coding_value's lease generation (`desktopGeneration`, from `qorpera_authorize_machine_coding_value`) to the machine's host, whose fence drops a box read across a take-over (v233 release review r1, F29 of #1661); a read without it is no authority (`MACHINE_CODING_VALUE_AUTHORITY_INVALID`).
- A member takes over an agent's desktop computer (20260930713000, stage 1b of the live view). The member listener takes `POST /v1/member-machine-screens/<companyId>/<machineId>/act` with the member's bearer token and `{"action": {...}, "epoch": n, "seat": s}` (JSON, at most 16 KiB; one of machine_screen's actions, never `open`, `parseCloudMemberMachineAction`; `epoch` and `seat` the hand-back epoch and the seat of the hold the member's page captured it under, each a safe whole number, 0 or more, passed on as `CloudMemberMachineActRequest.epoch` and `.seat` and never guessed: release review r1 of #1667, F24 and F26 of #1661): 200 `{schema: "qorpera.member-machine-act/v1", exitCode}`, 409 `{code}` (`CLOUD_MEMBER_MACHINE_ACT_REFUSALS`), 400 for a body that is not one action with its epoch and seat, 401, 403 or 503 as a look. The peer route `/v1/machine-commands/member-act` (peer listener only) carries the action's single-use id and the action, a typed text with it, to the machine's node once (`createHttpMemberMachineScreenPeer(...).memberAct`, `parseCloudMemberMachineActPeerRequest`, `parseCloudMemberMachineActAnswer`). SQL gets an action as `cloudMemberMachineActRecord` writes it: a text by its length alone. No error, answer or log carries the text.
- `DESKTOP_MEMBER_IN_CONTROL` (a member holds the machine's desktop) joins `CLOUD_MACHINE_SCREEN_REFUSALS`, `CLOUD_MACHINE_COMMAND_REFUSALS`, `CLOUD_MACHINE_CODING_VALUE_REFUSALS` and `CLOUD_MEMBER_MACHINE_SCREEN_REFUSALS`. The agent is told what it means and what to do (no pictures, no actions, no new commands and no keys off that computer until the member hands it back; tell them what it needs and end its turn, their I'm done on its takeover card wakes it) by machine_screen, machine_run's start and save_coding_value. ask_member's takeover guidance now says the card's Take over gives the member the computer's screen and I'm done wakes the agent; its description stays under the 4 KiB a tool's may have (4084 bytes).
