The tool catalog pins moved (cloud-native-company-runtime.test.ts, both).
- A Codex login's relay can let the voice call's control connection out (RUNTIME-INTERFACE-APPS.md §3a, "The call"; no SQL). Codex makes a call at `chatgpt.com` and then joins it over a websocket at `api.openai.com`, which the relay refused. `startNativeCodexEgress` now returns a `NativeEgressRelay`; on a Codex login's relay it has `allowRealtime()`, which lets `NATIVE_CODEX_REALTIME_EGRESS_HOSTS` (`api.openai.com`) through, under the same port, SNI and connection checks as the list's own hosts, until the closer it returns runs. Windows nest and each closer counts once; when the last one runs, new connections to the host are refused again and the open ones (even one still dialling) are cut, while tunnels to `chatgpt.com` and `auth.openai.com` stand. A ChatGPT plan's and a Claude sign-in's relays have no `allowRealtime`, so they cannot be widened. `launchIsolatedNativeProcess` passes it on, and the voice lane opens it just before `thread/realtime/start` and closes it when the call's stop is confirmed or given up on, when the provider closes the call, or when the run's process fails or closes. A run without isolation has no relay and nothing to open. Nothing opens a call yet, so the host is never let through in a real run today. The relay's 30 s idle limit and its eight connections are unchanged.
- A run's Codex app-server can place a voice call, for the tool that will give an explainer its voice (RUNTIME-INTERFACE-APPS.md §3a, "The call"; Jonas, 10 Nov 2026, on building the agents' explainer voice on ChatGPT's own voice through Codex: "Yes, build it"; no SQL). Nothing opens a voice call yet: no tool reaches it and the company runtime makes no channel, so runs behave as before. `runCloudCodexToolSession` (and `runCloudNativeRunToolSession`) take `voice`, a `CloudVoiceChannel` (new `cloud-voice-channel.ts`), and attach their lane to it once the agent's thread is bound, only for a Codex login's run (a run on a member's ChatGPT plan gets none), and detach it when the run ends. `open({offerSdp, voice?}, signal)` starts a side thread as the compactor's start (ephemeral, read-only, no tools), then `thread/realtime/start` (WebRTC, v3, `clientManagedHandoffs`, no startup context, `CLOUD_VOICE_READER_RULE` as its first item, a voice from `CLOUD_VOICE_NAMES` or none) and answers with the provider's SDP; `speak(text, signal)` appends one line and answers with the voice's transcript of it (at most 4 KiB), one line at a time; `close()` stops the session. A call is one at a time: one that ends keeps its place until Codex confirms its stop (a successful answer to the stop, or the session's close; a refused stop confirms nothing), the next `open` waits for that, and a realtime notification without a thread meanwhile is the stopping call's and is dropped; a stop not confirmed within 10 seconds ends the run's voice (`open` rejects with `CloudVoiceUnavailable`), and the run goes on. A call ends on `close`, on the abort of its signal, on a realtime error or close, or when its voice starts a turn: that turn is interrupted and the call rejects with "Native voice tried to act". Everything on the voice's thread stays on its lane: its requests are refused, its model requests (a prewarm, a turn it started) pass the result fence with every output answered as unavailable and are never the agent's context or counted as its requests, and a refusal of a voice request no longer marks the run `provider_failed`. A hung start of its thread still fails the run after the side-thread deadline, as a compactor's does. The result fence takes up to 28 connections at once (was 24), for the voice's thread. Without a lane attached, `open` rejects with `CloudVoiceUnavailable`; so does a pending line when the run ends.
- The machine's part of an explainer's voice (RUNTIME-INTERFACE-APPS.md §3a; Jonas, 10 Nov 2026, on building the agents' explainer voice on ChatGPT's own voice through Codex: "Yes, build it"; no migration, and nothing runs them yet). Three scripts for a company machine, held as text in `cloud-app-kit-explainer-voice.generated.ts` (sources `packages/runtime/kits/explainer/voice-*.cjs`; `npm run generate:app-kits`; a test fails while they differ): `voice-captions.cjs` prints the captions the running video speaks with their clip names, `voice-peer.cjs` is the other end of the voice call (it makes the WebRTC offer, takes the answer from a file and records one file a turn; it is handed no sign-in), and `voice-encode.cjs` writes the kept turns as `.mp4` clips into the app's `voice/` folder. `explainerSpokenMatches(caption, transcript)` says whether the voice said a caption as written: every word not written in digits, in order, and at most six other words for each number. The tool that places the call and drives them comes next.
- An agent can make an explainer: a short narrated video in place of a page (Jonas, 10 Nov 2026: "see if there are some interfaces that pressent better through a 3B1B style video, explaining concepts and ideas clearly but without needing to pin down an exact UI or design yet", and of such videos: "they need to be created with a voice over too though, they can't be mute"; RUNTIME-INTERFACE-APPS.md §3a; no migration). `save_app`'s manifest takes `kit: "explainer"`. The agent then writes one file, `scenes.js`: chapters, scenes, and in each scene the captions with what moves under them. `save_app` writes the other two into the app's folder with it: `engine.js`, the animation engine and its player (source `packages/runtime/kits/explainer/engine.js`, held as text in `cloud-app-kit-explainer-engine.generated.ts`; `npm run generate:app-kits` after a change, and a test fails while the two differ), and `index.html`, which makes the video, runs the scenes and starts it. An agent's call may not write those two (`APP_KIT_FILE`), a new explainer needs its `scenes.js` (`APP_SCENES_MISSING`), and a kit app has no page of its own (`APP_KIT_ENTRY`). On later saves they are written again only where they would differ (a new title, new voice clips, a newer engine), so a save of `scenes.js` changes one file. The captions are the narration: shown under the stage, and spoken when the app's folder holds voice clips (`voice/<hash>.mp4` or `.webm`, listed in `voice/clips.json`, the hash FNV-1a of the caption's text; `cloud-app-kit-explainer.ts`), which the page names so the web puts them in as it puts a video in. A spoken caption lasts as long as its clip; the player has Voice on/off, and the captions always stay. The tool that records the clips is the next step: until then an explainer plays with captions alone. The schema's `kit` field tells the agent when a video is the right form, the calls of a scene, how to write captions for the ear, how to pace and lay out the stage, and to look at more than the first frame before showing it (`index.html?t=SECONDS` opens the video paused there); a test holds every call, icon and colour it names to the engine. After a save, `look` names what a read of `scenes.js` found. `save_app`'s description stays within its 4096 bytes (4086). The tool catalog pins moved.
- A member's look at an agent's desktop computer reaches the machine's node (20260930712000, stage 1a of the live view). `HttpCloudMemberMachineScreenService` (`cloud-member-machine-screen-http.ts`), the coding executor's third listener, on loopback only: `GET /v1/member-machine-screens/<companyId>/<machineId>[/watching]` with the member's bearer token and `If-None-Match` answers 200 `image/png` with its etag, 304, 409 `{code}` (`CLOUD_MEMBER_MACHINE_SCREEN_REFUSALS`), 401 or 403; any other failure is 503 and logged by a fixed code, never with the token. The peer route `/v1/machine-commands/member-screen` (peer listener only, never a guest's ingress) carries the look's single-use id and the member's etag to the executor of the machine's node once (`createHttpMemberMachineScreenPeer`, `parseCloudMemberMachineScreenPeerRequest`, `parseCloudMemberMachineScreenAnswer`: a PNG whose sha-256 is its etag, or none when it is the member's), within the screen's 12 MiB bound.
- Every change in the company's code has a record an agent can read, undo and describe (own code storage, step 2b; 10 Nov 2026; migration `20260930720000`, SQL tests 1210 and 1211; the coding executor writes the records). `coding_finish` takes two optional fields: `notice`, one plain sentence on what a customer will notice (one line, at most 200 characters), and `picture`, the path in the repository of a screenshot the session committed (.png, .jpg, .jpeg or .webp, at most 300 bytes). The session's instructions ask for the sentence in words a founder who reads no code understands, and for a small screenshot under `docs/changes/` taken with the machine's headless Chromium when the change shows on a screen. Leaving either out never stops a hand-in; an empty one is one left out, and a wrong shape is refused with what to change, so the same turn hands in again. A new tool, `manage_code`, is offered to an agent's own company run (never a worker's), after `manage_worker`. `undo {operationKey, owner, name, change, andLater?, reason, notice?}` asks the coding executor to take a change out of main (`qorpera_folder_request_code_undo`), looks at the request every 2 s for at most 30 s (`qorpera_folder_code_request`) and answers in plain words: the change is undone and where its record is, later changes built on it and in which files (then `andLater` takes that change and every later one out), main no longer holds it, it is already undone, it never went in, or the undo is still being made (the same call again goes on waiting). `describe {operationKey, owner, name, change, notice?, picture?}` sets what a customer will notice and a picture from the company folder (`qorpera_folder_describe_code_change`). Neither action is reviewed: an undo puts main back to a state it had and can itself be undone. The company instructions say where a change's record is (`code/<owner>/<name>/changes/<number>.md`, with `changes/index.md` as the list), to take a wrong change out with `manage_code` before sending the session a follow-up that fixes it, and to add a missing notice or picture; the sentence that sent an undo to the session as a revert is gone. The three calls join the native host's list, the control's and the folder client's; the tool list, both catalog pins and the conversation and private instruction pin move, the worker's does not.
- Agents are told how to read a project's history, undo a change and hand over a whole project, for code kept in Qorpera (Jonas, 10 Nov 2026: step 2 after the audit; no SQL). A live test the same day showed a Co-Founder reading a repository's last three changes with git on a company machine in about a minute, without being told; the audit had listed history as missing. The standing instructions now say it: git in `/company/code/<owner>/<name>/main` (`git log`, `git show <commit>`, `git diff <a> <b>`, `git show <commit>:<path>`); to undo a landed change, a follow-up to its session that reverts the commit and runs the tests; to hand someone the whole project as one file, `git archive` or `git bundle` on a machine into a folder of the company.
- A member sends the company's email themself, with no agent: `qorpera_send_member_mail(p_environment_id, p_request)` (Jonas, 10 Nov 2026: "Let a member send an email with no agent"; contract section 19, step M2e; migration `20260930716000`, SQL test 731; SQL only, no runtime bundle cut). It amends the contract's "Mail is an event source (M2)" and the founder decisions of 1 October 2026 (RUNTIME-EVENT-INTAKE.md), under which company email left only through an agent. A signed-in member who may write in the company (owner, admin or contributor) queues a new plain-text email, `{sendId, from, to, cc?, subject, text}`, in the outbox the agents' email waits in, and the same sender (`mail-outbox`, unchanged claim) sends it. `send_mail`'s rules hold: the same address, count (10 with cc), subject (200) and text (20000) checks, a live mailbox of that company that is not paused, the sending switch, never to the company's own mailboxes, never as if from Qorpera, and the company's hourly and daily limits, counted together with the agents' email under the same lock. What differs: it goes out from "<member> at <company>", the text is the member's as written with no AI line, it never carries Auto-Submitted, and it passes no automatic review, since it is the member's own act. `sendId` is the client's id for the send: the same one again answers the email already queued (`{mailId, thread, status}`). The outbox says who sent each email, an agent's run and call or a member and their send id, never both (`qorpera_company_mail_outbox_sender`). No mail is private to a member: the email begins a thread every member reads, and an outside answer joins it and wakes the mailbox's routines as any received email does. Member reads say who sent it: a sent message of `qorpera_member_mail_thread` carries `member` (`{userId, name?}`) and a thread of `qorpera_member_mail_threads` carries `sentBy` when its newest message is a member's own. Agents read received email only, as before; `qorpera_send_agent_mail` is not touched. A paused company does not hold a member's send back. Not yet: a member's reply to a received email, and attachments.
- An agent sees and drives a desktop computer in one tool call, `machine_screen` (Jonas, 10 Nov 2026: agents request a desktop computer and must see and drive it; the 10 Nov audit measured 20-30 s and three tool calls, `machine_run` with `desktop ... --shot`, then `company_read`, per look-act-look step; migration `20260930708000`, SQL test 1198). `machine_screen {machine, action?, settleMs?, look?, intent?, operationKey?}` takes at most one action (click, double, move, drag, type, key, scroll, open; positions in screen pixels), waits (default 500 ms, open 2500, a look 0), and returns the screenshot to the model as a picture in the same tool result, with the active and open windows' titles and the screen's and the picture's size; nothing goes into the company folder, and it works without `/company`. It is offered with the machine tools to every run whose gateway has `screen` (company agents and workers). A look carries no receipt and is not reviewed; an action is one receipted call (`machine_screen:<key>`, sent once, never again after no answer: the machine's own per-boot marker refuses a second run) whose replay shows the stored answer with `replayed` and no picture. The action's shape (`parseMachineScreenAction`, `cloud-machine-screen.ts`) is the same check the tool, the executor, the machine's helper and SQL make. The picture is the machine's PNG converted here with `folderImageView` (fitted to 1920x1200 and 1 MiB; `scaled` says when the picture is smaller than the screen). Images from runtime tools: the tool boundary shows a runtime tool's image only after an answer whose JSON names its hash, checks the PNG's signature and size against the pinned fence (1..1920 x 1..1200) so a bad one fails its own call, and the context fence (`#contextView`) accepts a `machine_screen` image only from this run's own ok answer that names it as `kind: "screen"`. Review: a look is a read, move and scroll are internal, the other verbs external under the machine work rule (`machineWorkCheck`, with its own reason); the card names the verb, the machine and position, the intent, the window the agent last saw there (`prepare`), the text, keys and page, never pixels. The executor's route is `/v1/machine-commands/screen` (`CloudMachineScreenHttpRequest`, the gateway's `screen`, 30 s, answers up to 12 MiB, refusals as 409 with their code); the machine runs `cloud-machine-screen-main.ts`, packaged as `/opt/qorpera-machine/cloud-machine-screen.mjs`. `machine_start`'s description and the company context point at `machine_screen` for seeing and driving the screen and keep `desktop start` for putting files on it. Proven on a real 2 cpu / 4 GiB machine from a candidate image (lanes/desktop-computers-20261110/probe/screen-s1.log): a look on a running desktop takes 0.1 to 0.25 s, a click with its 0.5 s settle 0.75 s, a look that starts the desktop 1.9 s; pictures are 30 to 60 KB for plain pages, 0.4 to 0.7 MB for heavy sites and 4.3 MB at worst (noise). That probe's first look was black, because the session draws about a second after it starts: `desktop start` now waits until the screen is drawn. Review round 1 (PR #1635): a key is never an xdotool command word, in any case (`MACHINE_SCREEN_XDOTOOL_COMMANDS`, the same list as the database's call check: `xdotool key` runs a command that follows its keys, and `key exec id` ran `id` as root); the executor's authority answers `MACHINE_NEEDS_COMPANY_VIEW` for a disk that has had a company folder view its caller may not see, and the host `DESKTOP_BUSY` for a second action on a desktop, both carried to the agent with what to do. Review round 2: the helper runs each action command as a transient service (`machineScreenActionCommand`: `systemd-run --quiet --wait --pipe --collect --expand-environment=no --slice=qorpera-machine.slice -p RuntimeMaxSec=<seconds left> -p KillMode=control-group -p KillSignal=SIGKILL --setenv=... -- /usr/local/bin/desktop ...`; one deadline for the action's commands together; `MACHINE_SCREEN_KILL_MS` later its own kill as a backstop), so a killed helper's command and what it started die at the deadline; a command that failed at or after its limit (systemd-run answers 1 either way) answers exitCode null with no error line. Not coreutils' timeout: the image's is uutils', whose `--signal=KILL` ends only its direct child.
- Agents show changes as patch notes and wait for a member's yes before they go live (Jonas, 10 Nov 2026: "an update list/patch notes for changes before they go live … a patchnote rundown with visuals and very brief and easy to digest text"; asked whether changes should wait for his yes: "Yes, wait for me"; instructions only, no SQL). The company instructions say what going live is (an update to a published site, a new version of the product, a new price, a mail or post to many), that one posted app, `whats-new`, shows every change that is ready and not yet live as a picture and one plain line, several changes as one rundown, and that the go-ahead is one question card pointed at the app with "Put it live" and "Not yet", shown as buttons on the page too. Nothing in the rundown goes live before a member says yes; finishing, merging and testing stay the agents' own; a fix for something broken for customers right now goes live at once and is shown the same day. It is a rule of the instructions: `publish_site` is reviewed as before. The instruction pins move. Needs a runtime bundle cut to reach agents.
- An agent's desktop shows its own figure for the six newer figures too (Jonas, 10 Nov 2026; no SQL). Star, cloud, bolt, mushroom, moon and puzzle each have a backdrop, named for the figure, with the 24 hourly frames (`deploy/lxd-company-computer-guest/backdrops`, rendered from `backdrops-source`); a saved ghost gets the moon's. A guest image has them once it is built from this tree. An image built before lacks the files: there `prepareCompanyComputerScreenWallpaper` keeps to the first seven backdrops (the figure's own if it is one of those, the ghost's for the moon, else a stable one by the agent's id) instead of failing, so the desktop still starts; a file that is there but is no plain file is still refused.
- Six more agent figures (Jonas, 10 Nov 2026; migration `20260930709000`, SQL test 1199). `hire_agent`'s `figure` takes cloud-client's list: star, cloud, bolt, mushroom, moon and puzzle beside the six that stay, and no ghost. A saved `figure: ghost` is still read. On an agent's desktop the new figures have no backdrop in any image yet: the ghost's backdrop stands in for the moon that replaced it, and the other five get a stable one of the installed backdrops, by the agent's id, until an image draws them.
- A desktop is something an agent asks for, and it can start with chosen files on it (Jonas, 10 Nov 2026: agents live in headless machines and request a desktop computer that gets the compute a desktop needs, started with selected company files or programs; no SQL). `machine_start` takes `desktop: true`: the machine gets at least 2 cpus and 4 GiB (`CLOUD_MACHINE_DESKTOP_LEAST`; a smaller size asked beside it is raised), and only the size reaches the start and its receipt. On the machine, `desktop start` refuses a smaller machine and names `desktop true`; `desktop start <files, folders, URLs>` and `desktop open` put a company file or folder on the desktop as a link and open it (a folder with an index.html is served on the loopback and opened as a site); any action takes `--shot FILE` to save the screen after it; `desktop size`, `--lang`, `--tz` and `desktop selftest` are new; a desktop without a command for an hour stops itself. Fixed from the 10 Nov audit (lanes/computer-audit-20261110): pages had no 3D drawing (WebGL) at all, now on (software, 37 frames a second measured); `desktop type` wrote Danish capitals small or wrong, now pasted; the "only for automated testing" bar and the translate offer are gone; a screenshot into an unmounted `/company` is refused instead of lost. The image also carries Playwright (pinned) with its browsers, ffmpeg, CJK fonts and a `chromium` that runs from root's shell, since agents look at pages headless (44 Playwright commands against 0 desktop ones since 9 Nov) and installed Playwright on every fresh machine. The agent text says so: `machine_start` explains the desktop computer and the installed Playwright, and the company instructions send page checks to a machine without a screen and real-screen testing to a desktop computer. Needs a new machine image on node 2 and a runtime cut.

- `save_app` says which links in an app open and that every app starts from Qorpera's base style (the web now opens an app's links and gives it the style; no SQL). Its description no longer says "a link does not open inside an app" or that the brand is copied in: it names the links that open (`app:<name>`, `folder:<path>`, `https://…`, `#id`) and points to the schema's `files`, which now describes the base style, its colour variables, `QorperaOS/brand/brand.toml` and how a page follows light and dark. The answer's `look` no longer flags every link: it names the links that open nothing (a relative address, a platform folder, `mailto:`, `javascript:`), the first three of them. The description stays under the 4096 bytes a tool may hold. Both tool catalog pins move.
- `save_app` takes `writes` on the manifest: what the page may ask a member to change (Jonas, 10 Nov 2026; RUNTIME-INTERFACE-APPS.md §5 "Changes"; no SQL). It is a list of `answers` (an answer to an agent's open question that points at the app) and `table:<name>` (a row of that company table, never `areas`), checked as `reads` is and kept when a later call leaves it out; `reads` gains `questions`. How a page uses it is said on the field in the tool's schema, since the description holds 4096 bytes (it is at 4048): the page only asks, Qorpera shows the member a bar with what will be saved, and nothing is saved without their yes there; ask only from a click on a button that says what it does; `qorpera.questions()` and `qorpera.answer(id, value)`; `qorpera.setRow(table, key, cells)` and `qorpera.addRow(table, cells)`, one row a request, never a key, a member's or an agent's cell, or a removed row; and a `file_change` routine on the table's `rows.csv` to hear of a member's change. The tool catalog pin moves. Needs a runtime bundle cut to reach agents.
- Code kept in Qorpera, step 1 "Dependable" (Jonas, 10 Nov 2026, after the audit; migration `20260930707000`). A coding session's instructions say that in a repository kept in Qorpera the landing is at once and nothing checks it: it finishes only with work that is whole, writes tests with a change that can be tested, runs the tests and the build and sees them pass, never weakens a failing test, keeps a README with the install, test, build and start commands, and when blocked asks instead of handing in half-done work (on GitHub a blocked turn still hands in its draft). It is told that the secret values it was given are environment variables of every command and are its to use; before, "do not look for credentials" and the command tool's "No repository, provider or cloud credentials are available. Publication uses the trusted draft-PR path" read as if it had none. The company agents are told that a landed change is in main before they read its result and that rejecting takes nothing out: they check it (the files at `code/<owner>/<name>/main/`, its tests, what it built) and fix or undo by a follow-up, and they look under `code/` before starting a new repository. In SQL, the mark of a repository kept in Qorpera (`refs/qorpera/**`) is the platform's alone, a session's lease there is never for `refs/heads/main`, `packed-refs` or `shallow` (the kernel knows such a repository by its coding sessions on it, which lasts when git's cleanup packs the mark's file away), and a turn whose executor stopped while handing in says "may or may not have reached main", not "the draft pull request may exist".
- Interface apps show the company's state, and agents are told how to make one worth opening (Jonas, 10 Nov 2026: "agents lets you interact with your employees — interfaces let's you interact with your company directly", and the agents' apps "weren't all that great visually"; hire overlay v13, no SQL). A review of the seven apps the agents made in one company found six on one report template of text, drop-downs and links that do nothing, none reading live data, and several out of date. The company instructions now say that members talk with agents in conversations and look at the company in apps; that an app is not a report or a short version of a document; that each agent owning an area keeps one posted app showing how the area stands and changes it in the run that changes the state; and that what is new is marked on the thing itself, never as a dated headline. The Co-Founder checks those apps in its planning runs and keeps one for the company as a whole (`company`), drawn from the team's todos and agents. Before an agent links or posts an app it looks at it in a browser on a machine at 1280 and 390 px, as it already does for a concept. `save_app`'s description gains the design rules: the first screen answers how this stands; show a number, chart, map, diagram or picture instead of writing; about 300 words; nothing folded away; no links inside the page; the company's brand once it has one; phone width. Its schema says on `reads` how a page reads live data (`qorpera.read`, `list`, `table`, `todos`, `agents`, `onChange`, with one data file per app), since a tool's own description holds 4096 bytes, and sets a pace that stays inside the bridge's 120 calls a minute (`todos` counts as ten): a draw at most every ten seconds however many changes arrive, each source called once per draw, and after a failed read the last good data kept and the read tried again ten seconds later. Its answer carries `look` when the call wrote the entry page: what a quick read found (links, three or more `<details>`, over 500 words, over 150 words with nothing to look at), as advice. `post_app` says to post what members keep coming back to. The instruction and prompt pins and the tool catalog pin move.
- Agent instructions (no SQL): every agent shows its work in interface apps from day one, with one concepts app and quick choices; the Co-Founder opens the company's main mailbox in its first run (#1611). The money owner registers the company (CVR) and opens its account at a bank the Bank page connects, the founders choosing form, owners and capital, and keeps finance/bank-setup.json for the Bank page (Jonas, 10 November 2026).
- The Co-Founder hires a Branding & Identity specialist with the first team (Jonas, 9 Nov 2026: "the co-founder should hire a branding and identity specialist on company creation"; hire overlay v12, no SQL). Its first assignment is a first identity the company could use at once: the official logo as a square, self-contained SVG at `QorperaOS/brand/logo.svg` that reads at 32 px, with colours, fonts and a short brand guide in `QorperaOS/brand/`, kept up to date as the vision gets clearer. It leaves the company's name as it is: renaming is deferred (Jonas, 9 Nov 2026), since a new name also has to reach the business registry, mail, the bank and the rest. The company instructions say the app shows `QorperaOS/brand/logo.svg` wherever the company appears, that only the agent who owns the brand changes it, and that logo ideas go elsewhere until one is chosen. The webshop areas list names brand and identity. The instructions pins move.
- Agents are told what their notebook is and when to use it (Jonas, 9 Nov 2026: "we shouldn't force it, just explain what it is, and when to use it"; no SQL). Since 28 Oct agents called `update_memory` once in about 9,700 model requests, so almost nothing carried from one run to the next. A staff agent's instructions gain "# Your notebook", beside "# Skills": the notebook carries its own experience between runs, and suits how a member likes to work with it, a correction, a decision and its reason, a lesson, or where it found something hard to find; what the whole company should know stays in the company folder; and a run with nothing to note is fine. `update_memory`'s description says the same about company facts instead of sending every "fact about the company" away from the notebook. Workers get no section (they report to their agent). Needs a runtime bundle cut to reach agents.
- The Co-Founder deals the team out between the members (Jonas, 9 Nov 2026: "let's give the co-founder the tools to deal agents to the co-founders/members too"). Two new tools, the Co-Founder's only: `crew_and_members` shows each member with whether their ChatGPT can carry work and the agents that work for them, who works for nobody, and the open asks; `offer_agents_to_member` asks one member (by name, first name or email) to take up to 20 agents, with a one-line reason they see. Nothing moves until the member says yes on the Members page. Hire overlay v11: each agent works for one member whose ChatGPT carries the work handed to it; with two or more founders the Co-Founder deals the team out by area, and its daily plan offers agents on when one founder carries most of them or someone's ChatGPT cannot work. The sentence that the team runs on the login of the founder who started it is gone. Needs migration 20260930702000 (host calls `qorpera_cofounder_crew`, `qorpera_offer_agents_to_member`).
- An agent takes a coding value into the vault itself, and never sees it (Jonas, 9 Nov 2026; steps 2 and 3 on migration `20260930695000`, SQL test 1181; no SQL here). `computer_browser`'s `vault_capture` takes `{role, name, codingValue: {service, variable, repository, purpose}}` instead of a hook and a field (service `npm`, `github`, `stripe` or `resend`; the variable a name a coding value may have, in lower case; the repository owner/name in lower case; the purpose 1 to 300 characters), checked as SQL checks it; its schema says when to use it (a key a coding session needs from one of those services: make it on the site, name the button that creates or reveals it, never type, copy or screenshot a key) and to raise `ask_member`'s secret card (connectorId `environment`) for any other service, and that card's schema points back here. The review card reads "Save a key for coding sessions from a web page into the vault" with the service, the variable in upper case, the repository and the purpose. The native host asks the company's secret broker on a new route (`POST /v1/coding-value-captures`, `qorpera.coding-value-prepare-request/v1` before the office acts and `qorpera.coding-value-capture-request/v1` with the office's box after, the run's lease proved as on `/v1/event-hook-captures`; `createHttpCompanyComputerSessionFactory` takes `codingValues`, and without it such a call is refused before the office). The broker's `CodingValueVault` (`cloud-coding-value-vault.ts`) prepares the value's capture key: an X25519 pair it derives from the company's key like a Stripe hook's, under its own HKDF info `qorpera.coding-value-capture-key/v1` (`CloudSecretKeyring.codingValueCapturePublicKey`, `openCodingValueCapture`), for the id the value keeps (a new one when it keeps none, or the one the database names if that changed meanwhile), whose public half alone goes to `qorpera_folder_prepare_coding_value_capture`. What the key is derived from (the company, the environment, the authority's current key id, the id the value keeps) and whether the key the database keeps is current the broker asks first, through `qorpera_folder_describe_coding_value_capture` (migration `20260930698000`, broker-only, bound to the call's receipt as the other three, writing nothing; SQL tests 1181 and 306): a current key that is the vault's own is kept without a prepare, and a key id the keyring does not hold leaves the call unconfirmed. After the office's read it opens the box (`cloud-coding-value-capture-box.ts`: E3c's box, now shared, under the schemas `qorpera.coding-value-capture-box/v1` and `/capture/v1`, bound to the computer, its generation, the receipt, the service and the variable), checks the site and the whole value against the service's pattern from the database's read, seals `{value}` as the next version and stores it with `qorpera_folder_store_coding_value_capture`, which also grants it to the repository; it reads again after a 40003, as E3c does. A value equal to the version the vault holds is stored all the same, as the next version: the store is the only call that records the call's capture and issues the repository's grant, and the database never sees a value to tell them apart; the answer says `unchanged`. A service's pattern is used only when it means the same in JavaScript as in PostgreSQL (letters, digits, `_`, `-`, bracket ranges, groups, alternation and counted quantifiers; `codingValuePattern`), the whole value matching all of it. The office reads where the box goes itself (`qorpera_native_office_coding_value_capture`, `CloudCompanyComputerEffectControl.codingValueCaptureTarget`) and reads the page as it reads a Stripe hook's (`captureCompanyPageValue`, which `captureCompanyHookSecret` now calls with the hook's address and `whsec_` shape): the service's site, nothing shown before the click (checked again after a reload), the control in the main frame, the cover on every new document, one value read twice 300 ms apart and whole, every value seen hidden, the clipboard emptied, the page reloaded and masked again or the tab closed, a human check's stop. Only text the service's pattern matches counts as a value (`companyCodingValuesShown`), so a listing of keys cut off (`sk_live_…4xYz`) neither stops the read nor counts twice; a key is whole when no place cuts it off and some place shows it ending; a key a service shows once and no longer after the reload counts as masked again. A read the page fails while the value is awaited (a click that sends a form loads a new document) counts as none shown yet, for a hook too. The screen-wide shape guard also hides the four services' keys (`npm_`, `github_pat_`, `ghp_`, `sk_`/`rk_` with `test_`/`live_`, and Resend's `re_` shape), so a key an agent reveals itself is never shown to it. The tool boundary's cut of webhook signing secrets also cuts the four services' keys out of every tool answer and everything the host sends Codex (`redactCloudWebhookSecrets`: `npm_`, `ghp_`, `github_pat_`, `sk_`/`rk_` with `test_`/`live_`, each `_` also URL-encoded or JSON-escaped, and Resend's `re_` in its accepted shape wherever it starts; each cut keeps its prefix, never grows and keeps JSON valid), and so does SQL's cut of a context source's whole text (`qorpera_redact_webhook_secrets`, migration `20260930698100`, SQL test 1157). Resend's accepted shape is now its keys' own, `^re_[A-Za-z0-9]{6,16}_[A-Za-z0-9]{16,64}$` (migration `20260930698200`, SQL test 1181; review round 1 of #1581): step 1's `re_` and 20 to 64 letters, digits or `_` took a key of lower-case letters alone, which no guard could tell from a snake_case name, so every guard (the screen's, the tool boundary's, SQL's) now matches that shape in either case and wherever it starts (after `%20`, `+` or a JSON escape too), with no capital-letter or word-start rule, and `re_render_component_tree` is still never cut. The vault's capture helpers shared with E3c moved to `cloud-vault-capture.ts`, and the names a coding value may have to `cloud-coding-value-names.ts`.
- Every company machine has a desktop for work that needs a screen (Jonas, 9 Nov 2026: agents must be able to open a computer when they need a full machine, such as testing a product or driving a UI that needs a full screen; no SQL). The machine image (deploy/machine/build-machine-image.sh) adds TigerVNC's X server and a plain XFCE session on display :1 as the account `desktop`, started only by `desktop start`, with the coding image's pinned Chromium (sandboxed through its own AppArmor profile, `qorpera-chromium`) and the `desktop` command: start, stop, status, shot, open, run, click, double, move, drag, type, key, scroll and windows. An agent drives it with machine_run, saves `desktop shot` under /company and sees it with company_read. `machine_start`'s description says so, and the company instructions' Starting work list gets one line: use a machine's desktop only for work that files, commands or a headless browser cannot do. Proven on node 2's test machine with the real image build (scripts/prove-machine-desktop-vm.sh): a booted machine started the desktop, opened a page maximized in Chromium, took typed text and a click, saved a 1600x1000 screenshot and stopped with nothing left over; an idle desktop with Chromium open used 2.1% of one core (the idle rule's limit is 5%).
- An agent saves a coding value from its company machine (Jonas, 9 Nov 2026: "Save from the machine"; migration `20260930705000`, SQL test 1181 §7). Production has no offices, so `computer_browser vault_capture` reaches no one; agents work on their own machines, whose desktop has Chromium. A new machine tool, `save_coding_value {operationKey, machine, service, variable, repository, purpose}`, is offered with the machine tools to a company agent's own run (never a worker's) when the runtime has the vault (`CloudNativeCompanyRuntime` and `cloudMachineTools` take `codingValues`, the secret broker's client) and the executor gateway runs the clipboard helper. It never takes the value: its arguments are checked as `vault_capture`'s `codingValue` (the four seeded services, `codingValueNameAllowed`, the repository owner/name in lower case, a purpose of one line, 1 to 300 characters), and its description says to make the key on the service's site in the machine's browser, press the site's own Copy, then call it; never to type, paste, print, echo or write a key, nor take a desktop shot while one is shown (shots are kept in the company folder); and to raise `ask_member`'s secret card (connectorId `environment`) for other services or keys only a person has. That card's schema now points to `save_coding_value` first. One receipted call: the vault's prepare, bound to this `save_coding_value` receipt (the same `/v1/coding-value-captures` route and `CodingValueVault`), makes or keeps the value's capture key; the executor gateway's new `/v1/machine-commands/coding-value` route (`qorpera.machine-command-http/v1`, action `coding_value`, `{commandId, envelope}`; `CloudMachineCommandGateway.codingValue`, `HttpCloudCodingCommandService` serves it beside the machine routes) has the machine's executor run the image's clipboard helper for that receipt and answers its box (`qorpera.machine-coding-value/v1`) or a refusal as 409 with its code (`CLOUD_MACHINE_CODING_VALUE_REFUSALS`); the vault then opens the box, checks the key against the service's pattern again, stores it and grants it. The answer is `{saved: {service, variable, repository, version, unchanged}}` or `{saved: false, refused: {reason, message}}`, the machine's refusals in words that say what to do. The helper (`cloud-machine-coding-value.ts`, whose entry `cloud-machine-coding-value-main.ts` the guest package bundles on its own as `cloud-machine-coding-value.mjs`; the module itself runs nothing when the runtime loads it) takes SQL's target on stdin, refuses another boot, reads the desktop's clipboard on display `:1` into memory with `xsel` (no file, no log), takes the trimmed clipboard if it is a whole key of the service or else its one word that is (refusing none, more than one, an empty clipboard, a stopped desktop), seals it to the capture key and clears both the clipboard and the primary selection on every path (side by side; each xsel killed after `MACHINE_XSEL_MS`, 4 s, so a read and the clear fit well inside the executor's 20 s; with `--clear-only` it only clears, which the executor runs itself after a save that did not end cleanly); it prints one JSON line, the box or a code, and never the value. The box is the coding value box's sibling kind (`qorpera.coding-value-machine-box/v1`, its own HKDF info, sealed to the same capture key, `sealCodingValueMachineCapture`/`openCodingValueMachineCapture`, `CloudSecretKeyring.openCodingValueMachineCapture`), bound to the company, the machine, its placement, its boot, the receipt, the service and the variable, and naming no site (the capture box takes a `siteless` kind); the broker's read names the machine, its placement and its boot instead of a computer and its generation, and the vault opens whichever box the read binds. The review card reads "Save a key for coding sessions from a company machine into the vault" with the service, the variable in upper case, the repository and the purpose. `save_coding_value` joins the runtime's observed operations. The tool boundary's cut of the four services' keys stays as the backstop.
- An agent asks for a coding value in its thread (Jonas, 9 Nov 2026: coding values happen behind the scenes, not in Settings; migration `20260930693000`, SQL test 590). `ask_member`'s secret card takes `connectorId: "environment"` with the variable's name in lower case and `repository` (owner/name, lower case), and its schema says when to use it: a coding session needs an environment variable only a person can give, such as a token for private packages; ask once, before it is needed, never in chat. The card is refused for a repository beside another connector, a repository not in owner/name lower case, or a name the capture would refuse.
- Agents keep all new code in Qorpera, also when the company has a GitHub connection (Jonas, 8 Nov 2026: agents and their tools use Qorpera itself for codebases, not GitHub; no SQL). The company instructions say to start a repository in Qorpera without asking, to use GitHub only for code that already lives there or when a member asks for GitHub by name, and never to create a GitHub repository, move code there, or ask a member to connect GitHub, create a repository or give access for code an agent writes. `manage_worker`'s `host` description says the same.
- A company keeps its code in Qorpera, with no GitHub (own code storage, slice 1; Jonas, 7 Nov 2026: own code storage first, GitHub stays a connection for people who already have repositories; migration `20260930670000`, SQL test 1170). `manage_worker`'s coding repository takes `host: "qorpera"` beside `github.com`: owner and name in lower case, the folder `code/<owner>/<name>/`. The tool's description, its repository fields' descriptions and the agents' instructions say to start a new repository in Qorpera without asking anyone unless the code already lives on GitHub, that a finished change in Qorpera lands on the default branch at once (on GitHub it is still a draft pull request), and that members never hear about pull requests, branches, commits, diffs or checks, only what changed for them (8 Nov: a Co-Founder told its founder "PR #1"). A coding session's instructions say a repository kept in Qorpera may start empty, and how to merge the default branch when a landing conflicts. The wake bus takes a coding scope on either host.
- A machine command runs by the fixed work rule unless it deletes or overwrites data off the company's machines and folder (Jonas, 8 Nov 2026: "almost all of the subordinate agents have ended up having one or multiple requests to run commands on a company machine"; no SQL). The reviewer's guard `deletesOrOverwrites` became `deletesOutside`: it asks only about data on an online service or account, a remote server, a code repository's history or a member's own device. On production from 7 to 8 Nov, 15 of 589 machine commands were held on a card and 8 refused, nearly all as overwriting a file in /tmp, /root or /company, after three rewordings of the old guard on 7 Nov (`aab4fceba`, `f9c6c3c3e`). Files on a company machine are the agent's own work, and the company folder keeps the earlier version of every file changed, replaced or deleted there. A verdict that still names the old guard has no guards, so the reviewer's decision stands.
- The solution comes before the concepts (Jonas, 8 Nov 2026: the platform must "derive all of these things itself through it's own, reproducable process. it shouldn't require the founder to understand software"): a founder's example (a post, a picture, another app) is the need, never the way to build it; the team writes `strategy/solution.md` first (what paying customers must be able to do and get, what that demands, how the strongest companies build it and why quick ways fail, the foundation and where to start), and the brief's "how it works" comes from it. The example is general (a travel app), so a test company is not handed its answer.
- Concepts are made in four steps (Jonas, 8 Nov 2026, on Rumlig: "I don't think the agents are working nearly enough on shaping the idea before it goes to work on what feels like a random, unserious attempt"): a plain-words concept brief the founders correct first, a moodboard of real reference pictures that agents look at and judge, several still directions the founders choose from, then the chosen one built in rounds of screenshot critique until it looks and feels finished. A concept is its own app; a concept's directions are still pictures, not the explore-with-videos loop. Agents write to coworkers in plain sentences, never logs or check counts, because the founders read these threads.
- `save_app`'s manifest takes `concept` (true or false) and `cover` (a .png, .jpg or .webp of the app), kept on a change that leaves them out, and its description says so in one sentence (the web's Concepts viewer, 8 Nov 2026). The tool catalog pin moves.
- A run is shown its continuation to 32 KiB (perf sprint T7, 8 Nov 2026; no SQL, the bundle alone). When the context cuts a continuation short (4 KiB under `20260930657100`, 16 KiB under `20260930617000`), the host reads the pages after the excerpt as `read_context` reads them (`qorpera_read_company_run_context`, the source's id and hash, 32 KiB in all), beside the run's other start reads, and shows them as one excerpt from offset 0 with the last page's `nextOffset`. The composer cuts webhook signing secrets out of it, as the tool boundary cuts them out of a page. The protocol has the run read the whole continuation first, and runs do: from 7 Nov 11:30Z to 23:21Z, 47 of 298 runs paged one past 16 KiB, all as their first act, 105 model rounds at about 4.2 s. About 90 of them go. Same text as the pages, same place, and `read_context` reads on from the shown `nextOffset` as from any page. A page that cannot be read, or a whole text that does not join to the source by its length and hash, leaves the context's excerpt; it never fails the run.
- Nothing the host sends Codex carries a webhook signing secret (perf sprint T7, 8 Nov 2026, with the review of #1528; migration `20260930657200`, SQL test 1157 `context_source_secret_cut`). Three points cut, with one rule: the session's `request` (every request of the agent's, the reviewer's and the compactor's threads: their instructions and every turn's input, each text in it), the tool boundary (every tool answer, as before), and the result fence's composition reply (the run's work message); the host-written `AGENTS.md` is the thread's instructions, cut. The run's opening cuts the head (vision, job description, notebook facts) and the conversation views, and the composer the escaped context message, before they are hashed, released, recorded and compiled, so the session's cut finds nothing more and the bound context is what Codex got; a head part keeps the version it read. The rule now also takes a secret URL-encoded (`%2B`, the prefix's `%5F`) and JSON-escaped once or twice (`\/`, `+`), unit by unit, so a cut JSON text stays valid. And a context source's whole text is cut in SQL (`qorpera_redact_webhook_secrets` in `qorpera_company_context_source`) before any excerpt or `read_context` page, so no page boundary can split a secret into two parts that pass; every length and hash is of the cut text, so `read_context`'s handle holds.
- A run's context message reaches the model without webhook signing secrets (perf sprint T7, 8 Nov 2026; no SQL). The tool boundary cuts `whsec_` secrets out of every text tool result (E3c-2), but the context message skipped the cut: every source it shows (current inputs, the request, history, the job record and description, the continuation) and the wake's payload went to the provider in clear. Since #1468 (`20260930617000`) that held a continuation's bytes 4 KiB to 16 KiB too, which used to come as `read_context` pages, cut (`20260930657100` puts that excerpt back at 4 KiB meanwhile). The composer now cuts the whole serialized message with the boundary's own rule (`redactCloudWebhookSecrets`); each source's handle (sha256, offsets, lengths) stays the whole text's, so `read_context` reads on as before. The developer instructions (the head, conversation views) are not this message and are not cut here.
- A run's context shows its continuation's first 4 KiB again, not 16 KiB (perf sprint T7, 8 Nov 2026; migration `20260930657100`, SQL test 1157, which replaces 1104; no bundle needed). The tool boundary cuts webhook signing secrets (`whsec_`) out of every text tool result, and the context skips that cut: since `20260930617000` bytes 4 KiB to 16 KiB of a continuation, such as a command's output tail, reached the provider in clear, where they used to come as `read_context` pages, cut. The rest is read through `read_context` again, at the extra rounds 617000 had saved, until the composer cuts the context itself (#1528).
- Agents, `save_app`'s description and the save_app/post_app review cards say an app opens as a page of its own, no longer beside the chat (#1524; Jonas, 8 Nov 2026: a concept is a full product prototype and needs a page of its own); the instructions and tool catalog pins move.
- A tool result names its run, not every id of the run's identity (perf sprint A3, 7 Nov 2026; approved by Jonas with the contract's 2026-11-07 amendment): `provenance.identity`, 1.2 KiB of ids and hashes in every result that the model read again in every later round, leaves the envelope, and a tool now leaves the envelope 800 bytes instead of 2.2 KiB, so a `company_read` page shows up to about 7 KiB of plain text in one result, instead of about 5.7 KiB. `companyId`, `runId`, `callId`, `tool`, `source`, `argumentSha256` and `companyReadId` stay. Every identity field stays durable: the run row holds company, environment, staff agent and version, requester, sponsor binding and version, compute identity, provider, model, effort and policy, audience and bundle; runner, assignment, lease and provider connection are on the lease row and, once released, in `qorpera_native_provider_connection_lease_history`; receipts keep run, job and assignment. A step's attempt is found by its time against that history.
- The company builds the founders' product at their ambition (Jonas, 7 Nov 2026, after Rumlig's demos: "it haven't built demos for any sort of platform or app that I had described, it had built some costum/tailor made demos for my situation - except it's not a realistic visual... it's a useless product"). The Co-Founder treats the product as the brief, never swaps it for something smaller, treats a founder who uses it first as its first test customer, hires the people who build it once the product is clear, and writes the product into the vision at the founders' ambition. A concept, like any prototype, shows the product itself, on its platform, at the quality it promises: a realistic 3D render in Blender for a product that promises one, not a diagram. The first things shown to the founders are concepts: the vision realized, the finished product at full ambition, not a sketch of early work. They are called concepts, never demos (Jonas, 8 Nov 2026: vision alignment through concepts, aligning on what a finished version could look like) (Jonas: "the goal of the inital demos is to prototype what the vision would look like realized - not just scetch early work out. it's just as much a test of vision alignment and stress testing the ideas potential").
- An agent posts its own interface app without the automatic review (Jonas, 7 Nov 2026: "yes, no review of course"); posting another agent's or a member's app is still reviewed as delegation.
- The instructions name how to record a demo video on a machine, as a worker did in Rumlig on 7 Nov 2026: ffmpeg from apt and Chromium through Playwright, because no machine has them and Chromium from apt is a slow snap that failed.
- A worker's saved report ends its turn (perf sprint A4, 7 Nov 2026): `report_work` answers "stop now" when the folder saved the report, and the model's next round produced nothing anyone reads (24 of 26 worker runs in 27 h: 4 output tokens, about 2.7 s and about 88k cached input tokens). The result fence now ends the turn there, as it does after a final send. A report that was not saved still gives the model its next round, to report again, and so does a saved one whose answer tells of desktop paths the folder did not take (`companyFolder` with `notSaved`, `refused` or `more`), or whose model response made another call (one Codex answers itself included), whose answer would go unread.
- Asking which login pays for a conversation works out no room and writes nothing when nothing changes (perf sprint A6, 7 Nov 2026; migration `20260930606000`, SQL test 1106). On production `qorpera_select_member_session_account`, asked when a thread opens and every 30 s while one is open, was the slowest call of opening the app: 48 ms mean over 3 to 7 Nov, 102 ms in the quiet window of 7 Nov, 14 % of all database time there, with 2.1 WAL records per call. 96 ms of that was `qorpera_native_account_remaining` for each login, an answer it never read; it also held the conversation's row for the whole call and rewrote its saved row with the same values. `qorpera_member_live_account_bindings` now holds the rule of who is live, and `qorpera_member_live_accounts` is that plus the room. The selection decides without a lock, answers when the saved row is already right, and takes the row and decides again only when it would write. Same answers on every branch (test 1106 runs the old and the new text side by side), same authority first. The poll's reads take about 5 ms on production's rows.
- A run's context shows its continuation up to 16 KiB, not 4 KiB (perf sprint A4, 7 Nov 2026; migration `20260930617000`, SQL test 1104, no bundle needed). The protocol has a run read all of it first, and every run with one past 4 KiB did, page by page: 42 of 126 runs, 130 model rounds at about 4.3 s each. The first prompt of such a run grows by up to about 3 k tokens, with text the run was about to fetch; `read_context` and its pages stay as they are.
- When the answer is something to see, an agent explores (Jonas, 7 Nov 2026: "spin up a bunch of loops all iterating on different design ideas and create simple demo videos of the concepts, posting them as an interface"): three to five workers at once, each iterating on its own idea and recording a demo video, then one posted app that shows them all to compare.
- A login at its usage limit no longer makes the materializer the database's largest cost (perf sprint D1, 7 Nov 2026; migration `20260930611000`, SQL test 1111). On production 193,463 of its 193,994 calls from 3 to 7 Nov came in the one day a wall held and answered `idle`, at 170 ms each. The due list now leaves out work whose login has no room, by the same check the company's scan makes, last and only for companies the page can name; a check that fails lists the company, which then fails alone in its own call; `qorpera_native_account_remaining` works out each company's wall once, not up to 72 times for a login in 8 companies; `qorpera_native_account_usage_limit_reset_company` starts from the signals that can hold the connection. Every answer stays the same (test 1111 compares the old and new text on generated worlds).
- A run no longer goes silent for good when one message to the host is large (perf sprint, 7 Nov 2026; no SQL). Run 054c8a7b held an exec host slot for 86 minutes, its lease renewing, until a member cancelled it. Codex's log says `Failed to write to stdout: Resource temporarily unavailable (os error 11)`, then `stdout writer exited`, then `dropping message for disconnected connection`, right after a 258,669-byte tool result (a PNG from `company_read`, echoed in `item/completed`). A Unix socket buffers 212,992 bytes, and a blocking writer simply waits for the host. Codex's writer was not blocking: Node creates `process.stdout` and `process.stderr` when they are first read, and its own code reads them (`Readable.pipe` compares its destination with both, a `net.Socket`'s destroy with stderr). So the native entry's relay set `O_NONBLOCK` on the sockets Codex inherited, at Codex's first outbound connection, about 1 s into every run on every host. The entry now puts plain writers of the same descriptors in their place, so the real streams are never made. It does not need a new Codex. The entry is installed in the runner image (`/opt/qorpera-runner/`), so this goes live with the next release that pins a new image, on an exec host only once that host is rebuilt on it. A run already hung needs a stop. The model login helper ships its own copy of the entry and is installed by hand, so no release replaces it; its messages should be small (not measured).
- Tool calls are faster and read more per call (perf sprint A3, 7 Nov 2026; migration `20260930618000`, SQL test 1103). The run's check around each tool call makes its read-set and authority checks in one database call (`qorpera_check_native_run`) instead of two round trips: the same reads, with the read set first and its 3 s bound on the run's row, under one 8 s statement budget, and a database without the call gets the two calls. A tool now leaves the result envelope 2.2 KiB, not 3, so a `company_read` or `read_context` page shows about 17 % more; a `machine_read` or coding command page may use the same room instead of 4 KiB. A folder report that would cut an answer which fits without it waits for the next result with room, so a full page keeps its `nextOffset`. A worker's `machine_read` with `waitSeconds` looks at the kernel's status first, as before, then asks the machine once and answers at once when the command has ended, instead of waiting for the kernel to hear of it at the executor's next pass.
- An agent keeps what waits on members true on its wall (Jonas, 7 Nov 2026; seen in Rumlig: the Co-Founder answered in chat, left the answered question on its wall and did not put its new one on): the instructions and `update_wall` say that a question a member answered leaves waiting and a new question joins it, in the same turn, and that a message opening with a quoted line answers that question from the wall.
- `update_wall` items are `{headline, detail?}` (a plain line is a headline alone): a new headline is at most 80 characters (`WALL_HEADLINE_TOO_LONG` otherwise, one already on the wall is kept), a detail at most 240 (Jonas, 5 Nov 2026: "that's still too much, I wouldn't want that much text per healine. instead they should be clickable rows you can click to expand details"). `ask_member` question options take `preview`: the company file path of an image the agent made (`.svg`, `.png`, `.jpg`, `.gif`, `.webp`) or `app:<name>`; the web draws such options as big pictures (migration `20260930578000` lets the key into the kernel's validator). The agents' instructions say to sketch a visual choice (an SVG in the company file or an interface app) and give each option its preview, to show rather than tell with apps and sketches, and to write wall headlines of a few words with the rest in the detail.
- A run no longer fails when Codex hangs up on the result fence with a request open (perf sprint, 7 Nov 2026; no SQL). Any socket error on the fence failed the run with `NATIVE_CODEX_TURN_COMPLETION:AUTHORITY_UNAVAILABLE`, and a peer that closed without taking its answer causes one. A hang-up now ends only that request; every other socket error and every protocol breach still fails the run. Whether this is what failed `035f5e1b` (6 Nov) and `11469530` (7 Nov), each 3 ms after an action review's verdict, when the host interrupts the reviewer's turn, is inferred, not proven. So the host's journal now says it: `Native result fence: peer hung up (code, lane)` for each hang-up, and `Native result fence failed (site=...)` for the path that failed it (`binding` for a request that is not the run's, `replay` for a request id seen before, `frame` for size, bytes or schema, and `connection`, `composition`, `fence`, `socket`, `server`, `listen`), fixed words with no content; a run that simply ends writes nothing. If the same failure comes back with a site in the journal, the inference was wrong and the site says what to look at.
- Work at a usage limit waits on its own login and goes on by itself (Jonas, 5 Nov 2026, "wait + resume only"; migration `20260930575000`, SQL tests 933 and 953 new, 992, 505, 426, 549 and 306 changed). Nothing moves to another login by itself. A usage limit's wall ends at the provider's reset, else after 6 hours, and at once when a later usage reading shows room; a signal without a reset takes it from the login's newest reading. While a login is at its limit, the materializer leaves its conversations' messages and wakes queued and the coding executor claims none of its sessions; paused runs come back at the reset, or as soon as a reading shows room (the refresh's completion). The shared vault's upkeep pass now runs the daily usage check (`CloudModelLoginUsage.recheck`, `qorpera_begin_model_login_usage_recheck`): a login at its limit with a reading older than its limit, more than a day old or older than a reset that passed is read as Settings → Refresh reads it, at most once in three hours per member. The usage-limit notice names the login (`accountEmail`), and the agent's words say the work waits and goes on by itself.
- Good will tools (migrations `20260930566000`, `20260930567000`): `good_will_list` reads a month's payments into the company's bank and what counted for its good will (last month by default), `good_will_mark` sorts 1 to 100 of them at once (sale counts; member money, own transfers, refunds, moms back, loans and grants, other do not; a sale's moms defaults to a fifth). Every company agent has them; the Co-Founder's overlay (v9) has it sort last month in its first planning run of a month until an agent owns the company's money, then show the members what counted on an ask_member card.

### Added
- `company_search` answers more in one call and searches like rg (keyholes S4, 12 Nov 2026; contract 2026-11-12 amendment, proposed): it asks the node for pages until about 20 KiB shows, the search ends, 6 pages or 10 s (a later page that fails ends the call with what came and a cursor), and takes `regex` (RE2's kind: no back-references, lookaround or counts above 16), `context` (0 to 5 lines around each hit, shown as rg does: `path-12- text`, `--` between groups), `files_only` (`path: matching lines`) and a glob without a query (a find of paths). Of a node whose hello says search version 2 it asks version 2; of one that says nothing it asks version 1 and refuses the new parts with `SEARCH_NOT_HERE_YET`, sending nothing. The node's coded words on a pattern (`REGEX_…`) are passed on; a full or busy node is `COMPANY_FOLDER_BUSY`. A file count an answer ends inside shows `so far` and goes on with the cursor, closed by the call that finishes the file; lines over 1 MiB a pattern did not search are named. Its description and schema change (what the provider sees; a pattern is offered only "if the folder's search runs them; REGEX_OFF means it does not, so search for text", since patterns are off as the folder service ships); no catalog pin moves (the pinned run has no folder search). No SQL. An answer is released under the hash of the text the model is shown, after the boundary cuts a secret out of it (the S4 proof, 12 Nov 2026): before, an answer showing a line with a token (`ghp_…`, `whsec_…`, a Stripe or npm key) was cited under the hash of the uncut text, and the run stopped at its next request (`Context read provenance differs`). One answer never shows two versions of a file (the v240 release review, 13 Nov 2026): when a file the call's last page ended inside was replaced before the next page, the answer showed the old version's lines or unfinished count beside the new version's, and released both; now what the call showed of the old version is left out and not released (a file gone between pages likewise), and when an earlier answer showed part of it the answer says it changed and is searched again from its start, or that it is no longer as that answer saw it. A search whose own path (a file, or a folder holding the file a page ended inside) is deleted, or no longer the run's to read, before the call's next page answers `NOT_FOUND`, as a first page would, and shows and releases nothing; it showed and released the first page's lines of the gone file and gave a cursor no call could use (review r9 of #1698).
- A run no longer fails because a machine's executor did not answer its start (perf sprint, 7 Nov 2026; no SQL). When an executor stalled, `machine_run`'s start hit its 30 s and went unconfirmed though it had never reached the database. Its receipt stayed open, so every later effect of the run and its completion were refused (`RUN_COMPLETION_REFUSED`): 4 of 570 runs from 3 to 7 November, all inside an executor freeze, none with a command row. The start is now sent again as it was, same command id, only while the executor gives no answer (a timeout, a lost connection, a proxy's 5xx) and the kernel has no record of the command (`qorpera_native_machine_manage` status): at most 5 minutes in all, 1 s doubling to 15 s between sends. At the first record, a machine no longer running or a stopped run, it stops and the outcome stays unknown, as before. A refusal after an unanswered send also leaves it unknown: only a first send's refusal says nothing started. Whatever the executor did answer is not sent again. `share_app`'s start and `machine_cancel` are unchanged. `machine_read` waits 30 s, what the gateway and the executor give a request, not a database read's 5 s: 17 reads in 7 runs timed out at 5 s, each costing a model round. A stall a re-send carried over shows as a `machine_run` observation that succeeded after 30 s or more.

- `/v1/machine-commands/wake` on the coding gateway (`CloudMachineCommandGateway.wake`, perf sprint A5): `machine_start` of a machine that is starting or in line asks its executor for a pass now, instead of waiting for the next one (up to 10 s), and a worker's wait ends when the executor says the machine got somewhere (looks every 5 s remain as the fallback). It carries no authority and changes no answer: what SQL says stands.
- Readable cards (Jonas, 5 November 2026: "approval cards are completely unreadable"). `ask_member` refuses a new card whose body is over 5 short lines, a line over 280 UTF-8 bytes or 600 in all, or whose option label is over 40 bytes (the schema it is offered says 40), saying how to fix it (the kernel's limits stay, so stored cards still read); its description says a card is read in seconds: the title is the question, the body what is chosen, what matters and a link, no disclaimers or lists of what is unchecked, buttons of 1 to 3 words. The reviewer's card reason is one everyday sentence in the company's language (the founders', not English or Danish by the request; English when nothing shows it). Hire overlay v10: the Co-Founder writes the vision draft to a file and asks in one short question card, at most five short lines on what matters most and the link; "# Writing to people" says the same for any draft. Prompt, overlay and tool catalog hashes move.
- An agent's wall (Jonas, 5 November 2026: "a poster or wall of updates kept within rules, instead of many new messages"; migration `20260930573000`). `update_wall` keeps `QorperaOS/agents/<handle>/wall.md`: what waits on a member and what the agent is working on (each a whole list, or null to keep it) and what it finished, added on top with today's date; at most 6, 6 and 40 items, each one line of at most 240 characters with its links. It rewrites the file against the version read (a wall that changed in between is refused: call again), keeping the file's own head (the title and a note) as written; it refuses a wall with lines that read as no item instead of dropping them (`WALL_OUT_OF_SHAPE`: write it again whole with `company_write`) and a part a member filled past its limit kept by null (`WALL_TOO_MANY`); it drops the oldest finished items past the file's 16 KiB; it dates finished items in the company's time zone; it saves nothing when the wall is already so, does not add a finished item again within the week the poster shows, answers with both lists and the five newest finished items, and applies at once (review class internal). Only a company run of the agent has it, never a worker. `company_write` and `company_edit` refuse a `wall.md` that breaks the format (`WALL_INVALID`). "# Writing to people" tells the agent to keep its wall in place of status messages; the conversation and private instructions' hashes move.
- Interface apps (Jonas, 5 November 2026; deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-INTERFACE-APPS.md; migration `20260930572000`). `save_app` makes an app in `apps/<name>/` of the company folder (its folder, `app.toml` and text files in one change set, owned by the agent and not posted) or changes its manifest and files over the versions it reads; it checks the name, the manifest, each file's kind and size and the app's totals (64 files, 4 MiB) first, applies at once (review class internal) and answers the link `app:<name>`. `post_app` sets `posted`, every other byte of the manifest as written, and is delegation, which the automatic review sees ("Post an app for every member"). `company_write` and `company_edit` check an `app.toml` (`APP_MANIFEST_INVALID`) and refuse a change of `posted` (`APP_POSTED_BY_POST_APP`). Both tools are in `CLOUD_RUNTIME_TOOL_OPERATIONS` and the SQL list; a read-only worker has neither. The company prompt tells agents to show members what they made as an app linked as `[Title](app:<name>)` instead of long text; the pinned prompt hashes move.

### Breaking Changes

- The folder helper's `mount` names the mount's cache, and the folder program on a company machine keeps its caches on the machine's disk (MC3c; migration `20260930516000`, SQL test 538; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-FOLDER-MACHINE-CACHES.md` "Where the cache lives" and decision 1, amended 18 October). A mount request has `cache`: `desktop` for a `desktop-<id>` name (every desktop of the machine shares that cache, whoever holds it) and the command's owner, `agent-<uuid>` or `member-<uuid>`, for a `command-<id>` name; `parseCompanyComputerFolderRequest` and the helper refuse any other pairing or shape (`invalid`). The helper makes `/var/lib/qorpera-folder/cache/<cache>` (root, 0700; `cacheRoot`, `FOLDER_CACHE`), fails a mount whose cache folder is not a real folder of its own, and writes the mount's `/run/qorpera-folder/units/<name>.env` (0600) with `QORPERA_FOLDER_CACHE_DIR`, which `qorpera-office-folder-mount@.service` reads and passes as `--cache-dir`; a `switch` keeps it, and `unmount` and `drop` remove it and keep the cache. A command's view open answers its `owner`, and `openCompanyComputerCommandFolderView` closes a view whose answer has no owner of that shape, so the command does not start. A desktop's or a command's view renews only while its agent and member still read the folder's root: a member who leaves, or an agent whose root grant is revoked or lapses, has its views closed at the next renewal (the folder service renews every 40 s). From then on the core refuses the view's reads and publishes, and only files already in the cache can be read until the mount ends: a command's at once, a desktop's after the folder program's 60 s handoff grace (at most 40 + 60 s after the loss). Their caches stay where they are.
- `HttpCloudCodingCommandService`'s `execute` answers a `CloudCodingCommandExecution` (`{receipt, hidden(signal)}`) instead of the receipt (F3 step 5g-5 part 3; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-FOLDER-CODING-MACHINES.md` rule 4, `unreadable`). The service asks `hidden` once, after the call's final authorization and the receipt's checks, right before it answers, and only then builds the answer: `machine_stopped` or `unconfirmed` answers the receipt with nothing of the command (that state, no exit code, no output, an empty log at offset 0), undefined answers it as it is, and anything else, or a rejection, refuses the call (`coding_command_unavailable`). It is the one check that decides whether a receipt shows its command; a rebuild that ends after it, while the answer is on its way, is not caught, and that answer was true when it was decided. For a forwarded command it runs on the executor that holds the machine, and the forwarding executor's `hidden` answers undefined: its own checks after the forward can only refuse. `createHttpCloudCodingCommandGateway` accepts a receipt that shows nothing of its command for any page, so a paged read across a rebuild answers `machine_stopped` instead of failing as unavailable; any other receipt for another page is still refused.
- `machine_start` has no `repositoryWorker` (F3 6e-1; migration `20260930506000`, SQL test 684, `scripts/test-company-machine-v2-retire.mjs`; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-FOLDER-TEST-MACHINES.md` "What replaces `/mnt/repository`"): a coding session's work is in the company folder, at `/company/code/<owner>/<name>/worktrees/<workerId>`, and the tool's description says so. The tool and SQL refuse the argument as any unknown one. A company machine has no `repository_worker_id` and its view no `repository`; it is tied to no repository's node, so it moves and wakes like any other, and the operator's `codingWorkerId` is null for every company machine. The migration retires every current v1 placement (its open commands end lost) and refuses a second apply. It rolls before the node: see `@qorpera/runner-orchestrator`'s entry.
- An agent authors no page or table layout (C6, the Interfaces write-path boundary; migration `20260930510000`, SQL test 689; `QORPERA-REBUILD-SCOPE-AMENDMENT-2026-10-17.md` §2, §4). The folder core's save check (`qorpera_company_state_interface_paths`, run for every unit of every commit) refuses each unit an agent's commit makes (actor kind `staff_agent`: a run, routine, coding session, computer or test machine) that changes, makes, deletes, moves or copies onto anything at or under `interfaces/`, the `tables/` root, a table's folder or its `table.toml`, as `refused`/`structured_rule` with the problem `layout_authoring`, "page and table layout authoring is not available". It is the first rule of that check, so it holds for a tool call, a replayed or approved call, a proposal applied later (`not_applied`), a file write in a shell and a mounted folder alike. The commit's other units are decided as before. An agent still reads everything, copies out of `interfaces/`, and changes a table's `rows.csv` under the claim rule. Members and the platform (company creation's seed) keep every rule as it was; stored definitions stay, nothing is deleted. `company_write` and the other file tools answer such a refusal with `next` `COMPANY_FILE_LAYOUT_AUTHORING: nothing was written; …` telling the model not to make the change again, and their review text no longer names `save_interface`. SQL tests 864 and 450 follow the new rule.
- A run has no `interface_read` or `save_interface` (C6b, the Interfaces tool boundary; `QORPERA-REBUILD-SCOPE-AMENDMENT-2026-10-17.md` §2; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-INTERFACES.md` "Built: C6b"). The Company File tools keep `table_read` and `table_write`, and a worker's filter no longer names `save_interface`. A call to either name that still arrives, from an older thread or a replay, finds no tool: the boundary refuses it as `TOOL_UNAVAILABLE` before any review, receipt or effect, and observes it as `unknown_tool`. The automatic review drops both names, so it classifies them as any unknown name. `CLOUD_RUNTIME_TOOL_OPERATIONS` keeps both, as the SQL list of observed operations does. The tool boundary's `prepare` step stays, though no tool uses it now. An agent reads a page's files with `company_read`.
- The coding guest agent's `status` reports the session's usage (C1; `qorpera.coding-guest-status/v2`; P3 phase B run 11): the processor time of the session's slice and of its user's slice (`cpu.stat` `usage_usec`; the user manager and what it started count), and the bytes their processes passed to read and write calls (`/proc/<pid>/io` rchar + wchar, `idleRuleProcessIoBytes`; a command's relay, which only copies its output, is left out), read at the boot's `/proc/uptime` with the boot's id. The bytes come from one pass over the machine's processes, each placed by its `/proc/<pid>/cgroup`, so the cgroups the user manager may make (they are delegated to the session's user) cost nothing; the pass reads at most `CODING_GUEST.usageProcesses` (8192), and `ioComplete` is false when the machine ran more or `/proc` could not be listed. A unit or process that ends while read counts as nothing. `CodingGuestAgentPorts` gains `uptimeMs`, `cgroupRoot` and `procRoot`.
- A company run keeps its work log when its company is on the memory tree's list (memory tree W1c-3, production wiring; `RUNTIME-AGENT-MEMORY-TREE.md` §4.5, §5.7, §7.5; gate `20260930352000`). Before the run's first provider request `CloudNativeCompanyRuntime` opens the log (`openCloudRunLog`, `cloud-run-log-open.ts`) by asking `qorpera_memory_run_log_place` where it goes. An unlisted company answers `{ off: true }` (`CloudRunLogCompanyFolder.locate` throws `CloudRunLogUnavailable("off")`) and the run keeps no log, as does a run with no conversation tree (the place answers it off too). A log made once and no longer there (`gone`) fails the run before its first model request (`CloudRunLogGone`, stage `native-tools`, category `authority_unavailable`), so a resumed attempt never goes on unlogged past its earlier steps.
