With no log yet, an original attempt starts one under the platform's origin, and a resumed attempt whose earlier attempts kept none goes on without one. An existing log is continued only by a resumed attempt (`qorpera_memory_run_resume`, W5b-2); an original attempt refuses it (`log_exists`), and any other failure to open fails the run at stage `native-tools`. The boundary then steps every call into the log through `CloudRunLogRpcProtection` (`qorpera_memory_step_narrow`, `qorpera_memory_step_hashes`), and an acting call, as auto-review classifies it, has its pending line saved before it acts. The run's step lines compose each request's work (the opening's `composeContext`, under the conversation's cache key when the run has the view), their compactor turns on the run's own Codex session in the lane the conversation's tree uses (`CloudNativeRunToolAdapters.runLog`; every company run now gets the run's compactor, not only one with conversation lines). At closing the log commits its last steps and the lines build what is left, then the conversation's lines close; a run that fails on its own still commits the steps it finished, within 10 s, and a stopped run does not. `CloudRunLog.close`'s signal bounds all the log's work left: when it stops, so does a timed batch still running, and closing waits for no batch past it. A log found deleted mid-run fails the run the same way, at stage `codex-turn-completion`. A hook call's line (`list_hooks`, `manage_hook`) keeps its result without any `address`, `path` or `url` and names no path of it. Every company run now makes one more serial call before its first model request. The list gate drops for every folder-v2 company once W1d (erasure of work) is live, in `qorpera_memory_run_log_place` alone; the off answer for a run with no conversation tree stays.
- The coding guest's Git helper takes a seed of up to 256 MiB in 192 KiB chunks (`CODING_GIT_SEED_BYTES`, `CODING_GIT_SEED_CHUNK_BYTES`; P6), hashing and writing them part by part; a seed command may run 540 s and its Git steps 240 s of CPU. A seed records the session's base as `refs/qorpera/base`, and an export bundles only the commits since it: an unchanged head leaves out its parents, and a workspace without the ref sends its whole history. Needs the Runner Orchestrator from the same tree.

- `computer_transfer` is gone (F3 4f-1; no SQL; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-FOLDER-COMPANY-MACHINES.md` §4): a Company Computer works in the company folder at `/company`, and since legacy sweep step C every transfer was refused at authorize. No capability offers the tool. `parseCompanyComputerAction` refuses a `transfer` action (`INVALID_COMPUTER_ARGUMENTS`), `HttpCompanyComputerNativeEndpoint.authorize` has no transfer refusal, and the guest no `OFFICE_FILE_EXPORT_UNAVAILABLE` or `OFFICE_FILE_IMPORT_UNAVAILABLE`. The Linux workbench file reader and writer (`cloud-company-computer-file-reader.ts`, `cloud-company-computer-file-writer.ts`) are gone, with `CompanyComputerLinuxCommands.readFile` and `writeFile`, the optional `CompanyComputerGuestCommands.readFile` and `writeFile` hooks, their probes and `scripts/test-office-file-export-linux.mjs` and `scripts/test-office-file-import-linux.mjs`, and the desktop probe source whose script legacy sweep step C deleted. The company instructions say the company folder is at `/company` on the Company Computer instead of calling its disk a shared workbench. Observations keep `computer_transfer` as a tool name for older rows. The database's transfer source goes with legacy sweep step D (D13).
- A company run is admitted in two calls (M1b-4b-3; migration `20260930300100`, SQL test 947; contract §6, §7). `CloudNativeCompanyRuntime` admits the binary, bundle, reasoning policy and exact tool catalog with `qorpera_begin_native_company_execution_v3` before any provider work, without a launch or start; at the opening it settles the conversation's tree and composes the context on that admission, then binds the context's hash and bytes once with `qorpera_bind_native_company_context`, which writes the launch and the start through v2. A refused or unconfirmed bind ends the run before `thread/start`; a lost bind answer is not asked again. The company runtime no longer calls `qorpera_begin_native_company_execution_v2`, which stays for the coding wrapper. `memoryCommitView` takes settle's `MemoryViewWait` before its signal.

- `CloudRunLog` requires a trusted protection port before it can be constructed (memory tree W1c-3, component only; `RUNTIME-AGENT-MEMORY-TREE.md` §4.5). It classifies paths from complete arguments and tool results, every released read and current machine state before staging, stores only platform-keyed hashes and receipt identifiers for narrow steps, and preserves their full content only in the running process. `CloudRunLogRpcProtection` binds the port to one environment, job and run through the lease-scoped classification and keyed-hash calls, chunks complete inputs at their SQL limits and refuses malformed or unavailable answers. Pending results become final in order so an earlier narrow result cannot leave later public records; worker dispatch and followup wait for earlier results before committing the origin the worker inherits. Missing protection fails closed. A public step line names `paths`: each company path its whole arguments and result name, once and without `/company/`, never its reads (the reads copy and release records name those); a pending line names its arguments' only. Paths are never cut: a step that names more than 1,024 of them or more than 8 KiB is written narrow, and so is every later step of its run. A step line is at most 49 KiB. Runtime activation is still gated on matching SQL, closing/compaction and erasure support; this change does not enable run logs for companies.
- The runner worker runs only the native company lane (legacy sweep step C, C-R1; no SQL): its whole-tree native lane and its Podman sandbox lane served only snapshot-v1 and are gone, with `executeNativeCloudRunnerJob`, `NativeProviderSandboxBackend` and `ExecuteNativeCloudRunnerJobOptions` (`cloud-native-provider.ts`) and the `start` member of `NativeProviderComputerBridge` (`HttpNativeProviderComputerBridge.start` stays, unsent, until the native host's legacy lane goes in C-R3). A folder-v2 job (`company-path-v1` with a native subscription) runs through `executeNativeCompanyRunnerJob`, unchanged. The worker fails any other run with no fallback: a `whole-tree-v1` run ("Runner does not support a whole-tree company run"), another sponsor, or a runner with no native bridge. `EphemeralCloudRunnerWorkerOptions` loses `backend`, `nativeWorkspaceGid`, `temporaryRoot` and `environment`; `executeJob` is the company lane's executor (`CloudRunnerCompanyExecutor`), and an injected one starts its assignment as production does. The native lease is released by the worker only when no completion was staged; a staged one is released by the governed completion with its receipt. `cloud-runner-service.ts` no longer builds `RootlessPodmanSandboxBackend`; its configuration has no `image`, `podmanPath`, `entrypoint`, `capabilities` or `jobTypes`, but the file still accepts `image`, `podman_path`, `entrypoint`, `capabilities` and `job_types` and ignores them, because the providers still write them (`native_bridge_shared_gid`, `temporary_root` and `environment` are still read, by the LXD guest bootstrap only). The runner no longer calls the `runner-broker` Edge actions `inputs`, `transcript`, `operations`, `artifacts`, `register_artifact`, `model`, `bind_provider_session`, `prepare_company_revision`, `begin_company_revision_graph`, `append_company_revision_graph`, `complete_company_revision_graph` and `verify_company_revision`, or its object GET and PUT, and so no longer reaches `qorpera_runner_broker_list_inputs`, `_list_transcript`, `_list_operations`, `_list_artifacts`, `_register_artifact`, `_authorize_input_object`, `_authorize_output_object`, `_authorize_company_file_object`, `_confirm_output_object`, `_reserve_model_request` and `_finalize_model_request`, `qorpera_bind_execution_provider_session`, `qorpera_runner_prepare_company_revision`, `qorpera_runner_begin/append/complete_company_revision_graph` or `qorpera_runner_authorize/finalize_company_revision_verification`. Their broker client methods go in C-R6 and C-R8; the Edge actions and the SQL in step D/E. The runner still calls `describe` and the native authority's acquire, renew and release.
- Every company run's context names its agent's company folder (legacy sweep step C, C-P9 + AF; no SQL). `CloudNativeCompanyContext.folder` is required and `parseCloudNativeCompanyContext` takes `folder` as one of its closed keys, so a context without one is refused as `Invalid cloud company context` at stage `company-context`, before the wake is parsed and for a coding run too. C-P1's later refusal (`A company run needs its agent's company folder`, stage `prompt-composition`) is gone with it. `cloudAgentFolderPlace` always gives `{ core: "folder-v2", handle }`. `cloudMemoryTools(gateway, identity, place, now?)` takes the agent's folder place third and requires it (it was fourth and defaulted to the agent's id); `cloudSkillRoot`, `composeCloudAgentSkills` and `cloudSkillTools` require it too. The composed instructions, prompt and tool catalog are byte for byte as before: `cloud-native-company-context.test.ts` pins the sha256 of the instructions and prompt of a company conversation, a worker and a member's private conversation.
- Every native company run is a company-folder run (legacy sweep step C, C-P1; no SQL). `CloudNativeCompanyRuntime` refuses a company run whose context names no `folder` before it reads a file or starts Codex (`A company run needs its agent's company folder`, stage `prompt-composition`); a coding run is unchanged. Its `folderBytes` option is required. The snapshot-v1 run path is gone: the path gateway's file tools (`gateway.tools()`: its own `company_read`, `company_list`, `company_write`, `company_mkdir` and `company_delete`), the notebook and skill tools over the gateway (`cloudMemoryTools` and `cloudSkillTools` given the gateway), the gateway as the reader of the notebook, skills and vision at composition, and the gateway given to the event tools (so `save_attachment` is offered to no run). Every company run uses what folder-v2 runs already used: the folder core's file tools (`cloud-folder-file-tools.ts`, with Interfaces' `interface_read` and `table_read`), its notebook, skills and vision through `CloudFolderNotebooks`, the event tools' folder client, and the folder writers of the routine, sidebar, hiring, group chat, todo and worker tools, whose kernel-row branches no run now takes (C-P2 to C-P8 delete them). Its tool catalog, descriptions, instructions and prompt are byte for byte what folder-v2 runs had (the runtime test pins the default catalog). A company run no longer calls `qorpera_read_company_path`, `qorpera_list_company_paths`, `qorpera_read_company_path_v2`, `qorpera_confirm_company_path_read`, `qorpera_list_company_paths_v2`, the gateway's writes (`qorpera_check_company_path_mutation`, `qorpera_prepare_company_path_mutation`, `qorpera_commit_company_path_mutation`, `qorpera_native_company_file_write`, `qorpera_native_company_file_write_source`, `qorpera_verify_company_path_file`) or `qorpera_native_mail_attachment`. The path gateway stays for the run's context fence (`qorpera_assert_company_path_reads`, `qorpera_fence_native_context`, `qorpera_record_native_context_view`) and as an argument of the hiring and worker tools until C-P2 and C-P4.
- (legacy sweep step C, C-P4; no SQL) The hiring, group chat and event tools take no path gateway. `cloudHiringTools(identity, { folderHiring, folderGroupChats })` requires the folder writers: `hire_agent` saves through `CloudFolderHiring` and `create_group_chat` through `CloudFolderGroupChats`, as every run already did. The gateway branch is gone: its `mutate` of the agent or chat document, `settle` (which applied a proposal through `applyOwnOrganizationDocument`), and the unused `CLOUD_HIRE_TOOL_DESCRIPTION` and `CLOUD_GROUP_CHAT_TOOL_DESCRIPTION`. The tools still bind an `operationKey` to the first hire or chat that runs under it, and refuse the key with other arguments, before the review and again when a call runs, by pointing at `agents/` or `agents/groups/`. `cloudEventTools(binding, identity, control, folder?)` takes no gateway, and `save_attachment` is gone with `CLOUD_SAVE_ATTACHMENT_TOOL_DESCRIPTION` and its wait. So are the path gateway's `attachment` input to `mutate`, `CloudMailAttachmentContent` and `MAX_ATTACHMENT_BYTES`, `qorpera_native_mail_attachment` in `HttpCloudCompanyPathControl`'s closed list, and the action review's `save_attachment` case. Two texts no longer name the tool: `read_event`'s description says an email lists its attachments "without their contents, which no tool can open yet", and the hook wake's instructions say "the attachments' descriptions (no tool can open their contents yet)". Only `read_event`'s description changes in the tool catalog (the runtime test's pin moves with it). The runtime no longer calls `qorpera_native_mail_attachment`, nor, through the hiring tools' `settle`, the organization self-approval path (`qorpera_self_approve_organization_proposal`, then the approval's apply through `qorpera_prepare_company_path_approval` and `qorpera_apply_company_path_approval` or `_v3`). `save_attachment` stays in the observation vocabulary and `qorpera_native_mail_attachment` in the native host's call list, which tests pin to the SQL.
- The native host's call list no longer has `qorpera_native_mail_attachment` (legacy sweep step D, D4; migration `20260930333000`). The migration drops the request with its row in `qorpera_native_host_calls`, so `NATIVE_HOST_CALLS` refuses it by name as the database does. Nothing has called it since C-P4 removed `save_attachment`, which stays in the observation vocabulary. `cloud-native-host-dispatch.test.ts` now leaves out each call a migration deletes from the list.
- (legacy sweep step C, C-P5; no SQL) The path gateway's own model tools, reads and shared-export writes are gone, which no run has used since C-P1. `CloudCompanyPathGateway` loses `tools()` (its `company_read`, `company_list`, `company_write`, `company_mkdir` and `company_delete`), `read`, `list`, the confirmation of a released read, its record of reads awaiting confirmation, and the `source` input of `mutate` (a write copied from a shared export); `mutate` takes `{ path, operationKey, expectedVersion, text? }`, so the folder notebooks' `mutate` drops its refusal of a `source` input, which nothing can pass any more. Its constructor takes no `presence`, and `claimCloudCompanyPathPresence` is gone (`CloudCompanyPathPresence` stays). The context fence names no path: `assertReadableContext` and `fenceContextView` send `p_paths: []`, as every folder-v2 run already did, and a fence answer that keeps a path is refused. A company run's model reads, lists and writes through the folder core instead: `cloudFolderFileTools` over `CloudCompanyFolderClient`. The runtime stops calling `qorpera_read_company_path_v2`, `qorpera_list_company_paths_v2`, `qorpera_confirm_company_path_read`, `qorpera_native_company_file_write` and `qorpera_native_company_file_write_source`; `HttpCloudCompanyPathControl` no longer permits them, nor C-P2's `qorpera_manage_agent_todo`, `qorpera_confirm_agent_todo_publication`, `qorpera_manage_agent_worker` and `qorpera_report_worker_result`. Its 16 MiB response limit for path calls now covers only `qorpera_prepare_company_path_mutation` and `qorpera_prepare_company_path_approval`. The tool boundary turns the gateway's refused admission into `COMPANY_FILE_ACCESS_DENIED` only for `company_write`, `company_mkdir` and `company_delete`: no `company_read` or `company_list` reaches the gateway any more. The tool catalog and prompts are byte for byte as before.
- (legacy sweep step C, C-P6; no SQL) The path gateway's formation check and organization self-approval are gone, which no run has used since C-P4. `CloudCompanyPathGateway` loses `applyOwnOrganizationDocument`, and `applyApprovedCompanyPath` (`cloud-company-path-gateway.ts`) is gone. `mutate` no longer reads `organization/formation.json` before a write to it, so `CloudCompanyFormationRefused` (`cloud-company-path-refusal.ts`) and the tool boundary's `COMPANY_FORMATION_REFUSED` result are gone too; a folder-v2 company has no formation file (P28b). `cloud-company-formation.ts` (the `@qorpera/runtime/cloud-company-formation` subpath, with `COMPANY_FORMATION_SCHEMA`, `COMPANY_FORMATION_STAGES`, `COMPANY_FORMATION_STATUSES`, `COMPANY_FORMATION_PATH`, `isCompanyFormationPath` and `companyFormationWriteRefusal`) is deleted, and `scripts/generate-cloud-runtime-bundle.mjs` no longer lists `cloud-company-formation` in its tool catalog, context composer or provider bootstrap components. The Co-Founder overlay's pin (no formation file or stages, P28b) moves to `cloud-native-company-context.test.ts`. The runtime stops calling `qorpera_self_approve_organization_proposal`, `qorpera_prepare_company_path_approval`, `qorpera_apply_company_path_approval` and `qorpera_apply_company_path_approval_v3`: `CloudCompanyPathRpc` no longer names them and `HttpCloudCompanyPathControl` no longer permits them (the native host's call list keeps them, pinned to the SQL until step D). The control's 16 MiB response limit for path calls now covers only `qorpera_prepare_company_path_mutation`. The tool catalog and prompts are byte for byte as before.
- (legacy sweep step C, C-P7; no SQL) The path gateway's mutation lane is gone, which no run has used since C-P1: every memory and skill write saves through `CloudFolderNotebooks`. `CloudCompanyPathGateway` keeps `readTrusted`, `listTrusted` and the context fence, and loses `mutate` (its check, prepare, upload, tree verification and commit, its replay of a completed operation key and its quarantine of a lost answer), `stageCompanyPathCandidate`, and its parsers of a mutation's result and prepared answers; `CloudCompanyPathResult` stays, as what a notebook change answers. One exported interface, `CloudFolderNotebookGateway` (`cloud-folder-notebooks.ts`: `readTrusted`, `listTrusted` and `mutate`), replaces the three `Pick`s of the path gateway (`CloudMemoryGateway`, `CloudSkillGateway` and the notebooks' own alias of that name): `cloudMemoryTools`, `composeCloudAgentMemory`, `ensureCloudCompanyFolders`, `cloudSkillTools` and `composeCloudAgentSkills` take it, and `CloudFolderNotebooks` serves it (its `composition`, and each tool call's own gateway). `CloudCompanyPathPresence` loses `claim`, which nothing has called since C-P5, with its cached background observations; `observe` stays. A tool operation no longer completes with an output snapshot or a proposal: `CloudNativeToolOperationOutput` drops `outputSnapshotId` and `proposalId` with their check, and the control's `completeToolOperation` takes the new `CompleteToolOperationInput` (`receiptId`, `inputSha256`, `result`), so `qorpera_complete_native_tool_operation` is sent without `p_output_snapshot_id` and `p_proposal_id`, which default to null in SQL. A begun receipt's `output_snapshot_id` and `proposal_id` are still read on replay. The runtime stops calling `qorpera_check_company_path_mutation`, `qorpera_prepare_company_path_mutation`, `qorpera_commit_company_path_mutation`, `qorpera_verify_company_path_file` and `qorpera_verify_company_path_tree`: `CloudCompanyPathRpc` no longer names them, `HttpCloudCompanyPathControl` no longer permits them (the native host's call list keeps them, pinned to the SQL until step D), and the control's 16 MiB response limit for prepare is gone. The memory cases that drove the gateway's `mutate` over a fake PostgREST move to `cloud-folder-notebooks.test.ts` over the folder core, where they still apply. The tool catalog and prompts are byte for byte as before.
- (legacy sweep step C, C-P8; no SQL) The path gateway's trusted reads and its object store are gone, which no run has used since C-P1: every notebook, skill and vision read goes through `CloudFolderNotebooks`. `CloudCompanyPathGateway` loses `readTrusted` (its whole-file read from the content-addressed store: manifest and chunk fetch, digest check and UTF-8 trim), `listTrusted` (its paged listing pinned to one snapshot, started again after a PT412), its parsers of a tree entry and file reference, and its `store` input; it is only the run's context fence now (`assertReadableContext`, `recordContextView`, `fenceContextView`), built from `{ binding, identity, control }`. `CloudNativeCompanyRuntime` loses its `objectStore` option, and the native provider service its `NO_COMPANY_OBJECTS` store that refused every object; `folderBytes` still moves the folder's bytes. `readCloudAgentHeadVision` takes `Pick<CloudFolderNotebookGateway, "readTrusted">`. Folder-v2 reads instead through `CloudFolderNotebooks` (its `composition`, and each tool call's own gateway): `CloudCompanyFolderClient`'s `read`, `readRange` and `listAll`, so `qorpera_folder_read` and `qorpera_folder_list` as the run, with what was read released under the hash of the answer or of the composed context. The runtime stops calling `qorpera_read_company_path` and `qorpera_list_company_paths`: `CloudCompanyPathRpc` no longer names them and `HttpCloudCompanyPathControl` no longer permits them (the native host's call list keeps them, pinned to the SQL until step D; the member client's Company File editor in `packages/cloud-client` still calls them). Neither does the control permit C-P3's `qorpera_manage_agent_routine`, `qorpera_decide_agent_routine` and `qorpera_manage_agent_sidebar`, whose callers C-P3 removed; the union no longer names them either. `test/fixtures/company-file-postgrest.ts` (a fake PostgREST over the legacy path RPCs) is deleted with the gateway test's trusted-read cases, and the control test's cases that used the removed names now use calls the control still permits. The tool catalog and prompts are byte for byte as before.
- The native host's call list no longer has the agents' old todo, worker, routine and sidebar writers (legacy sweep step D, D6; migration `20260930335000`): `qorpera_manage_agent_todo`, `qorpera_confirm_agent_todo_publication`, `qorpera_manage_agent_worker`, `qorpera_report_worker_result`, `qorpera_manage_agent_routine`, `qorpera_decide_agent_routine` and `qorpera_manage_agent_sidebar`. The migration drops each request with its row in `qorpera_native_host_calls`, so `NATIVE_HOST_CALLS` refuses them by name as the database does. C-P5 and C-P8 already took them out of `HttpCloudCompanyPathControl`'s closed list and `CloudCompanyPathRpc`. Nothing has called them since C-P2 and C-P3 left the tools saving only through the folder writers. `cloud-native-host-dispatch.test.ts` pins the eight calls step D has taken out.
- A native host's Company Computer actions prove its run's lease (G2-6c, no SQL; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-NATIVE-HOST-KEY.md` "G2-6"). `createHttpCompanyComputerSessionFactory` and `HttpCompanyComputerNativeEndpoint` take a required `holder` (the run's, read per request) and send `x-qorpera-identity` with each perform and release, only in the job's own `job.<job id>.<lease token>` form. `CompanyComputerNativeGateway` proves it with `qorpera_native_binding_proven` (now in the registry's closed method list, which accepts its plain `true`) before a session opens, on every perform and on every release, and refuses a missing, malformed or unproven holder like any other refused request.
- The native host runs only the company lane (legacy sweep step C, C-R3; no SQL): its whole-tree execution lane served only snapshot-v1 and is gone. `NativeProviderHost.execute` takes only a `company-path-v1` request and runs it through its cloud runtime (`NativeCloudExecutionDriver`, `CloudNativeCompanyRuntime.execute` in production), as before; any other request, or a host built without a cloud runtime, is refused before the authority is asked ("Native provider execution runtime is not configured for this request"), with no fallback. Gone with the lane: `NativeLegacyExecutionRequest` and the `NativeProviderExecutionRequest` union (the control plane's `validateExecution`, `sendMessage` and `appendInteractionRequest` take `NativeCompanyExecutionRequest`), `NativeHarnessExecutionInput`, `NativeHarnessDriver.execute` and `OfficialCliNativeHarnessDriver.execute` (the official CLI now drives sign-in only), the host's own `CloudAgentMouth` loopback MCP server, its prompt from the canonical transcript and queued inputs, and its walk granting the workspace group access. A company run's failure still ends in ` [mouth=uninitialized]`, as it always did. The native service accepts only `qorpera.native-company-execution/v1` (closed: `schema`, `authority`, `job` with `job_id`, `attempt_count` and `lease_token`, `native_host`) and refuses `qorpera.native-provider-execution/v1`; its stage diagnostics no longer name `prompt-materialization`, `public-mouth-start`, `public-mouth-completion` or `workspace-normalization`. `HttpNativeProviderComputerBridge.start` (unsent since C-R1) goes, with the bridge's `qorpera.native-provider-execution/v1` projection; `startCompany` is unchanged. Tests: the legacy execution cases, `official-cli-harness-leftovers.test.ts`, `official-codex-mouth.test.ts` and the public mouth's process probes (`cloud-mouth-probe`, `official-codex-mouth-probe` and their fixtures) are gone; the folder-v2 cases C-R2 added stay, and the sign-in cases now also name the model-unavailable failures. No SQL stops being called: `qorpera_send_native_agent_message`, which the host's own mouth sent through, is shared and still called through the company runtime's sends.
- (legacy sweep step C, C-R4; no SQL) `CloudAgentMouth` has no MCP server: `start`, its loopback HTTP endpoint (`initialize`, `ping`, `tools/list` and `tools/call`), its bearer token, `CloudAgentMouthConnection` and `diagnosticState` (nothing reads it, and without the server only its `uninitialized` and `delivered` states were left) are gone. Only the legacy harness reached the mouth over MCP. The company runtime keeps `send`, `finish` and `close`, unchanged in what they do: `close` refuses every send that has not started and waits for the one in flight. `CLOUD_AGENT_SEND_INSTRUCTIONS`, the send parsers and the tool catalog are byte for byte as before. The native boundary probe (`test/fixtures/official-codex-boundary-probe.ts`) delivers its `send_message` through `CloudAgentMouth.send` in process, as a company run does, and keeps every fence check and its proof fields; `run-official-codex-boundary-probe.ts` is unchanged. `cloud-agent-mouth.test.ts` loses its server cases and runs its send, finish and close cases in process. No SQL stops being called: `qorpera_send_native_agent_message` is still called through the company runtime's sends (`cloud-native-provider-service.ts`).
- The native host requires `qorpera.native-context-composition/v1` (agent context tree W2a; design `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-AGENT-MEMORY-TREE.md` §6.3; `cloud-native-context-composition.ts`) and refuses a Codex without it before model admission. Before each provider request Codex sends a manifest of the request's items (kind, role, call id, provider id, size; never content) on the result fence's socket. The host answers with Codex's items kept by position and at most one `qorpera.run-work/v1` work message (at most 256 KiB). The host also requires `qorpera.native-prompt-cache-key/v1` (design §6.2), a marker of its own: the answer may set the request's prompt cache key to `qorpera:` and a SHA-256 in unpadded base64url, 51 characters within the provider's 64 (the hex form, 72 characters, would fail the request and is refused). The request is bound to the run's thread and turn (or a reviewer's) before and after it is composed, and its identity shares the fence's replay window. A run's `composeContext` (`runCloudCodexToolSession` option, `CloudCodexContextComposer`) composes the agent's requests; with none, and always for a reviewer's, every item is kept. The fence now takes eight connections at once (it was four). The patch's Codex check refuses a dropped instruction or user message, a call split from its output, a fold that is not the request's earlier work, a misplaced work message, and kept host-written text that passes for one (the model's own text may echo an envelope); on a resumed run the work message may stand with nothing folded.
- The Podman sandbox backend is gone (legacy sweep step C, C-R5; no SQL): `cloud-runner-podman.ts` (the `@qorpera/runtime/cloud-runner-podman` subpath) with `RootlessPodmanSandboxBackend`, `RootlessPodmanSandboxBackendOptions`, `NodePodmanCommandTransport` and the `PodmanCommandResult`, `PodmanCommandProcess`, `PodmanCommandLimits` and `PodmanCommandTransport` types, its unit tests, and its real Linux containment test (`cloud-runner-podman-linux.integration.test.ts`), which `scripts/test-ephemeral-runner-linux.sh` no longer runs. It ran the whole-tree sandbox lane, which served only snapshot-v1, and nothing has built it since C-R1; folder-v2 runs never used it (a folder-v2 job runs through `executeNativeCompanyRunnerJob` in the runner guest, unchanged). No SQL stops being called: the backend made no database or broker call of its own, and the model requests it relayed from the container reached the `runner-broker` `model` action, which the runner stopped calling in C-R1. `cloud-runner-container.ts`, `cloud-runner-container-main.ts` (the `qorpera-sandbox-probe` entrypoint) and both halves of `cloud-runner-model-socket.ts` stay until the runner image drops the probe (D-1). The runtime bundle manifest drops the file at the next release cut.
- `HttpNativeCompanyObjectTransport` (`cloud-native-company-objects.ts`) is gone (legacy removal, step C; migration `20260930190000`): the object gateway's `/v1/native-company-objects` served only snapshot-v1 companies. So are `createHttpCompanyComputerScreenshots`, `parseCompanyComputerCaptureGateway`, `createHttpCompanyComputerFileImports`, `createHttpCompanyComputerFileExports`, the `objectGateway` option of `createHttpCompanyComputerSessionFactory` and the `captureObjectGateway` option of `startCompanyComputerLinuxOfficeService`. `CloudCompanyComputerScreenshots` takes no `objects`: it publishes a capture only when the capture authority answers `storage: "none"` and the receipt names the capture, and reads it only from the pixels its perform answer brought, once. The native host always builds it for a computer session. The office offers no file import or export until the folder carries them. The office service no longer reads the `qorpera-office-objects` credential; `captureObjectGatewayUrl` stays in its configuration, unused, until the orchestrator stops writing it. The native host's object store refuses a snapshot-v1 path's bytes. Rides the next Company Computer image rebuild; the runtime bundle manifest drops the file at the next release cut.
- The runner broker client has no Company File revision or output publication lanes (legacy sweep step C, C-R6; no SQL): they served only snapshot-v1, and a folder-v2 run publishes through folder commits. `cloud-runner-broker.ts` loses `CloudRunnerBrokerCompanyFilePublisher`, `CloudRunnerBrokerOutputPublisher`, the types `RegisterCloudRunnerArtifactInput`, `PrepareCloudRunnerCompanyRevisionInput`, `BeginCloudRunnerCompanyRevisionGraphInput`, `AppendCloudRunnerCompanyRevisionGraphInput`, `CloudRunnerCompanyRevisionGraph` and `CloudRunnerCompanyRevisionVerification`, and the members `getObject`, `putObject`, `registerArtifact`, `prepareCompanyRevision`, `beginCompanyRevisionGraph`, `appendCompanyRevisionGraph`, `completeCompanyRevisionGraph` and `verifyCompanyRevision` of `CloudRunnerBrokerTransport` (and `SupabaseCloudRunnerBrokerTransport`, which no longer sends `x-qorpera-object-sha256`). `CloudRunnerJobBroker` loses the same methods except the object ones, plus `profileId`, `readObject`, `writeObject` and its `objects` and `companyFileObjects` stores. The sandbox lane's publisher interfaces in `cloud-runner-sandbox.ts` stay until C-R7, and the substrate reads, `describe`, the native authority and `parseArtifact` (read by `listArtifacts`) stay until C-R8. The runtime client therefore has no methods for the `runner-broker` Edge actions `register_artifact`, `prepare_company_revision`, `begin_company_revision_graph`, `append_company_revision_graph`, `complete_company_revision_graph` and `verify_company_revision`, or its object GET and PUT, nor for the SQL behind them: `qorpera_runner_broker_register_artifact`, `_authorize_input_object`, `_authorize_output_object`, `_authorize_company_file_object`, `_confirm_output_object`, `qorpera_runner_prepare_company_revision`, `qorpera_runner_begin/append/complete_company_revision_graph` and `qorpera_runner_authorize/finalize_company_revision_verification`. The runner stopped calling them in C-R1; the Edge actions and the SQL go in step D/E.
- The runner sandbox has no whole-tree execution lane (legacy sweep step C, C-R7; no SQL): it served only snapshot-v1, and nothing has called it since C-R1 (a folder-v2 job runs through `executeNativeCompanyRunnerJob`, unchanged). `cloud-runner-sandbox.ts` loses `executeCloudRunnerJob`, with its input materialization (the Company File tree copy and its ephemeral Git repository, the operation journal, the artifacts, the queued inputs and their images, the canonical transcript), its output collection and its wall-time watch, and the types `ExecuteCloudRunnerJobOptions`, `CloudRunnerSandboxInput`, `CloudRunnerSandboxRequest`, `CloudRunnerSandboxBackend`, `CloudRunnerSubstrateResolver`, `ResolvedCloudRunnerExecutionRun`, `ResolvedCloudRunnerExecutionSponsor`, `ResolvedCloudRunnerExecutionContext`, `CloudRunnerCanonicalTranscriptEvent`, `CloudRunnerQueuedInput` (the broker's own type of that name stays until C-R8), `CloudRunnerOutputPublicationInput`, `CloudRunnerOutputPublisher`, `CloudRunnerCompanyFilePublicationInput` and `CloudRunnerCompanyFilePublisher`. `CloudRunnerJobBroker` no longer `implements CloudRunnerSubstrateResolver`; its substrate reads stay until C-R8. The module keeps `CloudRunnerSandboxLimits`, `DEFAULT_CLOUD_RUNNER_SANDBOX_LIMITS`, `resolveCloudRunnerSandboxLimits`, `CloudRunnerSandboxGuarantees`, `CloudRunnerSandboxProcess`, `CloudRunnerOutputFile` and `CloudRunnerExecutionResult` (unchanged, `outputSnapshotId` included), which the native company lane, the worker, the runner service and the native provider bridge use. Its test file goes too; the native company runner's test now pins the default limits a run records and refuses an out-of-range limit before the bridge starts. The runtime calls no new or fewer SQL: the runner stopped reaching the whole-tree broker reads and the object store in C-R1.
- The runner broker client describes only a folder-v2 run (legacy sweep step C, C-R8; no SQL): its substrate reads and the whole-tree description served only snapshot-v1, and nothing outside the broker has called them since C-R1 (`beginOperation`, `completeOperation` and `appendTranscriptEvent` had no caller at all). `CloudRunnerJobBroker` loses `listInputs`, `listTranscript`, `listOperations`, `listArtifacts`, `resolve`, `resolveExecution`, `requestModel`, `beginOperation`, `completeOperation`, `bindProviderSession` and `appendTranscriptEvent`; `CloudRunnerBrokerTransport` and `SupabaseCloudRunnerBrokerTransport` lose the same members except `resolve` and `resolveExecution`. Gone with them: the parsers `parseVersion`, `parseSnapshot`, `parseArtifactIndex`, `parseOperation`, `parseOperationReceipt`, `parseProviderSessionBinding`, `parseTranscriptEvent`, `parseTranscriptContextEvent`, `parseArtifact` and `substrateOf`; the types `CloudRunnerQueuedInput`, `BindCloudRunnerProviderSessionInput`, `AppendCloudRunnerTranscriptEventInput`, `CloudRunnerOperationReceipt`, `CloudRunnerProviderSessionBinding`, `CloudRunnerTranscriptEvent` and `CloudRunnerTranscriptContextEvent`; the description's `version`, `snapshot`, `proposalSnapshot`, `artifactIndex` and `companyFileOutputLimits`, and `describe()`'s substrate checks; and the module's imports of `@qorpera/cloud-client/cloud-runner-model`, `@qorpera/cloud-client/hosted-primary` and `@qorpera/cloud-client/node/cloud-company-substrate`. `parseCloudRunnerBrokerDescription` accepts only the folder-v2 shape: `companyRuntime` is `company-path-v1`, with no substrate anywhere. A `whole-tree-v1` description is refused as an invalid Company File runtime selection. A version, snapshot, proposal snapshot, artifact index or Company File output limits, or a substrate version on the job or run, is refused ("Runner broker returned a run with a Company File substrate"). A proposal review is still refused as another job substrate. Every folder-v2 field parses to the same value as before. The runner worker's own refusal of a whole-tree run (`cloud-runner-worker.ts`) is gone with it, since no such description reaches the worker; it still refuses a run that is not native. Kept: `describe`, `acquireNativeAuthority`, `renewNativeAuthority` and `releaseNativeAuthority`, `#post` and `#transientFetch` (the native authority's actions keep their 20-second budget), the job, run, sponsor and native authority parsers, `CloudRunnerNativeAuthority`, `CloudRunnerStart`, `NATIVE_AUTHORITY_LEASE_SECONDS`, `BeginCloudRunnerOperationInput` and `CompleteCloudRunnerOperationInput` (the native operation receipts use them), and the `company_substrate` job capability, which the runner protocol's capability list, the routines and the native subscription control share. The runtime client therefore has no methods for the `runner-broker` Edge actions `inputs`, `transcript`, `operations`, `artifacts`, `model`, `begin_operation`, `complete_operation`, `bind_provider_session` and `append_transcript`, nor for the SQL behind them: `qorpera_runner_broker_list_inputs`, `_list_transcript`, `_list_operations`, `_list_artifacts`, `_reserve_model_request` and `_finalize_model_request`, `qorpera_begin_run_operation_receipt`, `qorpera_complete_run_operation_receipt`, `qorpera_bind_execution_provider_session` and `qorpera_append_runner_transcript_event`. Their only caller left is the `runner-broker` Edge function; it and the SQL are trimmed in step D/E. The broker test keeps its folder-v2 security checks (leased-job binding, credential refresh and placement, identity refusals, aborts, retries and incident budgets) over `describe` and the native authority, and refuses a description naming a substrate; its whole-tree, model, receipt and transcript cases are gone.
- The office's file transfer classes are gone (legacy sweep step C, C-F1; no SQL change): `CloudCompanyComputerFileImports` (`cloud-company-computer-file-imports.ts`), `CloudCompanyComputerFileExports` (`cloud-company-computer-file-transfers.ts`) and `readCompanyFileTransfer` (`cloud-company-file-transfer-reader.ts`). They moved snapshot-v1 artifact assets and Company File path manifests through company objects, and nothing has wired them since those went. The `fileImports` and `fileExports` options of `CompanyComputerGuestAutomation` and `CompanyComputerLinuxGuestOptions` are gone too, and so are `qorpera_native_office_file_import` and `qorpera_native_office_file_export` in the office registry's closed method list: the runtime no longer calls either (the SQL stays until step D). Folder-v2 has no file transfer yet. `computer_transfer` keeps its schema, so the tool catalog is unchanged, but `HttpCompanyComputerNativeEndpoint.authorize` now refuses every transfer with a `CloudToolAuthorizeRefusal` ("File transfer is not available on this computer yet."), which the model reads as the tool's answer, before the database or any receipt. Before, an export opened a receipt that the guest then failed with a plain error: the run's browser window closed, the model saw `OFFICE_NATIVE_UNCONFIRMED`, and the open receipt fenced the run's later operations. The guest still answers a transfer that reaches it with `OFFICE_FILE_IMPORT_UNAVAILABLE` or `OFFICE_FILE_EXPORT_UNAVAILABLE`. The physical workbench reader and writer (`cloud-company-computer-file-reader.ts`, `cloud-company-computer-file-writer.ts`, `CompanyComputerLinuxCommands.readFile` and `writeFile`) stay for a folder-carried transfer. Rides the next Company Computer image rebuild and runtime bundle; the runtime bundle manifest drops the three files at the next release cut.
- A Company Computer's computers and browser leave by the machine's agent card, through the node's agent exit (E6, guest half; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-AGENT-EXIT.md` §6; needs the office image that carries it, and merges with #925): a computer's `resolv.conf` (`COMPANY_COMPUTER_RESOLV_CONF`) names only the exit's name server `10.210.0.1` (`COMPANY_COMPUTER_AGENT_EXIT_DNS`) instead of 1.1.1.1 and 8.8.8.8. `startCompanyComputerBrowserEgress` without a `transport` resolves through the exit's name server and connects from the card's address (`companyComputerAgentPlugAddress`, the one IPv4 address of `qagent` in `10.210.0.0/16`) and fails with `OFFICE_BROWSER_EGRESS_UNAVAILABLE` on a machine without it. The managed browser policy sets `EncryptedClientHelloEnabled: false`, so the exit sees each TLS connection's server name.
- A native host's coding and machine commands prove its run's lease (G2-6b, no SQL; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-NATIVE-HOST-KEY.md` "G2-6"). `createHttpCloudCodingCommandGateway` and `createHttpCloudMachineCommandGateway` take a `holder` (the run's, read per request, or `null` for an executor forwarding to its peer) and send `x-qorpera-identity` when it is given. `HttpCloudCodingCommandService` takes a required `proveBinding`: on the guest-facing listener it proves the request's holder against its binding before either route's authorization, and refuses a missing, malformed or unproven holder with the plain 403; `null` only on the executors' peer listener. `isNativeJobHolder` (`cloud-native-tool-authority.ts`) is the one holder check the brokers and the executor share.
- A native host's requests to the secret brokers and the object gateway's folder route prove its run's lease (G2-6a; migration `20260930152000`, SQL test 986; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-NATIVE-HOST-KEY.md` "G2-6"). `HttpCloudSecretBrokerClient`, `issueModelLogin` and `HttpCloudCompanyFolderBytes` take a `holder` function and send `x-qorpera-identity: job.<job id>.<lease token>` with each request (`NativeHostDispatch.holderHeader()`, read per request); `HttpNativeCompanyObjectTransport` and the Company Computer's capture gateway take an optional one. `HttpCloudSecretBrokerService` takes `proveBinding`, asked before `/v1/operations`, `/v1/company-accounts`, `/v1/company-account-fills` and `/v1/model-logins` serve a binding; without it those routes refuse. The broker service and the shared vault ask `qorpera_native_binding_proven` through their own database principal.
- The native host and the guest bootstrap hold no service key (G2-5; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-NATIVE-HOST-KEY.md` "G2-5"): the bootstrap's exact credential lists no longer name `qorpera-supabase-service-key`, so a job, company or login guest given it is refused; the native host refuses to start with it (a credential or `QORPERA_SUPABASE_SERVICE_KEY`); `NativeHostDispatch` takes no `serviceKey` (it accepts only a publishable key, as before); the control plane, path, coding and computer registry clients send the publishable key, and the dispatch signs every request. The three guest native host units no longer load the key.
- Every native company run has a head (agent memory tree W3; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-AGENT-MEMORY-TREE.md` §5.6, §6.1): one line of escaped, labelled data, `qorpera.agent-head/v1`, in the developer instructions after the core protocol, the binding and the agent kind's behaviour. It holds `vision/vision.md` (its text, with its version), the job specification (the body of the agent's `agent.md`, with the `staffVersion` it is at) and the profile page's facts (with the page's version), each whole, with its path and trust label (`company_instructions_not_authority`, `job_specification_not_authority`, `own_notebook_not_authority`), within 16,000 bytes; a part that does not fit is left out whole and named in `omitted` with its part, path and size (`cloud-agent-head.ts`). The head is the same for every member, model and payer until one of its files changes, so an agent's runs share it as a cached prefix: what varies by wake (the standing-work rules, the wake's protocol, continuation rules and the closing reminder) now follows it. `compileCloudNativeCompanyInstructions` takes `parts` (`{head, views?}`); the views slot, after the head, is for M1; empty views add nothing. The context message leaves out what the head shows: `hireOverlay.jobSpecification`, and the profile page's facts (`memory.profile` becomes `{path, version, inHead: true}`). The run's context hash and bytes (`p_context_sha256`, `p_context_bytes`, and the folder release's `p_output_sha256`) cover the head, the views (none yet) and the context message (`${head}\n${views}\n${prompt}`), and the vision read is released with the run's other reads. Composition fails when the vision cannot be read; a vision a trusted read cannot hold as text is named by the size that read found (`CloudCompanyPathNotText` carries `bytes` from a trusted whole-file read). The head's protocol says only the vision part is its file's text, and that a change to any of these files starts from a fresh read (the profile page through `update_memory`). The runtime instructions may reach 256 KiB.
- The todo, worker and report tools save only through the folder writers (legacy sweep step C, C-P2; no SQL). `cloudWorkerTools(binding, identity, wake, { machines?, folderTodos, folderWorkers })` takes no path control or gateway, and both writers are required: `manage_todo` lists and saves through `CloudFolderTodos`, `manage_worker` and a worker's `report` through `CloudFolderWorkers`, as every company run already did since C-P1. Gone with the kernel branch: its calls, the todo document's publication to the Company File through the gateway and its confirmation, and the kernel page fitted to one tool result (`fittedPage`; the folder writers fit their own pages with `fitsOneResult`). `manage_todo` and `manage_worker` always carry the folder descriptions, so the tool catalog is unchanged. `describeCloudAgentOpenWork` is unchanged. The runtime no longer calls `qorpera_manage_agent_todo`, `qorpera_manage_agent_worker`, `qorpera_report_worker_result` or `qorpera_confirm_agent_todo_publication`; no caller is left in `packages/` or `scripts/`. The names stay in the native host's call list (`NATIVE_HOST_CALLS`) until step D, and in the path gateway's `CloudCompanyPathRpc` union and `HttpCloudCompanyPathControl`'s closed list until C-P5 (the gateway file takes one step C PR at a time).
- The routine, routine decision and sidebar tools save only through the folder writers (legacy sweep step C, C-P3; no SQL). `cloudRoutineTools(binding, identity, ownDynamicRoutine, folderRoutines)`, `cloudRoutineDecisionTool(binding, identity, { decisionOwed, decided, folderDecision })` and `cloudSidebarTools(binding, identity, { folderSidebar })` take no path control, and each writer is required: `manage_routine` lists and saves through `CloudFolderRoutines`, `decide_routine` decides through `CloudFolderRoutineDecision`, and `organize_sidebar` saves through `CloudFolderSidebar`, as every company run already did since C-P1. Gone with the kernel branches: their calls and the checks of the kernel's receipts. The three tools always carry the folder descriptions and `manage_routine` the folder schema (the file name rule for `routineKey`, and `cursor`), so the tool catalog is unchanged. A dynamic routine's run is offered `decide_routine` exactly as before (the wake bus gives a routine its key exactly when it is dynamic), now always with its folder decision. `CLOUD_ROUTINE_TOOL_DESCRIPTION`, `CLOUD_ROUTINE_DECISION_DESCRIPTION` and `CLOUD_SIDEBAR_TOOL_DESCRIPTION` stay, as the bases of the folder descriptions. The runtime no longer calls `qorpera_manage_agent_routine`, `qorpera_decide_agent_routine` or `qorpera_manage_agent_sidebar`; no caller is left in `packages/` or `scripts/`. The names stay in the native host's call list (`NATIVE_HOST_CALLS`) until step D, and in the path gateway's `CloudCompanyPathRpc` union and `HttpCloudCompanyPathControl`'s closed list until C-P5.
- Agents' machines follow one idle rule for every machine (2026-10-03 amendment, items 1, 3 and 7; migration `20260930055000`, SQL test 873; folder-v2 only; the coordinator chose the whole machine as what is measured and one snapshot at a stop, 4 October 2026). A task machine sleeps after the node's idle delay with no agent use and no busy machine: a running command no longer keeps it awake. The guest measures the whole machine apart from its own control (the watchdog, which now runs in `qorpera-control.slice`, and the LXD agent the executor's calls run under): its processor time from `/proc/stat` less those units' cgroups and the commands' relays' own time (utime + stime while they run, and each relay's own final time, which it appends to `/run/qorpera-machine/relays-ended.log` as it ends, also when systemd stops it; kept as a per-boot counter in `relays.json`, so a relay's work is never the machine's and nothing jumps when one ends), and the bytes its other processes read and wrote (`/proc/<pid>/io`, the relays aside; `idleRuleProcessIoBytes` in `@qorpera/contracts` idle-rule, where the shell's `companyComputerProcessIoBytes` moved). The observation is `qorpera.machine-observation/v2` (`at`, `cpuUsec`, `ioBytes`, `ended`); the executor calls the machine busy by `idleRuleBusy` between two looks (5% of one core, or 512 KiB in 30 seconds), keeps that owed until SQL took it, and records it as before (`p_busy`). Before a sleep it takes one last look. Ended commands' output tails share 16 KiB of the observation as JSON writes them (2000 bytes each at most), so the reply stays under the executor's 64 KiB. At a stop, once SQL granted it, the helper's new `stop-commands` lists what runs right before stopping the programs, Docker and containerd: this boot's running commands (at most 32, oldest first) and whether Docker ran containers (`qorpera.machine-commands-stopped/v1`). The executor records that snapshot (`qorpera_record_company_machine_stop`) before the power-off: those commands end with the cause the stop custody kept (`stop_cause`), and stopped containers are told once per machine and boot. A stop whose power-off fails is over; the next is a new stop with its own snapshot. A command a stop or restart ends says why (`slept`, `hours`, `stopped`, `lost`), and the agent's next run in that command's conversation (a worker's: the dispatching one) is told once in its open work (`machineStopped`, with `work` `command`, or `containers` for a machine it started, told where it started it: `started_conversation_run_id`). `machine_start` and `machine_run` say that only a busy program keeps a task machine awake and that always-on keeps a server up.
- The coding guest's observation is `qorpera.coding-guest-observation/v3`: each session also reports `ioBytes`, the bytes its slice's processes read and wrote so far (`/proc/<pid>/io` rchar + wchar, one pass over `/proc`; `idleRuleProcessIoBytes` in `@qorpera/contracts` idle-rule), with the command relays' own bytes left out (the command guest's `execute` step, which only copies its command's output to the log). The one idle rule's data signal for coding sessions (2026-10-03 amendment, item 1). The relay's processor time stays in the session's share, as before (the coordinator's decision, 5 October 2026).
- A folder-v2 file wake's payload is `qorpera.folder-file-wake/v1` (`schema`, `seq`, `commitId` equal to the wake's source id, at most 21 `changes` of `{path, op, version, priorVersion?, from?}` with `from` only on a `move_in`, and `more` up to 1000), parsed as closed (folder core C4, slice P29b). The snapshot-v1 file wake shape is no longer accepted. The prompt names `wake.payload.changes` and `more`, and the first presence is the first change's path.
- The native host makes every database call through an entry point, with no switch (G2-3bc; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-NATIVE-HOST-KEY.md` "G2-3bc"): a job's calls with the job's lease token as G2-2 built them (`QORPERA_NATIVE_HOST_DISPATCH`, `NATIVE_HOST_DISPATCH_DEFAULT` and `nativeHostDispatchMode` are gone), the eight calls it makes between jobs through `qorpera_native_identity_call` with the holder's identity credential (`NATIVE_HOST_IDENTITY_CALLS` replaces `NATIVE_HOST_KEY_CALLS`), and any other call on the project's origin but Storage is refused; an entry point's arguments must be one JSON object. Storage and Realtime keep the key until G2-4. Every native host requires the systemd credential `qorpera-native-identity` (`qorpera.native-identity-credential/v1`); every guest bootstrap's credential list names it and checks it is the guest identity's and independent. A login guest also requires `qorpera-native-identity-state` (its identity's recovery state, read by its gateway) and `QORPERA_NATIVE_LOGIN_GUEST` (`sign_in` or `account_refresh`), and a sign-in's host runs no account loop. An execution reaching an exec slot may carry `native_host.identity_credential`, which replaces the host's for that run; the runner sends its configuration's `native_identity_credential` (a new runner configuration key, with the native bridge only).
- Coding sessions stand by between turns: the guest configuration is `qorpera.coding-command-guest/v4` (its resource profile names only `maxLogBytes`), command requests are `qorpera.coding-command-request/v2` (`timeoutMs` may be null), and guest leases are `qorpera.coding-guest-lease/v2`, one generation per session attachment with the current turn and whether it is open. A new turn no longer stops the session's commands; a new attachment does. The turn context is `qorpera.coding-turn-context/v2` with `commands` (still running, stopped since the previous turn).
- The unreleased office unit now loads credentials from the current boot's immutable directory. Previously acknowledged installations without those files are refused; replay does not backfill missing credentials from the original installation.
- The platform's folders are under `QorperaOS/` (Jonas, 21 October 2026; needs migration `20260930534000` and a recreated company, no data is moved): `QorperaOS/vision/vision.md`, `QorperaOS/agents/` (with `groups/` and `sections.md`), `QorperaOS/rules/`, `QorperaOS/connections/`, `QorperaOS/skills/`, `QorperaOS/contacts/`, `QorperaOS/human-tasks/`, `QorperaOS/interfaces/` and `QorperaOS/tables/`, while `code/` and the company's own work stay at the top. Every run's instructions list them as `QorperaOS/<folder>/` with `code/` beside it (`CLOUD_COMPANY_TOP_FOLDERS` holds the full paths), the head and the first-run wake name `QorperaOS/vision/vision.md`, and the Co-Founder's role overlay is `qorpera.hire-overlay/v7` (the 2026-10-22 amendment's v6 text with the vision, `agents/`, `sections.md` and `human-tasks/` under `QorperaOS/`). The action reviewer's mandate names the vision `QorperaOS/vision/vision.md`. Every run's folder rules add that the platform writes `digital-post/`, `bank/`, `members/` and `hooks/`, which stay at the top (Jonas, 24 October 2026): agents read them but never put their own files there. The context names each agent's folder `QorperaOS/agents/<handle>`, and a member's chat image is accepted only under `QorperaOS/agents/`. `CLOUD_AGENT_VISION_PATH`, `CLOUD_COMPANY_SKILLS_ROOT` and the folder writers (hires, group chats, sidebar sections, todos, workers, routines, routine decisions, table rows) use the cloud client's `osPath`; the descriptions of `table_read`, `table_write`, `organize_sidebar`, `hire_agent` and `create_group_chat` name the new paths. A worker reports only into an `outputFolder` spelled `QorperaOS/agents/<handle>/todos/<todo>/<worker>`; a run log's place (spelled `QorperaOS/...` by the database, either spelling accepted), a human task wake's path, and the interface change notes of `company_*` writes and applied proposals are read below the root with `osRelative`.

### Removed

- Exports and files that only deleted tests still used are gone (18 Nov 2026, #1952; nothing in production, deploy or
  scripts called them):
  - `cloud-company-accounts`: `parseCompanyAccountOtpauth` and `parseCompanyAccountRecoveryCodes`.
  - `cloud-company-computer-leases`: `CompanyComputerScreenActionGate.replaceBinding`, and with it the
    `SCREEN_REPLACEMENT_PENDING` refusal. A fenced slot still refuses a lease or a fence with another binding.
  - `cloud-company-computer-screens`: `CloudCompanyComputerScreenManager`. `companyComputerScreenDisplay`,
    `waitForCompanyComputerScreenReady`, `CompanyComputerScreenLauncher` and `CompanyComputerScreenSessionHandle` stay.
  - `cloud-company-computer-state` (the whole file, the `@qorpera/runtime/cloud-company-computer-state` subpath):
    `assertCompanyComputerScreenRegistry`, `transitionCompanyComputerScreens`, `companyComputerScreenBinding`,
    `companyComputerScreenLease`, `companyComputerPowerIntent` and the `CompanyComputerScreenState`,
    `CompanyComputerScreenRegistry`, `CompanyComputerScreenCommand` and `CompanyComputerScreenTransition` types.
  - `cloud-runner-model-socket`: `startCloudRunnerModelSocketServer`, `StartCloudRunnerModelSocketServerOptions`,
    `CloudRunnerModelSocketServer`, `CloudRunnerModelSocketFactory` and `CloudRunnerModelBroker`. The container's client
    `requestCloudRunnerModelSocket` and the frame schemas stay.
  - `cloud-runtime-constitution`: `CLOUD_RUNTIME_CONSTITUTION_VERSION`.
  - `cloud-secret-broker-http`: `captureCloudSecret`. The web posts a capture to the broker's route itself.
  - `cloud-secret-broker`: `SupabaseCloudSecretBrokerAuthority` (production builds `PostgresCloudSecretBrokerAuthority`);
    `parseCloudSecretAccountLabel` is no longer exported.
  - `cloud-wake-bus`: `CloudNativeWakeBus` and `HttpCloudWakeControl`. `cloudCoworkerMessageTool` and
    `parseCloudWakeEnvelope` stay.

### Added

- Listener routines (L1; migration `20260930565000`, SQL tests 565; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-LISTENER-ROUTINES.md`): `manage_routine` takes the trigger `when_done` (`path`, a todo or human task file, `.md` optional; `latestAt` with its offset; `timeZone`; `report`). It compiles to a dynamic routine whose file has `after: <path>`: its run comes as soon as nothing open is left at the path (done, cancelled, declined or deleted), or at `latestAt`, and `wake.payload.done` says which. `decide_routine next` takes `whenDone` to wait again; a next run without it drops `after`. The review card reads "When the todo “Landing page” of product-builder is done, at the latest …", and a computer's proposal names it the same way. Standing work tells agents to use a listener instead of a guessed check time, and which follow-ups already wake them (their workers, coworkers' replies, the human tasks they asked for).
- A member's Claude sign-in runs in the shared vault's helper, as a Codex sign-in does (migration `20260930564000`, SQL test 564; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-LOGIN-VAULT.md` "Claude sign-in in the vault"). The helper runs the pinned official Claude Code (`claude auth login --claudeai`) in its isolation with Anthropic's sign-in hosts alone, the member pastes Anthropic's code into Qorpera's page, and the vault writes it once to Claude Code's input (`CloudModelLoginSignIns.submitClaudeCode`, broker route `/v1/model-logins/<company>/claude/code`, helper route `/v1/sign-in/code`). Claude Code's login files are sealed unread under the member's key into `qorpera_claude_logins`: no provider connection, capsule or sponsor, so no agent ever uses a Claude login. Only members on `qorpera_claude_sign_in_members` may begin one (Anthropic's written OK is still owed); a Claude sign-in never takes a login machine (until now it always failed there: `COMPUTE_IDENTITY_OCCUPIED` while the member's agents ran). The helper's `config.json` takes optional `claudePath` and `claudeSha256`.
- Digital Post letters, part 5: PDF text (`deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-DIGITAL-POST.md`, "PDF text"). A letter whose main document has no text or HTML version has its PDF's text read before it is laid out, and `letter.md` gives it under "From <file>:", with the summary's `text` and `snippet`. `digital-post-pdf-text.ts`: `digitalPostPdfToRead` picks the main document's PDF (by media type or its `%PDF-` header) when the letter has no text of its own; `DigitalPostPdfText` sends it to `/run/qorpera-pdf-text/socket`, where systemd runs one pdftotext per connection in its own sandbox (`deploy/cloud-secret-broker/qorpera-pdf-text.socket`, `qorpera-pdf-text@.service`: a throwaway user, no network, nothing to write, 256 MiB, 30 s), closes its side and reads at most 256 KiB of text in 40 s. It sends a one-time code first; the service's shell writes it back after the text only when pdftotext exited cleanly, and text without it is never kept. pdftotext reads 201 pages, and `digitalPostPdfTextOf` keeps 200 (`DIGITAL_POST_PDF_TEXT_MAX_PAGES`), turns page breaks into blank lines, drops control characters and says where the text was cut. No text, a failure, a timeout or no service keeps the letter as before, with a fixed log phrase (a missing service once). `layOutDigitalPostLetter`'s `extracted` is now `{text, from}`, the file the text came from. `CloudDigitalPostSyncs` takes `pdfText`; `composeCloudConnectorService` answers `digitalPostPdfText` with `digitalPost`, and both the shared vault and a single-company broker pass it on. `deploy/cloud-secret-broker/test/prove-pdf-text.mjs` proves the service on a host (`--transient` on a test machine).
- The database's CA can be our own, as an explicit deploy input (a self-hosted database, later): `database-ca.ts` gives the secret broker, the shared vault and the agent exit's rules reader their TLS options. By default nothing changes: Supabase's CA, by its pinned fingerprint. With two more credentials, the own CA file and its sha256 pin, always together, the service trusts that CA alone, only when the file is one PEM CA certificate whose sha256 is the pin; half the pair or a pin the file does not match stops the service, and `verify-full` stays required. `scripts/package-cloud-secret-broker.mjs` and `deploy/agent-exit/install-agent-exit.sh` take the pair as `--database-ca` and `--database-ca-sha256` and refuse the same.
- Digital Post letters, part 2: the company vault fetches each connected company's Digital Post (migration `20260930529000`, SQL tests 786 and 306; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-DIGITAL-POST.md`, "The path of a letter"). The connector service configuration takes `digitalPost: {environment, certificatePem, privateKeyPem}` (Qorpera's OCES3 certificate; it may be the only entry, and `parseDigitalPostApp` checks the key against the certificate), and `composeCloudConnectorService` answers `digitalPost`, the client. `cloud-digital-post-sync.ts`: `CloudDigitalPostSyncs.pass` claims the company's due sync (`qorpera.digital-post-sync-claim/v1`, with the page to start at), opens the sealed `api_key` only for the pass, lists the waiting letters, and for each one not held fetches it, reads and lays it out, keeps it (`keepDigitalPostLetter`: its summary and its files' names, media types, sizes and SHA-256), stages each file in 1 MiB chunks, seals it, and only then sends the receipt; a letter already kept is receipted again without staging, a letter it cannot read, or that Digital Post answers larger than Qorpera keeps (`DigitalPostAnswerTooLarge`, now the transport's size refusal), is held, never receipted. Held letters stay on Digital Post's list, so a pass reads on page by page (`available` takes a zero-based page) from the page the claim gives (`scanPage`, where the last pass stopped), for at most 20 pages: it stops after a page where it receipted something (`more`, from that page, since a receipt shifts the pages), after its 20 pages (`more`, from the next page), or at the end of the list (`current`, from the first page again), so every letter is reached however many are held before it; the vault asks for each page which of its letters are held (`heldDigitalPostLetters`, `qorpera_digital_post_held_among`, complete however many are held), and a pass starts no new page or letter after six minutes (`more`, from the page it is on; its deadline is thirteen minutes, within the claim's fifteen-minute lease), so a slow Digital Post costs passes, never progress. `finishDigitalPostSync` carries `scanPage`. It finishes `current`, `more` (a full list from which it took a letter), `failed` (a fixed phrase) or `refused` (Digital Post refused the key, or the sealed value is not a key). `CloudDigitalPostSyncUpkeep` runs it once a minute in a single-company broker; the shared vault's `syncDigitalPost` asks `qorpera_broker_principal_digital_post_syncs_due` once a minute and passes each due company in turn (`PostgresCloudSecretBrokerPrincipal.digitalPostSyncsDue`). `PostgresCloudSecretBrokerAuthority` implements `DigitalPostSyncAuthority`. The broker's capture seals a `digital_post` secret only as exactly `api_key` reading as a Digital Post key, and labels it with the system id.
- Digital Post letters, part 1 (inert; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-DIGITAL-POST.md`): `digital-post-memo.ts` reads a letter as Digital Post delivers it (MeMo 1.1/1.2 XML, at most 32 MiB) with a strict reader of its own (no document type, no entities beyond the five predefined and character references, UTF-8 only, at most 64 levels and 50,000 elements) and reduces it to `DigitalPostLetter`: the message id, title, notice, reply, mandatory and legal-effect flags, sender, recipient, case id, documents (main, additional, technical) with their files' bytes and links, and `record`, every element as JSON with each file's content replaced by its size and SHA-256. `digitalPostLetterText` takes the main document's text from its plain-text version, else its HTML version (`htmlLetterText`); a PDF alone has none yet. `digital-post-letter-files.ts` lays a letter out for the company folder: `digital-post/<year>/<yyyy-mm-dd> <sender> - <title> (<message id>)` (the sender's day in Danish time) with `letter.md` (who, when, flags, documents, links, text, and that it is outside content), `letter.json` (the record) and the documents under safe, distinct names, plus the summary the folder's index keeps. `cloud-connector-digital-post.ts` is the vault's client for a company's recipient system: `parseDigitalPostKey` reads the API key as Administrativ Adgang shows it, `parseDigitalPostApp` checks Qorpera's OCES3 certificate and key once, and `DigitalPostClient` lists the waiting letters (`available`, at most 50 a call), fetches one (`letter`, null when gone) and receipts it (`receipt`, a 404 counts as settled) over mutual TLS with the company's key, telling a refused key (`DigitalPostKeyRefused`) apart from every other refusal. Nothing calls them yet.
- Agents edit and move files (the contract's 2026-10-21 amendment "how agents start work and use the company folder", item 5; migration `20260930525000`, SQL test 547). `company_edit` replaces `oldText`, which must occur exactly once in the file at `expectedVersion`, with `newText`, and writes the whole file under that version as `company_write` does (its media type and executable bit kept; at most 512 KiB before and after). Not found or not unique is refused before any receipt (`COMPANY_EDIT_NOT_FOUND`, `COMPANY_EDIT_NOT_UNIQUE`); another version, or nothing at the path, is committed as a write under the model's version, so the core answers its conflict and a replay of the same call reaches its receipt. `company_move` moves or renames one file, link or folder (`from`, `to`, `expectedVersion`, `asOfSeq`) in one move unit. A folder moves as of the model's own listing of it: `company_list`'s answer now carries the folder clock its page was read at (`asOfSeq`), the move's call carries it through review and commit, a folder without it is refused before any receipt (`COMPANY_MOVE_NEEDS_LISTING`), and a change in the folder since is the core's `unseen_change` conflict; a file or a link takes `asOfSeq` null. The core reviews it as a deletion, so it needs the automatic review's approval of its call, which it records as `company_delete` does: the automatic review classes it `destructive`, its card names both paths, and the migration adds `company_move` to `qorpera_folder_record_review`'s tools. A read-only worker is offered neither. Both join `CLOUD_RUNTIME_TOOL_OPERATIONS` and the SQL observation vocabulary.
- `manage_worker` dispatch takes `saveTo`, the company folder where the finished files belong, such as `designs/` (item 4). It needs write access and is refused on a coding session. The tool adds one sentence (`workerSaveToText`) to the brief's context, within its 4000 bytes, so the worker saves there and lists the files in its report; the automatic review's card shows it as "saves in". The folder core already lets a writing worker write wherever its agent and member may, so no SQL changes; review by path is unchanged. Without `saveTo` a worker saves in its own folder, as before.
- A run woken by a post in a conversation gets `request` in its context (item 3): the post its wake names, or a card answer with the card it answers (`question`), each read whole up to one 16 KiB page through `qorpera_read_company_run_context` (`composeCloudRoomRequest`, alongside the composition's other reads), where the history held only its first 2 KiB. The composed history leaves the request out. A page that cannot be read keeps the history's excerpt, which `read_context` reads on from, and never fails the run. A card the history no longer holds (asked before its 32 latest entries) is read by its id through `qorpera_read_company_run_card` (migration `20260930525000`, SQL test 548: an interaction or authorization request of the run's own conversation at or before its transcript start, as the history shows an entry, its first 16 KiB; a native host call with the run's binding), and `read_context` admits it (`cloudCompanyContextTool`'s `admitted`). `readCloudContextPage` is the one page read `read_context` and the request share.
- A tool may raise its own argument bound (`CloudNativeTool.maxArgumentBytes`) up to `MAX_TOOL_ARGUMENT_BYTES` (960 KiB, below the native session's 1 MiB message); every other tool keeps 64 KiB.
- The Enable Banking client and the bank summary reduction, inert until the broker wires them (Bank connection part 1). `cloud-connector-enable-banking.ts` signs every call as the application (RS256 JWT, `kid` the application id, an hour), lists a country's banks, starts a member's authorization, makes a session from the bank's code, reads balances and every page of transactions (money out negative) and closes a session; a consent that ended (`EXPIRED_SESSION`, `REVOKED_SESSION`, `CLOSED_SESSION`) is `EnableBankingSessionEnded`, apart from rate limits and other refusals. `bank-summary-reduce.ts` reduces balances and booked DKK transactions to the web's `qorpera.bank-summary/v1`: saldo, disponibel and reserveret, a year of daily saldo back from today's, twelve months in and out, this month by who paid and by category (salaries, taxes and VAT, rent, utilities, subscriptions, fees, other; at most eight), each receiver's rhythm and next payment, what repeats every month, quarterly and yearly costs within 120 days, and six weeks ahead.
- The vault connects a company's bank and keeps its bank summary current (Bank connection part 2; migration `20260930523000`, SQL test 691). The connectors credential takes `enableBanking: {applicationId, privateKeyPem}` (an RSA key; it may be the only entry), and the vault then serves the connector `bank`, which is not a catalog connector and gives agents no tool. A company ticket for `bank` starts through the same route as any connector's, with `bank: {name, country, psuType}` in the start body (`qorpera.cloud-connector-oauth-start/v2`; only a bank ticket may carry it, and it must): `CloudConnectorOAuthFlows.beginBank` checks the bank against Enable Banking's list for that country and kind of customer and asks for the bank's longest consent, at most 180 days; the bank's sign-in returns through `/v1/oauth/callback` with the company-routed state, and the confirm turns the code into a session. The sign-in lasts at most twenty minutes and never past the capture ticket, the code becomes a session only while the ticket has half a minute left, and a session the vault could not seal is closed again (`CloudConnectorOAuthFlows.abandon`). The sealed fields are `session_id`, `accounts` (JSON of the shared accounts), `bank` and `valid_until`; the account label is the first account's last four digits (`••6243`), and a sign-in that shared no account is `declined`. `cloud-bank-sync.ts` (`CloudBankSyncs`): the company's authority claims its sync when due (`qorpera_claim_bank_sync`: right after a connect or connect-again, then every six hours) under a lease of its own (a late answer from an earlier claim is refused), opens the session, fetches each account's balances and transactions (the longest history after a connect, else from two weeks before the newest booked day the last current fetch reached, which a failed or unfinished fetch never moves, each to the bank's last page; a fetch that does not get there keeps what it brought, publishes nothing and fails, and the same fetch is tried again), keeps the booked ones in batches of 5000 (`qorpera_record_bank_transactions`, accounts by a hash of the bank's identity, transactions by a hash of their reference or content), reduces what is kept plus what is pending to `qorpera.bank-summary/v1` and records it (`qorpera_finish_bank_sync`). An ended consent marks the summary paused and stops until the member connects again; a failure is recorded by a fixed phrase and tried again after 15 minutes, doubling to six hours. A single-company vault runs it once a minute (`CloudBankSyncUpkeep`); the shared vault asks once a minute which of its companies are due (`qorpera_broker_principal_bank_syncs_due`, `PostgresCloudSecretBrokerPrincipal.bankSyncsDue`) and syncs them in turn. A Disconnect, and a deleting company's finisher, queue the bank session's close at Enable Banking through the connector token revocation queue (`bank` joins it; `CloudConnectorTokenRevoker` takes `bank` and closes the sealed session). `EnableBankingClient.transactions` takes a page limit (the sync's is 1000, a guard only). Writing the summary to the company folder is the object gateway's (part 3).
- A coding turn's context takes the stop reason `MACHINE_NEW_DISK` (F3 5g-5 part 3; migration `20260930505000`, SQL test 524): the executor gave the session's machine a new disk because files it had read from the company folder are no longer readable, and that ended what its commands ran. The coding instructions tell the agent to check those commands as after `MACHINE_STOPPED`, and that a new disk keeps only `/company`, which holds the repository: home and cache folders, Docker images and anything installed outside `/company` start fresh. A runtime without it refuses such a context (`CODING_TURN_CONTEXT_INVALID`), so it goes out before the migration and the executor that record it.
- A coding turn's context says whether the session's machine got a new disk since the previous turn began (F3 5g-5 part 3, PR C; migration `20260930515000`, SQL test 536; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-FOLDER-CODING-MACHINES.md` "Reads" rule 4, `unreadable`): a new top-level key `machine: {newDisk}`, true when the machine (the session's `vm_allocation_id`) has a rebuild's `replaced` record at or after the previous turn's begin (its wake occurrence, or when it opened if it never began), false on a first turn. A session not attached when its machine got the new disk has no `MACHINE_NEW_DISK` event, so this is how it learns. The parse takes `machine` as optional, reads a context without it as `{newDisk: false}`, and refuses any other shape (`CODING_TURN_CONTEXT_INVALID`); the coding context carries it, and the instructions add one line, only when it is true, that only `/company` was kept. Today's runtime refuses a context with `machine`, so this runtime goes out before the migration. Runtime tests in `test/cloud-native-coding-runtime.test.ts`.
- Members read mail by direction, and a thread's older messages a page at a time (Interfaces Mailbox, C6e; migration `20260930512000`, SQL test 695; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-EVENT-INTAKE.md`, `supabase/CLIENT-CONTRACT.md`). `qorpera_member_mail_threads` takes `direction` (`received` or `sent`): only the threads whose newest message went that way, the `direction` each row shows, kept before the page or a state's window is cut, so `before` and `nextBefore` page through every such thread (new index `qorpera_mail_threads_direction`). `qorpera_member_mail_thread` takes a third argument, `p_before` (`{at, kind, id}`, the `nextBefore` an answer names while `more` is true): the 50 messages before that one, oldest first. Without it the answer is the newest 50, as before; the function is now `(uuid, text, jsonb)` and the two-argument form is gone. Who may call, account mail's hidden parts, the texts and the waiting sends are unchanged.
- A coding session's environment answer also brings the older values used on its machine, for the output's mask only (F3 step 5j-4, review r3 of #1272; migration `20260930514000`, SQL test 613; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-FOLDER-CODING-MACHINES.md` "Secrets, MCP and the vault relay"). Each `environment_secret_used` audit row records the machine the session was attached to (`coding_machine_id`, a plain uuid, null when it had none and on every other event). `qorpera_authorize_coding_environment` also answers `maskSecrets`: the sealed versions used on that machine by a session of the same member, of a secret the vault still serves there (active, the session's environment, a name the vault allows, the member's own or the company's), sealed by this broker under its current key, and none the answer serves; newest use first, at most 64 (`CODING_ENVIRONMENT_MASK_MAXIMUM`) and 256 KiB of envelopes. A revoked secret (Disconnect, or its member's departure) never comes back, and serving them writes no audit row. `CloudSecretBroker.codingEnvironment` checks each item as it checks `secrets` (a name may repeat), opens it, and answers `{schema: "qorpera.coding-environment/v1", environment, mask}`: the distinct values, none the environment holds, newest first. One bad item refuses the whole answer with the same opaque error. An answer without `maskSecrets` (a database before the migration) has an empty `mask`; a broker before this one refuses an answer with it, so the migration goes out with this runtime or after. Runtime tests in `test/cloud-coding-environment.test.ts`, `test/cloud-coding-environment-client.test.ts` and `test/cloud-secret-broker-http.test.ts`.
