The credential is checked first, with no lock, and each call is held to the host's orchestrator and adapter: a run only while its orchestrator holds the run's current claim (an accepted claim only while its runner holds the leased job), an identity only with that run, its adapter's runners and run machines of runs its orchestrator claimed. Also: `qorpera_provision_execution_runner` loses anon and authenticated EXECUTE.
- Added the native host's identity credential, database side (G2-3a; migration `20260930005000`, SQL tests 860 and 861; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-NATIVE-HOST-KEY.md`; inert until the node host binds credentials in G2-3c and the native host uses them in G2-3b): `qorpera_native_identity_call(p_compute_identity_id, p_credential, p_name, p_args)` (the publishable key, 8 s) runs the eight calls a native host makes outside a job (the identity's recovery state and vault custody, the account list and record, a sign-in's and an account refresh's describe and complete) with a credential the node host binds to the holder's machine allocation (`qorpera_bind_native_identity_credential`, service role; only the sha256 is kept, a bind again replaces it, a hash is bound once). The credential is checked per call and with no lock: its allocation unreleased, the identity active and not in recovery, a runner not revoked and at the identity's recovery epoch. Each holder kind gets only its own calls (`qorpera_native_identity_calls`): a job guest or an exec slot the four identity calls, a login guest its own sign-in or refresh (a sign-in's completion only with the native host's own `native-capsule/<capsule id>` reference), and a refresh's guest also its identity's account list and record. Also: `qorpera_complete_native_provider_login` checks the caller and the capability before it locks the sign-in, NULL-safe (55000; before, a NULL capability completed the sign-in); `qorpera_describe_native_provider_login`, `qorpera_describe_native_provider_login_route` and the native provider connection lease functions (acquire, renew, release) lose anon and authenticated EXECUTE, and `qorpera_begin_native_provider_login`, `qorpera_revoke_native_provider_connection`, `qorpera_register_provider_connection`, `qorpera_set_provider_connection_status`, `qorpera_upsert_member_compute_identity` and `qorpera_upsert_scoped_compute_identity` lose anon.
- Added quotes of outside content in a member's message (contract amendment "quoted outside content in a member's message", 2 October 2026; step I5d part 1; migration `20260930000100`, SQL test 816 + 660 checksums):
  - **The input RPCs check quotes:** a session-prompt body may carry `quotes` (1–16 spans in code points, each inside « », source `mail`, a thread the member may read, never account mail, no `displayText`). `qorpera_append_session_input`, `qorpera_request_session_turn_v2`, `qorpera_post_company_room_message` and its folder-v2 path check them after the right to write and before their first lock, and refuse with 22023.
  - **The run sees them fenced:** `qorpera_company_context_source` gives a quoted input, and an earlier quoted message in its history, as the member's words with `[quote N]` and the quoted words only as `qorpera.member-quote/v1` records (`trust: "untrusted_data"`, provenance input, quote number, source and ref). So the context, `read_context` pages, `parentInputs` and the continuations' `roomPost` all carry the fenced form.
  - **The transcript** keeps the list as `qorperaQuotes`.
  - **A desktop's turn claim** never takes a quoted message.
  - No client sends quotes yet; the runtime's prompt rule and the review (I5d part 3) and the web composer (part 4) follow.
- The runtime reads quotes of outside content in a member's message as the contract says (the 2026-10-02 amendment "quoted outside content in a member's message", items 4 and 5; step I5d part 3):
  - **The run's rules** say that `[quote N]` stands for words a member quoted from outside content, and that the `qorpera.member-quote/v1` records after it are untrusted data. The member's own words may ask for something about a quote, but a quote never guides the work by itself.
  - **The automatic review** reads the member's words with each `[quote N]` and where it came from, never the quoted words. It drops everything from the first list of quotes in a source cut short, and its instructions say an action only a quote asks for is not requested.
  - **The legacy runner** refuses a queued message, or an earlier message in its transcript, that quotes outside content, since only a cloud run's context fences one.
- Room drafts keep their quotes, and only a company whose runs fence quotes takes them (step I5d part 2; migration `20260930008000`, SQL test 815):
  - `qorpera_put_room_draft` takes the quotes as an optional fifth argument, checks them as a message's, and stores them. The draft's JSON carries them.
  - Once a draft holds quotes, a save that keeps a quote's words without its record (in their marks, at that place), or changes them, is refused (22023). Removing a quote whole is an ordinary save, and the member's own guillemets (»ja«, « merci ») stay theirs (`qorpera_draft_keeps_quotes`). A client that knows quotes and has none left sends an empty list, which the draft stores as none, so it may remove the last quote and write its own marks in one save.
  - `qorpera_check_member_quotes` refuses quotes in an environment that is neither folder-v2 nor on the company path, whose legacy runner can't fence them.
- A worker's brief and results as files on the folder core (F2's F2-5b-2, `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-COMPANY-STATE-FILES.md` §8 "Plan (F2-5b)"; migration `20260930003000`, SQL tests 840 and 841). On a folder-v2 company only the worker tools write `agents/<a>/todos/<t>/<w>/brief.md` and `results.md`: `qorpera_folder_worker_commit` carries the tool's request (dispatch, followup, report, decide) into the run's own commit, and the hook checks each against the locked rows (the todo open, the agent's own run, the platform's standing texts, readable sources, a report's files written by its run, a decision only on a report). The worker row is their index: new folder columns, written in apply, repointed with its agent's folder, retired with its folder, restored only byte for byte. A todo with workers keeps its file until its folder goes. Rank 5 gains `qorpera_runtime_agent_todos` and `qorpera_runtime_workers`: every todo write takes its row FOR NO KEY UPDATE and a worker change takes its todo FOR SHARE, so a dispatch and a done wait for each other. `qorpera_folder_cancel_agent_worker` and `qorpera_folder_agent_workers` cancel and list on folder-v2. Inert: no worker runs on folder-v2 before the drain (F2-5b-3) and C4's materializer.
- Added long-running shells with a real network on every computer (an agent's screen; founder decisions 2026-09-28). `computer_shell` now starts a command in the agent's own shell on its computer and replies after `waitMs` (at most 15 seconds, and never past the native call's deadline) or when it ends; the command keeps running as long as the work needs. `read`, `write` (stdin, default wait 1 second), `stop` (one command, or everything in the shell) and `list` follow it. Commands share the shell's localhost and /tmp (512 MiB); each keeps its last 1 MiB of output and a reply shows at most 16 KiB, both storage bounds that never stop a command. Each computer's shell and desktop get a network of their own from a root plumber in the new image: the public internet on any port and protocol, with the machine's firewall blocking every private and special range, the machine itself and the other computers, and the same ranges filtered again inside each computer; the desktop reads the image's public CA store, so its Terminal's HTTPS clients check certificates; the plumber refuses without the firewall and plumbs only a namespace still at its start gate; browser profiles stay unmounted. Under one idle rule for every machine (2026-10-03 amendment; migration `20260930029000`, SQL test 870 on a folder-v2 company's computer; folder-v2 only), a computer's shell keeps its machine awake only while it is busy: above about 5% of one core over the last 30 seconds, counted from the processor time of everything in the shell's namespace, or moving at least 512 KiB in that sample (about 17 KB a second, the bytes its commands read and write; `@qorpera/contracts` `idle-rule`) (`qorpera_report_runtime_office_shell_activity`, per screen, for 90 seconds after the last busy report). A command that only waits, such as a dev server, keeps nothing awake, and the computer sleeps after its quiet window. The report also keeps one row per command with the run that started it and that start's effect receipt; a command whose start is not the screen's own recorded effect for that run is left out, never refused. Sleep ends what still runs as slept; a seat that changes hands, any other stop of the computer, a pause or a lost generation ends it as closed, paused or lost; one the office stops listing without reporting its end ends as unknown and is never told (only from a screen whose lists are whole: each report fits one registry request, ends first, and a screen whose lists had to be cut says so); while the operator has paused the company, the report tells the computer, which stops every shell with the reason. The agent's next run in that conversation (for a worker, the conversation that dispatched it) is told once, in its open work (`computerStopped`). The office's operation check (`qorpera_native_office_operation`) takes every `computer_shell` action through `qorpera_valid_office_shell_action`. Memory safety: shell processes are the kernel's first out-of-memory choice, and when the machine nears its memory or task limit the shell holding the most is stopped first, with the reason on its commands. Starting a command and typing into one stay reviewed; reading, listing and stopping do not. Waiting for output runs after the action's effect has drained, outside the screen's action gate, so a cancelled wait never drains the desktop. The shell namespace starts in the background: a start returns at once and its command is sent when the shell is ready; if the shell cannot start, its commands end "stopped" with a note, and a start whose namespace could not be confirmed gone makes closing the screen report an unconfirmed drain. The 15-second form is still accepted until the legacy cleanup pass removes it.
- Added the account tool's database side (company accounts A1-4a; migration `20260928439965`, SQL test 813; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-COMPANY-ACCOUNTS.md`): `qorpera_find_company_accounts(binding, {todo?, site?})`, a native host call bound to the agent's run, answers the company's accounts that are not closed (newest 20, on one website when asked), its mailbox addresses, and the id of the agent's own open todo by its key; an agent's account must sign up with a tagged address of one of the company's mailboxes (`<mailbox>+<tag>@<mail domain>`), which a trigger enforces with a refusal the agent can act on; observations name `company_account`. Inert until the tool is offered (A1-4b) and accounts are switched on.
- Added `company_search` (F3, the founder's "Light threads" decision, 2 October 2026: a chat turn has no shell): a folder-v2 run on a shared execution host with the node's folder search (`QORPERA_FOLDER_SEARCH_SOCKET`) searches the company folder on its node's folder service with its own view, opened once per run (`qorpera_folder_run_view_open`) and kept over one connection through the slot's relay. Literal text in the lines of text files, or with `names_only` in names, with `glob`; each call answers one page as `path:line: text` lines, fitted to one tool result, with a cursor to go on (a page shown in part goes on from the first hit it left out). Before the model sees a page, everything it drew on is released with the run actor under the hash of the exact text shown, and the answer cites the release; a read the run may no longer make withholds the page. Calls take turns on the run's one connection. Review class "read"; observed as `company_search` (migration `20260928449960`, SQL test 811).
- Added the run's own folder view for the native host (F3, the server-side `company_search`; founder decision "Light threads", 2 October 2026; migration `20260928439970`, SQL tests 802 and 812): `qorpera_folder_run_view_open(p_environment_id, p_actor)` opens the `run_box` view with the run actor and is listed for G2's entry point with scope folder (86 listed calls). A second open in the same job closes the earlier view as superseded, and two first opens at once leave the later one's view; the order is the run completion's (job, the job's run views, the view open's run entry), and a refused actor is turned away before any lock. The release that goes with a search page is `qorpera_folder_record_release` with the run actor, already listed.
- The folder core checks a website account's connection file (F2's A1-7b, `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-COMPANY-STATE-FILES.md` §2 and §8; migration `20260928439950`, SQL test 801): with `service: website` the parse carries `site` (the vault's host rule), `twoStep` and `openedFor`, and the status may also be `pending` or `needs_person`; every other connection keeps its exact shape and `connected` or `broken`. Inert until A1's vault writer writes such files. Release after A1-7a's parser is live on every writer.
- Added the Company Computer's stop at a page that asks for a person (company accounts A1-4c; office guest code, ships with an office image; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-COMPANY-ACCOUNTS.md`): before and after every page action (navigate, click, fill, press, vault_fill), with every snapshot and before desktop click, scroll, press and type, the office reads the shown frames of CAPTCHA and human check services inside a page, an interstitial's whole title, and the words at the top of a short page (`cloud-company-computer-site-stops.ts`); such a page gets nothing clicked or typed, its site is recorded for all the computer's screens while the office runs, and every later page or desktop action on that site is refused before it starts. The answers are results the agent reads, not errors: `refused: SITE_STOPPED` before, `stop: SITE_STOP_<REASON>` after an action or in a snapshot, each with a message to stop, never retry and mark the account `needs_person`. Nothing tries to pass such a check: a person continues.
- Added the agent's tool for the company's own website accounts (company accounts A1-4b; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-COMPANY-ACCOUNTS.md`): `company_account` finds the company's accounts and mailboxes, opens an account for an open todo of the agent's with the company's own tagged address and a password only the vault knows (refused as its arguments are read, before any review, for a service with an official connection, GitHub, Google, Microsoft, Slack, a site with strict bot checks, Apple, LinkedIn, X, Meta, Amazon (its regional stores too), or a payment provider, PayPal, Stripe; never a second account on a website the company already has one on, under any of its names, among the site's accounts and the company's newest 20), and records the website's answer (`connected`, `needs_person`, `closed`), each through an effect receipt and the vault's `POST /v1/company-accounts`; `computer_browser` offers `vault_fill`; closing an account is reviewed as destructive; the agents' instructions and the computer tools' words carry the rules (a CAPTCHA, bot check or block page ends the attempt, never retried). Offered with the vault on the host, to company runs (not workers, not folder companies); inert while accounts are switched off. Needs #691's migration `20260928439965`.
- Added members' reading of company mail for the Interfaces Mailbox (Q1 step I5a, migration 20260928439980, SQL test 808, `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-EVENT-INTAKE.md` "Members read mail (I5a)"): threads keyed by a hashed root Message-ID (`qorpera_mail_threads`, kept by the mail door and `send_mail`, deleted 30 days after their last message), each received message recorded for members for 30 days (`qorpera_mail_received`, apart from the delivery's 7-day headers and body), `mail-outbox` reading back the provider's Message-ID of sent mail so an answer to an email an agent started joins its thread, and five member calls (`qorpera_member_mail_addresses`, `_threads`, `_thread`, `_waiting`, `qorpera_mark_mail_thread_read`) that check membership before anything else, take no lock, answer empty while mail is off and show account mail (A1's lookup, once it exists; decided on arrival and asked again on reading) without its subject, text, recipients or attachments. A waiting send the member may answer names its card's conversation (`sessionId`, migration 20260930002660).
- Added the mark on a company account's own mail (A1-5a; migration `20260928439960`, SQL test 809; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-COMPANY-ACCOUNTS.md`): `qorpera_company_account_mail(profile, environment, addresses)` answers the newest of the company's accounts, closed ones included, whose login carries a tag and is one of a delivery's delivered-to addresses (whole, any letter case, tags included; a bare mailbox address or a username never marks, so no account hides a whole mailbox), with its site and the agent that last used it, so the company Mailbox shows that mail without its text. Definer-only (no API role, no broker), a plain read; Q1's member mail read calls it.
- Added typing a company account's login, password or authenticator code into the Company Computer without any agent, person or the native host seeing it (A1-3b; needs migration `20260928429975` from A1-3a and a new office image). The office service makes an X25519 key in memory at start and reports it with readiness (`CompanyComputerStartupControl` `fillPublicKey`); the `computer_browser` action `vault_fill {role, name, account, field}` (parsed, not yet offered to agents: A1-4); for it the host's office endpoint asks the vault (`POST /v1/company-account-fills`, runtime token), which opens the account's envelope, makes the value (an authenticator code waits for the next one when under 3 s are left) and seals it to that computer's key for this call (`cloud-company-account-fill-box.ts`: X25519, HKDF-SHA256, AES-256-GCM; bound to company, computer, generation, receipt, account and field; the hosts and a 90 s limit inside); the host carries only the box; the office opens it inside the same call (a box for another call, key or field, or dated outside its 90 s, is never typed) and types it with CDP `Input.insertText` into the one box named, after the page has loaded, only on the account's own https hosts and only in the page's own document; it marks the box private, and a value that landed elsewhere because the page moved the focus is emptied there. The office keeps the typed secrets per screen while it runs (an authenticator code for 5 minutes, and only as a number of its own): snapshots, titles, addresses and tab names hide them, also percent- or form-encoded, without hiding the page's other typed text; no browser or whole-screen picture is taken while a page shows one as text, in its address or title, in a box not marked private or inside an open shadow root (checked before and after the picture, within 5 s). A value that lands whole in its box counts as typed even if the page then moves the focus on. A refusal (switched off, no such account, an older computer, no vault on the host) is the call's answer and never reaches the office.
- Human tasks on the folder core (F2's H2b, `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-COMPANY-STATE-FILES.md` §8 "Plan (H2)"; migration `20260928429986`, SQL tests 803 and 805): the index tables `qorpera_folder_human_task_index` and `qorpera_folder_contact_index`; the person's change (one tick, done with every listed proof, declined with a reason) under the person's own member actor, or the platform's on a contact's task; the company's side (open, accepted, returned, cancelled; final states never change); references checked when set or changed; a task's folder that never outlives its file and belongs to its person while the task is open; contacts that stay, with their email, while a task names them; rank-5 row locks on the two new tables only; `human-tasks/` and `contacts/` among the seeded folders that are never deleted or moved (Jonas, 2 October). No wakes yet (H2c, with C4's folder listener). Inert for snapshot-v1 companies.
- Added mail attachments (Q1 step M2c-1, migration 20260928429984, SQL test 804, `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-EVENT-INTAKE.md` "Attachments (M2c-1)"; Jonas, 2 October): `save_attachment` saves a received email's attachment into the company folder. The first request (`qorpera_native_mail_attachment`, also on G2-1's native host allowlist) records a fetch that the new Edge Function `mail-attachments` streams from the mail provider into the company's objects as 4 MiB chunks, at most 40 MiB, while the email's text is kept. The runtime waits up to 90 s, then writes the chunks through the path gateway as `company_write` does (`CloudCompanyPathGateway.mutate` takes `attachment`, read through the object gateway and checked against its digests); it is internal for the automatic review. `send_mail` takes `attachments`, up to 10 company files the agent may read and 28 MiB in all, recorded as queued and shown on the review card; `mail-outbox` sends them from the company's objects in Storage. folder-v2 companies are refused both ways for now.
- The folder core knows human-task and contact files (F2's H1b, `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-COMPANY-STATE-FILES.md` §2 and §8; migration `20260928429900`, SQL test 785). `human-tasks/<name>.md` and `contacts/<name>.md` are structured paths: a save carries the shared parser's parse and the parse is checked by its shape, limits and cross-key rules. `human-tasks` and `contacts` are places, and a task's folder (`human-tasks/<t>`, `human-tasks/<t>/proof`) follows the naming rule. A folder move or copy into or out of either root cannot change a file's kind. Only the platform marks a task's secret saved, unless the file the save replaces already had it for the same task and label. No index, lock or review class; inert until H2. Release after H1a's parser is live on every writer.
- Added filling a company account into the Company Computer, database side (A1-3a; migration `20260928429975`, SQL test 807; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-COMPANY-ACCOUNTS.md`). An office boot reports an X25519 fill key with its readiness (`qorpera_report_runtime_office_ready` takes an optional fifth argument; the key is kept on that activation once, never a low-order point); the browser action `vault_fill {role, name, account, field}`; and the broker's `qorpera_fill_company_account`, bound to the agent's executing `computer_browser` receipt of exactly that call, the company's open website account and the computer the run's screen lease holds now, whose current boot must have a key. Inert until the office and the vault use them (A1-3b); a boot without a key gets no fill. Closing an account (`qorpera_set_company_account_state(…, 'closed')`) now revokes its vault row at once, which wipes its envelopes; a repeat close answers the closed account (A1-7 needs the row revoked to remove the account's file); accounts closed before this migration are revoked by it.
- Added `find_mail` and plus addresses for accounts the company owns (Q1 with A1, migration 20260928429982, `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-EVENT-INTAKE.md` "Plus addresses and find_mail"): `local+tag@` reaches the mailbox `local@` and its delivery records `delivered-to`, `from`, `subject` and `authentication-results` (control characters as spaces, sized to fit 8 KiB, counted in the company's hourly bytes); the runtime tool `find_mail` (a read) searches the company's received mail of up to 7 days by mailbox, delivered-to address, sender and subject parts, since and before, up to 10 newest first as fit 8 KiB with `more` when there are others or the 2000-mail bound was reached (one ordered index scan per mailbox, planned with its own values) and `nextBefore` to page on, and with `waitSeconds` (up to 120) asks again every 3 seconds until a match arrives (`qorpera_find_agent_mail`, also on G2-1's native host allowlist); `send_mail` refuses the company's own mailboxes under any tag.
- Added the vault side of company website accounts (A1-2, `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-COMPANY-ACCOUNTS.md`; needs migration `20260928419970` from A1-1). `cloud-company-accounts.ts` holds what the vault keeps for one account (password, the password before a change, an RFC 6238 authenticator, recovery codes) and the values only the vault makes: passwords drawn without modulo bias, authenticator codes, and the readers for a setup QR code's `otpauth://totp` link and for recovery codes as websites show them. The broker's `POST /v1/company-accounts` (runtime token; `qorpera.company-account-request/v1`) opens an account for an agent's open todo, with a password the vault makes and seals under the company's key for the account's own id, or records a website's answer (`connected`, `needs_person`, `closed`). It answers only the account's facts (`qorpera.company-account/v1`) or the database's refusal an agent can act on, in its own words (`qorpera.company-account-refusal/v1`: 0A000, 22023, 23505, 54000; the agent reads the message, never a meaning keyed by the code); every other failure, a paused company's 55000 included, is "outcome unconfirmed". A value never leaves the vault. Nothing calls the route yet (the agent tool is A1-4); the vault's nginx needs the `/v1/company-accounts` location when it is turned on.
- Added the vault's records for accounts the company owns on outside websites (company accounts A1-1, `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-COMPANY-ACCOUNTS.md`; migration `20260928419970`, SQL tests 794 and 797; built switched off by `qorpera_company_account_settings`). An account is a company-owned `website` vault secret plus a `qorpera_company_accounts` row (site and sign-in hosts it may be filled into, login, two-step, status, the open todo that needed it). Broker-only calls bound to the agent's executing `company_account:<action>` effect receipt read what an opening seals (the run's company and environment, the company's key id), open an account (refused without an open todo of the run's own agent: never unprompted), use its envelope, save a new version by compare-and-swap, and record its state; members list the facts (never a value) and owners or admins remove an account, which deletes its envelopes at once (any revoke of a `website` secret, the generic one included, wipes them and closes the account). Folder-v2 companies are refused until the vault writes their connection files (A1-7).
- Added the native host's switch to call the database without the service-role key (G2-2; `cloud-native-host-dispatch.ts`, built switched off: `NATIVE_HOST_DISPATCH_DEFAULT = "off"`, `QORPERA_NATIVE_HOST_DISPATCH` for a host started by hand). When it is on, each of the 83 calls the host makes inside a job goes to `qorpera_native_host_call`, or to `qorpera_native_host_call_long` for the lease validator (migration `20260928409985`). The call carries the publishable key and that job's lease token, with its arguments spliced in as sent. Only the host's between-job calls (`NATIVE_HOST_KEY_CALLS`: logins and account refresh, until G2-3) and requests outside the database (Storage, Realtime, until G2-4) keep the service key. Any other database request is refused on the host, and so is a listed call outside a job or without the token, so nothing new reaches the service key unnoticed. A test classifies every name in the host's clients, and another checks that each client gets the dispatch. The runner sends the job's lease token (`job.lease_token`) and its publishable key (`native_host.publishable_key`) with each company execution. The host holds them for that execution only and drops them when the execution has unwound, after its cleanup, also on a warm host, whose next job brings its own. A missing or invalid credential is refused with the fixed code `NATIVE_HOST_CREDENTIAL`, and a host still holding another job with `NATIVE_HOST_BUSY`. The credential stays off the execution request the runtime passes on, out of the model's sandbox, and out of the host's log (its rejection line also redacts any token-shaped text). The Company File client reads a 42501 answered with 401, as PostgREST answers on the publishable key, as a refusal, like 403. Runner and host change together; they come from the same guest image. Before switching on: a live check that hosted PostgREST applies the entry points' statement budgets (8 s, 25 s), not the publishable key's 3 s; and Postgres must not log these calls' parameters (the body carries the token), so keep `log_parameter_max_length` at 0 wherever slow statements are logged.
- Added the native host's entry points without the service-role key (G2-1; migration `20260928409985`, SQL tests 792 and 793; inert until the native host uses them in G2-2): `qorpera_native_host_call(p_job_id, p_job_lease_token, p_name, p_args)` and, for the lease validator only, `qorpera_native_host_call_long` with the same arguments. Both are callable with the publishable key alone. They first check the runner job's lease token, by the job's primary key and with no lock: the job must be an agent turn's (its type and capability), leased, with a current lease, and the token's sha256 must be the job's `lease_token_sha256`. They then allow only the 85 calls the guest's native host makes, listed in `qorpera_native_host_calls`, each with a scope rule that ties its arguments to that job's run: its binding, job, run, compute identity, company, environment, a `kind: run` folder actor of this job, or a path proposal this run left. Any `p_job_id`, `p_run_id`, `p_environment_id`, `p_profile_id` or `p_compute_identity_id` must be this run's, whatever the scope. The call goes to the one public function of that name by its declared argument names, reading the values as PostgREST does (`jsonb_to_record` with the catalog's types, values only through `USING`). It runs as the platform's service caller, on the function's own RPC path (`request.path`, which the shared company readers choose their lock strength by), and returns the result as jsonb, as PostgREST returns a scalar; a void call returns null. Each call keeps today's statement budget, because PostgREST applies the entry point's `statement_timeout`: 8 s (authenticator's), or the lease validator's own 25 s through the long entry point. Without it, a call would get the publishable key's 3 s. The called function's errors reach the caller unchanged; the entry points' own refusal is one 42501 "native host call is refused". The listed functions stay unreachable with the publishable key, and service_role cannot call the entry points. The office VM's calls, the login guest's calls and calls made between jobs are later G2 steps.
- Added signed hooks (Q1 step W1b, migration 20260928409980, `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-EVENT-INTAKE.md` "Verification in the vault (W1b)"): owners and admins create `stripe`, `github` and `hmac-sha256` (Standard Webhooks, also Svix's headers) hooks whose signing secret is a company vault secret (connector `event_hook`, name `h<hook id>`, field `signing_secret`, named by the descriptor's `signingSecret`); a signed delivery waits as `received` until the company's broker, or the shared vault for each company it has something due for (`qorpera_broker_principal_event_verifications_due`), claims it (`qorpera_claim_event_verifications`), opens the secret in memory, checks the signature (`cloud-event-verification.ts`: Stripe `t=`/`v1=`, GitHub `sha256=`, Standard Webhooks `v1,` over `<id>.<timestamp>.<body>`, 300 seconds from arrival, constant-time) and records verified with a sender id the signature covers (Stripe's event id, `webhook-id`, GitHub's `sig:<signature>`) and the type, duplicate or refused with a fixed reason (`qorpera_finish_event_verification`, which refuses instead when the hook or its secret went since the claim, and offers the delivery again when the secret's current version is no longer the claimed one); a retry waits 1, 5, 25 and 125 minutes and the fifth attempt refuses; removing a hook revokes its secret.
- Added the runs switch for rolling updates (rolling step 1; migration `20260928409960`, SQL test 795; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-IDENTICAL-NODES.md`). `qorpera_set_runtime_node_runs` stops new runs and sign-ins on a node (its orchestrators claim nothing new, run and sign-in candidates and the ledger leave it out) while its computers, coding machines and agent machines stay admitted; the full serving switch is unchanged. Drain status adds `runsServing` and `readyForNodeRelease`. With dispatch roles unset, the node chooser no longer places a run on a provider whose live orchestrators are all declared for another bundle (a six-argument `qorpera_runtime_node_placement_decide`; the run reservation passes the run's bundle). `qorpera_declare_runtime_node_bundle` declares one node's orchestrators at once. `RUNTIME-ROLLING-UPDATE.md` uses all three.
- Added `decide_routine`'s rewrite on folder-v2 (thin writers W4c-2): a next run that also changes the routine's title or instructions is written with the decision and saved with the automatic review's approval recorded for exactly that change set (C2 R1), instead of being refused. Without the approval it is refused before the receipt; a routine past its end is settled; a rewrite the core refuses is reported as not saved, and the run decides again without it. SQL test 781 §7.
- Added `manage_routine` as a thin writer of a folder-v2 agent's routine files (thin writers W4c-1, `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-COMPANY-STATE-FILES.md` §5): `agents/<handle>/routines/<routineKey>.md`, whose name is the key (lowercase letters, digits and single hyphens, at most 64 characters). `upsert` writes the whole file (`every`, `weekly`, or `dynamic` as `once`), `pause`, `resume` and `conclude` edit its settings, `remove` deletes it; each save carries the automatic review's approval recorded for exactly its change set (C2 R1), and `upsert` and `resume` pin the requester's routine login first. A change that changes nothing answers so before the receipt. `list` reads the routine index (`qorpera_folder_agent_routines`), a page at a time or one routine whole. File and event routines are refused on folder-v2 for now. Migration `20260928399960`: a save that changes nothing ends a pause the platform recorded in the index only (a member's Stop, a failing account, a wait for a model account) and pays like a resume; an unpaid one keeps it. SQL tests 780 and 781. Snapshot-v1 runs are unchanged, except that the approval card for any resume now says it switches the routine back on, also after a member's Stop or a failing account.
- Added `create_group_chat` as a thin writer of a folder-v2 company's group chats (thin writers W4b, `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-COMPANY-STATE-FILES.md` §5): one `new_folder` unit `agents/groups/<handle>` with `group.md` (the run's agent and the coworkers named, and the requester unless left out), saved under the call's receipt with the automatic review's approval recorded for exactly that change set (C2 R1); the commit opens the chat's room and conversation. The file is checked with the arguments, before the review. SQL test 777.
- Added `hire_agent` as a thin writer of a folder-v2 company's agent folders (thin writers W4a, `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-COMPANY-STATE-FILES.md` §5): one `new_folder` unit `agents/<handle>` with `agent.md`, the agent's folders and the first assignment as an open todo, saved under the call's receipt with the automatic review's approval recorded for exactly that change set (C2 R1). The files are checked with the arguments, before the automatic review, and the first assignment holds at most 4000 bytes on folder-v2. Contract 14.1 (migration 20260928399940): an agent that cannot delegate the new agent's default access hires only on an owner's or admin's answer (the review asks the owners and admins on a card; the recorder checks again). The new agent does not start by itself yet; the result says to wake it with send_coworker_message. SQL test 774.
- Added the folder review recorder (C2 R1, `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-COMPANY-FOLDER-C2.md`): `qorpera_folder_record_review` (migration 20260928399890) records a folder-v2 run's approved action review of one call (its own allow, or a card's allow it used, with the member who gave it; a delegation or deletion by a folder-writing tool, matched on the tool and the call's argument digest) as the `qorpera_folder_reviews` row of exactly one change set, bound to the commit's own digest, so the commit saves that run's delegation and deletion units and names the approver. The action review gate passes the approval's id to the tool (`actionReviewId`), and the folder client stages a change set before it records its review and commits it (`stage`, `recordReview`, `commitStaged`). Inert until the W4 writers use it. SQL tests 769 and 770.
- Added the usage-limit signal for the member, chat and coding (Sign in with ChatGPT switch-on gate 2a; migration `20260928379930`, SQL test 782): `qorpera_get_execution_run_recovery` adds `paid_by_chatgpt_plan`, and a coding turn that stopped at its login's usage limit now says so in its `failed` event (`usageLimit: true`, `paidByChatgptPlan`), from the turn's run's usage signal. Before, that turn's failure carried only the executor's summary and read as a step refused on Qorpera's side. Codex logins: a coding usage limit is now told as a limit; nothing else changes. Kernel only: `qorpera_coding_turn_usage_limit(occurrence)`.
- Added that a member's own Turn on for their ChatGPT plan (`qorpera_set_chatgpt_plan_work_while_away`, on) also starts the agents' routines that wait only for it (Sign in with ChatGPT; migration `20260928369985`, SQL test 779; built switched off with plan logins): paused with `needs_model_account`, running for that member and paid by that same plan, never a listener, a routine file, one past its end, one whose agent is archived, another member's or another login's, nor in a company being deleted or on the folder core. Each is re-pinned to the plan's unattended binding for the version just turned on (the one `qorpera_ensure_unattended_sponsor` makes), gets a new version that changes only the sponsor (hashed as an agent's save, so the agent's next identical save is no new version) and a schedule cursor, and resumes. The answer adds `resumed_routines`. The new version records the resume (`resume_reason = 'plan_turned_on'`), and `qorpera_list_cloud_routines` names it (`resume_reason`, `resumed_at`, `resumed_by_user_id`). Locks: the company row KEY SHARE first (the new version's foreign key takes it anyway), then the routines FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED (a busy one stays waiting), then the consent's own locks; the switch waits at most 2 s for any one lock (`lock_timeout`) and then answers 55P03 "Busy right now. Try again in a moment." with nothing changed, as it does when PostgreSQL breaks a deadlock on its side. Also: the switch now reads the caller's own revoked plan as unavailable (42501), as any other connection.
- Added a kernel guard for a ChatGPT plan's work while away (Sign in with ChatGPT; migration `20260928369980`, SQL tests 776 and 778; built switched off with plan logins). `qorpera_consent_provider_connection_autonomous` refuses to turn a plan login on (55000) unless the call comes through the new `qorpera_set_chatgpt_plan_work_while_away(profile, connection, on)`, the member's own switch, which marks the call for its transaction only and makes the same consent. An older web build, which consents every connection it binds, can no longer turn a plan on. The switch answers only for the caller's own plan in that company. `qorpera_ensure_unattended_sponsor` (an agent's routine save) reads the member's newest version again under the connection lock: before, a save that waited for a member turning their plan off wrote a new "on" version in the member's name (from #614; race test 778). Turning a plan off and every Codex login are as before. Tests 768 and 772 turn a plan on through the switch.
- Added the rules a member's ChatGPT plan login keeps (Sign in with ChatGPT slice 1b, built switched off; migration `20260928359950`, SQL tests 768 and 775). The kernel never consents a plan login for work while its member is away (`qorpera_ensure_unattended_sponsor` skips one awaiting consent; the member turns it on through `qorpera_set_chatgpt_plan_work_while_away` since 20260928369980). A plan never pays for a listener (file change, webhook, mail): refused when the routine version is saved, and when a wake it would pay for is a listener's or follows from a listener run (`qorpera_wake_authorized`). A plan pays for follow-up work only of runs it pays for (`qorpera_starter_sponsor`, and so a review's seed, a coworker's or a worker's wake and a retried review): a run's own plan stays its payer also at the usage limit, which stops the work, and no work another payer started moves to a plan (a listener run's paid by the company account, or a login's at its limit). A conversation the plan pays for keeps it (`qorpera_select_member_session_account`) while the plan can pay; a new conversation or first run starts on a login with room. A coding worker takes the member's other login before a plan, and a plan only when the asking run is paid by it (55000 otherwise, at dispatch). A member connects one usable plan per company: a plan sign-in beside a usable plan login is refused when it begins and when it completes (55000 "Disconnect your other ChatGPT plan first"; completions of one member take a lock in turn). Only the member whose plan pays retries a failed run or a change's review on it, and a review's retry follows the review's own payer. Plan use stops for a company being deleted. The new helpers are kernel-only. Also fixed: a coding worker for a member without a coding payer yet failed (22P02) since `20260922230000`. Test 647 re-pins `qorpera_wake_authorized`'s snapshot-v1 text. A run in progress is never handed to another login since #601.
- Added the end of a deleting company's ChatGPT plan logins (Sign in with ChatGPT slice 1c, built switched off; migration `20260928359960`, SQL tests 771 and 773). Each pass of `qorpera_finish_company_deletion` revokes the connections of the company's members' plan logins (`qorpera_revoke_company_chatgpt_plan_logins`), so the vault's upkeep ends each login at OpenAI, and waits while any is open (`holdings.chatgpt_plan_logins`, `qorpera_company_chatgpt_plan_logins_open`), at most six hours after the deletion request. A connection that still pays a queued or leased job is revoked on a later pass, so the revocation never waits for a job under the company fence. A login still open after six hours is deleted with the company and counted in the database log (`qorpera_abandon_company_chatgpt_plan_logins`). Until now the purge deleted plan logins with the company and left their refresh tokens live at OpenAI. Codex logins are unchanged.
- Added the reads the web needs for ChatGPT plan logins (Sign in with ChatGPT slice 5a, built switched off; migration `20260928369970`, SQL test 772). The member's connection list names each login's kind (`login_kind`: `codex`, `chatgpt_plan`, or null for a node login), its work-while-away consent (`allow_autonomous`) and when the member's first-routine card was shown (`routine_offer_shown_at`). `qorpera_record_chatgpt_plan_routine_offer` records that showing once per member and company, also across a new sign-in, for the caller's own active plan login only and never in a company being deleted (table `qorpera_chatgpt_plan_routine_offers`, closed to every API role). The routine list names a paused routine's `pause_reason` and the member its current version runs for (`runs_for_user_id`). CLIENT-CONTRACT.md, "ChatGPT plan reads".
- Added runs on a member's ChatGPT plan (Sign in with ChatGPT slice 4, built switched off). A run host that gets a plan login from the vault writes the plan's access token alone to `<run root>/chatgpt-plan/access-token` (0600 in a 0700 directory) and rewrites it whenever the vault renews. The run's Codex uses the provider `qorpera_chatgpt_plan`: OpenAI's API (`https://api.openai.com/v1`) with the token as the bearer, read by Codex's token command (`/bin/cat`, again each minute and after a refused request), at standard speed; the boundary checks Codex applied it. The sandbox binds the token directory read-only and reaches `api.openai.com` alone. A 429 on a plan run is the plan's usage limit. The fallback is the token in the process environment at start, with no warm spare for a plan run (switched by `CHATGPT_PLAN_AUTH_DEFAULT` in a runtime release, or `QORPERA_CHATGPT_PLAN_AUTH` on a host started by hand). A Codex login's run may not use the plan provider; any other provider its command sets stays allowed. The namespace builder `nativeIsolationInvocation` is pure, so a run's sandbox shape is tested anywhere. Coding turns run the same way. Runbook: RUNTIME-LOGIN-VAULT.md, "Runs (slice 4)".
- Added Sign in with ChatGPT's callback (slice 3, built switched off): the Edge Function `chatgpt-plan-callback` takes OpenAI's redirect (`verify_jwt = false`), finds the company's vault with `qorpera_chatgpt_plan_callback_target`, forwards the state and code there, and returns the browser to the web app with a one-time confirmation id or a failure reason in the page fragment, never a code or token. It forwards only to the vault route of the state's own company. `native-provider-login` forwards the web client's `…/chatgpt-plan-confirm` to the same vault and waits 40 s for it (the vault answers within 30 s). Return page: `QORPERA_CHATGPT_PLAN_RETURN_URL` (an allowed web origin's HTTPS page with no query and no `?` in its fragment; empty counts as unset). Its tests run with the other function tests on the test machine.
- Added the vault side of ChatGPT plan logins (Sign in with ChatGPT slice 2, built switched off). `ChatGptPlanOAuth` (`cloud-chatgpt-plan.ts`) reads Qorpera's client registration from the optional credential `qorpera-cloud-secret-chatgpt-plan` (`qorpera.chatgpt-plan-client/v1`), takes OpenAI's endpoints from its discovery document (each on https://auth.openai.com), builds the authorization address (PKCE, the plan scopes, the resource https://api.openai.com/v1, the host id), exchanges a code with the ID token checked against OpenAI's keys (RS256 of at least 2048 bits or ES256 on P-256, the key's published `alg`, issuer, audience with `azp` when there are several, expiry, nonce; keys are refetched at most once a minute, one fetch shared by concurrent callers) and the plan scope required, renews (one refresh token, used once; OpenAI's keys and endpoints are loaded before it is spent; refused tokens, withdrawn plan use, an ID token for another account or a pass not later than the saved one mark the login for a new sign-in and end the new refresh token at OpenAI; `invalid_client` never marks a login), and revokes. A plan login records `openai-subject:<sha256 of OpenAI's subject>` as its account reference. A plan credential the vault cannot parse is logged and leaves plans off without stopping the vault. A refresh answer without a new refresh token keeps the old one; a 200 the vault cannot read is refused; a code exchange OpenAI answered but the vault refuses ends that refresh token at OpenAI, and OpenAI's keys are loaded before the code is spent. OpenAI's discovery and key fetches are shared under their own deadline. The confirm route answers within 30 s. `ChatGptPlanSignIns` (`cloud-chatgpt-plan-sign-in.ts`) answers a plan sign-in's poll with OpenAI's address, parks the callback's code under a one-time confirmation id (`POST /v1/model-logins/<company>/chatgpt-plan/callback`), and exchanges, seals and completes it only when the web client confirms with the sign-in's capability (`POST …/chatgpt-plan/confirm`); a login the database refuses to keep is ended at OpenAI. The vault reads sign-ins with the v2 read, hands a run a plan login's access token alone (`loginKind: "chatgpt_plan"`), renews plan logins under the same renewal lock, and its upkeep ends unused plan logins at OpenAI. A run host refuses a plan login until the run side ships. Runbook: RUNTIME-LOGIN-VAULT.md, "ChatGPT plan logins".
- Added the folder core's garbage collection, the SQL (core C1d-2a, `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-COMPANY-FOLDER-C1D.md` §9, migration `20260928349860`, tests 766 and 767): a side table for when a folder-v2 object lost its roots and the deleting mark with its lease; one root check across every folder-v2 environment of a company; an intent sweep, a claim that marks under `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` with no company fence, and the unlink's begin, finish, abandon and quarantine state for the gateway lane (C1d-2b); and refusals of a marked object, with the runtime's QRC01 409 conflict, at every place that takes a new reference (core §8 G3 amended: refuse instead of wait). Only folder-v2 companies whose every environment is folder-v2 and that are not being deleted are collected. Inert until C1d-2b's worker phase.
- Added a second kind of vault login, a member's ChatGPT Plus or Pro plan through OpenAI's "Sign in with ChatGPT" (slice 1a, database only; built switched off until OpenAI approves Qorpera). `qorpera_model_logins.login_kind` (`codex` or `chatgpt_plan`) and `renew_not_before` (OpenAI's earliest renewal time); sign-ins carry `model_login_kind`. Two switches, both off: the platform setting `qorpera_chatgpt_plan_settings.enabled` and the per-company list (`qorpera_set_chatgpt_plan_company`, service role, only for a company whose sign-ins go to its vault). Members begin with `qorpera_begin_chatgpt_plan_sign_in` (own member identity only; a plan login is never company-owned) and ask `qorpera_chatgpt_plan_available`; each member compute identity gets one stable `urn:uuid` host id. The vault reads both kinds with `qorpera_describe_model_login_sign_in_v2` (v1 keeps its exact keys and reads Codex sign-ins only), completes plan sign-ins with `qorpera_complete_chatgpt_plan_sign_in` (consent version `qorpera-chatgpt-plan-v1`, unattended use off) and saves plan renewals with `qorpera_commit_chatgpt_plan_renewal`; each commit accepts only its own kind, and a plan renewal is saved even while switched off. The run's issue and idle upkeep name `loginKind`; a run gets a plan login only while its company may use plan logins. A run renews a plan login past half its pass, as for Codex; idle upkeep renews a plan login only once its tokens are 7 days old, and never before OpenAI's earliest time. `qorpera_chatgpt_plan_callback_target(state)` (service role) names the vault the sign-in callback forwards to. Once everything that used a plan login is revoked (the member disconnected or left; a disabled connection keeps it), the vault takes it under its renewal lock (`qorpera_take_chatgpt_plan_revocation`), ends it at OpenAI and marks it revoked (`qorpera_finish_chatgpt_plan_revocation`). Plan logins are left out of the Codex usage read and the node switch-off's account record, and a node never completes a plan sign-in. Migration `20260928329830`, SQL test 764.
- Added `decide_routine` as a thin writer of a folder-v2 dynamic routine's own file (thin writers W3, `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-COMPANY-STATE-FILES.md` §5): `next` sets a new `at` (with effort and reply), `conclude` sets `status: concluded` and the conclusion, saved under the tool call's receipt with the version read. A title or instructions change (delegation, which the folder core cannot record before core C2) and a next run past the routine's end are refused before the receipt; a routine that already moved on is settled, as today; a routine file renamed while its run works is found again by the routine's id in its folder. Snapshot-v1 runs are unchanged.
- Added `organize_sidebar` as a thin writer of a folder-v2 company's `agents/sections.md` (thin writers W2, `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-COMPANY-STATE-FILES.md` §5): sections are named by name, the company's notes before the first section are kept, a change already in the sections saves nothing however the file is laid out, and a change saves under the tool call's receipt with the version read; a refused or conflicting save shows the sections as read. Snapshot-v1 runs are unchanged.
- Added `manage_todo` as a thin writer of a folder-v2 agent's todo files (thin writers W1, `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-COMPANY-STATE-FILES.md` §5): a run whose context names its agent's folder gets a folder client over the object gateway's folder route (the provider service passes `folderBytes`), and its todos are `agents/<handle>/todos/<todoKey>.md`. `upsert`, `done` and `cancel` save the file under the tool call's receipt with the version read (a changed file comes back as a conflict, redone under a new operation key); `list` pages the todo index (`qorpera_folder_agent_todos`, migration `20260929770000`, test 763: the run's plain read check, a company run that is not a worker, read authority on its todos folder and on each todo's file), which the commit updates with the files, so a page releases nothing; `todoKey` reads that todo's file and releases it with the result. A refusal before any save (a closed todo, a missing one, a file that does not parse) is a `CloudToolRefusal`, which the tool boundary now relays as `REFUSED: <reason>` instead of an unknown outcome; the tool-result instructions say what it means. Snapshot-v1 runs are unchanged.
- Added the founder's sender-name rule for company mail (decision of 1 October 2026, migration `20260928319810`): `send_mail` is refused when the agent's name, the company's name or the mailbox's local part reads as Qorpera (case, separators, accents, full width and look-alike letters or digits folded), and `qorpera_create_company_mailbox` reserves such local parts. The AI line and `Auto-Submitted` on replies only stay as they are.
- Added sending from company mailboxes (Q1 step M2b; contract section 19, item 9). The agent tool `send_mail` sends a plain-text email from one of the company's live mailboxes as the agent: from "<agent> at <company>" at the mailbox's address, to at most 10 addresses in `to` and `cc`, and a reply to a received email (`inReplyTo`, its deliveryId) joins that email's thread and carries `Auto-Submitted: auto-replied`. The automatic review checks every email first as an outside action. The service-role `qorpera_send_agent_mail` queues it, with a last paragraph saying an AI agent wrote and sent it, in `qorpera_company_mail_outbox`; the same tool call again is the same email. Mail loops are guarded: received mail the mail door marks `automated` (Auto-Submitted, Precedence, List-Id) is never answered, and a company never emails its own mailboxes. The Edge Function `mail-outbox`, called each minute by pg_cron with a Supabase secret key, claims a batch under a lease and sends it through Resend with the platform's key (idempotency key: the email's id; the address is fixed at the first attempt), retrying timeouts, rate limits and server errors. Sending is built switched off (`qorpera_mail_settings.sending`), within each company's hourly (60) and daily (300) limits on recipients; email from a removed or paused mailbox or of a company being deleted is cancelled, email not sent within a day fails, an attempted one says it may have gone out, and texts are cleared seven days after they settle. `list_hooks` and the wake protocol name the tool. Migration `20260928309740`.
- Added folder receipts to the runtime's tool operations (thin writers W0, core C1c-2b): an operation's effect may return the folder commit that carried its receipt (`folderCommitId`), which the company runtime's control plane completes through `qorpera_folder_complete_run_receipt` (`completeFolderToolOperation`, optional on the interface); a replayed receipt that names its commit is the operation's revision, and a folder-v2 completion's `folder_commit_id` is checked (in the lower case SQL echoes). A folder commit is refused beside another revision or on an operation that declared none, and `completesFolderCommits` lets a writer check before it commits; the company runtime's control plane must complete folder receipts. Nothing returns a folder commit until the thin writers do.
- Added the folder core's byte path, part 2 (core C1d-1b): `HttpCloudCompanyFolderBytes`, the production `CloudCompanyFolderBytes` for a folder-v2 run. It stages a file as a canonical version-2 manifest under an upload intent (open, put what the catalog lacks through the object gateway's folder route, renew a long upload, seal) and reads a file back chunk by chunk, each checked against its hash. The gateway's 423 is `stopped`, its 409 `contended`, and a 503 (busy) is asked twice more. A read checks that the chunk sizes add up to the file's before fetching, reads into one buffer, and checks the whole file's hash; a file over 256 MiB is `invalid_arguments` before anything is sent. `CloudCompanyFolderBytes.put` takes the executable bit, which the manifest carries; `qorpera_folder_open_upload`, `_seal_upload` and `_renew_upload` join the Company File control's allow-lists. Nothing constructs it until the thin writers do.
- Added company mailboxes, receiving (Q1 step M2a; contract section 19, item 9). Owners and admins create a mailbox, `<name>@<the platform's mail domain>`, with `qorpera_create_company_mailbox`; each is a platform-owned hook with the proof `mail`, paused and removed like any hook, and removing it frees the address. The Edge Function `mail-intake` checks the mail provider's (Resend's) signed `email.received` webhook, fetches the whole message and hands it to the service-role `qorpera_receive_mail`, which stores it as a verified `mail.received` delivery within the hook's minute and the company's hourly budget. Routines wake on a mailbox with the event trigger, and agents read a message with `read_event`; `list_hooks` and the wake protocol describe mailboxes. Company mail is built switched off and without a domain (`qorpera_mail_settings`). Migration `20260928299650`. Sending follows (M2b).
- Added batch observation writes and scheduled observation retention (migration `20260928319370`). `qorpera_record_runtime_observation_batch(run, observations)` (service role) records up to 50 events of one run in one call; each goes through `qorpera_record_runtime_observation` in order, so validation, vocabulary, references and replay are unchanged, and a refused event (`22023`, `42501`, `23505`) gets a refusal in its slot while the others are recorded; it waits at most 1 s for a lock (`55P03`, retried by the runtime), so a retry never queues behind its own abandoned first attempt. `qorpera_run_runtime_observation_retention()` deletes what the 30-day purge deletes (terminal jobs only), 200 at a time and at most 5,000 a call; the migration schedules it with pg_cron every five minutes when pg_cron is installed (`qorpera_schedule_runtime_observation_retention()` does it later, idempotently).
- Added the folder core's byte path, part 1 (core C1d-1a, `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-COMPANY-FOLDER-C1D.md`): a folder-v2 actor stages a file under an upload intent (`qorpera_folder_open_upload`, `_authorize_object_upload` per chunk, `_confirm_object`, `_seal_upload` into a version-2 proof with the media type and executable bit, `_renew_upload`) and reads bytes it may read (`qorpera_folder_authorize_object_download`), with no profile row, fence, company_files or job lock. An actor references only bytes it possesses: bytes it put under its own intent, or bytes it may already read. The commit now holds the manifest and every chunk of each file it writes, creates or copies (step 6) and refuses a reference whose chunks are missing, that the actor does not possess, or that disagrees with its version-2 manifest. A folder-v2 company's usage has its own self-seeding row with a soft quota, and replication of its objects takes no fence. Snapshot-v1 paths are unchanged (their definitions are pinned by digest). Nothing calls the new entry points until the gateway route and runtime adapter (C1d-1b). Migration `20260928279520`, tests 715, 761 and 762.
- Added event wakes (Q1 step W2; contract section 19, items 1, 4, 5 and 7; acceptance item 33 of section 21). An agent's routine may listen to one of its company's hooks: `manage_routine` takes the trigger kind `event` with the hook's id and, optionally, up to 20 event types. Verified deliveries wake it once per burst: the wake carries up to 20 events in the room the rest of the wake leaves, bodies cut by their JSON bytes (`wake.payload.events`, `more`), and the rest wake it again. A wake that ends without reading its events is retried after 1, 4, 16 and 64 minutes, then its first event is left behind. Quiet hours, pauses, expiry and the pinned sponsor apply as to every routine, and events are outside content in the wake protocol. New tools `list_hooks` (the company's hooks; addresses only in runs an owner or admin started) and `read_event` (a whole event body in pieces, the agent's own company only); the automatic review treats both as reads. Migration `20260928269290`: listener cursors with a watermark, a drain in the materializer, the wake reader's events, `qorpera_list_agent_event_hooks`, `qorpera_read_agent_event` and the intake's `public_url` setting. Today's routine rows (`snapshot-v1` companies) take the trigger.
- Added upstream revoke of connected-account tokens (company delete D6, migration `20260928269500`): Disconnect, a member's removal and a company's deletion queue each version of a Gmail, Drive or GitHub connection in `qorpera_connector_token_revocations`; the broker that sealed it (a company broker's `CloudConnectorTokenRevocationUpkeep`, or the shared vault for each company it serves) claims its entries once a minute, opens each envelope and cancels the token with `CloudConnectorTokenRevoker` (Google `POST /revoke`, GitHub's single-token `DELETE /applications/{client_id}/token`; typed tokens are `not_ours`), recording the outcome or a fixed error phrase. A Google account still connected in a living company is left alone (`kept_shared`, founder's option A); only a Google secret's current version is revoked (older ones are `superseded`), and a disconnected Google entry waits ten minutes. GitHub answers other than 204 are checked with GitHub's token check. The finisher revokes a deleting company's connector secrets, waits for its queue up to six hours and marks the rest `abandoned` before the purge. `composeCloudConnectorService` returns the revoker.
- Added the folder core's run client (core C3, `CloudCompanyFolderClient`): `read`, `readText`, `list`/`listAll`, `commit` and `release` over `qorpera_folder_read`, `_list`, `_commit` and `_record_release`, as the run (`{kind:'run', runId, jobId, runnerId, assignmentId}`), with typed unit outcomes, same-key retries of QRC01, 40P01, 23505, 55P03 and lost answers, typed failures (`stopped`, `unavailable`, `read_revoked`, `contended`, `unknown`), and each structured file's parse from the shared parser. Byte staging and reads go through `CloudCompanyFolderBytes`, which core C1d provides. The four RPCs join the Company File control's allow-list with room for a full listing page and a change set's outcomes, and their errors keep their SQLSTATE, categorized by code, with SQL's message kept off the error's text (`CloudCompanyFolderRpcError`). Nothing uses the client yet; the thin writers follow core C1c-2b and C1d. Test 760 pins the SQL shapes it parses.
- Added `qorpera_prepare_routine_login(binding)` (service role, F2-1b part B): sets up the login a run's requester pays with for unattended work, as an agent's routine save does today, and returns it (`qorpera.routine-login/v1`, `login` null when they have none). The routine file writer will call it before its commit, which reads that login without a lock. Migration `20260928269440`, test 751.
- Added event intake (Q1 step W1a; contract section 19, "events wake agents"). Owners and admins create and manage hooks, each a public address where another service posts events (`qorpera_create_event_hook`, `qorpera_set_event_hook_status`, `qorpera_list_event_deliveries`); every member lists them with `qorpera_list_event_hooks`, addresses for owners and admins only. The Edge Function `event-intake` (`POST /functions/v1/event-intake/<address>`, no JWT) hands each request to the service-role `qorpera_receive_event`, which verifies an address-proven delivery at once and records a duplicate by the sender's id or, without one, by the same body within 10 minutes. The intake is built switched off (`qorpera_event_intake_settings`) and bounded per hook (bodies of 64 KiB and 60 deliveries a minute by default, at most 256 KiB and 120), per company (50 hooks and an hourly budget of stored deliveries and bytes) and by the switch; a refused request writes nothing. Hooks exist for `snapshot-v1` companies until the folder core's writer lands, and `qorpera_folder_enable` refuses a company with hooks. `qorpera_purge_event_deliveries` clears bodies and headers after 7 days and rows after 30. Nothing wakes agents yet (W2). Migration `20260928259260`; design `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-EVENT-INTAKE.md`.
- Added the shared vault's deploy (Vault By Default, part 3): `qorpera-shared-vault.service` and `shared-vault-secrets.py` in the broker package. The script makes the principal secret and master key on the vault host as root-only files and prints only hashes; it hands them, with the vault's database login, runtime token, connector clients and coding executor token, from node 1 to node 2 once, host to host, on a ticket that never leaves node 2, and checks node 1's digest before writing anything; every write is staged and renamed, so a failed step leaves nothing behind. The shared vault runs as its own user with its own login helper token (`helper-token`); the helper takes an optional `qorpera-model-login-helper-vault-token` credential and answers a sign-in only to the client (company brokers or the vault) that began it. The login helper's config takes optional `signInLimit` and `signInCompanyLimit` (defaults 6 and 2). Runbook: `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-LOGIN-VAULT.md`.
- Added the shared vault's broker mode (Vault By Default, part 2): config `qorpera.cloud-secret-broker-service/v3` names a broker principal and loads `qorpera-cloud-secret-principal` and `qorpera-cloud-secret-master-keys` instead of one company's capability and keyring. `SharedCloudSecretBrokerCompanies` registers every new company the principal lists, serves each registered company with its own broker, login vault, sign-ins and usage on its own authority (capability derived from the principal secret by `sharedBrokerCapability`), picks each request's company from the path or `?company=`, and runs sign-in collection and upkeep per company. `CloudSecretKeyring` accepts master keys whose per-company keys are derived with HKDF-SHA256. One-company brokers (config v2) are unchanged, except that connector sign-ins now allow at most 32 flows in flight per company (of 256).
- Added vault-by-default registration (founder decisions 2026-09-28, part 1): a broker principal (`qorpera_cloud_secret_broker_principals`) is the shared vault's identity; broker-role `qorpera_broker_principal_companies` lists the companies it serves and the companies created after its start time with no authority, and `qorpera_register_company_with_broker_principal` creates such a company's authority row (the principal's origin and key id, the derived capability hash) and switches it to vault sign-in (only the call that creates the authority does; two copies registering the same company at once get the same answer; a capability hash naming another company's authority is refused with `22023`; read committed only). The companies answer names the principal's `keyId`. Existing broker functions serve registered companies unchanged. Migration `20260928248930`.
- Added the vault switching off a member's old node Codex login by itself once they signed in to the vault with the same ChatGPT account (Stateless Compute step 1 upkeep, founder decision 2026-09-28). Each upkeep pass records the account digest of new vault logins, then, while the company takes sign-ins in the vault, moves the member's pins from an older node connection whose current login is the same account to the vault connection (`qorpera_move_member_login_pins`) and, once no job, lease, queued wake, queued turn or queued change review names it, makes the same updates as Disconnect. Other accounts, other members, Claude logins, newer node logins and companies switched back are left alone. Migration `20260928243500`.
- Added the coding storage helper's `observe` action: per attached session, how many commands still run, the slice's processor time and the watchdog lease (generation and time left), published root-owned at `/run/qorpera-coding/observation.json` (`qorpera.coding-guest-observation/v2`), with each session's running commands (turn and first 300 bytes) beside it in `observation-<workerId>.json`. Every file stays within 64 KiB, one read of LXD's file API. Added `manage_worker` action `archive` for coding sessions.
- Added idle renewal in the vault (Stateless Compute step 1 upkeep): every 5 minutes a broker with a login helper renews its company's logins past half their pass one at a time, so a login nobody used no longer lapses before its next run. `qorpera_take_idle_model_login_renewal` (broker role) takes the renewal lock with a run's own conditions, only for a login behind an active pointer of an active connection; the renewal, save and release are a run's own. Migration `20260928236000`.
- Added vault usage reads (Stateless Compute step 1, change 2C): an account refresh of an identity whose Codex usage is the vault's (a vault login and no Codex login a node would read) goes to `<broker origin>/v1/model-logins/<company>/account-refresh`; an identity still moving keeps the node path. The broker reads the refresh with the node route's checks and up to 8 current vault login versions, reads their usage with the pass at once (the node's own `wham/usage` reader), records the display metadata for the connection version that uses each, and completes the refresh; no helper or machine is involved. Migration `20260928185000`.
- Added members signing in to the vault (Stateless Compute step 1, change 2B): for a company an operator switched with `qorpera_set_model_login_sign_in` (refused until vault login runs and vault usage reads exist), the login gateway chooser sends a Codex sign-in to `<broker origin>/v1/model-logins/<company>/`. The broker checks the unchanged web request against the database's pending sign-in; the login helper runs the node's own `codex login --device-auth` driver in its isolation (sign-ins bound to company, identity and capability; 6 at once, 2 per company); the broker collects finished sign-ins in the background, seals each as version 1 and completes it in one transaction (pointer capsule, vault login, connection version; no node key backup). The helper checks its sandbox with the pinned Codex at start. Migration `20260928184000`. Claude sign-ins and unswitched companies are unchanged.
- Added vault login runs (Stateless Compute step 1, change 3): a run whose capsule names a vault login asks `POST /v1/model-logins` for the login copy, keeps it fresh from the vault every minute (atomic file swap) and never saves a login back. `QORPERA_NATIVE_LOGIN_CUSTODY=vault` starts a native host without a capsule key, epoch file or key backup, only after the cloud confirms the custody. One-run guests with `company_runtime.login_custody = "vault"` refuse an identity volume or identity material and hand the host empty key and epoch sources. Migration `20260928180600` adds `qorpera_compute_identity_login_in_vault` and lets claim and placement skip the identity-home rule for such identities.
- Added the vault's model login helper (Stateless Compute step 1, change 2, part A): a separate `qorpera-model-login-helper` service that renews a Codex login with the pinned official Codex (`codex app-server`, `account/read` with `refreshToken`) in the existing isolation launcher, in a private in-memory Codex home it wipes after each renewal, and answers the broker over a Unix socket with a bearer token. A refused renewal is read from Codex's own error line with Codex's own rule (401, a refresh-token code in the JSON body, or a 400 `invalid_grant`); anything else may be retried. Budgets nest inside the vault's 35 s (Codex 25 s, helper 28 s, broker wait 30 s), and a renewal once asked runs to its budget whatever the caller does. The helper checks bwrap, node, its shipped isolation entry point and the pinned Codex at start, re-checks the binary before each launch, and a stop lets renewals in flight finish. The broker renews through it when given the optional `qorpera-cloud-secret-login-helper` credential. `npm run pack:model-login-helper` packages it; CI now also runs the broker and helper package tests.

- Added model logins in the vault (Stateless Compute step 1, change 1): the cloud secret broker keeps a member's Codex login as immutable envelope-encrypted versions and serves `POST /v1/model-logins` under the runtime token. A run the native lease validator would accept (its conditions read without locks, company pause included) receives the current version's known fields without its renewal token; a host that already holds the current version receives no login. Past half its pass life, a login is renewed by the one caller the database hands that login's renewal lock (one row; nothing company-wide is locked; after a failure the next try waits 1, 2, 4 ... minutes, at most an hour), and the renewal is saved only by the lock holder on the version it renewed from, for a later pass, idempotently after a lost answer; a refused or unsaved renewal marks the login for a new sign-in. Nothing asks for a vault login until later changes.
- Added agent skills in the SKILL.md shape Grok Bots, Claude and Codex share: a company library (`skills/<name>/SKILL.md`) and each agent's own library (`<agent folder>/skills/<name>/SKILL.md`, the agent folder of contract 5.6 from `@qorpera/cloud-client/cloud-agent-folder`, `agents/<staffAgentId>` until agents have named folders), both versioned Company File objects. Every turn composes a skill catalog beside memory (own skills described, the company library described while small and named once it grows); `read_skill` loads a body in fitted pages with its exact version, `search_skills` searches names and descriptions across both libraries, and `update_skill` creates, updates (keeping other front-matter keys) or deletes a skill through the governed gateway; update and delete name the version the agent read, so another writer's edit is never overwritten unseen, and refusals come back as typed receipts. Only company-audience runs save skills. A member calls a skill as `/skill-name` in a message; the agent loads it before acting. SKILL.md parsing lives in `@qorpera/cloud-client/cloud-skill-file`. Migration `20260928032000_runtime_agent_skills` adds the three tools to the observation vocabulary; skill files need no path rule of their own.
- Added a service-only host inventory view including managed, infrastructure and unmanaged machines. Pages retain one observation's entries, preserve original times and reported states, and mark stale, superseded or unavailable current state as unknown. Physical inventory cannot release allocations or imply free capacity.
- Added a service-only paginated compute-request view covering office/login/native demand before allocation, recorded placement, capacity refusals and typed dispatch failures. New assignment failure transitions retain database time; historical missing times remain unknown. Queries expose no raw errors or capabilities and never retry admission or release resources.
- Added an operator-only node query separating distinct allocated computers, companies and current associated members from observed human computer activity. Explicit bounded windows use retained viewer redemption and admitted control inputs, deduplicate people, preserve historical placement and return unknown when activity is unobserved. Membership, agents, input execution and continuous presence are not inferred from those observations.
- Added a paginated operator view of current allocated computers, exact placement, pending capacity/refusals and fresh physical state. Job leases and shared screen/resource records distinguish running computers with active jobs, without recorded active jobs, and uncertain activity; none of these states releases capacity. Missing health and human-activity measurements remain explicit.
- Added an operator-only retained computer lifecycle query separating admission, service, VM boot requests and company/node allocation records. Database receipt times remain distinct from estimated physical history; service stop never implies VM closure or released capacity.
- Added separate immutable retention for screen observations first delivered after authority loss. Exact historical startup and record-hash receipts support outbox recovery without changing canonical history, work grants or usage. Bounded operator queries expose confirmed prefixes and unconfirmed suffixes, including never-registered lifetimes, while omitting private challenges.
- Added private durable screen-event custody outside model-visible mounts, with exact replay after reopen and independent bounded SQL delivery. Production launch and confirmed drain no longer wait for diagnostic export. Backed-up heartbeats are omitted without filling unknown intervals; unconfirmed records are retained. Guest-destruction retention remains incomplete.
- Added service-only screen lifetime discovery and bounded usage queries. Original heartbeats supply at most 15 seconds of estimated readiness; stale gaps remain unknown. An exact provider stop acknowledgement bounds a lost guest's lifetime without fabricating a guest close event or releasing its allocation. Private challenges remain hidden, and retained event history is unchanged.
- Connected the production Linux guest's shared/private screen lifecycle to its admitted service invocation. Ordered, bounded publication preserves uncertain event bytes and original times; actual readiness and physical reaping determine ready/closed records. Viewer and native-session closure remain independent. Heartbeat failures retry without stopping authorized work; local recovery and bounded screen-hour projection are described in the subsequent entries.
- Extended `company_write` to copy an explicitly selected completed shared export into the live Company File. Binary bytes use the existing manifest verifier and atomic mutation/proposal gateway, with exact path versions, immutable source provenance and a source retention hold. Ordinary writes become live; protected writes still require human approval.

- Added binary Company File imports selected by exact live path version. The existing cloud path gateway authorizes the source and tracks its read set; the adapter verifies its immutable manifest, every chunk and assembled file hash before workbench installation. Original native receipts retain source artifacts and complete object closure without changing canonical files.

- Added explicit imports of completed shared export assets into the workbench. Current source/office authority is checked around object reads, broker-private staging keeps partial files out of agent namespaces, and atomic no-overwrite publication records the original native receipt and retained source provenance.
