Unknown or changed public destinations are never deleted as cleanup.

- Added explicit versioned owner/admin desktop grants without widening existing shell grants. SQL admits bounded desktop actions and desktop screenshots through the current native receipt, screen, grant and privacy checks; each tool requires its own capability.

- Added explicit workbench-to-object file exports through the original native effect receipt. Descriptor-bound Linux reads reject aliases and changed files; the authenticated immutable object gateway stores bounded bytes before SQL publishes source provenance and a retention hold. Other transfer directions remain separate work.

- Added run-authorized Company File presence to the headless native host. Signed, expiring observations carry opaque policy-scoped path tokens over the existing private channel; current reader and publisher authority gates relevant context. File-event prompts and confirmed file reads include lossy presence without changing canonical authority or creating activity events. Private-audience runs do not publish into the shared channel.

- Extended protected boot configuration to stopped predecessors from later boots, including intervening service successors. The helper reads their actual typed invocation and acknowledged stop from sealed history, rechecks persistent public boot records against the archive, and preserves the permanent preparation receipt and mutable workbench. Current-boot credentials remain immutable; no prior `/run` files or preparation replay are needed. Provider composition for repeated VM cycles remains required.
- Added typed same-boot restart after a retained-boot activation. The root helper preserves the original boot intent, receipt and credentials while binding a distinct service successor to its acknowledged predecessor stop. Immutable claims prevent repeated unknown starts; nonce inspections and later stops select the actual per-activation completion. Disposable Linux and actual-systemd probes cover repeated restarts and stop recovery. Provider dispatch and SQL activation for this lane remain separate work.
- Added explicit stop evidence selection for the first activation after a retained boot. Stop binds the new boot's public start, configuration and adoption records, seals a separate activation archive and refuses missing or mixed history. It reads neither secret credentials nor the mutable workbench; provider recovery after the new boot remains separate work.
- Added protected first service start after retained-boot configuration. The helper verifies original adoption, shutdown history, current credentials and retained directory custody, requires a never-started unit and empty cgroup, and records its claim before dispatch. Ambiguous starts are observed without retry; fresh inspection binds the same invocation. Provider admission and combined real-systemd acceptance remain required.
- Added protected per-boot credential custody selected by systemd's kernel boot ID. Initial configuration publishes separate immutable copies while preserving original preparation and credentials; start/restart verify both sets under the existing flock. Missing or altered acknowledged files cannot be repaired by replay. New-boot adoption and admission remain separate work.
- Reused the portable stop-history parser for root sealing and reads, preserving immutable manifest bytes and all Linux custody checks.
- Added immutable root-disk stop history selected by boot and activation. Protected stop seals bounded evidence before publishing completion; interrupted publication can finish from identical retained facts without repeating stop. Missing or altered sealed history is refused, and historical reads survive loss of the boot-scoped inbox. Restart requires the intact predecessor archive; retained history also prevents redispatch after loss of both temporary stop records. The archive grants no new boot or runtime authority.
- Added protected same-boot service restart under the existing kernel flock. Immutable per-activation completions and one child claim per predecessor preserve earlier start/stop evidence through repeated cycles. The helper verifies retained stop, preparation and credentials before one start, recovers unknown outcomes by observation and supports independent nonce inspection. Subsequent stops select immutable activation history; SQL admission remains separate.
- Added provider-only SQL successor admission after acknowledged service stop, retaining activation history and one current invocation on the same allocation. Preparation and activation preserve private state, reject later drain/privacy changes and forbid historical invocation reuse. Protected successor start and provider worker composition remain required.
- Added provider-only SQL physical-stop challenges and acknowledgement. A five-second window binds fresh inspection to the current office revision and drains; expiry is rechecked after potentially blocking cleanup. Acknowledgement ends viewers/resources, records the stop time and survives competing calls or lost replies while preserving private state, closed admission and charged capacity. Historical replay leaves newer drains untouched; provider worker composition remains required.
- Added protected whole-service stop under the existing kernel flock, with retained revocation/configuration/startup evidence, an immutable claim before dispatch, and fresh nonce-bound inspection. PID 1 namespace and descriptor-bound cgroup checks require all descendants gone; first-start replay is fenced after the stop claim. Recovery observes unknown dispatches without repeating stop and never reads or rewrites the mutable workbench. Provider acknowledgement and successor activation remain separate work.
- Added an explicit SQL lifecycle revocation fence for the activated guest. It closes admission before recovery, records action/time/identity, preserves pending screen drains and private sign-in state, and survives lost replies and database restart. It stays available during execution pause or provider preparation-disable. Provider recovery workflow composition and replacement activation remain unimplemented; screen acknowledgements alone cannot complete this transition or release capacity.
- Packaged guest image installation and explicit boot-time display/browser/capture custody plus a private sign-in tmpfs mount. The ordinary service waits for tmpfiles setup; installed systemd references satisfy the existing root preparation controller. The installer leaves the guest unconfigured and inactive, preserving adoption and activation authority.
- Added durable agent waiters for accepted native office starts, including requests coalesced into member intents. Activation resumes confirmed work through the existing agent wake queue after the original run completes. Continuation retains attributed parent inputs, receipts, sends, causal identity and publication policy; current grants, generation, staff, sponsor, pause and capacity checks remain authoritative. No human input or duplicate public reply is created.
- Added protected ordinary-service start and independent inspection under the existing kernel flock. The helper rechecks completed preparation, exact installed configuration/credentials and fixed systemd units, persists a claim before starting, and retains the measured invocation/PID/cgroup. Recovery never repeats an unknown start; inspection cannot start a unit or reconstruct a missing acknowledgement. Provider activation still requires fresh inspection and the matching SQL readiness handshake.
- Added restricted startup authority and readiness reporting bound to the installed configuration, guest boot and systemd invocation. Native TLS and viewer listeners bind with work admission closed until the provider activates the matching invocation and initial supervision succeeds. Startup rejection or cancellation drains both listeners and the guest; provider activation RPCs remain outside the office registry transport.
- Added a protected configuration helper that verifies the original completed preparation, exact adoption receipt, current boot and inactive ordinary unit before installing six fixed service credential files. It validates configuration binding, token separation and TLS material, publishes root-only files without overwrite and retains hash-only completion evidence. Partial installation resumes the same claim; acknowledged missing or changed files are refused. The provider delivers the shared bounded request and verifies installed bytes; cloud activation and ordinary service startup remain separate phases.
- Published the copied-state adoption receipt atomically from protected root preparation control after the completed invocation and report are verified. The receipt remains outside guest-owned roots, uses root:root 0444 and is bound into the completion. Replay preserves identical bytes; an acknowledged missing or altered receipt is refused without repair. Ordinary startup still requires fresh active SQL authority.
- Added protected root preparation control with an existing process-owned kernel flock, atomic no-overwrite request/claim/completion publication, actual-boot binding and fixed systemd inspection/start commands. It compares installed units with packaged copies, requires ordinary office inactivity, and correlates a fresh completed invocation with the exact protected report. Recovery inspects the original claim without repeating start; protected service configuration, activation and actual systemd acceptance remain outstanding.
- Included systemd's invocation ID in preparation reports and retained the completed oneshot invocation for provider correlation. The standalone process refuses an absent/invalid invocation ID; the provider must still verify the actual unit completion and protected report.
- Added a separate read-only Linux guest preparation process and unit. It loads a protected bounded request, verifies current boot, kernel limits, writer identity and persistent mount, and compares the exact copied tree across two descriptor-based passes. Its report contains hashes and identity only; it opens no office listeners and cannot activate the office.
- Extracted headless cloud execution and custody services from the desktop package.
- Ported shared office computer state, leasing, tools, guest automation, desktop supervision, adoption planning, and private viewer components with their tests.
- Added admitted tool-session resource cleanup before successful completion.
- Added durable office registration and native screen lease control, with immutable workbench identity, versioned staff grants, expiry fencing, and exact cleanup after revocation.
- Connected the explicit office-session factory to native run admission, the existing effect ledger, and mandatory cleanup. Provider activation and the remaining Step 4 integrations remain gated.
- Added receipt-bound office work leases, company-audience enforcement, monotonic guest cancellation, and acknowledged takeover/drain challenges. Unknown effects retain occupancy until physical drain is proven.
- Added owner-controlled browser-profile consent, native grant checks, closed browser effect admission, and whole-office sign-in privacy transitions that require confirmed viewer shutdown, resource drain and private surface preparation. Consent is never inherited from a subscription or restored after a changed login.
- Added durable single-use viewer tickets, one member driver per screen, sequenced input admission, revocation checks and exact cancellation that retains occupancy until physical drain acknowledgement.
- Added the TLS WebSocket viewer gateway, active stream privacy barriers, bounded registry transport and service-lifetime reconciliation of expired viewers. Recovered private state does not replay sign-in preparation or rewind newer screen fences.
- Connected native service configuration to the private TLS office transport using a distinct systemd credential. The office re-derives the admitted native principal, consumes the existing effect receipt once, and confirms physical session cleanup before releasing occupancy.
- Added office service lifetime composition for the native and viewer listeners, shared action gate, privacy and drain supervisors. Startup waits for reconciliation; lost supervision and cancellation close both transports and await guest cleanup, including input admissions in flight.
- Added the Linux command and screenshot adapter with descriptor-bound workbench/home mounts, isolated process/network namespaces, zero host capabilities, finite kernel resource ceilings, bounded output and confirmed descendant cleanup. A disposable Linux probe exercises real Bash, X11, screenshots, cancellation and mount/authority failures; office guest composition remains gated.
- Added the contained Linux XFCE/TigerVNC desktop launcher with private Unix X11/RFB sockets, existing-state admission, persistent settings and viewer-independent lifetime. Real Linux probes cover two displays, shared workbench access, independent pointers, restart and detached process cleanup.
- Added contained persistent Chromium with broker-private DevTools pipes, an authority-checked public HTTPS relay, managed profile/UI policy and bounded physical shutdown. The Linux probe exercises real page tools, renderer sandbox state, profile isolation, cookie persistence, revocation and cleanup.
- Added a fresh private sign-in X server and contained browser lifetime with a dedicated empty tmpfs, exact owner/profile viewer binding, independent authority renewal, physical revocation cleanup and preserved profile state. The service-only SQL check rejects undrained screens, changed profiles and removed/rejoined owners; entry acknowledgement rechecks this authority while exit cleanup remains available.

- Added whole Linux office guest composition with receipt-admitted preparation, idle shared-desktop authority renewal, persistent desktops, private recovery and tracked physical drains. The Linux service factory connects guest failure to TLS/WSS shutdown and preserves cleanup errors.

- Added mandatory Linux service preflight for protected adoption receipts, current boot identity, persistent workbench/private tmpfs mounts, kernel limits and current cloud machine allocation/image/bootstrap binding. The real Linux probe covers ownership, aliases, stale identity, mount mismatch and preserved working files. Provider/NAS attestation and activation remain separate integration work.

- Bound office screenshots to the original native operation envelope and receipt, staged them through the authenticated immutable object gateway, and published artifact identity with retention holds. Completed capture reads recheck run, audience, grants, privacy version and bytes.
- Added authorized native screenshot pixels, exact call/envelope/hash binding, capture metadata in context-view lineage and current read-set authorization after compaction. Capture-enabled startup requires the new native capability. The pinned Linux release executable passes twelve image/compaction cases and the existing tool-boundary probes against an offline scripted provider; combined physical guest, SQL and object-gateway acceptance remains required.
- Added the headless office process entrypoint, closed protected configuration, bounded descriptor-based systemd credential reads, TLS validation and signal-owned cleanup. A separate local packager includes the service unit, browser helper, pinned Playwright resources and existing artwork. Provider activation/adoption and combined physical service acceptance remain required.
- Added native `computer_start` discovery while the shared office is offline. The existing native receipt ledger records generation/grant-bound cloud demand without contacting the guest; accepted requests survive the native session ending. Current authority still gates result release, including activation races. Provider activation and the subsequent agent wake remain separate lifecycle work.
- Added durable office start intents, shared machine reservations and fresh provider preparation checks, with exact image/bootstrap/configuration binding. Founder cancellation and revoked requester authority fence preparation without releasing retained capacity. Member status recovery exposes only the closed current-generation intent.
- Bound office start reservation/preparation to an enrolled provider ID and immutable policy for the office generation. Added provider-only status discovery without exposing enrollment through the office runtime registry transport; cancelled/disabled work remains visible for reconciliation without granting physical effects.
- Preserved runtime adoption/layout exports while moving their pure mapping and validation into shared contracts, so provider migration uses the same screen and profile identity rules.
- Added service-only adoption authority checking exact screen generations, current staff access, prepared browser grants, owner membership incarnation and confirmed surface drains in the provider preparation transaction. Copying a profile creates no grants and does not activate the office.

- Added protected retained-workbench adoption and configuration for a distinct resumed boot, binding the original adoption, shutdown archive and pre-boot intent. Root checks retain directory descriptors and measure the current mount without comparing mutable files to their original inventory. Current-boot receipts and credentials are published under the kernel flock; acknowledged missing files are never repaired. The normal Linux boundary accepts the new receipt while preserving current SQL checks. Provider lifecycle composition and ordinary-service admission remain separate work.
- A member's turn in a conversation runs on Codex's Fast service tier (`service_tier = "fast"`, sent as the priority tier) unless that conversation turned Fast off. The model policy declares `fastMode: "thread"`; the kernel pins the tier (`fast` or `standard`) on the run at admission (migration `20260927161500_thread_fast_mode`) and returns it in the run's company context (`serviceTier`), not in the shared tool identity that company computers also read. The host passes it to Codex (standard as `service_tier = "default"`, which no model catalog default overrides), refuses a Codex whose configuration did not apply it, and refuses a context without a tier. Routines, coworker wakes, coding turns and other providers run at standard speed.
- Added machines for workers (2026-09-28): an agent's background workers get the seven `machine_*` tools with their agent's authority, without `notify`. A worker waits inside `machine_start` (while its machine starts) and `machine_read` (until its command ends) with `waitSeconds` (1 to 300), looking every 5 s at the kernel's new `status` action of `qorpera_native_machine_manage` instead of at the machine; a stopped worker's wait ends with `WORK_STOPPED`. Conversation runs keep `notify` and do not wait. Migration `20260928259055`.
- Added the machines gate (2026-09-29): a company run is offered the `machine_*` tools only when `qorpera_company_machines_available()` answers true, which it does once some node runs an enabled machine executor. Any other answer, or a failed call, keeps the tools away and the run goes on. Migration `20260928259350`.
- Each machine command records the member its run works for (migration `20260928449950_runtime_machine_command_requester`): `qorpera_company_machine_commands.requested_by_user_id`, NOT NULL, from the run identity's `requesterUserId` when the kernel first authorizes the command, as the machine itself records it. It always equals the requester of the command's run, so earlier commands take it from their run. Attribution only (computer hours are the company's); a worker's command records the member its parent run works for.
- The agent that started a coding session is asked, once each after 1, 3 and 7 days without a turn, whether the session can be archived (migration `20260928349850_runtime_coding_quiet_checkins`). The question arrives as one worker-completion report in the shape every runtime already reads (occurrence key `coding-quiet:…`); this runtime gives it its own short instruction instead of the report protocol. The agent archives with `manage_worker` action `archive` or leaves it; a new turn withdraws a question no run has taken; stopping a question run stops nothing else. The 30-day automatic archive stays.
- Company creation on the folder core (C2-create, P14a; migration `20260930010000`, SQL tests 856, 857, 858; deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-COMPANY-FOLDER-CREATION.md): the founding lobby's open, seed and ready do real work. Open makes the company on `folder-v2` with nothing seeded; Start records one creation; the object gateway's worker (P14b, off) claims it and calls `qorpera_folder_company_create`, one transaction with the platform's creation commit, the Co-Founder and its silent first run (the kickoff and its turn request; the wake comes with C4). Ready is a completed creation; the hub hides the company until then, and the lobby shows `start.companySetup`. Requeue and an operator's path for the proof company, owner role only. The vault's connection-file claim (P22a) skips a company whose creation is not made yet, since its creation writes `connections/`. Creation stays off until C5.
- A repeat of a call its run was cut off in answers `OUTCOME_UNKNOWN` (agent context tree W5b-1; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-AGENT-MEMORY-TREE.md` §12; no SQL). A resume settles the cut-off attempt's `executing` receipts as `outcome_unknown`, final (W5a-1, migration `20260928519120`), and the folder-v2 receipt begin answers a repeat of such a key with that state. The tool executor now takes that answer (with no result or revision) as settled: it links the receipt to the call's span, never runs the action, finishes the span `unconfirmed` / `ambiguous_effect` and throws `CloudToolOutcomeUnknown` (exported from `cloud-native-tool-boundary.ts`). The tool boundary answers it with status `unconfirmed` and the text `OUTCOME_UNKNOWN: …`: the action may or may not have happened, this key cannot run it again, so check, and repeat it only if it did not, under a new operation key. Before, the answer was `TOOL_OUTCOME_UNCONFIRMED`, whose rule (the same key, never a new one) would have kept the model on a key that can never run. `CLOUD_TOOL_RESULT_INSTRUCTIONS` names `OUTCOME_UNKNOWN` as the one exception to that rule. No live run reaches it yet: `qorpera_run_resumes_from_log` answers false until W5a-2.
- Account model logins (founder, 3 Nov 2026; migration `20260930548000`, SQL test 685; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-LOGIN-VAULT.md` "Account logins"): the shared vault keeps one Codex login per member and every company of the member that it serves uses it. `CloudSecretKeyring` derives a member's key from the vault's master key under its own label (`qorpera.member-kek/v1`, `sealMember`/`useMember`/`hasMemberKey`; an envelope bound to the member and the login). `CloudModelLoginVault` hands a run its member's account login (the issue answer names `scope: "account"` and the member), saves or gives back its renewal with the shared vault's own calls (`AccountModelLoginAuthority`, implemented by `PostgresCloudSecretBrokerPrincipal`), and runs the vault-wide account pass (`renewIdleAccount`, `recordAccountLogins`, `switchOffReplacedByAccountLogins`), which `SharedCloudSecretBrokerCompanies` runs once per upkeep cycle. `CloudModelLoginSignIns` completes an account sign-in under the member's key (`completeAccountModelLoginSignIn`), and `CloudModelLoginUsage` reads an account login's usage. The run's answer is unchanged.
- Company sites: `publish_site` and `unpublish_site` (founder, 3 Nov 2026; migration `20260930550000`, SQL test 552; `supabase/functions/site-serve`; `deploy/sites/`). An agent publishes a static website from the company folder (a folder whose `index.html` is the home page, or one `.html` file) to a private link, `https://sites.qorpera.com/<slug>/`, which visitors open with the site's password: made at the first publish, never shown to an agent, read, copied and changed by owners and admins in Settings → Sites. Only web files go out (HTML, CSS, JavaScript, JSON, text, images, fonts, audio, video, PDF; at most 500 files, 25 MiB, 10 MiB a file); hidden files, links and other types are left out and named. The tool reads the source with the run's own view of the folder before the automatic review (`prepare`), so the card names the link, the files and their size; a first publish, and a live site's publish from another folder, is a built-in access check (`siteAccessCheck`: an owner or admin approves it on a card unless a company Allow rule covers it, and the database publishes it only on that approval in the same run), any other update is reviewed as an outside effect, and taking a site offline as a deletion; the reviewer reads every path that goes out (`reviewContents`). The tool reads the files again after the review and refuses if they changed, then publishes in three database calls through the native host (`qorpera_site_publish_begin`, `_put` in batches of at most 4 MiB or one larger file, `_finish`; one open upload a site), which switch the site to the new version at once; unchanged files are copied in the database. `cloudSiteTools` is wired into a company agent's run (never a worker's); `NATIVE_HOST_CALLS`, the path control and the observation vocabulary (`publish_site`, `unpublish_site`) list the new calls. The edge function `site-serve` serves each file under a visitor's session path (`/<slug>/~<token>/…`) with a CSP sandbox (no `allow-same-origin`), never framed or indexed, behind a password page, reading only the bytes a request needs (none for HEAD, a range's for Range), with 7-day sessions and wrong passwords limited per network (an HMAC of the address, an IPv6 /64) and per slug; publishing and serving are switched off until `qorpera_site_settings.enabled` is set at rollout.
- Agents create GitHub repositories: `github_create_repository` (live company-building test, 3 November 2026: the Co-Founder had to ask the founder to create `wristband-shop` by hand; migration `20260930556000`, SQL test 557; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-STEP-7-CLOUD-CONNECTORS.md` "GitHub repositories"). A new GitHub catalog operation, `create_repository` v1 (`cloud-connector-catalog.ts`; adapter in `cloud-connector-github.ts`), takes `name`, an optional `owner` (an organization; left out, or the connected account's own login, it is the account itself), `description`, `private` (default true) and `auto_init` (default true: a README, so a default branch). It posts `/user/repos` or `/orgs/{owner}/repos` and answers the full name, visibility, default branch, links and creation time. GitHub's refusals are said plainly: a token that may not create repositories (403 or 404) answers that "the GitHub connection needs to be connected again with permission to create repositories", an organization's refusal that an owner of it must allow it or the connection be connected again, an unknown organization so, and a taken name in GitHub's own words ("name already exists on this account"). The automatic review shows "Create a GitHub repository" (or "Create a public GitHub repository") with the account or organization, who can see it, its description and what it starts with. It is an outside effect, and a public one is a built-in access check whatever the reviewer called it (`repositoryAccessCheck`, as `siteAccessCheck` for a site): an owner or admin approves it on a card unless a company Allow rule covers it. The migration adds the catalog row (`scripts/generate-cloud-connector-catalog.mjs` now renders into it; `scripts/test-gmail-draft-catalog.mjs` keeps reading the Gmail migration) and `qorpera_backfill_connector_operation_grants`, which issues the new operation to every agent that may open issues on a shared company GitHub connection, under the same issuer and expiry; later shares and hires issue it as every operation. A GitHub sign-in already holds the `repo` scope, so no connection has to be made again; a typed fine-grained token needs Administration write. Needs a release, in this order: the migration and the runtime bundle, then the secret broker (vault) build with the adapter, last. The public-repository check is the runtime's alone, and a run on an older bundle holds the tool once the migration ran; until the vault has the adapter it refuses every call of the operation.
- Private app links: `share_app` (Jonas, 3 Nov 2026: "Yes, build it"; migration `20260930562000`, SQL test 562; `deploy/sites/APP-PREVIEW.md` section 2). A company agent shares one port of one of the company's always-on machines at a private link, `https://<slug>-preview.qorpera.com/`, behind a site's password (an app site is a company site of kind `app`; members read the password in Settings → Sites, and `unpublish_site` turns the link off). Arguments `{slug, title?, machine, port}`. Before the automatic review (`prepare`) the tool asks `qorpera_site_share_check` (slug, machine, port) whether the machine can serve a link, whether the slug makes a new link or updates the company's own, its link, and whether that needs a review, and reads the machine list: a machine the share would refuse (`APP_MACHINE_NOT_FOUND`, `APP_MACHINE_NOT_ALWAYS_ON`), one that is not running (`APP_MACHINE_NOT_RUNNING`), a run without machines, a taken slug or one of the company's websites (`APP_NAME_TAKEN`) is refused there in plain words, with no card and no share. A new link, and one moved to another machine or port, is an outside effect and a built-in access check (`siteAccessCheck`: the card reads "Share a private, password-protected app link", "… from another machine" or "… from another port", with the link, "from machine", "port" and what it had until now); sharing the live link again on the same machine and port is not reviewed. Then `qorpera_site_share_app` issues a one-time claim, and the tool starts the link program on that machine itself, as `machine_run` starts a command (a `machine_run` receipt, no time limit, no notify; the run's machine gateway and operations reach `cloudSiteTools` from the company runtime): `curl -fsS <clientUrl> -o /root/.qorpera-app-link-<slug>.mjs.tmp && mv -f … && exec node /root/.qorpera-app-link-<slug>.mjs <slug> <port> <claim> <host suffix>`, each word checked to be a plain shell word, the link the check's and the program from the link's own host. The platform starts the program; the tool's answer never holds the claim (it is a word of the command, which the machine and the members' command list show, spent by the program within seconds and usable again only by that program). The answer names the link, the program's command id and state, and whether the password is new (the database's `newPassword`); a program that did not start, whose start could not be confirmed, or that ended at once is said so, with what to do. The database's refusals (no approval, switched off, a machine that changed meanwhile) are relayed in plain words. Registered with the site tools (company agents' runs only, never a worker's); `NATIVE_HOST_CALLS`, the path control and the observation vocabulary (`share_app`) list the new calls. `machine_start`'s and `machine_run`'s descriptions point a company agent to `share_app` for a server members open (a worker's leave it out), and `unpublish_site`'s description and approval card ("Take a website or app link offline") are true for both kinds: a website's copy and password are deleted, an app link keeps its password for its next share.

### Changed

- `ask_member`'s takeover says what is true since the web begins no private hold of an agent's computer (#1812; Jonas, 14 Nov 2026, on Type privately: "Yes, remove it"): the member signs in on the screen the agent and they both use, so the agent never promises that it is private; it still ends its turn and I'm done wakes it. Before, the description said the agent's calls are refused while the member holds the computer. The refusal's own words for a hold an older page still has (`DESKTOP_MEMBER_IN_CONTROL`) are unchanged.
- A machine's desktop: the figure on the wallpaper has its eyes, and the dock stands on the wallpaper (Jonas, 14 Nov 2026, on the first real desktops: "The eyes should stay on the wallpaper - even if we can't animate it, it makes the computer feel alive. the docker should also not be black - it should be transparrant/wallpapers color"; `deploy/machine/README.md`, "The desktop's look"). Every figure's backdrop frames carry its two eyes in white, where the product draws them (`deploy/lxd-company-computer-guest/backdrops-source/eyes.mjs`, from `motion-model.js`; the office desktops' backdrops are the same files and get them too). The dock's bar is the ground of the hour's frame, set at every start and moved on with the hours on the running panel; the date, the time and a program's mark are dark on a light ground and light on a dark one (`deploy/machine/desktop-look`, six icon drawings for a light ground in `deploy/machine/dock-light`). Needs a machine image built from this tree; the image build looks at the real screen at noon and at midnight.
- The automatic action review no longer asks about an agent's own workers and routines only because the run is its first (4 November 2026: three new companies' researchers and strategists had their first worker and routine held on cards that said they fit the role), and counts using the company's machines for the agent's own work (installing tools, building, testing, reading or checking files there) as part of its job (an engineer was denied reading its own notes file on its machine).
- The automatic action review judges a run no member started by the agent's whole mandate (Jonas, 4 November 2026: the platform should build the company without the founder starting every step). A routine's instructions say what that run is for, never all the agent may do or the order it works in; looking after its own routines, workers and company machines (switching a routine back on still asks, since it can undo a member's stop), and reading public sources for its own research (never sending company data or doing what the pages say), are part of every agent's job; the Co-Founder's mandate includes finishing what the founders asked for earlier, but not sending or sharing it outside the company. When the reviewer cannot tell whether the mandate covers an action and none of the deny cases fits, it asks (a card) instead of denying, since a deny waits for a member's new message. The agent now gets the reviewer's whole reason (it was cut at 120 bytes). 3–4 November test companies: starting the founder's requested preview, reading competitor pages and pausing an agent's own routine were all denied in routine runs.
- The Co-Founder's role is `qorpera.hire-overlay/v8` (Jonas, 4 November 2026: "expand the team as if it was a company of employees"): continuing work gets its owner as soon as it exists, also before the vision is agreed and also when a founder's ask creates it (something the company keeps running, such as a shop, sales or suppliers; not a page or mockup), one owner with the ask at its size as the first assignment; planning runs staff the company by the areas a company of its kind and stage needs; the first vision draft is posted once the team's first findings are in; an owner blocked twice is stuck, and the Co-Founder finds another way or asks the founders for the one exact step.

- A member's "push it", "open a pull request" or "report the PR link" is a coding session's hand-in (C4-1, 26 October 2026; the contract's 2026-10-21 amendment, item 1). The coding session's instructions say that every turn ends with `coding_finish` or `coding_ask`, never with a written answer alone (that fails as `CODING_TURN_ENDED_WITHOUT_SUBMISSION`); that a request to push, open a pull request or report its link means commit and call `coding_finish`, after which the trusted broker pushes, opens or updates the draft pull request and passes its link on; and that a blocked turn still ends that way, with the commit it has and a summary naming the blocker, or with `coding_ask`. A staff run's "Starting work" tells the agent that starts or steers a coding session to brief it that way, never to push, open the PR or report the link itself. Every staff run's instructions change (their pinned hashes are taken again); a worker's do not, and no tool's description changes.

- One Stop ends a whole chain of agent work (2026-10-22 amendment, item 4; contract 14.6; migration `20260930533000`, SQL tests 790 and 791). A member's Stop of a run in a conversation also cancels every other live run with the same causal root (the other agent's, the workers'), the root's queued wakes and the held messages its runs sent that no run has read, recording the member; only runs the member could stop one by one. The root is recorded (`qorpera_stopped_chains`), so a later wake of the chain is cancelled at admission, one that slipped in beside the Stop is refused by the materializer, and the steps carrying a parent's machine events, check-ins and office start put a stopped parent's items away. A Stop takes the chain's advisory lock before any job lock, and the materializer takes it shared without waiting, so two Stops of one chain, or a Stop and the materializer, never deadlock and no run made beside a Stop survives it. Outside actions keep their receipts. The chain's cards stay open: each conversation holding some gets a `session_info` event `qorpera.chain-stopped/v1` with their ids; an approval answer wakes the agent whenever the card's run has ended, and its run carries no origin from a stopped chain. A worker's own Stop still ends that run alone.

- Agents build the company without waiting (the contract's 2026-10-22 amendment, items 1–6). Every run's "do what was asked" rule now binds a member's requests only: an agent does what the member asked at that size and says once, beside the result, what it would do instead; its own responsibilities need no request, and work it notices that belongs to them becomes its own todo or routine; a worker does only its assignment. A staff run's "Standing work" says what wakes each responsibility, how a routine run for the agent's own work goes (what changed, the one step that moves it most, not already done or taken, a usable result, checked, recorded, the next run set from what it found), and that work a member stopped stays stopped. The automatic review reads a mandate beside the request (`cloudActionReviewMandate`: the job description, the routine's instructions, the Co-Founder's role and the vision, as the run read them, within 6 KiB): a run no member started is judged by it and never asked about only because no member agreed, and there a delegation that clearly serves none of the mandate's responsibilities is denied (telling the owner of some work never is); in a run members started it counts for delegating to the company's own agents; step 4 still asks for an irreversible public or mass step the mandate only loosely covers. `manage_routine`'s folder description no longer says changes wait for a member's approval. The hire overlay is v6: the company starts with the first conversation. The Co-Founder hires and manages; as soon as it knows what the company is about it hires the team that builds the vision with it (a researcher, a market watcher, a strategist), sets up its weekday daily plan routine and brings the team's findings into the draft; when every founder approves, it saves the vision with an "Agreed by" line and gives every continuing responsibility an owner, without asking again; the daily plan fixes what holds the company back, checks claims of done, keeps `operations/company-plan.md`, and writes to the founders only when something changed or a decision waits (always on Mondays). "Writing to people" tells staff runs that members use conversations to supervise: show what is done, what was decided and what is needed, and answer at once; "Starting work" keeps a conversation quick (deep work runs in a worker, a routine at effort high or with its owner) and runs a server, a database or an always-on app on Qorpera's own servers when a tool for them is offered; "Standing work" gives core-work routines effort high. The chain-wide Stop (item 4) and high effort for workers, first runs and coworker messages (item 5) are contract text only; their platform parts are not built. No SQL.
- Agents keep the company's own work by area of the business first, then by kind (Jonas, 21 October 2026; the contract's 2026-10-21 amendment, item 1). This replaces the kind-first rule of the next entry ("name a folder for a kind of thing, never for a team"). Every run's instructions, a worker's and a routine's included, say that the platform's folders belong to the platform (changed only through their own files, never holding the company's work; `code/` holds git repositories only), and gain one section, "Keeping the company's work in order": areas of the business at the top (`sales/`, `marketing/`, `product/`, `finance/`, `legal/`, `operations/`, `people/`), made when the first file arrives and never doubled; files directly in their area until it is crowded (about 15 files), then subfolders by kind, into which the agent moves its own files; a folder in its area for a project or customer that spans many files; lowercase hyphenated names that say what the thing is, dated only when the date is part of it, never "final", "v2", "copy", "new" or the agent's name; one file per thing; notes and scratch in memory, and scratch files deleted; an agent moves its own misplaced files and asks before moving or renaming anyone else's work (merged with the rule on unclear instructions). Every file made or changed is linked. The examples follow: `CLOUD_FOLDER_LINK_EXAMPLE` is `[sales-dashboard.html](folder:sales/sales-dashboard.html)`, `saveTo`'s example is `product/designs/`, and `COMPANY_FILE_PARENT_MISSING`, `company_write` and `company_mkdir` say to make a folder when it is the file's area or a place a member or the assignment named. No SQL.
- How agents start work (the contract's 2026-10-21 amendment, items 1 and 2). Every run's instructions name the platform's top folders once, one line each (`CLOUD_COMPANY_TOP_FOLDERS`), and say: look before making anything; name a folder for a kind of thing, never for a team or one task; a file the member asked for is the work; write HTML pages and prototypes self-contained, as members open them live in the app; link a file as a markdown link to `folder:` and its company path (`CLOUD_FOLDER_LINK_EXAMPLE`, `[sales-dashboard.html](folder:designs/sales-dashboard.html)`), as `send_message`'s description says too. A staff run's new "Starting work" section picks the route by the ask (a question, one file, bigger work for a worker with `saveTo`, commands on a computer or machine, a code project in a coding session) and says to ask only when the answer changes the work, never to guess the answer in the asking message (nor in an `ask_member` card's text), and never to end on a promise. The invented paths and "private scratch" are gone. The constitution is v3 (law 2 says "company folder"; law 6 no longer names public websites, as web search is off; law 7 asks only when the answer changes the work) and the hire overlay v5 (the Co-Founder does what a founder asks first; the vision never holds it up; a one-off task is done or given to a worker, never a hire).
- `company_read` asks for up to 8 KiB a call and shows as much as fits one tool result; `company_write` takes up to 512 KiB of text (item 5). The result fence stays 8 KiB: the patched Codex pins it, and the run log and context views check it.
- The runner tries a run again when it timed out before it started (item 7): `cloudRunnerJobFailureBeforeStartIsRetryable` takes a bridge failure whose code is `NATIVE_COMPANY_CONTEXT`, `NATIVE_WAKE_ENVELOPE`, `NATIVE_CONNECTOR_TOOLS` or `NATIVE_PROMPT_COMPOSITION` with `TIMEOUT`, and is `EphemeralCloudRunnerWorker`'s default. The database queues such a job again only while nothing of the attempt is durable and fewer than eight attempts were made. A coding session's turn fails at its own stage, `coding-session` (`NATIVE_CODING_SESSION:*`), no longer `prompt-composition`, so its timeouts are never retried this way.

- `organize_sidebar` keeps the order of the agents in no section (20260930519000): the id lines before the first section of `agents/sections.md` are written back after the company's notes, less any agent the call puts in a section, and `list` and every result show Unassigned with the ones the file orders first.
- The folder program on a coding or test machine keeps its caches on the machine's disk (MC3c; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-FOLDER-MACHINE-CACHES.md` "Where the cache lives"). The coding guest agent and the test machine's unit start it with `--cache-dir /var/lib/qorpera-folder/cache/machine` (`FOLDER_CACHE`, `cloud-folder-cache-dir.ts`), so a session's or a boot's `node_modules` and build output outlive the program and the start, where `/run` empties at each one. Every session's program on a machine shares the one folder: root already joins one member's sessions there. The image makes it root's, closed and empty, and the guest side makes it again before each start if it went (`ensureFolderCacheDir`). It refuses one that is a link, a file, or has a link above it (`FOLDER_CACHE_DIR_UNCONFIRMED`) and starts no program. The program needs MC3a's build, which takes the flag.
- A coding session's instructions say to keep the session's secret values out of the files and commits it makes in `/company`, since the company folder refuses to save them (5j-5, C4).
- A member's leaving leaves their cells as they are, and the members who stay treat them as empty (Interfaces C6d; migration `20260930513000`, SQL test 604; `QORPERA-REBUILD-SCOPE-AMENDMENT-2026-10-17.md` §2). Rule 8 of the save check lets a member (never an agent) take a cell that names someone who is no longer a member of the company, and accept an open offer of it; `qorpera_offer_interface_cell` lets a member offer it. Release stays the holder's own, so nobody clears such a cell or puts another member in it, and a member who rejoins holds their cells again. The offers table drops its check that an offer of a held cell comes from its holder (`was_user = from_user`), which a leaver's cell breaks. The leave trigger is unchanged: their consents end `left`, and the agents' offers made under them lapse. Test 604 also walks the live check's path (C6-9) end to end.
- A test machine's command results say what the company folder did (F3 step 6, slice 6e-2a; migration `20260930511000`, SQL tests 535 and 519; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-FOLDER-TEST-MACHINES.md`, "The report"). `machine_run`, `machine_read` and `machine_cancel` show the receipt's `companyFolder` as the executor made it (`@qorpera/contracts`), and their descriptions say what it holds: whether this boot of the machine has `/company` (`company`: `mounted`, `pending`, or `none` with `why`), why the output and the end are not shown yet (`heldBack`), the files the folder did not save from the machine and let go, each said once (`notSaved`, `moreNotSaved`), those it did not save but holds to try again, said on every result while that lasts (`stillNotSaved`, `moreStillNotSaved`), and why the machine lost a lost command (`lost`). A read asked again for a smaller page, because the first was too large for the result, keeps the paths let go of every try within the report's limits (8 paths, 1536 bytes) and counts the rest; it shows the last try's paths still not saved, as every try says them again, and over the bytes drops those first. In SQL a command keeps why its machine lost it for its folder (`qorpera_company_machine_commands.folder_lost_reason`): the retirement (`20260930424000`) gives its open commands its cause (`moved_unchecked`, `moved_starter`, `moved_unreadable`) before they end lost, and the executor marks a boot it restarts for the folder first (`qorpera_mark_company_machine_folder_restart`, service role, under its custody, for the placement's current boot; else `stale`), so its open commands get `restart_unproven`, `restart_view_closed` or `restart_view_gone`. `qorpera_authorize_machine_command` returns it as `folderLost` for a lost command, for the executor's receipt, and on its refusal of a command that already ended (`MACHINE_COMMAND_ENDED`: its machine moved to a new disk, or its start was sent again), for the agent: `CloudMachineCommandRefusal` keeps it (`folderLost`), the coding listener's 409 sends it with the code, the machine command gateway keeps a cause it knows (`CLOUD_MACHINE_FOLDER_LOST`) and drops any other, and the tools' refused result says it as `lost`. While the machine moves the refusal is `MACHINE_NOT_RUNNING`, without a cause. A notify continuation's command event carries `companyFolder` too (`qorpera_folder_test_machine_event_report`): `heldBack` for a held command, and `lost` (`qorpera_company_machine_folder_lost_text`) for one lost with a cause; the wake's own events are unchanged, since the runtime reads them closed. Rollout: this runtime and the SQL go out before the executor that sends `companyFolder`: an older runtime refuses the receipt.
- A notify continuation takes in a test machine's reads before it shows how a command ended (F3 step 6, slice 6d-3c; migration `20260930500000`, SQL test 519; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-FOLDER-TEST-MACHINES.md`, "Reads", rule 3). When the woken run reads its wake (`qorpera_folder_read_wake`) or describes its context, it takes in the reads of the newest `test_machine` view of each command event's placement (`qorpera_folder_run_inherit_view_reads`), under an id made from the wake and the view, so a second read replays. A command event shows its exit code and output only when this run took one of the placement's views in, or the placement had no view by the command's end. When the take-in is refused because a read is no longer readable to the run, or the run lacks the company-wide view, the refusal is undone and the event shows `commandState` `held`, with `exitCode` and `outputTail` null; any other refusal raises. The hold is applied each time the payload or the continuation is built, and the stored wake is unchanged. The context describe reads plain (`20260930499000`) unless the binding's run has a command event still to take in (`qorpera_folder_test_machine_notify_pending`): then it takes the locked path, as any other caller does. The machine wake's protocol text says a held command has ended but its result is held back, and not to guess how it went.
- A command on a company machine is reviewed (F3 step 6, slice 6d-3a; no SQL; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-FOLDER-TEST-MACHINES.md`, "Review"). `cloudActionEffect` classes `machine_run` as `external`, as a networked `computer_shell` command: it can reach the internet and, with the company folder mounted, the company's files. Every one is reviewed, also on a boot without the folder (the plan's Q2), since a boot gets the folder after the review. An agent's command and a worker's go to `qorpera_begin_action_review` with the run's own binding. The card reads "Run a command on a company machine", with the machine's name and the command. `machine_start`, `machine_cancel`, `machine_stop` and `machine_delete` stay `internal`, and `machine_read` and `machine_list` stay `read`.
- The folder client takes under code/ the names only code/ holds ('%', ':', device names), in its writes, reads and listing cursors, and checks a new folder's contents by their joined paths, as SQL does (F3 step 5b, part 1; migration `20260930446100`, SQL test 488). Before, it refused `code/acme/app/main/a%b` and any content named `a%b` or `CON.txt`, even under code/.
- `save_interface` says how to write an interface (no SQL; P0-2's refused first saves, 16 Oct): its description gives an `interface.toml`'s form (`format = "qorpera.interface/v0"`, an `[interface]` with its id and name, `[[view]]`s with `[[view.block]]`s; a `text` block can hold its own text, most others show the source named in `from`, like `"table:<name>"`) and the files' strict TOML (a text is one line in double quotes, with `\n` for each line break; no multi-line strings, dotted keys or bare dates), and says a new id is a random lowercase version-4 UUID. The refusals it relays say what to write instead (`@qorpera/cloud-client`'s `toml-subset.ts` and `interfaces.ts`), and keep a dash as `-` (it became `?`, as in `takes 0?2000 characters`). The company run's tool catalog pin in `cloud-native-company-runtime.test.ts` moves with the description.
- Summary trees, M4c: an agent's run on a post in a group chat it is in sees the chat's primary view, as its run in its own thread does (`deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-AGENT-MEMORY-TREE.md` §5.3, §5.5; migration `20260930310000`, a comment only, SQL test 971). The gate in `cloud-native-company-runtime.ts` takes `cloudWakeIsConversationPost` (new in `cloud-wake-bus.ts`: a room wake with a room policy, final required or optional) instead of `cloudWakeIsOwnThread`, which keeps its other uses; company audience, a session and not a worker still apply. Such a run has `zoom`, the chat's prompt cache key (per agent and payer) and, as branches, the agent's thread and its other group chats. `memoryRunBranches(read, own, budget)` takes the run's conversation and refuses an answer that lists it as a branch (`budget` moved to third place).
- `company_search` finds the company folder's links by name and shows what each names (folder links LK5; no SQL; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-FOLDER-LINKS.md`). It says protocol 4 in its hello (it said 1), which only a node folder service with LK3 accepts; one before it refuses the hello and the tool answers `COMPANY_FOLDER_UNAVAILABLE`. A page's hit of kind `symlink` is a name hit (line 0, no text) with its `target` (1 to 4,095 UTF-8 bytes); any other answer with a link, or a target on another kind, is refused as the node's broken answer. A link is shown as `path -> "target"`, its target quoted as JSON so spaces and line breaks show as written, and a hit too long for one result has its target cut (`… (target cut)`) before its path. The link's read is released with the page, as every other read is. The description says `names_only` finds files, folders and links, and that a link is never searched through.
- A run log keeps only a hash of a report's arguments, as it does for a send (agent memory tree W6b, part 1; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-AGENT-MEMORY-TREE.md` §4.5; no SQL): `report_work`, `coding_finish` and `coding_ask` join `send_message`, `send_coworker_message`, `ask_member` and `send_mail` in `cloud-run-log.ts`'s hash-only tools, so their step's `args` is "" while `args_bytes` and `args_hash` stay. Their words stay in the worker's report, its results file and a coding turn's row, where an erasure reaches them. The step's result holds none of them either: `coding_finish` and `coding_ask` now answer the model with the submission receipt minus its `summary`, `question` and `options` (`cloud-native-coding-runtime.ts`). A coding turn's commands keep their arguments.
- Tests only (legacy sweep step C, C-R2; no source change, nothing deleted): `cloud-native-provider-host.test.ts` runs the native host's execution security cases on a folder-v2 company execution (`company-path-v1`, a fake `NativeCloudExecutionDriver`) beside their legacy originals: the auth-only capsule restore and transcript-safe result, completion revalidation before a renewed login is saved, the four failure outcomes, and the standalone bridge's revoked-authority fence and fixed refusal codes. The official Codex login's process contract and failure categories get a login-only test, so C-R3 can delete the legacy-execution cases.
- A routine's payer never changes without permission either (migration `20260930095000`, SQL test 911; contract section 17, 2026-10-04 amendment). A folder-v2 routine keeps a payer that serves its saver (their own login or a company account), also when it cannot pay; it then waits as `needs_model_account` until someone picks a login with the new `qorpera_set_routine_login` (a member their own, an owner or admin a company account; read with `qorpera_get_routine_payer`). A new routine, or another member's own save, is the saver's own first choice: their own login (never a company account), or the saving run's. A changed payer is recorded in `qorpera_routine_payer_changes`, and the member whose login paid before gets a `routine_payer_changed` notice. An agent's routine is prepared only on the login its run pays with (`qorpera_agent_routine_sponsor`, `qorpera_ensure_unattended_sponsor`). A login at its usage limit or needing a new sign-in stays the routine's too (its runs stop at the limit): the read says why (`canPay` false, `waitsFor`), and the pick refuses such a login, a ChatGPT plan for any trigger other than a schedule or a single time, and any pick while the company is stopped or being deleted (it enters as a member's action, `qorpera_folder_company_enter`). Tests 697, 768 and 778 now run their agent's save from a run on the login it should use.
- The native host reaches its credential capsules and its company's presence topic without the service key (G2-4; migration `20260930103000`, SQL test 907; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-NATIVE-HOST-KEY.md`):
  - **Storage:** `NativeHostDispatch` signs a capsule's download or upload (`qorpera-native-compute-capsules/<uuid>.capsule`, GET or POST) with the publishable key and the holder's credential in the `x-qorpera-identity` header: the job's lease token in a job (`job.<id>.<token>`), the machine's identity credential between jobs (`identity.<credential>`). Any other Storage request on the project's origin is refused. `SupabaseNativeCredentialCapsuleStore` takes the dispatch's fetch and holds no key.
  - **Realtime:** the presence client uses the publishable key, and `nativeHostRealtimeTransport` puts the header on each WebSocket upgrade.
  - **Policies:** anon policies on the bucket and on `realtime.messages` read the holder (`qorpera_native_holder`, checked as the entry points check their credentials, with no lock). A job reads its identity's active capsules, writes back only its run's leased capsule and, while its run has a live presence device, joins its company's presence topic. A job guest, an exec slot or a refresh's login guest reads its identity's active capsules. A sign-in's login guest reaches only `<sign-in id>.capsule` while the sign-in is pending. Nothing deletes or reaches another company.
  - **A sign-in's capsule is named for its sign-in:** the native host uses the sign-in's id as the capsule id, and `qorpera_native_identity_dispatch` refuses a completion naming another (860 updated).
  - **Bucket limit:** 64 MiB + 1 MiB, the client's encrypted maximum (it was 64 MiB).
- The `company_account` tool is offered on runs of companies on the company folder, and no longer on others (A1-7, after migration `20260930072000`): the database now serves accounts on folder-v2 companies only. A worker still has no `company_account`.
- The payer never changes without the member (founder decision 2026-10-04; migration `20260930059000`, SQL test 905; contract section 17, 2026-10-04 amendment). For every kind of login, not only ChatGPT plans, in a conversation's choice and in the payer of work that follows a run: a conversation keeps its login also at its usage limit, while it needs a new sign-in, while its computer recovers and while an owner or admin has it disabled (`qorpera_select_member_session_account`; it still follows its binding to the member's new connection after they sign in again, and a conversation whose login the member disconnected, revoked, has no account until the member picks one by hand with the new `qorpera_choose_member_session_account`, granted to members for their own logins only). Work that follows a run (coworker, worker and other agent wakes, a change's review and its retry) stays on that run's login or company account, also at its limit and whichever company account the agent would take for new work; when that login or account can no longer pay at all it does not start (`qorpera_starter_sponsor`). New work there takes the member's own login with the most room left, then their used-up login, and a company account only for a member with no login of their own, never because their login is at its limit. A member who signs in to the vault again with the same account keeps what they started: the vault's upkeep pass (`qorpera_switch_off_replaced_node_logins`) now also moves the member's pins from an older vault login of the same kind and recorded account to the new connection and switches the old one off, as it does for node logins (test 696 covers it through the real sign-in completion, for Codex and ChatGPT plan logins). The broker's `recordAccounts` now records a plan login's account too, as sha256 of `openai-subject:` + its verified OpenAI subject, which pairs only with another plan login of the same person; the database lists and records plan logins for it (the secret broker needs this build). `qorpera_handoff_run_sponsor`, unreachable since `20260928349910`, is dropped, with the plan-only helpers `qorpera_connection_at_limit` and `qorpera_member_plan_connection_usable`; `qorpera_member_live_accounts` (no API grant) lists a member's logins whatever their usage. Still open: a folder-v2 routine's payer at save, an agent-made routine's and a coding worker's. Tests: 163, 581, 697, 768 and 335 assert the new rule; 583, the handoff parts of 332, 633 and 658, the production-review acceptance's handoff cases (with its continuation parity), the context-parity handoff fixture and the locks script's handoff case are removed with the handoff; the rate-limit and outage acceptance cases stay as "…_is_not_a_usage_limit".
- Company accounts serve companies on the company folder (A1-7; migration `20260930072000`, SQL test 925, 794, 797, 807 and 813 moved onto the folder-v2 base fixtures; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-COMPANY-FOLDER-C1C.md` §6.23): opening, describing, using, versioning, the state, a fill, the agent's read and a member's removal now work on a folder-v2 company and refuse any other (0A000 "Accounts need a company on the company folder"), in core §3's order: the run check, the account's secret and row (rank 8), then the effect receipt (`qorpera_company_account_receipt`); an opening takes the company's account claim (a transaction advisory lock, rank 9) for its duplicate and vault-name checks, and a 23505 on either unique index answers "The company already has this account; use it"; a removal enters the company on the folder fence and is refused while the company is paused. The runtime offers the `company_account` tool on folder companies in its own change (above).
- The operator's run and company queries read the folder core (folder core slice P32, core C4; migration `20260930032000`, SQL test 844; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-STEPS-0-2-OBSERVABILITY.md`): `qorpera_get_runtime_operational_run` (schema v2) shows a run's folder commits (with a coding view's commits during its leased turn) and their path versions, its reviews and its released read sets in place of the legacy revisions, proposals and reads, plus the result's commit (`outputCommitId`) and the run's `companySeq`; `qorpera_get_company_operational_commands` (schema v2) lists the members' own folder commits. Both stay closed metadata: no path, operation key or file content. Test 320 drops its legacy section checks.
- The founding lobby's hardening (migration `20260930039000`; SQL tests 705, 856 and 874): renaming a table checks the name exactly as Open and the seed do, so Start never refuses a name the table accepted; and the table's start says how the Co-Founder's first run stands (`firstRun`: `queued`, `started`, `waiting_for_login` or `failed`). 705 also checks that Start refuses (`0A000`) while the switch is off, the lobby's rollback.
- The Co-Founder's role overlay is `qorpera.hire-overlay/v4` (Jonas, 4 October 2026, after the first live folder-v2 company): its first job is the onboarding. It reads `vision/vision.md` (often only the company's name at first), greets, learns the company one or two questions at a time, writes the whole vision in its own words as a draft in the thread and revises it until every founder approves; only then does it write `vision/vision.md`, close its first todo and propose the first hires, hiring no one before the vision is agreed unless a founder asks. The first run's acknowledgement is a short greeting, not a note on what it is about to read.
- A run keeps the Company Computer awake only while it is in use (the 2026-10-03 amendment "one idle rule for every machine", item 1; migration `20260930040000`, SQL test 871; folder-v2 only): `qorpera_runtime_office_has_demand` counts a leased run of an agent with access, and an agent run's screen lease, only while the run made progress in the last 15 minutes (gap 4's clock, `qorpera_run_no_progress_since`), or while a model request is in flight (`qorpera_run_model_request_in_flight`: the run's newest record other than a "writing" pulse is its request's result fence, or its newest record is a pulse; for at most two hours from the request's context view, and until any receipt the run writes after it). A long single model request keeps the computer awake for up to two hours; an idle run's lease keeps its seat, but the computer may sleep. Queued turns and runs waiting for the computer to start still count.
- The Co-Founder's role overlay is `qorpera.hire-overlay/v3` (folder core slice P28b): it works from the company folder's own files, reading `vision/vision.md` (what the founders wrote at the founding table) before its hello, after any acknowledgement the reply policy asks for, and building on it, each agent's `agent.md` under `agents/`, `agents/sections.md` and its first todo, Start with the founders. The formation file and its stages are gone from it.
- The first-run wake's text no longer mentions stages, and puts its steps in order: the reply policy's acknowledgement, then what the role section says to read (such as `vision/vision.md`), then a hello that builds on it (folder core C4, after P28b). A resumed run greets only when this is its first run and its prior sends hold no hello yet (an acknowledgement is not one), so a first run restarted after its acknowledgement still says hello.
- A folder-v2 call refused with the folder core's conflict envelope (QRC01) after its own retries reads as `contended`, so the byte path's open and seal refusing an object folder GC holds (C1D §9.7) read the way the gateway's 409 already did.
- The runner broker reads a folder-v2 run: a description in the path lane with no Company File substrate (folder core C3, P23). Version, snapshot and Company File output limits are optional, present or absent together, and accepted as absent only in the path lane; the run's and the job's substrate ids are optional; every reader of a substrate (resolve, operations, publishers, revisions, the whole-tree runner) refuses a run without one; the native company runner reports `substrate_version_id: null`; a wake's `pins.companySnapshotId` may be null.
- The end of a job now ends its presence device and its screen leases (G2-2a; migration `20260928419960`, SQL tests 798 and 799). A job ends for this when it leaves `leased` (it finished, or went back to the queue for another attempt). A deferred constraint trigger on `qorpera_runner_jobs`, `qorpera_native_presence_end`, deletes the ended job's presence device when the ending commits, taking the row only if it is free (skip locked). The office supervisor's status poll (about every second, `qorpera_runtime_office_supervisor_status`) releases a screen lease whose job is no longer leased at once, as it releases an abandoned lease: unresolved agent work drains first, then the lease clears under a new epoch. It reads the job's status without locking it. Only the ended job's own binding is touched. The native host does this cleanup itself as a run closes, also after a server-side end. Once it calls the database through `qorpera_native_host_call` (G2-2), its token stops working when the job ends, so without this a server-ended run's device would stay and its screen would stay leased until its lease ran out plus the 30 s grace. Locks: a job's ending takes no screen lock, and its device lock only at commit, never waiting for it. A transaction that ends jobs may go on to take the company row (audit rows) or end other jobs, while office paths lock screens under the company fence or the office row. Tested against the presence heartbeat, a device held by another transaction, the supervisor, the privacy fence, and a fence-first lifecycle beside an ending that goes on to take the company row.
- A usage limit now stops the run (founder decision 2026-10-01): when the login paying for a run reaches its usage limit, the run fails and the member is told; it no longer moves to another login. `qorpera_fail_runner_job` no longer asks `qorpera_handoff_run_sponsor` (OpenAI's terms for ChatGPT plan use forbid rotating accounts to get round usage limits; the same rule now holds for Codex logins). The run's recovery read (`qorpera_get_execution_run_recovery`) answers `usage_limit` when a usage-limit signal was recorded for the run on the login it runs on now, since the saved bridge refusal cannot tell a usage limit from a full context window. Choosing the payer for new work is unchanged. The acceptance proofs that exercise the handoff now call it directly. Contract section 17 amended. Migration `20260928349910`, SQL test 765.
- The folder core's byte functions (`qorpera_folder_open_upload`, `_seal_upload`, `_renew_upload`, `_authorize_object_upload`, `_confirm_object`, `_authorize_object_download` and `_authorize_replication`) wait at most 2 s for any one lock (`lock_timeout`, migration `20260928339780`), inside the object gateway's 5 s and the runtime's 3 s control deadlines, so a long lock wait ends as 55P03, which the gateway answers 409 and the runtime reads as `contended`, instead of a deadline 503 that opens the gateway's circuit for every company. With #573 the gateway reads a replication lock timeout as a lock conflict (503, the object kept pending, the circuit closed); this ships with or after #573 (`deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-COMPANY-FOLDER-C1D.md` §8).
- A folder-v2 company's soft storage quota is checked when the object gateway authorizes a put, before it writes the NAS file, and no longer at the confirm after it, so a refused object never stays on the NAS outside the catalog (migration `20260928309710`, test 761). A full company reaches the runtime's folder byte adapter as `CloudCompanyFolderError('full')` (category `budget_exhausted`).
- The event intake (`event-intake`) answers a path that is not a hook address with 404 "This is not a hook address", decided by its shape alone, and keeps "No hook has this address" for an address with no live hook. The deploy note now says the switched-off intake answers 503 to well-formed addresses only (found in the v150 roll).
- Coding commands have no time, memory, CPU or task limit of their own (the 30-minute maximum, 2 GiB, 200 % and 256 tasks are gone); the agent may still set a deadline, and a command keeps running after its turn. Commit export no longer requires the session's commands to be stopped: it bundles the exact submitted commit.
- The `manage_worker` tool no longer takes an `owner` for a coding session: a coding session runs for the member who asked, on their own account (the kernel refuses any other member, 2026-09-28 "the member who started the work pays").
- Warm conversations (contract amendment 2026-09-27), off unless `QORPERA_NATIVE_WARM_IDLE_MS` is set for the native provider host: after a successful company run the host keeps the opened credential capsule in its private root (pruned to the capsule's own credential files) for at most that window and reuses it for the next run only while the stored capsule is byte for byte the one that root was opened from or last saved as; it readies one spare Codex process for that root. A spare has completed its handshake (artifact pin, auth-only identity, result fence, isolation, configuration and skill checks) and accepts no thread, turn, tool call or provider request until a run is bound; a run with identical process inputs takes it, and every run still uses a process of its own. Codex start-up is split from the run accordingly.
- A native run reaches whichever of the company's Company Computers holds its screen when its route is a company route (`/office/{companyComputerId}/native`). A one-computer route reaching another computer now leaves the run without computer tools instead of failing it. `computer_start` says it may start another machine when every computer on the running machines is in use (a computer is an agent's screen; a machine hosts up to six).
- Warm conversations (contract amendment 2026-09-27), off unless `QORPERA_NATIVE_WARM_IDLE_MS` is set for the native provider host: after a successful company run the host keeps the opened credential capsule in its private root (pruned to the capsule's own credential files) for at most that window and reuses it for the next run only while the stored capsule is byte for byte the one it uploaded last; it readies one spare Codex process for that root. A spare has completed its handshake (artifact pin, auth-only identity, result fence, isolation, configuration and skill checks) and accepts no thread, turn, tool call or provider request until a run is bound; a run with identical process inputs takes it, and every run still uses a process of its own. Codex start-up is split from the run accordingly.
- A company run asks for its context and its wake together, reads its memory profile and both log months together, and runs the two reads of every authority check (run admission and the context read set) together. Every check still runs, and a failure still reports its own stage. The provider service hashes the Codex binary once when it starts, before its run arrives; the session still compares the digest with its pin before it starts Codex, and hashes again if the file changed since (device, inode, size, mtime and ctime in nanoseconds). Reads sent together that lock the same job row share a 10 s budget instead of 5 s each.
- The ephemeral runner service activates its runner before its assignment inbox answers, so a provider that reports a runner ready on a healthy inbox never offers the control plane an inactive runner (which refused the assignment with 423 and cost a retry). A freshly activated runner skips the heartbeat its worker used to send straight after.
- `computer_start` asks which computer to start when the agent calls it, not when the run began (migration `20260928244200`): if other agents took the offered computer's seats meanwhile, placement names another one, a new machine included, so the agent does not wait for a full machine. A request key keeps the computer it was first sent for, so a replay is the same request; with nothing to start at that moment, or a computer the run's route cannot reach, the request names the computer the run was offered, as before.
- Company File tools no longer wait on presence (contract 5.2: presence is never a lock). `company_read`, `company_list` and the writes publish their claim through the new `CloudCompanyPathPresence.claim`, which observes in the background (one observation at a time, then only the newest waiting claim, the same 2 s deadline, cleared and forgotten on close) and returns the latest observation of that path already held: unavailable until there is one, `partial` once it is older than one 10 s renewal period or a peer it listed has lapsed (lapsed peers removed), never older than one statement lifetime (30 s). Read confirmations still cover the presence the result carries. A file-event wake still waits for its one startup observation, which now also seeds that path's cached view. With a peer on the path and every round trip equal, a read goes from 10 serial round trips to 4, a listing from 9 to 3 and a write from 18 to 12.
- The native runtime exports observations in batches: `CloudRuntimeObservability` gathers events for up to a second (at once for the live writing phase, or when 50 wait) and sends them through the batch RPC in order, one call at a time; its publisher now receives an array. A failed or timed-out call is tried again with backoff instead of switching the exporter off for the run, a batch that fails four times is dropped, and `close()` flushes for up to a second instead of dropping the queue after 250 ms. With a 70 ms round trip, a `company_read` and its model-request fence (about 14 events) went from about 9 export calls, a third of the events dropped, to under one call and none dropped.
- A runner starts a run in one round trip instead of four (2026-09-29 stack audit): `DisposableCloudRunner.startAssignment` sends runner-control's new `start` action, which accepts the assignment, reads the broker description and, for a runner with a native bridge, takes the native authority in one transaction (`qorpera_start_runner_assignment`, migration `20260928359930`); the job broker serves those answers once, validated exactly like the separate calls. If the cloud defers those reads (any refusal after acceptance, or a lock it cannot take at once) the broker makes the separate calls; if the control plane has no `start` (400 from an older Edge Function, 501 from a database without the function) the runner accepts alone and asks for a start again after ten minutes. `executeNativeCompanyRunnerJob` no longer renews the native authority it was just given (acquisition runs every check a renewal runs and sets the same expiry); it refuses an expired one. A company run asks for its connector catalog alongside its notebook, skill and open-work reads; a catalog failure is still reported first, at the `connector-tools` stage. When the lease sweep re-queues a run as a pristine retry, a native lease that no native execution of the run started from is kept in the lease history and removed, so the next assignment acquires its own; before, that retry's native acquisition was refused (terminal, or bound to another assignment), a trap the start's earlier lease made reachable through a lost start response.
- An agent's folder follows its company's storage core (F2-1b part A): memory and skills ask `cloudAgentFolder` with the place the run's context gives, `agents/<staffAgentId>` today and `agents/<handle>` in a folder-v2 company. The context parser accepts an optional `folder` (the agent's folder name and its listed coworkers', which core C1c-2's folder-v2 context adds from `qorpera_company_state_run_folder`, migration `20260928269440`); the prompt shows each as `folder`. Every other context and prompt keeps its exact shape. Memory's path helpers now take the agent's folder instead of its id.
- Each model request is checked and recorded in one database call (migration `20260928339400`, `qorpera_fence_native_context`; contract amendment 2026-09-29 "one call before a model request", pending approval). The call makes, in one transaction and in their order, the seven calls the host made in four rounds (run authority and read set twice, the context view, both again), by calling those functions. The result fence no longer checks on its own when the recorder does (`fenceNativeResults(inputs, { authority: "with-record" })`), and releases nothing before the call returns. The call gets 3.5 s of the fence's 4 s.
