The host sends each read-set path until the one call reports it kept under exactly that name; the database keeps the run's read set (one row per path, letter case aside) and checks all of it every time, and a second spelling of a kept path is sent every time, as before. The call is made through the run's own authority check: refused before sending once the run is closed or its authority lapsed, and its authority held to the rules of the run's own reads. Without the function (PostgREST `PGRST202`) the host makes every separate call. Per model request: 4 rounds and 7 calls become 1; a simple reply goes from 9 rounds and 15 authority calls to 6 and 9, a tool round from 6 and 11 to 3 and 5.
- The company machine image is built in the node's image build space (2026-10-01): `deploy/machine/build-machine-image.sh <package> <node-version> <node-sha256> <build-project> <build-network> <storage-pool> [machines-project]` makes its builder a build machine there (no profiles, its own root, the isolation NIC, the builder marker), so the node stays ready while it builds. The image lands in the store the machines project uses: published there when the build space shares the default project's images (node 2's does), exported and imported with the same fingerprint when it has its own. It refuses the machines and default projects and a build space in use. The build project, network and pool are now required.
- Core work runs deep (contract 17, the 2026-10-22 amendment; migration `20260930532000`, SQL test 789). The model policy declares `unattended: high`. Under a bundle that declares it, a run no member waits on runs at high effort, or at the `effort` of its agent's agent.md when the file names one: a worker's own run, an agent's first run (a session turn whose only input is a hire's kickoff), a coworker's message and a member's finished task. Work that carries on a chain (a worker's report, a machine event or check-in, an office start: a wake with an origin run) keeps the effort the chain's first run was pinned with. A member's turn keeps its conversation's Model menu effort and fast tier, and a routine run its routine's effort. `qorpera_execution_runtime_manifest` accepts `unattended` (low, medium or high), `qorpera_manifest_allows_reasoning_effort` allows it, and the pin trigger takes the run's effort from the new `qorpera_resolve_run_reasoning_effort(run, manifest)`. Under a bundle without `unattended` every run resolves as before. Needs a release cut: the activation of a bundle that declares `unattended` must require `20260930532000`.
- The member company gateway passes a folder object's bytes through as they arrive (17 Nov 2026, the direct byte path; `supabase/functions/member-company-gateway`). `GET /v1/member-folder-objects/` no longer reads the whole object, up to 8 MiB, into the function's memory before answering: the upstream's status and allow-listed headers go out as soon as they are in, and the body follows piece by piece, still cut off past 8 MiB. One function worker has 150 MB, and a member's page asks for up to 32 pieces of 4 MiB at once, so a large app or several members could exhaust it. The route's deadline (25 s) now bounds the wait for the upstream's answer and each wait for its next piece, not the member's own download, so a slow line is no longer cut off. A failure before the first byte answers as before (the upstream's own refusal, 502 for an answer that declares more than 8 MiB, 503 otherwise); one after it ends the response, and the member's client, which checks every object against its hash, reads it again. Every other route still answers from one buffer. Deploy: the function, with any release.
- Agents are told to save an app's pictures as WebP, and save_app names the heavy PNGs an app shows (Jonas, 19 Nov 2026: "Agents save smaller", step 4 of the slow-loads design). Measured on a member's test company: the eight PNGs of one board app, renders 1280 to 2560 px wide that its agent wrote from a machine, were 9.4 MB; the same pixels as WebP at quality 90 are 0.72 MB, and every member downloads each picture an app shows. `app_guide` says it at the end of every data kit's guide and of `style`, for a page written by hand (`cloud-app-guides.ts`, one paragraph: save a photo, a render or a screenshot as .webp with `cwebp -q 90` or Pillow, which the machine image has; .png for a small flat graphic that must stay exact, .svg for what is drawn). A save of a data kit's app whose data names PNGs of more than 400 KiB in the app's folder says so in the answer's `look`, with their names (three at most) and their size together (`kitHeavyPictures`, `cloud-app-kits.ts`). Nothing is refused and no file is changed: the agent decides. `PLATFORM_UPDATES` gains `app-pictures-webp`, so a Co-Founder hears of it in its next planning run. No tool's schema changes.

### Fixed
- `manage_worker`, `manage_todo` and a worker's report now say which field to correct when they refuse their arguments (20 Nov 2026; no SQL). Every check gave a bare `INVALID_ARGUMENTS`, so an agent whose coding session start failed a check could not see why, called it a platform error and asked the founders for Qorpera's support (mywave, 17-20 Nov). Each check now raises a `CloudToolArgumentRefusal` naming the field and what it takes, never the value sent.
- A run no longer stops after an agent reads a company file, a listing, a link or a table that shows a token-shaped string (keyholes lane, 13 Nov 2026; no SQL, no tool text changes). The tool boundary cuts a secret out of every answer (a `whsec_` webhook secret, `npm_`, `ghp_`, `github_pat_`, `sk_`/`rk_` test or live keys, `re_` keys, also URL-encoded or JSON-escaped) and hashes what is left, and the context view cites a read under that hash; `released()` released the answer under the hash of the uncut text, so SQL refused the next model request (`42501 Context read provenance differs`, "Native boundary execution stopped"). Live since the cut covered every tool answer (8 Nov 2026). `released()` now cuts first, releases and returns the text as the model is shown it, and counts its bytes for the observation; the cut never lengthens a text and cutting again changes nothing, so the boundary's own cut and every fitted answer are unchanged. Covers every tool that releases through it: `company_read`, `company_list`, `company_write`'s link answer, `table_read`, `table_write`, `save_app`, `post_app` and `update_wall`. Proof: `scripts/prove-read-token.mjs` (the real patched Codex; a control run at master stops at the first token read).
- A stall of the job lease's renewal no longer starves the native lease, and a stage's timeout or lost transport goes on by itself (reliability audit B1, B2 and A2, 10 November 2026; no SQL). The runner renewed the native lease (120 s) only after the job lease's renewal had its answer, and that renewal keeps asking every 10 s for most of its 6 minutes, so a stall of about 75 s left the native lease unrenewed and the run was cut at its end, then sat silent until the job lease lapsed. Both leases are now renewed at once each round, each with its own retries and deadline, and while the job lease's renewal is still being asked the native lease is renewed again every 45 s, so a stall of the job's renewal alone ends the run only by the job lease's own rule (about its 6 minutes); the native lease's next renewal keeps its due time across rounds, so a late job renewal and a slow heartbeat start the next round at once instead of an interval later (the review of #1630: a 10 s answer then came after the lease's end); a refusal of either still stops the run at once, the heartbeat still follows both and never overlaps a renewal (both use the runner's session token, which the heartbeat rotates), and a stall of both renewals still ends the run at the native lease's end (the lease is 120 s; that length is unchanged). A failed run was reported retryable only for five timeouts before its start; now also a `TIMEOUT` or `TRANSPORT_UNAVAILABLE` in a stage that ends before the model's turn can have sent anything (the run's context, wake, connectors, prompt and admission, the host's login download, first authority check and login opening, and Codex's start up to its thread), before or after the start, on the job's first and second attempts (`cloudRunnerJobFailureIsCutOff`). The database still decides: after the start `qorpera_resume_run_from_log` (20260928519120) resumes it as a cut-off, its open receipts turn `outcome_unknown` and the agent checks before it repeats a step, eight attempts at most. A failure during or after the model's turn (the turn's start and completion, the run's tools, its public completion, a coding session's turn) stays final for now: a run that keeps a log is resumed without asking whether it already sent its final message (the 20260930539000 gate; 20260930547000 checks that only for a resume from receipts), and widening the rule waits for that gate. The cap of two keeps a failure that repeats from spending more of a member's model usage; the third attempt's failure is final and shown. Every other failure stays final.
- A run a member is waiting for no longer queues behind older wakes (migration `20260930602000`, SQL test 1102; tests 563, 599, 640, 643 and 810 updated). The claim took the oldest job, so a burst of wakes released together when a login's usage limit reset stood in front of a member's message: on 4 November 2026 a room post waited up to 1430 s, against 0.8 s when nothing was queued. The claim now takes `qorpera_run_member_waits(run)` first: a room delivery, or a session turn with a member's message. A session turn whose only input is a hire's kickoff is not one of these (the 20260930532000 rule). A fast host's refusal for being full or retiring (`SANDBOX_CAPACITY_EXHAUSTED`, `SANDBOX_RETIRING`) makes such a run wait 2 s instead of 15, so the next free slot goes to it, and that 2 s never shortens a wait already running. A node's refusal of a fresh VM (`NODE_CAPACITY_EXHAUSTED`) keeps 15 s: each try is a whole LXD acquire and leaves the attempt bound to that provider. A wake keeps 15 s. Who pays, logins, authority and leases are unchanged.


- The shared secret vault no longer stalls for seconds at a time (perf sprint A5, 7 Nov 2026; 6 to 12 s measured on node 1 after 4 days): its token revocation, event verification, bank and Digital Post passes each run under a plain signal of their own (`abort-scope.ts`, the runtime's copy of the runner orchestrator's), not under the vault's closing signal, which Node 22's `AbortSignal.any` made gather a reference per call and walk them all at each collection.
- A turn that ends without what its run owes gets one more turn before the run fails (Jonas, 10 Nov 2026, on the card "one of its steps was refused" under his own message: "look into it", then "Yes, build step 1" of the reliability audit; no SQL). Run `49ae00a9` read three files for a member's question, ended its turn on four tokens of private text and never called `send_message`; the run failed as `NATIVE_PUBLIC_COMPLETION:UNKNOWN` and the member got no answer. A run had exactly one model turn, and the only guard was a sentence in the instructions.
  - **One more turn.** `runCloudCodexToolSession` takes `turnCorrection`: asked once when the agent's turn has completed, it answers what the model is told in one more turn of the same thread, with everything the first turn read still in front of it (`CodexBoundaryRpc.beginNextTurn`: the thread, its request ids and its fenced outputs carry over; the turn binding, its end and its observation start anew, each turn's observation counts its own usage, and nothing binds to the ended turn's id again). A company run asks for the final send it owes (also after a progress message, which is never a run's last word, and a dynamic routine's decision first); a worker for its `report_work`; a coding turn for `coding_finish` or `coding_ask`, and in a repository kept in Qorpera for `coding_finish` only with work that is whole and passes its checks, since a hand-in there lands at once (`cloudNativeTurnCorrection`). What the added turn leaves undone fails the run as before.
  - **Its own code.** A run that still ends without its final send fails with `[code=AGENT_TURN_ENDED_WITHOUT_FINAL_SEND]` (`CloudAgentNoFinalSend`, read by its type), not `NATIVE_PUBLIC_COMPLETION:UNKNOWN`, which the web read as a refused step.
  - **A reply's save is asked again.** `send_message` and `ask_member`'s card waited 5 s for the database, once. Both are safe to repeat (by idempotency key and event id) and take the run's row, so each try now waits as long as the database may run it and a lost or late answer is asked again while the run's authority holds, as a receipt's begin and completion are (`cloudRunRowWrite`, `CLOUD_RUN_ROW_WRITE_MS`). The tool call's own signal goes with it, so nothing is asked again once the tool boundary has closed, though the run is still being closed. Before, a slow database told the model "outcome unconfirmed" for a message that may have been stored, and a model that did not repeat the call failed its run as one that never answered.
  - **A control request has to be answered.** Codex's handshake (`initialize`, `config/read`, `skills/list`), every `thread/start` and every `turn/start` now fail the run as `:TIMEOUT` when Codex has not answered in 120 s and no model request for the very thread or turn being started has reached the fence meanwhile (that start is under way then, and its answer may be waiting for this host; a request of another thread, such as the compactor's, lifts nothing). Each is answered at once (a turn's start before its first model request), so one left unanswered is a stuck process, and the run used to wait on it for good, holding its conversation. This is no cap on a run: a turn still runs as long as its work takes.
- A company no longer stops when its founder stops (live test companies, 4 November 2026: all 23 routines woke at 09:00 the next morning and nothing ran for 16 hours; migration `20260930570000`, SQL test 570; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-COMPANY-PACE.md`). Follow-ups: when an agent's open todos (not blocked, no active worker, no open human task, no member's Stop) changed outside its routines since its dynamic routine last ran and stayed quiet for 30 minutes plus the agent's offset, while the agent is active, runs nothing, has nothing sooner and the routine is outside its quiet hours, the folder tick brings that routine's queued run forward to now with `wake.payload.followUp` (`step`, `todos`) and the attempt of the run that gave way; a run already brought forward is never brought forward again, each one doubles the next wait, and a routine run's own saves bring nothing forward, so a run that only notes "still waiting" ends them. Spread start times: `cloud-routine-pace.ts` gives each agent an offset of 1 to 20 minutes from its id, and a start it sets on the hour or half hour (weekly `localTime`, `firstRunAt`, `latestAt`, `decide_routine`'s `at`) moves that much later, never to the routine's end. The `manage_routine`, `decide_routine` and dynamic routine wake texts say the next run is minutes to a few hours away while the agent can move the work without a member, not the next morning.
- A run no longer fails when the node holding its attempt's machine reservation is full (migration `20260930563000`, SQL test 563). The LXD provider reserves the attempt before its node check and keeps the reservation when the node refuses for capacity; every other provider's acquire of that attempt then failed with a plain 422 (`PROVIDER_ACQUIRE`), and `qorpera_release_runner_job_claim` counted it, so after eight the job failed (4 November 2026: a coworker message between agents was lost). Such a release now defers that orchestrator for 15 s with `placement_deferred` `ATTEMPT_RESERVED_ELSEWHERE` and counts nothing.
- A failed coding turn reads as its own, in plain words (live company-building test, 3 November 2026; migration `20260930560000`, SQL test 560, 553 edited; no runtime code). Session 27038e92's second turn worked eleven minutes, then ChatGPT answered Codex with its own overload (`codexError=serverOverloaded` in the host's journal; the native adapter's `provider_outage` signal). Its files were all saved in the company folder's worktree, which the next turn keeps as it is, but the agent read "The coding turn did not finish: Native provider bridge request failed (422) [code=NATIVE_CODEX_TURN_COMPLETION:PROVIDER_FAILED]; durable attempt output requires review" and the Co-Founder passed "422 NATIVE_CODEX_TURN_COMPLETION:PROVIDER_FAILED" to the founder; the conversation also showed the coding run as "An earlier run of Co-Founder did not finish". `qorpera_coding_turn_failure` now reports a turn whose run failed with a `provider_outage` signal (for the version it pays with, from the job's last attempt, not paused or cut off afterwards, as for usage limits) as reason `provider_outage` with fixed words (ChatGPT overloaded or a server error; usually brief; the login is fine), and any other turn the executor reported with its run's error and a code in fixed words for the code's class (ChatGPT refused, lost contact, no answer in time, lost connection, login not loaded, no submission, out of computer hours, an unfinished save, else Qorpera's side), keeping the code as its reason; every such summary says the files stay in the workspace and a follow-up continues from them. A login's words that carry the turn's own report carry these words. `qorpera_list_staff_execution_activity` no longer lists a failed wake run that is a coding session's turn as its agent's failure (the session's card says it), unless it left an operation of unknown outcome (an executing or ambiguous receipt, or `RUN_COMPLETION_REFUSED`), which only the run's recovery card settles; a running coding turn and the 542000 supersession are unchanged.
- A coding session's `git commit` no longer fails for a missing author: before each command the coding guest writes `[user]` (`Qorpera coding agent <coding-agent@noreply.qorpera.com>`, `CODING_GIT_IDENTITY`) into the home's `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME/git/config` once, when neither it nor `~/.gitconfig` names an email (`ensureCodingGitIdentity`; best effort). `~/.gitconfig` and the repository's config still override it. Codex's first commit in the SAML shop session failed with "Author identity unknown" (3 November 2026).
- Agents never paste error codes to members: the company context's "Writing to people" says so, with the example of `422 NATIVE_CODEX_TURN_COMPLETION:PROVIDER_FAILED` read as "ChatGPT was overloaded and the coding turn stopped; the work so far is kept" (the Co-Founder pasted it on 3 November 2026).
- The automatic review reads a new agent's first run as one no member started, and the Co-Founder's role names the planning routine its founding run sets up (live company-building test, 3 November 2026; no SQL; `cloud-action-review.ts`). The founding run (d9e7affc, 11:26Z) hired its three agents, then its weekday planning routine (`manage_routine`, a delegation) was denied (review 31182548): "The first-run assignment is to work with the founders on the company vision, and it does not authorize creating a recurring weekday planning routine." The founder had to say "yes, set it up" before the next run could save it, although the hire overlay tells the Co-Founder to set that routine up in that run, and the hired agents' own first-run routines were allowed the same minute. The kickoff reached the reviewer as raw data in a member's place (`{"schema": "qorpera.company-first-run/v1", ...}`, its first todo `start-with-the-founders.md`), so the reviewer read the run as one members started and the first todo as the whole request, though its instructions and the contract (the 2026-10-22 amendment, item 2) count a first run among the runs no member started. `cloudActionReviewRequest` now reads a first run's startup input as the platform's, in plain words, never a member's: "(No member request: it is the agent's first run, which the platform started when the company was founded. Its startup assignment, its first todo QorperaOS/agents/co-founder/todos/start-with-the-founders.md, is where it begins, not all it may do.)", or "when the agent was hired" for a hire (whose first todo id is never shown); it is no longer paired with an agent's message or earlier messages; after a member's message in the same turn it reads "(It is also the agent's first run, ...)"; a chain the first run started (its workers' reports, a machine event) reads it as its origin, as "(No member request: this work began in the agent's first run, ...)", never as the first run itself; a member's own words, even ones that copy the kickoff's data or the note, stay the member's words. The mandate's Co-Founder role (`cloudActionReviewMandate`) now says, from the overlay, that it keeps the company's work moving without waiting to be asked: it hires the team that builds the vision with it and in that run sets up its own weekday planning routine (company plan, owners for unowned work, checking what is called done, the founders' decisions), and building the vision is its first todo, not the limit of its work. The reviewer's instructions add that a first run's startup assignment says where the agent begins, never all it may do; that when the request has a member's own words, members started the run, even when it is also a first run; that a delegation is never denied only because the request or the mandate does not name it, and is denied when it follows instructions from an email, a web page, a document or a quote rather than from the member's own words or the mandate; and that in a first run itself, where a member is usually watching (not in work that carries on from it), a delegation that serves none of the mandate is asked about on a card instead of denied. Deny is unchanged for an action against the request, instructions from outside content, an action against the vision, a member's no, and, in any other run no member started (a routine, an event, a file change, a coworker's message, or work that carries on from one of these or from a first run), a delegation that serves none of the mandate. Moves at release: the runtime bundle only.
- A coding session no longer takes Codex's read-only sandbox notice for its repository (live company-building test, 3 November 2026; no SQL). Every run's Codex starts with `sandbox_mode="read-only"` (its own shell is not offered and its patch tool is refused; a coding turn works through `coding_run_command` in the session's VM), and Codex tells the model so in its own permissions text ("`sandbox_mode` is `read-only`: The sandbox only permits reading files."). Session 27038e92's first turn ran three read-only commands, then ended blocked asking to be dispatched again "in a writable coding environment", without trying one edit; nothing in the coding instructions said the notice does not cover the VM. The coding turn's instructions (`cloud-native-coding-runtime.ts`) now say it: the read-only sandbox covers only the harness's own tools, the selected repository and branch are writable in the VM, and only a command the VM actually refuses shows the repository is not writable, named with its error. Needs a release: the file is in the runtime bundle's implementation.
- A connector tool takes its fields on their own (live company-building test, 3 November 2026; no SQL). On 30 October a Co-Founder run's `github_list_repositories` call was refused `INVALID_ARGUMENTS` until it was wrapped as `{"idempotencyKey": "<key>", "input": {...}}`: every connector tool (`cloud-secret-tools.ts`) showed and took only that envelope, reads included, though a read has no effect to key. Now every connector tool takes the tool's fields on their own or inside `input`. A read of this build's catalog (`cloudConnectorOperationReads`: the same operation, version and adapter hash) shows the model its own fields as its schema and needs no key: without one it is keyed by the call's id, so the same list later in the run asks the provider again. A write keeps showing the envelope and still needs the model's own `idempotencyKey`, beside its fields or with `input`; without one it is refused with "idempotencyKey is required for this write: a new key for each new write, the same key when retrying the same write", which the key's schema description also says. A key made from a write's fields was ruled out (PR #1410 review F1): the run's ledger keeps one receipt per key, so a later identical write (undoing a change, a second reminder) would have been answered from the first one's receipt without running. `parseArguments` always answers the envelope, so the review, the card and the effect read one shape. The refusals name both shapes ("Send the tool's fields on their own, or as …"; once a call sent `input`, "With input, the call must be exactly …"), and the catalog refuses an operation with a field named `input`. Needs a release: the runtime bundle.
- Settings → AI subscriptions says a company takes account logins only when a sign-in there would become one (3 November 2026, found by #1403's builder; migration `20260930554000`, SQL test 685). `qorpera_list_account_model_logins` answered `enabled` when the switch was on and the shared vault served the company, but the sign-in scope (`qorpera_model_login_session_scope`) and the linking also need the company to take sign-ins in the vault (`qorpera_model_login_sign_in_url`). A company the shared vault serves that was switched back to node sign-ins (or whose registration had not finished) read `enabled`, and a sign-in begun there became a company-only login. `enabled` now asks all three.
- Company sites can never be served through the API's name, even past its 404 (follow-up of the company sites review, 3 November 2026; no SQL). The front's api host passed a visitor's `X-Forwarded-Host` on unchanged (the gateway adds one only when it is absent), so a request to api.qorpera.com that named `sites.qorpera.com` there reached `site-serve` as if it came on the sites host; the function also took a comma list's first entry, and without the header it took `Host`. The api host in `deploy/sites/envoy-front-sites.yaml` now drops a visitor's `X-Forwarded-Host` and sets the Host the request asked for (the value the gateway already set for every request without one, so no caller sees a change). `site-serve` serves only when `X-Forwarded-Host` is exactly the sites host: one value (no list, no second header), no other port, no trailing dot, letters in any case; `Host` never decides. The password limits keep `CF-Connecting-IP` as their only key (Cloudflare's edge sets it on every request and replaces a visitor's; `X-Forwarded-For`, `X-Real-IP`, `True-Client-IP` and `Forwarded` were never read), now only when it holds one IPv4 or IPv6 address, else one shared key, so no spelling makes a fresh key; an IPv4-mapped address counts as its IPv4. Slugs were already one counter each (lowercase only, an escaped letter refused). `deploy/sites/envoy-front-sites.test.mjs` runs the block in api-front's own Envoy (v1.39.1) on the node test machines and shows what reaches a function for each Host and `X-Forwarded-Host` a visitor can send. Moves at release: the `site-serve` function and the front's `virtual_hosts:` block.
- A first login choice takes a login that works and compares room fairly (live company-building test, 3 November 2026; migration `20260930559000`, SQL test 559; no runtime code). A new conversation (`qorpera_select_member_session_account`), new work (`qorpera_starter_sponsor`) and a new coding session (`qorpera_coding_owner_login`) took the login with the most room left: the founder's ChatGPT workspace plan, whose only window is 30 days long and 3% used (97 left), beat a weekly window at 50% (50 left), and that plan refused every Codex turn (`[code=NATIVE_CODEX_TURN_COMPLETION:PROVIDER_FAILED]`). They now order the logins by `qorpera_native_account_rank`, best first: a login whose provider refused its last three finished runs, the newest in the last six hours, comes after every other one (`qorpera_native_account_turns_refused`; never switched off; chosen as before six hours after its newest refusal, or once a run completes or fails another way); then one at its usage limit; then one that completed a run in the last seven days with at least 20% left in each window first (`qorpera_native_account_turn_completed`); then the most room on one scale (`qorpera_native_account_paced_room`: each window's remaining percent divided by the share of the window still to run, 5% to 100%, the tightest deciding; 50% of a week that resets in a day is 350, 97% of 30 days with 27 to go about 108); unknown usage last. An account login's companies count together, as for its usage limit. Which logins may pay, the consent and plan rules, and every kept login (a conversation's, work that follows a run, a pinned coding session) are unchanged: the payer never changes without the member. `qorpera_list_member_native_execution_connections` also returns each login's `recent_turns` (`refused`, `completed`). A new index on the native leases (`provider_connection_id, created_at desc`) keeps the reads short.
- A coding session whose login cannot pay says so, and its owner can move it to another of their logins (live company-building test, 3 November 2026; migration `20260930551000`, SQL test 553, 549 and 001 updated; no runtime code). Session 774b2e2c's founder signed out the login it was dispatched on (connection revoked); the Co-Founder's retry then had its wake cancelled at admission ("Wake execution authority is unavailable": the session's sponsor binding still pays with the revoked connection, as the payer never changes without the member), no run began, and the executor completed the turn failed with "The coding turn did not finish." and no reason, which the agent could not act on. Now `qorpera_coding_session_login_state(worker)` reads whether the session's login can pay for its next turn as admission checks it (`ready`, `removed`, `disabled`, `needs_sign_in`, `not_allowed_away`, `unavailable`: that kind of login is switched off or its credential is not active), and `qorpera_complete_coding_turn` reports a failed turn through `qorpera_coding_turn_failure`: a login that cannot pay first (reason `login_<state>`, fixed words: why the turn could not run, that the session does not move by itself, and that its owner can choose another login on the session's card; a turn whose own run ran keeps the executor's summary after them), then a run stopped at its login's usage limit (20260930549300's words, now pointing at the session's card; reason `usage_limit`), then a wake cancelled for its payer's authority (`login_unavailable`); any other failure keeps the executor's summary with its own code as the reason (the `[code=...]` of the summary or of the run's last error, else `turn_not_begun`, `run_not_started`, `ended_without_submission`, `stopped` or `turn_failed`). The reason is the worker result's `reason` (so the turn's `outcome.reason`) and the `failed` event's `code`; the session view adds `loginState` and `reason`. The owner reads the logins that may pay for the session with `qorpera_list_coding_session_logins` and moves it with `qorpera_choose_coding_session_login` (their own login for the harness, allowed to work while they are away, with an active credential, not a ChatGPT plan; never while a turn runs; a login is the session's `current` one only while the session pays with that very version and can, so the same login allowed to work while away again in a newer version is offered again): the session then pays with the binding `qorpera_coding_owner_sponsor` would find or make for that login, from its next turn, and a `step` event `{loginChosen: true, from, to}` (the connection ids) records the move. Contract section 17, 2026-11-03 amendment.
- A worker stopped at a usage limit tells its agent so in plain words (live company-building test, 3 November 2026; migration `20260930549300`, SQL test 549; no runtime code). A coding turn whose run stopped at its login's usage limit reached the agent as "The coding turn did not finish: Native provider bridge request failed (422) [code=NATIVE_CODEX_TURN_COMPLETION:BUDGET_EXHAUSTED]", and the Co-Founder told the founder it might need platform support. When the worker's run has a usage-limit signal for the login it runs on, from an attempt that was not paused and resumed afterwards (W5a-1 keeps signals per attempt), `qorpera_complete_coding_turn` (a failed turn's own run, by its turn row) and `qorpera_folder_worker_ended` (an in-house worker, reason `usage_limit`) now report fixed words instead (`qorpera_worker_usage_limit_text`): the login paying for this work reached its usage limit; it resets on <day> at <time> UTC (the signal's reset, else the latest reset of the login's full usage windows, else left out); the work does not move to another login by itself; the member can choose another of their logins for it (the conversation's Login menu) or wait. The worker's result, its events and its results.md say it; the coding `failed` event keeps `usageLimit`. Other failures keep their summaries.
- A run's admission and context bind wait as long as the database may run them (3 November 2026; no SQL). Run f4634342 (04:49Z) failed `NATIVE_EXECUTION_ADMISSION:TIMEOUT` while the hosted database answered every caller in 3 to 6.6 s for about 8 s: `qorpera_bind_native_company_context` was cut by the path control's own 3 s default and `cloudToolRequest`'s 5 s, and `qorpera_begin_native_company_execution_v3` (and a coding turn's `qorpera_begin_native_coding_execution`, through the same `beforeModelStart`) by the same 5 s. Both company calls now pass `CLOUD_RUN_ROW_READ_MS` (20 s: PostgREST's 10 s pool wait, the standard entry point's 8 s statement bound and 2 s for the round trip) to `cloudToolRequest` and to `CloudCompanyPathControl.call`, and `beforeModelStart` gives the run's admission the same. Each is a write sent once: nothing here asks it again, the runner never tries a failed admission again (`cloudRunnerJobFailureBeforeStartIsRetryable` names only the context, wake, connector, prompt and login-download timeouts), and the database refuses a second start of the same attempt, so a longer wait cannot apply either twice. The calls before them in the opening are prompt composition, whose timeouts the runner already retries from the start.
- A short outage of the vault's database no longer kills runs (3 November 2026; no SQL). Both shared vaults lose their database sessions in bursts through the session pooler (28 Oct to 3 Nov), and the vault answered every caught error with 403 `unavailable`, which a run host's login download takes for a refusal (`NATIVE_CREDENTIAL_DOWNLOAD`, final). The vault's one database client (`cloud-secret-broker-service-main.ts`) now marks each failure with its class (`cloud-vault-database-failure.ts`: its SQLSTATE, or for one without a SQLSTATE a system error code, `pool timeout`, `connect timeout`, `query timeout`, `connection ended` or `no sqlstate`), and a broker call that passes its own 5 s is marked `call deadline` (`oneCloudSecretRpc`, never when its caller stopped it). A request whose failure is the database's own unavailability (no SQLSTATE, class 08, 57P01-57P03, 53300, 57014, or XX000 as the pooler's own answer: FATAL or without the source routine every Postgres error carries, since Supavisor reports its pool and session failures that way while this repository's SQL raises XX000 on purpose for broken invariants) is answered 503 `{"error":"busy"}` with `retry-after: 5`, and the vault logs that line with the class only; a SQLSTATE the database raised on purpose (42501, 55000, 22023, P0001, XX000 with a routine, ...) and every other failure stay the opaque 403. On the shared vault, a company not yet served while the refresh that would list it found the database unavailable (a restart during an outage) is answered busy too, not 404. The run host's model-login client throws `CloudSecretBrokerBusyError` for a 503, which the host takes for a lost answer (`credentialDownloadLost`): the download fails as `NATIVE_CREDENTIAL_DOWNLOAD:TIMEOUT` and the runner tries the run again from the start (`cloudRunnerJobFailureBeforeStartIsRetryable`), within the database's existing limit of eight attempts while nothing of the attempt is durable. An older host takes the 503 for a refusal, as it took the 403; a newer host never sees one from an older vault. The vault's background passes now log the failure's class (`Shared vault: a background pass failed (class: 57P01); ...`, and likewise the model login upkeep and connector token revocation lines), never its text, and the pool's `error` event, which an idle session the pooler ends raises and which had no listener (Node ends a process on an unhandled `error`), is logged by class.
- The host's journal names Codex's own error for a failed turn (3 November 2026; no SQL). A turn that failed reported only `category=provider_failed`, so a login whose turns ended after two seconds with no tokens could not be told apart. `nativeCodexErrorToken` turns Codex's `codexErrorInfo` from the last non-retry `error` notification and the failed `turn/completed` into a fixed name: a pinned name as it is (`unauthorized`, `badRequest`, `usageLimitExceeded`, ...), `<name>:<status>` for the four status forms (`httpConnectionFailed:401`, `responseStreamDisconnected:null`), `null`, or `unknown` for anything else; a name is kept over a later `unknown` or `null`, which say nothing of the cause, and an interrupted turn has none. It travels in the failure's metadata (`codexError`, checked against the same lists) to the line `Native provider request rejected: Native provider execution failed (stage=codex-turn-completion, category=provider_failed, codexError=unauthorized) [code=NATIVE_CODEX_TURN_COMPLETION:PROVIDER_FAILED]`. The stored code is unchanged: it keeps its two parts, which this runner and older ones check exactly (`bridgeRefusalCode`), and the run's observation rows have no field for it (the SQL ingestion refuses unknown keys).
- A coding turn's read whose answer is cut off after its headers is asked again, as one with no answer at all (release PR #1390 review F2, 2 November 2026; no SQL). R2's entry below tells a read's lost answer apart only while the request waited for its headers. A connection lost while the body came (undici's `TypeError: terminated`), or the control's own 30 s cut then, was thrown as it came, so it ended the turn's first reads and the run's authority check instead of being asked again. Now `HttpCloudCodingControl` answers it `transport_unavailable` for `qorpera_authorize_coding_turn` and `qorpera_read_coding_turn_context`. Nothing says its statement ended, so the caller waits out the copy before it asks again. The turn's cancellation, a body over 256 KiB or not UTF-8 or JSON, and every failure of a write are thrown as before. Needs a release: the file is in the runtime bundle's implementation.
- A resumed attempt that has not recorded its resume uses no approval and gets no new review, card or email (A3, 1 November 2026; RC_A_RESUME_REVIEW_CARD_FENCE and RC_A_RESUME_MAIL_FENCE; migration `20260930546000`, SQL tests 509 and 546; no runtime change). G1 b's entry below said such a run "repeats nothing", and F9's that "Three take none" of the effects that take no receipt. Both said too much, and both are corrected in place, only in those words ("repeats no effect that takes a receipt", "At least three take none"); F9's entry still quotes G1 b's words as they stood. Before its first receipt such an attempt still used an approval (`qorpera_begin_action_review` answered `approved` and marked the approval used, for the woken run's own card or for the action's latest answered review), was held on a waiting card (`pending`: the runtime holds the call there and awaits the answer), got a new review (`review`, after which `qorpera_record_action_review` wrote a new review row and, for an ask with a card, the card: its session event and the event's company operation; the replay of an ask with a card answered with that card, and the runtime holds on it) and queued a new email (`qorpera_send_agent_mail` wrote an outbox row and its mail thread). This card is the approval card, not F9's `ask_member` card. Now each refuses such an attempt with G1 b's `55000` ("Run resume is not recorded") before its first new write and before an approval is used. It is F9's check, nested the same way (`qorpera_memory_run_resume_of` may also refuse with `55000`, "Run resume is unavailable"). Where it sits: begin checks at four points, right before each of the two updates that mark an approval used, before `pending` and before `review`, so after the run check, the request check and the review reads; a denial (`declined`) and an approval another call used (`used`) answer as before. Record checks in the replay of the same call, after the other-content check (`23505`), only for an ask with a card (an allow, a deny and an ask with no card replay as before), and, for a new review, after every check of the record's shape and values (`22023`, P40's `apply_proposal` check among them; only reads lie between) and before a card's read recheck, its conversation lock, the review insert and the card. The email checks after the run check, the request checks, the same call's replay, the mailbox checks and the folder-v2 attachment refusal (`0A000`), and before the rest of the attachments: for a company outside folder-v2 the attachment loop records the run's reads first (`qorpera_company_path_remember_read`, a write), so the recipient limits (`54000`) and the check that nothing goes out as if from Qorpera (`42501`) stay after it. Unchanged: who may call (an attempt a newer one replaced is refused `42501` before these checks, as is any caller other than the service role); every earlier check, its order and its error; the other replays, read-only as before; the run and row locks (a row lock a refused begin took is released with the refusal); an original attempt, a recorded resume, a run that keeps a log and a run in Agent tree mode; the tables, grants, settings and data. G1 b's and F9's migrations stay as written. Limits: only these effects are fenced; this claims nothing about any other effect. After a denial (`declined`, or the replay of a deny) the runtime may close the proposal an `apply_proposal` call names (`qorpera_folder_close_proposal`); that close is not fenced here. A recorded resume goes on as before: the fences hold an attempt only until it records its resume, and they do not make a resumed run's effects exactly-once. The paths are traced from the source and shown on synthetic fixtures; nothing was seen live, and no email was sent. The migration refuses unless each of the three bodies is exactly the one it replaces (by md5) and each anchor occurs once; its catalog check refuses unless exactly these three public functions changed, only in their bodies. Test 509 (66 cases) shows the unrecorded next attempt refused, with no table changed, an unused approval (also the woken run's own card), a held card, a new review of an action whose approval was used and of one never reviewed, a new ask, allow and deny, `decide_routine`'s new review and the replay of an ask with a card; a denial, an approval another call used, the replays of an allow, a deny and an ask with no card answering as before with no table changed; the shape, value and other-content refusals first, as before; the replaced attempt and a member refused `42501`; an unreadable resume refused `55000`; a run that keeps a log and a run in Agent tree mode working unrecorded; and the attempt reviewed afresh once it records its resume. Test 546 (23 cases) shows the unrecorded attempt's new email refused with no table changed, after an attachment's `0A000` and the request and mailbox refusals, which come first as before; the replay of the first attempt's email answering the stored email with no table changed; the replaced attempt and a member refused `42501`; an unreadable resume refused `55000`; a run that keeps a log and a run in Agent tree mode mailing unrecorded; and the attempt mailing again once it records its resume. Test 609 now lists the three calls among the host calls with nothing to gate: they only read whether the attempt recorded its resume. Deploy order: either.
- A resumed attempt that has not recorded its resume makes no new send, card or rule (F9, WB-001 G1 b's follow-up, 31 October 2026; RC_A_RESUME_SEND_FENCE, RC_A_RESUME_CARD_FENCE and RC_A_RESUME_RULE_FENCE; migration `20260930545000`, SQL test 508; no runtime change). G1 b's entry says that until the runtime follows, a resumed run in Agent notebooks "is refused at its first receipt and repeats nothing". That held only for effects that take a receipt. At least three take none, so such an attempt still made them new, also after its steps were refused: the agent send (`qorpera_folder_send_agent_message`, which every progress and final send reaches through `qorpera_send_native_agent_message`) wrote its send row, its session event, the event's company operation and its speaker; the interaction card (`qorpera_folder_append_interaction_request`, `ask_member`'s card) wrote its session event and the event's company operation, and a card raised before a refused final stayed; the "Ask first" rule (`qorpera_folder_add_agent_review_rule`) wrote its rule row. Now each refuses such an attempt with G1 b's `55000` ("Run resume is not recorded") before that effect. It is G1 b's check, read one step at a time: the run resumes from its receipts, then this assignment has recorded no resume, then the run has a resume to record (`qorpera_memory_run_resume_of`, which may also refuse with `55000`, "Run resume is unavailable", as on the receipt path). Where each check sits: in the send and the card, right after the read recheck (`qorpera_folder_run_reads_current`) and right before the session lock, so after every existing check (in the send: the run check, the argument checks, the same-key replay, "already final" and the wake policy with its phases, budget and acknowledgement; in the card: the run check, the request checks and the group-chat check). The card skips the check when an event with the card's id already exists, so the append still returns that event, or refuses other content (`23505`), as before. In the rule it sits inside `if not found`, after the run check, the recheck, the wake's work gate, the company-agent and request checks and the replays (the same key, other text under it with `23505`, the same rule's text), right before the insert. Unchanged: who may call (an attempt a newer one replaced is refused `42501` before these checks, as is any caller other than the service role); every earlier check, its order and its error; the replays; the run locks; an original attempt, a recorded resume, a run that keeps a log, a run in Agent tree mode and a coding session's turn; the tables, grants and data. G1 b's file and entry stay as written. Limits: the read recheck runs before each check and, when a read changed, rewrites the run's read set (its own bookkeeping); a refused call rolls that back with the call, and a call that goes through, a replay among them, keeps it, as today; this does not fence it. Only these three effects are fenced; this claims nothing about any other effect. The three paths are traced from the source and shown on synthetic fixtures; nothing was seen live, and what the runtime does after a refused final is not settled here. The migration refuses unless each of the three bodies is exactly the one it replaces (by md5) and each anchor occurs once; its catalog check refuses unless exactly these three public functions changed, only in their bodies. Test 508 (54 cases) shows the unrecorded next and third attempts' new progress and final sends, cards and rules refused with no table changed; a card raised before the final refused itself, so no card stays; the replays answered as before with no table changed; the replaced attempt and a non-service caller refused `42501`; the wake's work gate refusing first; a run that keeps a log and a run in Agent tree mode working unrecorded; a read no longer readable refused by the recheck first (`42501`, as before); and the third attempt working once it records its resume. Deploy order: either.
- A new version of an existing sponsor binding keeps who pays (F8, 31 October 2026; migration `20260930544000`, SQL test 506; no runtime change). `qorpera_create_execution_sponsor_binding` lets an owner or admin add a version to any binding of the company by its client id, and it checked only the new login. So an admin could put their own login or a company account on another member's binding: the binding's newest version then named that login, and work that follows the member's runs on that binding (`qorpera_starter_sponsor`) found no payer. A member's binding could also become a company account's, and the other way round. Now, under the binding's lock and after the authorize check, a new version of an existing binding is refused when the newest version pays with a member's login whose owner is not the caller (`42501`, "execution sponsor binding pays with another member's login"), or when it would change the binding between a member's login and a company account (`22023`, "execution sponsor binding cannot change kind"). A newest version the API can't make (no version, its login missing, a member version on a login with no owner, an unknown kind, a company version on a member's login) fails closed (`55000`). A first create, the member's own refresh and their move to another login of theirs, company-to-company versions by an owner or admin (also one who did not create the binding, and between the two company kinds), `qorpera_move_member_login_pins`, `qorpera_coding_owner_sponsor`, the grants and the stored rows are unchanged. The cloud client's `createCompanySponsor` (its only caller outside SQL) sends only company kinds: given the client id of an existing member binding it now throws with `42501` (another member's) or `22023` (the caller's own) in the message, and a new or company binding works as before. Test 506 shows each refusal with no new version and the binding's payer unchanged, and each allowed case.
- A new coding session starts on its owner's login with the most room left (F1, 31 October 2026; migration `20260930543000`, SQL test 505; no runtime change). `qorpera_coding_owner_login` chose among the owner's logins for the harness by the consent for work while they are away, then a login other than a ChatGPT plan, then the newest, so a member whose newest login had reached its usage limit had each new coding session start on it and stop there while an older login had room. New conversations and new work already start on the login with the most room (`qorpera_starter_sponsor`). Now, after the consent and the plan rule, the order is the starter's: a used-up login last, then the most room, unknown usage (-1) after measured room, then the newest, then the connection's id. The room is `qorpera_native_account_remaining` of the login's newest active version, the number the starter reads. The worker rules' check and the payer (`qorpera_coding_owner_sponsor`, which pays exactly that login) agree as before. Only a new session's first choice changes: the payer is still taken once, at the dispatch, and a session keeps it through follow-ups, a member's answers and each turn's wake, also at its limit. Which logins may pay, the refusals, the plan rule and the grants are unchanged; a member whose logins are all used up still gets one (the work stops at its limit). Test 505 shows the older login with room chosen and pinned to the dispatched worker, logins without consent or of another provider, harness or member never chosen even with more room, today's refusals, unknown usage after measured room and ties as today, a pinned session staying on its login at its limit while another has all its room, and the plan rule ahead of the room.
- A coding turn's reads are asked again while the database is slow (R2, 31 October 2026; no SQL). The turn read its authority (`qorpera_authorize_coding_turn`) and then its context (`qorpera_read_coding_turn_context`) once each, 5 s each, before its session started. `HttpCloudCodingControl` answered every failure as a plain one, so the run's checks (`CloudNativeToolRun.retry`) asked again only after a local cut. Now the control tells a read's lost or late answer apart: no answer at all, a 5xx, 408 or 429 with no PostgREST error, or a transient SQLSTATE is `transport_unavailable`, and one whose statement ended (a lock or statement timeout, a serialization failure, a deadlock, `PGRST003`) is `CloudControlRpcEnded`. The turn's two first reads each wait up to `CLOUD_RUN_ROW_READ_MS` (20 s, as long as the database may run them) and are asked again within `CLOUD_CODING_TURN_READ_BUDGET_MS` (30 s from the first attempt), pausing 250 ms doubling to 2 s; a lost answer is asked again only once its copy may have ended, an ended one after the pause. The run's authority check and its turn check, already in the run's retry, now also ask these answers again, for as long as the run's authority holds. A refusal (`42501`, `55000`, `22023`, `P0001`, any other SQLSTATE, or a 4xx with no PostgREST error) and an answer that does not parse are not asked again, and the turn's cancellation stops the asking at once. Writes are unchanged: begin, the context view and the submission are sent once, and every failure of theirs stays plain. The command tools' check, the tool steps' authorize and the submission's turn check still ask once. A turn whose first read stays lost now fails as `NATIVE_CODING_SESSION:TRANSPORT_UNAVAILABLE` instead of `NATIVE_CODING_SESSION:UNKNOWN`. Needs a release: both files are in the runtime bundle's implementation.
- A tool call whose receipt begin the database refused after finding no receipt under its key is answered as refused, not as an unknown outcome, because it started nothing (30 October 2026; no SQL). On production a worker's `manage_worker` call was answered `TOOL_OUTCOME_UNCONFIRMED`: its begin was refused (`55000`, "run has an unresolved operation") because an earlier `manage_todo` receipt in the run was still executing. That begin is one plpgsql call that rolls back whole, the folder core raises that refusal only after it looked the call's key up in the run, under the run's lock, and found no receipt, and the only check before the begin in the call is the authorize, which changes nothing, so the call began nothing. Now `CloudNativeToolOperations.execute` counts the begins it sends. When the database refuses the only one sent with one of the two refusals it raises after that lookup (`55000`, "run has an unresolved operation" or "run operation budget is exhausted"), the call has no receipt and its signal is not aborted, the call's observation finishes `denied` (`authority_unavailable`) and it throws `CloudToolRefusal` with a fixed reason. The model then reads `REFUSED: An earlier change in this run has an unconfirmed outcome, so this run cannot start another change; this call started nothing` for the first, and `REFUSED: The database refused to start this call, so this call started nothing` for the budget. The reasons never repeat the database's words, an id, a key or a supplied value. A repeat of the same key in the run gets the same answer without asking the database again, as a refused authorize before the receipt does. Everything else stays unconfirmed: any other refusal of the begin (the run check, the wake's work gate and the argument checks come before the key lookup, so after a resume the key may already hold an earlier attempt's receipt), a lost or late answer to a begin, a begin asked again (the copy before may begin after it), an abort, and any failure once the receipt exists, including a refused authorize, effect or completion. The one-unresolved-operation rule, receipts, recovery, replay and the retries are unchanged; no tool description changes.
- A coding run's own authority checks wait out a lost copy before they ask again (R2c, 31 October 2026; no SQL). In a coding run, the run's checks (`CloudNativeToolRun`'s opening read, its refresh and each tool step's check) ask `qorpera_authorize_coding_turn`, which takes the run's budget row (the run mutex). R1 gave that wait to the turn check only, so after a lost or late answer the run's check was asked again after 250 ms while its copy could still be waiting for the row. Now a reader can say that its read takes the row: `CloudNativeToolAuthorityReader.runRowMs`, the longest the database may run it after it was sent. The coding runtime's reader sets it to `CLOUD_RUN_ROW_READ_MS` (20 s), and the run's checks pass it to `retry`: a copy whose answer was lost or late is asked again only 20 s after it was sent, unless the database answered that it ended. Each of those checks' attempts now waits that long too, as the turn check's already do (they were cut at 5 s, or 10 s for the check paired with the context read): an authorize that takes 12 s now lands at 12 s with one copy; before, it was cut at 5 s and sent again after 250 ms, so three copies queued on the row and it landed only after about 28 s. Before the first authority a check that would have to wait out its copy past the opening's 30 s gives up at once. What is asked again, the run's deadline and hold, and refusals stay as they were; `open` refuses a `runRowMs` that is not a whole number from 1 ms to 300 s. Readers without it (the native host's describe and the office gateway's, which read the run without the mutex) are asked again as before. No write changes and none is asked again.
- A connector call with malformed arguments is refused with the rule it broke, so the model can correct the call (30 October 2026; no SQL). On production a Co-Founder run's one GitHub list-repositories call was denied as a bare `INVALID_ARGUMENTS`; the same tool worked three minutes later as `{"idempotencyKey": "<key>", "input": {"limit": 50}}`. Now the connector tools (`cloud-secret-tools.ts`) refuse with `CloudToolArgumentRefusal`, so the boundary answers `INVALID_ARGUMENTS: <reason>`. The reason says which rule failed: the call's exact shape (`The call must be exactly {"idempotencyKey": "<key>", "input": {...}}`, adding which of the tool's fields were sent beside `input` instead of in it, or that `idempotencyKey` or `input` is missing); the key's pattern; an unknown field, with the fields the schema has; a missing required field; or a value of the wrong type or out of its range, with the field's path and what the schema allows (its type, length or range, pattern and listed values). Reasons are built only from fixed words, the catalog schema and supplied keys that are plain identifiers (`^[A-Za-z][A-Za-z0-9_]{0,63}$`; any other key is "a field the tool does not take"), never from a supplied value. Listed values are shown only as short printable text, and a reason over 1 KiB gives way to a shorter fixed one. The refusal still comes before any receipt, broker call or effect, its observation is still `invalid_arguments`, and arguments the boundary cannot serialize or that exceed their bound keep the bare `INVALID_ARGUMENTS`. The broker's second check (`validateCloudConnectorInput`) throws the same words, which it does not pass on. No catalog schema, tool description or retry changes.
- A run whose completion the cloud refuses for good fails at once, instead of going silent until the lease sweep fails it as lost contact (30 October 2026; no SQL). On production a Co-Founder room delivery ended with one save's receipt still executing during a database stall, and the reply had posted. `qorpera_complete_runner_job_v6` refused the completion (`55000`, "Governed execution has unresolved or ungoverned work"; the runner's 423). The worker had already stopped renewing, sent no failure, and kept the refused completion staged. Six minutes later the sweep failed the job as "Runner lease expired before durable completion", and the web said Qorpera lost contact. Now a 423 for a completion, whether in the run or replayed after a restart, swaps the staged completion for a staged failure in one saved step (`DisposableCloudRunner.replaceStagedCompletionWithFailure`). It then fails the job, not retryable, with the fixed `RUN_COMPLETION_REFUSED_FAILURE` (`[code=RUN_COMPLETION_REFUSED]`, no id). The cloud ends it as the sweep would: an executing receipt turns ambiguous, nothing is marked completed, the run waits for review, and the native lease ends with the job. One difference: the sweep may resume a run that can resume from its log or receipts, and this failure never does. A failure whose answer is lost is sent again after a restart, never the refused completion. Any other answer to a completion keeps today's handling: a lost or late answer, or a 503, keeps it staged for the replay, and a 401, 403 or 409 drops it on replay. A 409 also stands for a transient `40001`, so it is not taken as final. Refused completions are not asked again; item 7 already asks a lost answer again with the same receipt and result. The `runner-control` edge function now logs one line for every rejected call, with the function's name and its SQLSTATE (`runnerControlRejectionLog`): never the error's message, details or hint, an argument or a token. Deploy order: either; the edge function deploys with the release.
- A tool call's receipt begin and finish are asked again while the database stalls, and wait for the run's row at most 3 s (item 7, R1's follow-up; migration `20260930541000`, SQL test 468). They take the run's budget row (the run mutex) as R1's reads do, and on 28 Oct 2026 a run's calls waited 1 to 7.6 s for it. The runtime gave each 5 s (`cloudToolRequest`) and never asked it again: one that waited longer failed its tool call as "outcome unconfirmed", and a begin that went through after that left its receipt begun, its outcome unknown. Now `CloudNativeToolOperations` sends both through `CloudNativeToolRun.retry` with `CLOUD_RUN_ROW_WRITE_MS` (20 s, as `CLOUD_RUN_ROW_READ_MS`) as each attempt's transport and as `runRowMs`: a lost or late answer is asked again once the copy it replaces has ended, and an answer that says the copy ended (`CloudControlRpcEnded`) after F1's short pause, for as long as the run's authority holds. Every attempt sends the same receipt id, key and input, and the finish the same result, so a copy that did begin answers by its key with the same receipt, and one that did finish answers the same result again; the tool runs once. A refusal (`55000`, `23505`, `42501`, `22023`) and a missing receipt (`QRC01`) are not asked again. `SupabaseNativeProviderControlPlane` now throws such an ended answer (a SQLSTATE other than a lost connection's, or `PGRST003`) as `CloudControlRpcEnded`, as R1's path control does; other lost answers stay `transport_unavailable`. In the database `qorpera_folder_begin_run_receipt` and `qorpera_folder_complete_run_receipt` (the legacy native begin and finish hand every call to them) give up a wait for a lock after 3 s (`lock_timeout`, function-level; the native host's 8 s before), answering `55P03` as before. Test 468 shows both give up within about 3 s while another session holds the run's row, with no transaction left open and the receipt as it was, and that with the row free each answers, and a repeat answers the same. The tool steps keep the entry point's 8 s.
- One run's reads no longer pile up on its budget row while the database stalls (R1; migration `20260930540000`, SQL test 467). On production (28 Oct 2026, the DB stall lane's windows B and D) one run's calls queued 11 to 14 deep on its budget row (the run mutex, `qorpera_folder_tool_run`) for 1 to 7.6 s each, and PostgREST ran out of connections (`PGRST003`, 18:22 UTC). The run's check before each tool call sent its admission and its context read together, and both F1's retries and the fence's asked a read again as soon as an attempt was cut, while the cut copy still waited for the row in the database. Now the check sends the admission first and the context read after it. A read that takes the run's row (the context read, the one-call fence, and the resume calls `qorpera_memory_run_resume`, `qorpera_memory_run_resume_receipts` and `qorpera_memory_run_resume_record`) passes `runRowMs` to `CloudNativeToolRun.retry` (and `assertActiveWith`): a copy whose answer was lost or late is asked again only once the longest the database may run it has passed since it was sent, `CLOUD_RUN_ROW_READ_MS` (20 s: PostgREST's 10 s wait for a connection, the native host's 8 s statement bound, 2 s for the trip) or `CLOUD_RUN_ROW_LONG_READ_MS` (37 s, the long entry point's 25 s) for the fence. Each such attempt, and the context and resume reads' transports, wait that long too (the context read waited 10 s, the resume calls 3 s, and the fence's first attempt 10 s). An answer from PostgREST that says the copy ended or never began (a SQLSTATE other than a lost connection's, such as `55P03` or `57014`, or `PGRST003`) is asked again after F1's short pause: the path control throws it as `CloudControlRpcEnded` (`transport_unavailable`, no code; `cloudControlEnded`, `cloudControlEndedCode`). What is asked again, the refusals, and how long a read is asked again (until the run's authority would lapse) are as before. In the database `qorpera_folder_assert_run_reads` and `qorpera_fence_native_context` give up a wait for a lock after 3 s (`lock_timeout`, function-level; the native host's 8 s before), answering `55P03` as before; a run's reads of its own row through the escalation list and the wake bus get the same bound. Test 467 shows, on two sessions and one run's row, that a `statement_timeout` set inside a function does not bound the statement already running, that a lock wait under `SET LOCAL lock_timeout` does end, and that both reads give up within about 3 s with no transaction left open. The writers keep the entry point's 8 s.
- A run in Agent notebooks that has sent its final message no longer resumes from its receipts (release PR #1390 review F1, 2 November 2026; migration `20260930547000`, SQL test 551; no runtime change). G1 b's check (`qorpera_run_resumes_from_receipts`) did not ask whether the run had sent its final, and neither did the cut-off. So a run whose runner died after its final committed and before its job completed (its lease ran out, it reported a retryable failure, or it failed at its login's usage limit) was queued again, and its next attempt could not end: every new send is refused once the run has a final (`55000`, "agent public output is already final"), a send under the old final's key replays the old message, and the completion counts only that attempt's sends, so a result that names the old final is refused (`55000`, "Native completion does not match durable public delivery"). Now the check also needs the run to have sent no final. Such a run takes v190's path again: its job fails, it waits for review, and it is never queued again. The fences of G1 b, F9 and A3 stay as they are: they hold only a resumed attempt that has not recorded its resume, and such an attempt cannot send its final (F9). Unchanged: the function's name, argument, result, language, volatility, settings, owner and grants (nobody may call it); its callers; a run that has sent no final (it resumes as before, 466); a run that keeps a log (its gate, `20260928549150`, reads no final, as before; not changed or checked here) and a run in Agent tree mode; the tables, settings and data. Limit: a run queued again before this applies (only possible between `20260930539000` and this migration) is not reconciled. If the roll goes back to bundle 190, the migration stays applied, as `20260930539000`, `20260930545000` and `20260930546000` do; that is safe, since it only turns the receipts resume off for a run that has sent its final, and such a run then takes v190's own path. The migration refuses unless the body is exactly G1 b's (by md5) and the marker occurs once; its catalog check refuses unless exactly this function changed, only in its body. Test 551 shows a run with no final still queued again when cut off; after its final, its lease running out, a retryable failure and a failure at its usage limit each failing the job with no cut-off or pause and a review pending; the run completing after its final as before; and a resumed attempt that recorded its resume and sent its final failing the same way when cut off, and completing as before when not. Test 508's one check that the run still resumes from its receipts after its final (sent in Agent tree mode) now checks that it does not; its other cases are unchanged. Deploy order: after `20260930539000`.
- A run in Agent notebooks that is cut off mid-task goes on with the same task, from its receipts (WB-001 G1 b; migration `20260930539000`, SQL test 466; `cloud-run-resume.ts`, `cloud-run-log-open.ts`, `memory-tree/run-work.ts`). Such a run keeps no work log, and the resume gate (`qorpera_run_resumes_from_log`) needed one, so a run whose runner's lease ran out mid-task failed and waited for review. The gate now also takes a run in Agent notebooks that has no log and is no coding session's turn (`qorpera_run_resumes_from_receipts`); a narrow run, a run in Agent tree mode and a refused run take the same path as before. The next attempt is the same task, job and run, and it counts toward both caps of 8. It reads the run's receipts from the new `qorpera_memory_run_resume_receipts` (the service role's only, through the run's binding; null for an original attempt and for a run that keeps a log): every outcome_unknown receipt and the newest 64 completed ones, each by its key and status. No stored result is read. The run may never have seen one: the check of the call's authority before its release refused it, or the run was cut off first. The runtime asks for them beside the log's place, so a run waits for no extra round trip, and builds the attempt's one work message from them (`openCloudRunReceiptsResume`, `cloudRunResumeComposer`). It says which effects completed, which are unknown and must be checked first, and each one's receipt key. It also says how a call's own key appears in a receipt key (most often after the tool's name and a prefix of the tool's own, as in `hire_agent:hire:<operationKey>`). It shows no result: the model reads the current state, or calls the same tool with the same key and arguments, whose replay checks the call's authority again before it returns the stored result. The message goes before the attempt's own work in every request, and nothing is folded. The attempt records the unknown receipts it was told of (`qorpera_memory_run_resume_record`, with no steps read) before it may begin any receipt, a replay too (55000). At its login's usage limit such a run ends as before, with no pause. Receipts work as before: the same key with the same inputs replays its stored result, an outcome_unknown key never runs again, and other inputs under a key are refused. Nothing new is stored, the receipts table's grants are unchanged, and nothing is written to the company folder. Deploy order: the migration before the runtime (a run in Agent notebooks on this runtime fails at its start without it); until the runtime follows, a resumed run in Agent notebooks is refused at its first receipt and repeats no effect that takes a receipt.
- A conversation runs one run at a time, a wake's too (WB G5; migration `20260930538000`, SQL test 465). On production (28 Oct 2026, 16:46 UTC) a member's message in an agent's thread became a second run (4256) beside the agent's running wake: a coworker's message wakes the agent in its own thread, which is also the web's main chat, and on folder-v2 a turn request waited only for a live turn in its conversation while a wake waited for nothing. `qorpera_folder_materialize_company` and `qorpera_folder_materialize_due` now count any queued or leased run of the conversation (a turn's, a wake's or a room delivery's): a turn request waits behind it, and so does a wake, queued with no run and no reason (it is not due and not scanned; a pass that made a run in its conversation first skips it). In a group chat (a conversation with no agent) a wake waits only for its own agent's run there, so the agents of one group chat still answer side by side. A wake with no conversation (a worker's dispatch) never waits, and an agent may still run in two conversations at once. A wake that waits is also cancelled (an audience it can no longer use, a consent withdrawn) only once its conversation is free. Deploy order: either.
- Try again on a failed folder-v2 turn starts a new run (WB F4; migration `20260930536000`, SQL test 464). On production (28 Oct 2026, 16:35 UTC) a member's Try again on a Researcher's failed first run released its input and hid the card, but no run came: the materializer makes a run from a turn request, and the input's one request was already materialized. `qorpera_resolve_failed_execution_run` now needs the run's own materialized request, in an open conversation, and queues a new request for the released input with a new id (so the run, job and runner are new), copying the old request's requester, payer, computer and policy; `recovery_released` names it (`retry_turn_request_id`). A request may follow one earlier request of its own input (`retry_of_request_id`), so an input has one first request and one chain of retries. The materializer is unchanged: its checks and refusals apply to the new request as to any, so a payer that is no longer usable fails it and the input with its reason. The new request is the newest in its conversation, so turns queued before the Try again run first, one at a time. A retried run's wake is its own occurrence (`input:<input>:retry:<run>`), and the first run's state, queue and start read the head of the input's chain. A run with a durable operation outcome or an open proposal is still refused, and so is a Try again by a member other than the one whose ChatGPT plan pays. An input an earlier Try again released (one on production) is not queued again: a new message in its conversation, or a repair, carries it on. Deploy order: either; the web hides the failed card once `recovery_released` exists, as before.
- A run survives a short control-plane outage (WB-001, 28 Oct 2026; no SQL). In a 20 s outage (about 16:04:53 to 16:05:16 UTC) a running Co-Founder turn failed `NATIVE_CODEX_TURN_COMPLETION:CANCELLED`. The run's tool authority on the host (`CloudNativeToolRun`) goes on for the validity its last read answered, which the cloud caps at 30 s; its reads in the outage hung or failed, so it closed 30 s after the last read that answered, its signal stopped the Codex turn, and the worker failed the run. `CloudNativeToolRun.open` takes an optional `holdMs` (at least the window, a whole number; otherwise "Cloud tool authority hold is invalid"): after an answer of the full window the run goes on for `holdMs` from that read, while the reads that follow are asked again as before. A native run passes the native lease's length (120 s). An answer shorter than the window (a lease or the run's deadline within it) is kept exactly, and a refusal still closes the run at once. The provider host's authority checks (every 15 s, and at the run's end before its login is kept) ask a lost or late answer (`cloudControlRetryable`) again while the next try can begin within 120 s of the last check that answered; a refusal or any other failure still stops the run. The worker asks a lost or late native lease renewal again every 10 s until the native lease or the job's is 10 s from its end, each try ending by the native lease's end, and drops an answer that comes after its try was cut; the worker's deadline now also stops the run's execution when the native lease ends, so a held run never goes past it. The broker's transport now throws `CloudRunnerBrokerRequestError` (`status`, `transient`) with the same messages, and also when an answer is cut while its body arrives (a malformed body, or one whose request was stopped, is thrown as before). A lost or late heartbeat (429, 5xx, its deadline, or no answer) stays due for the next round, without holding up the renewals, while the lease and the runner's session reach 10 s past that round; a heartbeat still in flight is joined rather than sent again, and a refusal or an answer that the runner is not active still stops the run. An outage longer than about two minutes still ends the run. The runtime bundle's manifest moves at the release cut.
- A run whose machine came up, but whose runner never started its attempt, gets a fresh runner on its retry (WB-002 F2; migration `20260930535000`, SQL test 598). On production (28 Oct 2026) the attempt's runner activated and was offered the assignment, never accepted it, and node inventory released the machine; the claim then expired, which moves nothing. Every retry was the same attempt, so both providers asked for the same active runner (the shared execution host cannot replay one, the other node is refused it with 23505), and the run failed after eight dispatch failures. `qorpera_release_runner_job_claim` now treats an active runner like a pending one when the attempt's allocation is released and the runner never started: it accepted no assignment of the run and holds no leased job. It revokes the runner with its sessions and moves the job to its next attempt, whose claim names a new runner and allocation. A runner whose machine is still allocated, or that accepted, is never taken; the revoked runner cannot accept, start, heartbeat, activate or be provisioned again. Both caps of 8 are unchanged: an abandoned attempt counts one attempt and one dispatch failure, as for a pending runner. Providers and provisioning are unchanged. Deploy order: either.
- A run whose login download got no answer is tried again (WB-001 G2; no SQL; `cloud-native-provider-host.ts`, `cloud-runner-worker.ts`). The native host downloads the run's login, from the login vault or as its capsule from storage, before the harness starts, so before any model request or tool call. When that download timed out or lost its connection, the host said only `credential-download stage failed`, the runner got `NATIVE_CREDENTIAL_DOWNLOAD`, and the run failed for good: on 28 Oct 2026 (16:49 UTC, while the database stalled) a run failed with that code. The host now names a download that got no answer a timeout (`credential-download stage failed (timeout)`, code `NATIVE_CREDENTIAL_DOWNLOAD:TIMEOUT`): its request's deadline passed, or its connection was refused, reset or cut (a transport code such as `ECONNREFUSED`, `ECONNRESET` or `UND_ERR_SOCKET` among the error's causes). The runner tries such a run again from the start, as it does a timeout reading the run's context or composing its prompt (`cloudRunnerJobFailureBeforeStartIsRetryable`; the database still decides whether a try is left). An answer stays final: the vault's or the store's refusal (a revoked login, a binding it does not accept), a malformed answer and a redirect. So does a download cut short because the run was stopped, whatever stopped it. The vault answers its own failure, a database error included, with the same 403 as a refusal, so that case still fails for good; telling the two apart needs a change in the vault.
- A run's dispatch retries wait longer each time (WB-002 G3; migration `20260930537000`, SQL test 599).
