On production (28 Oct 2026) WB-002 spent its eight dispatch attempts in about 23 seconds: the job waited (15 s) only while the attempt's machine was still reserved, and a fresh retry after a lapsed lease was queued again at once, so a stall of a minute or two could fail a run. Now every orchestrator leaves the job alone for `qorpera_runner_dispatch_wait(n)` = min(15 s × 2^(n-1), 5 min): after its n-th counted dispatch failure (`qorpera_release_runner_job_claim`, any release that is not a typed admission refusal), and after a fresh retry of its n-th attempt (`qorpera_terminalize_runner_attempt_before_native_start`: its lease lapsed, its runner was revoked or reset for re-pairing, or the runner reported a retryable failure before the start). The waits are 15 s, 30 s, 1, 2, 4, then 5 min; the seven before the eighth failure add up to 17 min 45 s, so eight failures span about 20 minutes. A counted failure clears an earlier refusal's `dispatch_refused_by`, so the wait holds for every orchestrator. A job queued again (an abandoned attempt, a fresh retry) is queued from the end of its wait, so the claim turn's head start and its day in the queue count from there. The `infrastructure_retry` event names `deferred_until` while the job waits. Both caps of 8, the typed refusals (5 s or 15 s), the resume of a cut-off started run from its log, and authorization, leases and receipts are unchanged. The wait function is nobody's to call. Deploy order: either.
- A reply's References lists the thread's ids as `<id>` tokens again (migration `20260930521000`, SQL test 542; `supabase/functions/mail-intake/mail-intake.ts`). The mail provider sends a References header with several ids as a list, and the mail door stored the text of that list (`["<a@example.org>","<b@example.net>"]`). `send_mail` split the stored References at spaces, so the text was one id, and the provider put it in angle brackets: on 20 Oct 2026 (21:20 UTC) a reply went out with `References: <["<A>","<B>"]> <C>`. `qorpera_send_agent_mail` now takes the `<id>` tokens of the stored References, whatever is around them, so a plain header, the text of a list and anything else give a clean list, for mail stored before the migration too; a token with a control character is left out on its own, where it left out the whole References before. The email answered still comes last, and the first id and the newest ids still fill one header line (980 characters). The mail door now stores In-Reply-To and References that come as a list, or as the text of one, as `<id>` tokens joined by spaces: the first id and the newest that fit 2048 characters, a bare id in angle brackets. A plain header is stored as it came. What agents see of queued and sent email, its review, limits and recipients are unchanged. Deploy order: either; each fixes replies on its own.
- A quiet company machine no longer wakes itself to refresh firmware metadata (C8 MA-5b on v184; `deploy/machine/build-machine-image.sh`, so a new image at the v186 cut; no SQL). The Ubuntu image kept fwupd's `fwupd-refresh.timer`, which fires at random times 30 minutes to 2 hours apart. Its `fwupdmgr refresh` starts `fwupd.service` over D-Bus, and that work counts as busy, as any work does. So test machine c8-v184-b took busy looks at 18:41, 19:11 and 20:08 UTC on 20 Oct 2026, each about 11 s after a refresh started, with no command after 18:05, and never slept. The image now masks `fwupd-refresh.timer`, `fwupd-refresh.service` and `fwupd.service` beside apt's units (a masked unit refuses every start, D-Bus activation too). The build refuses an image where a fwupd timer, a fwupd unit that starts at boot, or a fwupd bus name could still start anything. The idle rule, the guest's accounting and its own units are unchanged.
- The run a member's Allow once or Always allow wakes is reviewed against the request the agent was carrying on (C6-8b; migration `20260930518000`, SQL tests 677 and 678; `cloud-action-review.ts`). On production (20 Oct 2026) a Co-Founder routine's run asked to send a mail and its card parked. The member's Allow once woke the agent in its thread, and that run sent the mail on the card's approval, but its next call, a routine change (`manage_routine`, a delegation), was reviewed against the card answer alone and denied as not requested. `qorpera_wake_origin` now gives such a room wake an origin too: the run that asked the card, or that run's own origin. It does so only when the wake's source is the requester's own approval answer (`qorpera.interaction-answer/v1`, surface `approval`, outcome `approved`, `allow_once` or `always_allow`) to an asked card of the same company and agent in that conversation, and the card's run is of the same company, environment, agent and requester. The outcome is read from the answer itself, since the card's answer is written after the wake; the trigger sets it once. The agent's chain that run starts (its workers, their reports, a machine event or check-in, an office start) carries the same origin rather than the card answer alone, while a plain post's room run is still its own chain's origin. `cloudActionReviewRequest` adds that run's request after the card answer, under "(This run carries on the agent's own earlier work: a member approved its card. The request that started it:)", with member text masked as before and within the request's 4096 bytes, and the reviewer's carry-on examples name it. The card itself is still not read. A Deny, another member's answer, an ask_member answer and a plain post read as before. The review still decides every call: the approval covers the card's exact action once, and every `manage_routine` operation but `list` stays a reviewed delegation. Deploy order: either. Today's runtime never reads a room run's origin, and this runtime finds none on room runs until the migration applies.
- A company machine with no work sleeps again (C8 MA-5b on v183; `cloud-machine-guest.ts`; no SQL). The guest counted the system's own plumbing as the machine's work: each run of the watchdog's 10 s timer has systemd (pid 1, `init.scope`) start and stop it and the journal and syslog log it, about 123 KB every 10 s outside the control's cgroup, and each look adds its own, so a machine running nothing (c8-smoke-1 on node 2) was busy every 20 to 40 s and its idle hour never ended. It also counted `/proc/stat`'s steal (the host's) as busy, and guest and guest_nice twice (they are already in user and nice). `init.scope`, `systemd-journald.service` and `rsyslog.service` (where the image has it; a unit the machine lacks is skipped) are now left out like the control, both their processor time (`cpu.stat` `usage_usec`) and the bytes their processes move, and the busy processor time is user + nice + system + irq + softirq. Commands, containers, dockerd and containerd and programs left behind still count; the idle rule's thresholds, the watchdog's cadence and the executor are unchanged. Rolls with a new machine image on node 2; no SQL.
- A cancelled test machine command is said `cancelled` (F3 step 6f-1, no SQL; `cloud-machine-guest.ts`; seen in 6f's live proof, run `20261017T223030-44948-6bf2dec4`). The cancel stops the command's transient unit, and when the command ends cleanly on the stop's SIGTERM, systemd (259, as on the nodes) forgets the unit at once (`LoadState=not-found`), so the guest said the command `unconfirmed`. A cancelled command whose unit is gone is now `cancelled`; one that outlives the stop's timeout and is killed stays loaded, failed, and is `cancelled` as before. A cancel that comes between the command's intent and its unit finds no unit to stop: the start now stops the unit as soon as it is made. Until the start is past `systemd-run` (its new `launched.json`), such a cancel is `unconfirmed`, never `cancelled`, so nothing is kept as cancelled while the unit may still come. The marker is read before systemd is asked, so a look whose "no such unit" came before the unit was made is never taken for the end of it. If the start's stop fails, the command is said `running`, and the next cancel stops it again.
- A run that carries on the agent's own earlier work is reviewed against the request that started it (finding 881f1f21, L12.2; migration `20260930497000`, SQL test 678). A run woken by its workers' report, a worker's own run, a machine event or check-in, or an office start had no member request, so the action review denied its follow-up to its own worker (TESTCO run 3e4ecb18). Each such wake now records the chain's first run (`origin_run_id`, set by a trigger from the platform's `parent_run_id`, same agent and requester only), and the run's context carries that run's request as `origin` (its member inputs, room posts or routine intent). `cloudActionReviewRequest` uses it only when the run names no request of its own, under a line that says the run carries on earlier work, with member text masked as before; the reviewer is told that steering or stopping that work is covered by that request. The model never gets `origin`: a worker still starts blank. It carries only that request, not the agent's message or card a member's short reply answered there, so a chain started by "Yes please" reads that reply alone. A coworker's message is never the request (contract 9.2): a coworker-woken run still reads "No member request", a chain it started has no origin (so its reviewed steps, such as dispatching or steering a worker, still read "No member request"), and the review's read of a coworker's `payload.message`, which never existed, is gone. No origin found reads as before. Deploy order: this runtime on every node first, then the migration in a late lane after the node roll, never in a roll's migration step (an older runtime refuses a context with the unknown key `origin`, so its chain runs would fail); before a reverse to such a runtime, restore `qorpera_describe_company_run_context` without the origin statement first.
- A routine can watch a folder written with a slash at the end (v181; no SQL). `manage_routine`'s `file_change` trigger split its path on `/` and refused an empty part with a plain error, so `inbox/l23/` was answered `INVALID_ARGUMENTS` with no reason, before any review (run a2d48302, 16 Oct 2026). It now drops slashes at the end, as the routine file's parser does for `watch`, and refuses any other bad path (empty, absolute, an empty, `.` or `..` part, over 1024 bytes) as a `CloudToolArgumentRefusal` with that parser's reason: "path is a folder or file path inside the company, like sales/leads". `match` is unchanged (a slash at the end does not make it `subtree`).
- A routine's trigger, quiet hours or report is refused with a reason the model can act on (v181; no SQL). `compileTrigger`'s other checks threw plain errors, so the model saw `INVALID_ARGUMENTS` with no reason. These are an unknown kind, a missing or unknown key, `minutes`, `weekdays`, `localTime`, `timeZone`, `report`, `match`, `hookId` and `only`. Each now throws a `CloudToolArgumentRefusal` with one plain line that names the argument and shows a good value, such as "timeZone is an IANA time zone, like Europe/Copenhagen". `quietHours` (`start`, `end`, `timeZone`, equal bounds) and `decide_routine`'s `report` use the same checks, so they are refused the same way. A refusal of a trigger's keys lists the keys its kind takes, with an example; it never repeats the model's own keys, because the boundary drops a reason that is not plain text. The `hookId` refusal points to `list_hooks` instead of giving an example id, which a model could call as it is. The tool's other plain errors (operation and routine keys, text, effort, `maxOccurrences`, a missing `quietHours` key, an unknown top-level key) are unchanged.
- A folder-v2 change that was not saved is observed as denied, never as succeeded (P5 pass 2 finding 1; no SQL; contract, Minimum operational observability). The file tools, `save_interface`, `table_write` and `apply_proposal` answer a refused change as content (`state` or `outcome` `"not_applied"`) instead of throwing, so `CloudNativeToolOperations` finished the call's observation as `succeeded` (or `replayed`) and the boundary's later finish was a no-op; a refusal answered before any receipt (a write over a link, a blocked interface save, a table refusal) took the boundary's success finish. `execute` takes a `refused` reader of the confirmed answer, and a `CloudNativeTool` may have one for answers before a receipt; `cloudFolderRefusal` names why from the answer and its `files` or `notSaved` rows: `stale_version` for a conflict or an existing, missing or changed path, `parent_missing`, `authority_unavailable` for a denied, unreviewed, claim or not-a-writer change, `invalid_arguments` for any other refusal. A replay reads the same answer, so it is denied too. A saved, partly saved or unchanged change still succeeds and a parked one is still proposed; the model's answer and the envelope are unchanged. A claim refused at the commit is `invalid_arguments`, as its answer's problems carry no code.
- A run's start no longer fails on a short wait in the database (v181; no SQL). `CloudCompanyPathControl.call` takes the caller's own budget as an optional fourth argument (`timeoutMs`), and `HttpCloudCompanyPathControl` waits that long instead of its own 3 s stop, which cut every call before a longer outer budget could apply. The run-start reads (`qorpera_describe_company_run_context`, `qorpera_read_native_wake`), the connector catalog (`qorpera_list_native_connector_tools`, which had the 5 s default and now has the same 10 s) and the read-set check before each model request (`qorpera_assert_company_path_reads`, through `CloudCompanyPathGateway.assertReadableContext(signal, timeoutMs)` and the `assertContext` adapter) get their paired 10 s down to the transport; the provider usage signal gets its 10 s. Two TESTCO runs (62cd3036, 5ca8ded9, 16 Oct) failed at 3 s while their reads waited up to 4 s in line for the run's job row. A call with no budget of its own still stops at 3 s, and the one-call fence still waits as long as the run's retry gives it.
- The action reviewer reads a member's earlier messages as context, never as the request (P5 pass 2 finding 4a; no SQL). A production review judged a hire against an earlier post in the conversation as if that post were the run's request (the run's wake named one input). The earlier messages' header now says they are context only and request nothing on their own, and the reviewer's instructions say so: when the request clearly refers to one (a short "go ahead"), what it refers to is requested; one about something else never makes the request's action unrequested. The room for earlier messages is measured from the header, so the request stays within 4096 bytes.
- A held call keeps holding through a lost or late answer (production pass 2, finding 2; no SQL). `CloudActionReviewGate` ended a hold at its first failed `qorpera_await_action_review` poll: on 16 Oct 2026 (run 6c920397) one poll in about 150 waited past the lock timeout behind a same-run step, the 5 s park on the way out failed the same way, and the held `manage_routine` call ended `REVIEW_UNAVAILABLE` after 165 s with its card not parked. A wait whose answer was lost or late (`cloudControlRetryable`, which covers PostgREST's 57014 and 55P03) is now asked again, at the poll's pace before the hold runs out and with backoff after it, while the run lives. Since a wait can use an approval, and `qorpera_begin_action_review` tells a woken agent's repeat that its approval was used only within 30 s of the wake, a failed wait is asked again only for 5 s after the answer could first have woken the agent (checked before its pause and again as it goes out, so a stalled pause cannot send it late): a heartbeat (a wait that holds, 10 s) puts that off, while a card found waiting or a park can wake it at once. A failure that lasts longer, a refusal, a malformed answer or a stopped run still ends the hold. A hold that ends early asks its leaving park, which uses nothing, again through lost or late answers for up to 30 s (it tried once, for 5 s), so its card is left parked. Every wait was already idempotent; the card, its answers and the wake are unchanged. Taking the wait off the run's mutex is the database's half, separate.
- A held call asks a failed wait again until its hold runs out, then until its card parks (fix B's follow-up; no SQL here; needs `20260930489000`, #1232, live first). The hold above asked a failed `qorpera_await_action_review` again only for 5 s after its answer could first have woken the agent, because `qorpera_begin_action_review` told the woken agent's repeat that a held call used the approval only within 30 s of the wake. `20260930489000` has begin read the card that woke the run first: if another call used its approval, the woken run is told `used`, however long after the wake that was; if the woken run uses it first, the held call's next wait finds it used and the call does not run. So the 5 s bound, its 10 s heartbeat tracking and its second check as a retry goes out are gone: a lost or late answer is asked again, at the poll's pace before the hold runs out (5 minutes) and with backoff after it, while the run lives. A refusal, a malformed answer or a stopped run still ends the hold, and its leaving park is still asked again for up to 30 s.
- A machine's folder helper takes the folder program's real mount (F3 step 6c follow-up; no SQL). The program mounts directly as root, so the kernel lists it as `fuse qorpera-company`, as the office helper already knew (#1007's mountinfo, Q3's run). `folder-mount` accepted only `fuse.qorpera`, so a good mount could poll out as `unanswered`. It now takes `fuse` or `fuse.qorpera`, still only with the source `qorpera-company`; the guest tests use the recorded `fuse` line, and a new test covers both types and a wrong source. Found by qorpera-49's #1167 review.
- The agent exit's dns unit can be reloaded (agent exit E8b-2; no SQL, no runtime code). Its `ExecReload` sent SIGHUP from inside the unit's capability bounding set, which has no CAP_KILL, to dnsmasq running as nobody, and failed with "Operation not permitted": after an unplug the plug's reload would have failed and the removed lease stayed in dnsmasq. The reload now runs outside the sandbox (`ExecReload=+/bin/kill -HUP $MAINPID`), as the companies' resolvers' does; `test/prove-names.sh` proves both under systemd.
- Dormant run-log protection drops path needles containing C0 controls, DEL or unpaired UTF-16 surrogates before its JSONB RPC (review #1006, round 2). Those strings cannot be company paths; NUL and unpaired surrogates made PostgreSQL reject the request before classification, blocking every later step. Valid Unicode paths still participate unchanged, and even an empty filtered set still checks the current machine and log flags. Adapter and boundary regressions cover refused arguments and JSON text results followed by a successful write and log close. No production activation or SQL change.
- Dormant run-log protection handles refused calls without wedging later effects (W1c-3, review #1006; no SQL change). Path needles strip `/company/` before empty strings and strings over the folder's 4096 UTF-8-byte path limit are ignored; non-string inputs still fail closed. Scalar `paths` and string leaves in arrays under `path`, `from`, `to` and `paths` participate like SQL's lax JSON path rules. The protection port returns the log-wide narrow flag separately from machine/path causes: a later pending narrow line no longer redacts an earlier public fill. The writer preserves the trusted narrow start and refuses an unknown start; inherited whole-log narrow status still begins at step zero. Final public or erased steps release their complete argument copy, while this process's narrow work remains available in full. Tests cover the real protection adapter through logging and the tool boundary, including a 5000-character invalid path followed by a successful effect. Logging remains unconstructed in production.
- A quote's words may hold neither « nor » (contract: the 2026-10-02 amendment "quoted outside content in a member's message", item 1, as amended on 5 October 2026; migration `20260930087000`, SQL test 933; #860's review): `qorpera_check_member_quotes` refuses such a quote with 22023, so every path that takes quotes (a conversation input, a turn request, a room message on either path, a room draft) does too. A quote is always «words»: quotes can no longer nest or cross, an inner mark can't hide a missing outer one, and a draft can't hold a quote together with a quote of words inside it, which made its later saves fail. The migration deletes room drafts stored with such a quote.
- Auto-review no longer denies a hire the member agreed to in a short reply. Found live on 4 October: run 22b287a3 denied `hire_agent` after "Agreed, hire both.". When members started the run, the reviewer's request now carries what the member was answering, at most 1,200 bytes and marked as the agent's words. For a card answer, that is the card itself (an `interaction_request`, found by its interaction id), or nothing. For a message, it is the agent's latest message or card before it. An approval card is the platform's and is never used. It is context for what the reply refers to, and requests or approves nothing itself. The instructions say that delegating to the company's own agents (hiring, routines, workers, group chats, messages) is denied only when the member said no or it goes against what they said. When the reviewer cannot tell whether the member agreed, it asks with a card.
- An account is no longer downgraded to the metered allocation while a folder-v2 company it owns stores more than that allowance (migration `20260930068000`, SQL test 920; found by A1's legacy sweep): `qorpera_set_account_entitlement` read only `qorpera_company_files.storage_used_bytes`, which folder-v2 never fills. It now reads the folder quota's own count, `qorpera_folder_storage_usage`, against the same allowance (the smallest limit of the company's files), and no longer reads the snapshot-v1 count. Test 240 drops its snapshot-v1 downgrade case.

- A model request's fence waits for a slow database (control calls, part 2): no stopwatch decides a model request's checks any more. The host's result fence has no deadline (4 s before): the checks end only with the run, its authority or lease gone, a refusal or a stop. The one-call fence is asked again on a lost or late answer (10 s an attempt) for as long as the run's authority holds, while the run's refresh keeps renewing it, so a fence slower than the 30 s authority read at its start no longer stops the run. An attempt cut at its deadline gives the next one twice as long, within the authority, so a fence slower than one attempt, queued behind the transaction its cut attempt left running, still lands. The fence's own transports no longer cut an attempt before the run does (the path gateway's 5 s and the path control's 10 s did, so each attempt was in fact 5 s); the native host's authority read waits up to 30 s, so it grows the same way, and the read-set check keeps its 3 s per attempt. The database still bounds each attempt: the fence runs as one statement under its entry point's statement budget (25 s since the change below; `qorpera_native_host_call`'s 8 s before), and an attempt it cancels is asked again like a lost one, at the same length. So the doubling helps a delay outside the statement (a connection pool wait) and a statement within that budget, and a fence that needs more than the budget in the database does not land: the request ends at Codex's backstop. Answers apply in the order their reads started, so a fence answer older than a refresh never shortens the authority. The pinned Codex's 5 s native deadline becomes a 10-minute backstop against a host that is alive but hung (`0001-cloud-result-fence.patch`, `source.json`'s `patchSha256` c4ffb5a9…). Until the release that rebuilds the Codex binary, the pinned 0.155.1 build keeps its 5 s.
- A model request's fence gets 25 s in the database (migration `20260930109500`, SQL test 998; the coordinator asked after #839's review r2): `qorpera_fence_native_context` is listed with the `long` budget and sets 25 s for itself, as the lease validator does, so the native host sends it through `qorpera_native_host_call_long` (`NATIVE_HOST_CALLS`). Each attempt has 25 s for its statement (8 s before) and still 8 s for a lock wait. A fence that needs more than 25 s in the database is still asked again at the same length and does not land; the request then ends at Codex's backstop. Each entry point refuses a call listed with the other budget, so the migration and the runtime ship in one cut.
- Control calls: refusals are refusals, slowness is not failure (Q1's onboarding test, 4 Oct 2026, where two first runs stopped while every database call took 1-3 s). A run's authority read (`qorpera_describe_native_tool_authority`: at open, before each tool call and in the 10 s refresh) and its read-set check are asked again when the answer is lost or late (a timeout, a connection or gateway error, an unreadable 5xx, PostgREST's connection errors, or SQLSTATE 40001, 40P01, 55P03, 57014, 53300, 08xxx), pausing 250 ms doubling to 2 s, until the run's authority would lapse (its validity, which the cloud bounds by the run's lease; 30 s at open). A model request's one-call fence is asked again within its 3.5 s budget (it replays its view by request id). A refusal (42501, 55000, 0A000, P0001, a QRC code, 22xxx), a changed identity or a lapsed authority still closes the run at once. Every control client keeps a refusal the database raised as a refusal (`CloudControlRpcRefused`, with its SQLSTATE and words), never a lost connection; the tools whose first effect is that call (manage_worker, manage_todo, report_work, the routine, sidebar and review-rule tools, machine stop and delete, coworker messages) answer `REFUSED: NOT_AVAILABLE: <the database's words>`, so the agent does not try again. A fence slower than about 4.5 s still ends the turn until the pinned Codex's 5 s native deadline is raised.
- An agent's model reaches its runs on folder-v2 (migration `20260930081000`, SQL test 930). The roster index of `agent.md` never set the agent's model, so an API-key account could not run a folder-v2 agent and an owner could not make an API-key company account its standing account (22023 "staff execution authority is unavailable"). The index now sets the model provider `openai` and the model id from the file's `model`, or `gpt-6-astra` when it names none; agents indexed before take the same from their current file.
- The native model relay (`cloud-native-egress.ts`) no longer caps a run: it counted every byte and connection over the run's life and stopped the run after 64 MiB or 128 connections, which a long run with many model requests, a big context or screenshots reached. Its bounds are now per connection only: 8 at once, 5 s to connect, 30 s idle.
- The founding table's `firstRun` reads `queued`, not `waiting_for_login`, in the seconds before the follow-up queue has converged a new Co-Founder (migration `20260930067000`; since P15 the converge queues its turn request in the transaction that makes the company ready). Once the company is ready, a missing request still means no usable login.
- Fixed an agent's screenshot on a company on the new company folder leaving its operation open (core C1c-3l part 3, P06c; migration `20260930030200`, SQL test 891; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-COMPANY-FOLDER-C1C.md` §6.22): the screenshot is now refused at authorize, before its receipt begins, so the run's later operations go on, and the model is told why (`REFUSED: Screenshots are not available on this computer yet…`). The Company Computer's effect control turns an authorize's `{state: "refused", message}` (plain ASCII, 1 to 512 characters) into a `CloudToolAuthorizeRefusal`, and the operation executor relays it only from the authorize before the receipt; every other failure is still an unknown outcome. Real screenshots there follow in their own design.
- A folder-v2 routine file saved past its `ends`, or with its runs used up, no longer revives its expired routine (migration `20260930056000`, SQL test 867; from #775's review). The file of an ended routine still says `status: active`, so before this any save of it (a member changing only its quiet hours, say) set it active again, and the scheduler then offered slots that were each refused until the next tick expired it. Such a save also counted as a restart, so a member whose login does not pay could not save the file at all. Now a save ends a routine whose `ends` has passed at once, revives an ended one only when its end is later than now (or removed) and runs remain, and is no restart while it leaves the routine ended.
- A folder-v2 routine past its end date ends again (migration `20260930027000`, SQL test 889; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-COMPANY-FOLDER-C1C.md` §6.9): the legacy expiry sweep ran only in the Company File drain, which P28 took out of the reconcile RPC, so such a routine stayed active and the scheduler's every slot was refused. The folder-v2 dynamic routine tick now lists any folder-v2 routine past its `expires_at` (active or paused, paused company included) and its restore sets it `expired` under the fence, the company row and the routine's own lock, answering `ended`. A company being deleted is left to its purge, and a routine changed meanwhile answers `changed`. The routine file keeps its status until core C2's queue writes platform statuses into files, so a later save of that file revives the routine until the next pass ends it again. A snapshot-v1 routine no longer expires (the legacy sweep has no caller).

- Fixed sign-in machines taking the company row `FOR UPDATE` on a folder-v2 company, after the sign-in (folder core P13; migration `20260930035000`, SQL test 900, 824 updated; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-COMPANY-FOLDER-C1C.md` §6.14, C1B §8):
  - **Reservation:** reserving a sign-in's or an account refresh's login machine takes the folder fence and the company row `KEY SHARE`, the requester's member row and the identity's machine lock (`qorpera.identity-machine:<id>`, a transaction advisory lock), then the sign-in. Two machines of one identity stay serialized, and the second is refused `COMPUTE_IDENTITY_OCCUPIED`. A caller with an unknown sign-in or a wrong capability is refused before any lock (on either core).
  - **Release and node reservation:** releasing such a machine, and its node reservation, take the fence and the row `KEY SHARE` too, and a paused company still releases.
  - **Deadlocks fixed:** a member removal and the account metadata take a member's model logins before its compute identities (C1B §8). Before, a removal and a sign-in's completion could deadlock. The account metadata's new order holds on both cores.
  - **Window, closed by P13's second part** (`20260930049000`, below): a login's or refresh's identity recovery request took the sign-in and then the gc fence, and could deadlock with this reservation on one sign-in from two gateway processes.
  - **Unchanged:** snapshot-v1 companies keep the gc fence.
- Fixed identity recovery and retirement taking the company row `FOR UPDATE` on a folder-v2 company (folder core P13's second part; migration `20260930049000`, SQL test 901, 838 updated; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-COMPANY-FOLDER-C1C.md` §6.17, C1B §8):
  - **Recovery:** activation, cancellation, consent, the owner's, a login's and a refresh's recovery request, and `qorpera_recovery_locked` (the first step of a key release, its commit and the recovery's completion) take the folder fence and the company row `KEY SHARE`, then every member row of the company `FOR SHARE`, then the identity as before. An effect that holds only the company (`KEY SHARE`) no longer waits for a recovery, nor a recovery for it. Member writes (role changes, removals, rule saves, display names) wait for a recovery call while it holds the member rows.
  - **Decisions:** a member's recovery decision (`qorpera_decide_member_identity_recovery`) takes those locks and the identity `FOR UPDATE` before its review, so two decisions on one identity, or a decision and the removal of the identity's owner, queue instead of deadlocking.
  - **No sign-in held:** a login's or refresh's recovery request reads its sign-in with no lock, and a key backup's preparation takes it only `FOR KEY SHARE` (its backup and receipt reference it). Both check it again under the identity's lock: pending, not expired, and re-pinned to no other recovery epoch meanwhile. Holding the sign-in `FOR SHARE` while waiting for the identity deadlocks with a new sign-in of the same member, which cancels the earlier one; taking it after the identity would deadlock with a completion. This closes the window above. A sign-in pinned to an older epoch than the identity's still recovers, as before.
  - **Fence only:** a recovery target's registration, the recovery member role and an identity's retirement confirmation take the fence and the row `KEY SHARE`. A registration and a confirmation then take the identity's machine lock, so two of them on one identity still serialize and the second replays the first's target or receipt instead of failing with 23505.
  - **Unchanged:** snapshot-v1 companies keep the gc fence.
- Fixed the presence and room drafts Realtime policies letting a member read other topics' broadcasts (migration `20260930017000`, SQL test 837; found by #742's review). Realtime checks a private join with `realtime.topic()` set to the joined topic, and a table's policies count together, so `qorpera_presence_member_receive` and `qorpera_room_drafts_member_receive`, which asked only whether the member may join that topic, let a member joined to their company's presence topic (or a room's drafts topic) read every other topic's broadcasts: folder hints, other rooms' drafts, another company's presence. `qorpera_presence_member_publish` likewise let a member write onto another topic, such as a room's drafts topic, where only the database publishes. All three now also require `realtime.messages.topic = (select realtime.topic())`, like the orchestrator wake and folder hint policies; the membership checks are unchanged. Realtime's own join check only reads and writes rows of the joined topic, so no client is known to have reached this.
- Company account fill hardening (A1-3b review round 3, before agents get `vault_fill` in A1-4): a typed secret is also recognised through up to three layers of percent-decoding in either letter case (runs of escapes read as UTF-8, or byte by byte), so a page address or title that shows one (a redirect that wraps the address) has its query and fragment, or the whole title, hidden (the whole address when the value is in its host), a link address the snapshot quotes is read whole, and a snapshot that still carries one in another spelling is refused; the screen check refuses a picture when the page holds more than 4 MB of text instead of reading part of it (long or many boxes and shadow roots are read whole and pictured); a value a page script moved into something that cannot be emptied (an editable area, a closed shadow root) closes that tab (`ACCOUNT_FILL_LOST`), opening a blank one first when it was the window's only tab, and the agent's next action is told its tab closed instead of moving to another; a snapshot hides values only in what can be returned (about 20 KB; while something is hidden the cut falls between words, so no part of a value is left over; a page with nothing to hide is cut as before, and one with too little before a word's end, such as a minified page, has its secrets hidden across the whole snapshot before its first 16 KB is returned); percent escapes are decoded leniently in linear time, never failing on what a page holds; a password with spaces is also recognised as a script writes it in a query (a space as +).
- Fixed the vault's database functions letting a missing proof through (migration `20260928429988`, SQL test 806; found by #653's review). `qorpera_describe_cloud_secret_capture`, `qorpera_complete_cloud_secret_capture` and `qorpera_authorize_cloud_secret_operation` compared the broker capability, the capture capability, the envelope's digest and length, the operation facts the broker pins and the receipt's input and downstream digests with `<>`, `!~` or `not between`, which are unknown, not true, when the caller passes NULL, so the refusal was skipped. Every broker and the shared vault share one database login, and the capability hash is the only proof a call is for its company: with NULL, a holder of that login who knew a company's authority id and a capture ticket's id could describe or seal that ticket, or, with a run binding, a grant and an executing receipt, read its sealed envelope and record an authorized effect. Each comparison is now null-safe and refuses with the error a wrong value gets; nothing else changes. Every other function the login may call was already null-safe.
- Fixed "deadlock detected" when a member disconnects, grants, withdraws a grant of, shares or starts rotating a connection (Gmail, Drive, GitHub) while an agent's tool call uses it, and when the vault seals that rotation (migration `20260928419980`, SQL test 800; found by #653's review). The tool call's lease locks the company row and then share-locks its grant and secret; `qorpera_revoke_cloud_secret`, `qorpera_grant_cloud_secret_operation`, `qorpera_revoke_cloud_secret_grant`, `qorpera_grant_cloud_connector`, `qorpera_begin_cloud_secret_capture` (a rotation) and `qorpera_complete_cloud_secret_capture` locked the secret, grant or ticket first and met the company row only at their first insert's key, so one side was killed. Each now takes the company row's key share before any vault row (rank 0 of the one lock order). The Disconnect, the grant and the withdrawal read the caller's membership plainly first and `FOR SHARE` after the key share, which also ends their deadlock with a role change (a rotation started by a member who loses the role while it waits is refused instead of killed). The share reads the role again after the key share, so an admin demoted while it waited no longer shares. A caller refused for its authority, or for a secret, grant or ticket nobody has, is still refused before any lock, with the same error; `qorpera_revoke_cloud_secret_grant` now refuses before locking the grant too.
- Fixed the vault's lock order on a company that uses the new company folder (folder-v2; C1c-3d-2, migration `20260930003500`, SQL test 850, `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-COMPANY-FOLDER-C1C.md` §6.7; the follow-up to `20260928419980`). There an agent's tool call takes the company row only as a key share, so vault changes no longer wait their turn behind it: a Disconnect, a share and the seal of a company connection (first capture or rotation) could deadlock with a tool call or with each other, a single grant with an agent being archived, and a seal or a rotation's start with the removal or demotion of the member who started it. Each now takes the folder core's order: the member row, then the agent's staff row, then capture tickets, grants and secrets, each by id. A grant created while the change waited is taken without waiting and, when busy, refused with 55P03 (contended, retry). Companies on the old snapshot keep their order; no company uses the new folder yet.
- Fixed the Company Computer's browser snapshot showing what is typed in a password box (company accounts A1-0; office guest code, ships with the next computer image). The snapshot is Playwright's aria snapshot, which copies every input's value, so a password a member typed during a takeover reached the agent's next snapshot until the form was sent. While a private box (password, one-time code, card, or a text box named for one, an empty or unknown type included; never a checkbox, radio, button, hidden, file, range or color input: `COMPANY_COMPUTER_PRIVATE_FIELD_SELECTOR` in `cloud-company-computer-private-fields.ts`) holds a value, every typed value in the snapshot reads `[hidden]` (text boxes, search boxes, spin buttons and combo boxes, also under a placeholder and when Playwright quotes the whole key), and values of four or more characters are hidden wherever else the page shows them (as text, inside a name or a quoted key, escaped, or with spaces collapsed). The values are read before and after the snapshot; a snapshot that still shows one in another form is refused (`PRIVATE_FIELD_VISIBLE`), and an unreadable answer refuses it too (`PRIVATE_FIELD_UNREADABLE`). Browser screenshots mask the same boxes in every frame (before: only `type=password` and `autocomplete=one-time-code` in the top frame, so a password shown as text through a page's eye button, or a card number in an iframe, was visible). A whole-screen (desktop) screenshot, which cannot mask, is refused with `PRIVATE_FIELD_ON_SCREEN` while a shown tab of the browser window on that screen (whether or not the run drives it) holds a private value in any frame, checked before and after the capture; a tab that cannot answer within 5 s (crashed, hung, or still loading a page or frame: Chromium answers nothing until the navigation commits) refuses it with `PRIVATE_FIELD_TAB_UNREADABLE`, so a member closes that tab or the agent uses a browser screenshot.
- Fixed the check refusing an account refresh row that took its times from the defaults (migration `20260928409940`, SQL test 783): `qorpera_native_account_refresh_requests` defaulted `created_at` and `expires_at` to two separate `clock_timestamp()` calls, so `expires_at` could land a few microseconds past the five minutes its check allows (23514). Now `expires_at` has no default; a row inserted without one (or with a null one) expires five minutes after its own `created_at`. Only tests wrote such rows: `qorpera_begin_native_account_refresh` passes one clock reading for both, so no member's refresh was refused.
- Fixed coding refused for a member who has a login that may work while away (migration `20260928379920`, SQL test 775): `qorpera_coding_owner_sponsor` took the member's newest login and refused the session when that one login's newest version did not allow work while away, without looking at the member's other logins. Now a login that may work while away comes first, then a login other than a ChatGPT plan, then the newest. Live for Codex and Claude Code logins: a member whose newest login for that tool does not allow work while away (never allowed, or turned off since), but who has another login that does, gets coding on that login instead of a refusal. For Sign in with ChatGPT (switched off): a run a member's plan pays for is no longer refused because the member's Codex login may not work while away. A plan still pays only for work started on it, and a member with no login that may work while away is refused as before.
- Fixed a ChatGPT plan's usage limit reached mid-stream reading as a generic failure (Sign in with ChatGPT switch-on gate 3; built switched off with plan logins): the pinned Codex patch (`0001-cloud-result-fence.patch`) maps OpenAI's `subscription_sharing_usage_limit_exceeded`, mid-stream (`response.failed`, which Codex 0.155.1 retried five times and then reported as `other`) and at HTTP 429 (reported as `responseTooManyFailedAttempts`, which the host already read as the plan's limit), to Codex's quota error, reported as `usageLimitExceeded` and not retried. The host stops the run at the usage limit for it. Only a response carrying this exact code changes path, so Codex logins are unchanged. Needs a new native build and its own release (a Codex binary swap rides alone); `QORPERA_CODEX_FENCE_BINARY` boundary cases cover it.
- A native object PUT or GET that hits a lock conflict is sent again instead of ending the tool call unconfirmed. When the object gateway answers 409 (or 503) with `code: "lock_conflict"` (a serialization failure, deadlock or lock timeout on the company's rows, rolled back, nothing committed), `HttpNativeCompanyObjectTransport` retries at most twice, after 100–300 ms and then 300–700 ms, and only when the pause and an attempt as long as the last one (a lock timeout answers after the whole 2 s lock wait; never counted below 500 ms) end inside the tool call's 5 s budget, or the transport's own deadline if shorter. A retry that still runs longer is cut when the budget ends and reported as a timeout, so the retries add nothing to either deadline. Both requests are safe to repeat: a GET only reads and runs both authority checks again, and a PUT is addressed by its content, so the second attempt finds the same bytes ("exists") and its confirmation writes the same catalog row. A retried PUT also rewrites the gateway's journal entry that the failed confirmation left unconfirmed, so no blocked entry or degraded replication health outlasts the next reconcile pass. Denials, stops (423), other 409s (a GC fence, a deletion in progress), other 503s and stalled or lost answers are never repeated, and a denial's body is still never read.
- Fixed a coding turn's own failure code never reaching the runner: the host wraps every harness failure with the original as its cause, and `nativeProviderFailureCode` only read the outer message for a `CODING_` code, so a coding turn that ended without `coding_finish` or `coding_ask` reported `NATIVE_PROMPT_COMPOSITION:UNKNOWN` instead of `CODING_TURN_ENDED_WITHOUT_SUBMISSION` (since v109). The code is now looked for on the error and its causes, with the same closed check; other causes still give the stage and category.
- Fixed Codex stdout buffering taking quadratic time: the host kept an unfinished line as one string and rescanned all of it on every pipe read. `CodexLineReader` scans each read once and keeps the unfinished pieces with their byte count, so one 10.7 MiB capture line in 64 KiB reads costs about 11 ms of event-loop time instead of about 285 ms (650 ms in the audit's run). Line limits are unchanged.
- Fixed the recreated-root check of a governed reset (`assertCompanyComputerResetRootClean`, the reset evidence readers and the code package tool) missing other boots' credentials: it now also refuses another boot's `/etc/qorpera-office/boots/<boot>/credentials`, even empty; only the current boot's is accepted. A root that was reset before keeps its credentials only there, so a surviving one is now refused. `assertCompanyComputerResetRootClean` takes the current boot ID.
- Office controls own the output LXD records on the host: the eleven `/opt/qorpera-office` control helpers and the two temporary software tools start through `runCompanyComputerControlEntry`, which writes nothing on success and exactly the control's fixed failure line once on any refusal, uncaught exception or unhandled rejection, drops console and stream writes and removes Node's warning printer. Before, only awaited failures became the fixed line; an error outside the awaited work, or any console write, would have reached the host logs as Node printed it. The Recorded exec output section of the guest README states the contract; new unit and Linux root tests run the packaged controls with injected faults. Guest code: ships with the next runtime cut.
- Fixed a run whose machine failed to start being retried on the same attempt until it failed (migration `20260928249240`): a claim release that counts an infrastructure failure revokes a reserved, never activated runner whose machine was released, moves the job to its next attempt (failing it at the attempt cap) and records the failed node, which the node chooser and the claim turn's placement then rank last for that run.
- Fixed a vault login guest refusing to start on a node ("A vault login guest must not mount an identity volume"): the bootstrap unit's own `ReadWritePaths=` makes systemd mount the plain identity folder onto itself, which the check took for a volume. The check now refuses only a mount of another device than the root filesystem.
- Fixed runs whose login is in the vault failing every dispatch when the first provider to acquire them was then refused by the node chooser: that provider's pending runner stayed bound to it, and the preferred node's provider was refused the attempt's runner (23505). Provisioning now gives a pending runner no machine was ever reserved for to the provider acquiring the attempt now, with that provider's activation, and records a registration receipt; a runner with a reservation, active or revoked stays as it is. Migration `20260928235000`.
- A native run saves its credential capsule back only when it changed the login, and only while the store still holds the login the run started from (compared by credential content, read again once on failure); otherwise it keeps the newer stored login, logs that this run's copy was not saved, and does not keep that root warm. Every run used to reseal and upload at the end, so with several runs on one login an older copy could undo a renewal and force a new sign-in. A warm root starts from the stored login's digest, so a renewal made in the kept root between runs is saved by the next run. The check and the upload are two requests, not one atomic write.

- The Co-Founder is told the company formation keys (organization/formation.json: schema, the seven stage keys in order, the one-step and revision+1 rules, statuses), still never to be named to the founder. `company_write` normally refuses a formation write the organization projection would refuse as `COMPANY_FORMATION_REFUSED`, naming the current stage and the one allowed next write, before anything is staged or proposed. That projection only runs when a member approves the proposal, and its refusal never reached the agent.

- The company computer keeps running around agents and members. An agent action whose own page or command work failed and ended (a slow page, a target that never became clickable, a command stopped at its own limit, a password field) no longer closes the screen's browser and is drained like a success, so releasing the screen no longer drains the whole desktop. One authority check that gets no answer (network, timeout, 5xx) no longer ends the desktop, its apps or the agent's session: only a clear refusal, or 30 seconds without an answer, does, recorded as `AUTHORITY_LOST`. The browser relay follows the same rule and, when SQL answers `idle` (nobody entitled present), keeps the window open with its network paused instead of closing it; while idle it asks every five seconds, and a new connection asks at once and waits for that answer. An authority check between an action's steps that gets no answer fails the action as its own instead of closing the browser. A run whose desktop was ended mid-run opens a fresh guest session under the same lease. Expired screen leases that were never released are reaped by the supervisor status.

- The company browser's /tmp is now 1 GiB instead of the 64 MiB other office namespaces keep. With `--disable-dev-shm-usage` all of Chromium's shared memory lives there, so renderers failed allocations and crashed on ordinary sites ("Aw, Snap!", reported as SIGSEGV because the crash handler's `alarm()` tripped seccomp).

- Agents create a Company File folder only as the home for a category of files, named for the category, never on their own just to hold a single file. A folder a member asks for or an assignment names (such as a worker's output folder) is always fine. The run instructions no longer forbid folders nobody requested, and the `COMPANY_FILE_PARENT_MISSING` result and the `company_write`/`company_mkdir` descriptions now tell the model to put a single file in an existing folder where it fits unless a member, its assignment or a category of files calls for the missing folder.
- `company_write` and `company_mkdir` into a folder that does not exist are a definite `COMPANY_FILE_PARENT_MISSING` refusal (denied, `errorCategory` `parent_missing`) that tells the model when to create the folder with `company_mkdir` and repeat the call (see the folder entry above), and never to replace a file to make that folder. SQL refuses it at the check (PT424), before presence and before any bytes are uploaded, and again at prepare and commit. Previously the runner uploaded the bytes, failed while building the candidate tree and reported an unconfirmed, unknown outcome.

- Preserved office startup, supervision, desktop and cleanup failure causes in bounded structured diagnostics. Logs include only allowlisted stages, error codes and categories, never raw messages, credentials, paths or stacks.

- Loaded Chrome for Testing's managed browser policies from its actual Linux path. The browser uses a measured dedicated Bubblewrap copy and enforcing AppArmor profile so Chromium's nested sandbox can start on Ubuntu without changing the shell/desktop profile or granting host capabilities. Browser startup refuses a missing or different enforcing profile when AppArmor is enabled.

- Required exact pending-launch drain confirmation before reporting a screen closed when desktop startup timed out without returning a handle. Registry failure still permits physical cleanup, while unconfirmed private or shared cleanup cannot produce a successful drain.
- Kept screen-lease renewals on their original schedule during frequent authority checks and preserved renewals that became due while a check was in flight. Confirmed renewal alone advances the schedule; expiry, changed identity and revoked access still abort the handle.

- Hashed office effect, startup, capture and export idempotency keys at the SQL boundary to match the fingerprint stored by the native receipt control plane. Tool transport continues to carry the original stable key.

- Kept accepted office continuations waiting during maintenance while the stopped predecessor retains its historical active record. Successor activation delivers once; later restarts preserve delivered activation provenance and recheck current authority.
- Passed an empty environment directly to the protected control child, removing the redundant external environment launcher whose distribution symlink prevented packaged root commands from running. Executable custody, inherited kernel lock and child-process-group cancellation checks remain enforced.
- Admitted private service-owned NAS/adoption ancestors through a descriptor-relative, no-symlink directory walk. State paths still reject foreign owners and writable/shared parents; executable and attestation artifacts retain root-only ownership. The guest unit now lets protected configuration and runtime preflight select the exact adopted roots instead of requiring obsolete fixed directory names.

- Made the Linux browser sandbox probe verify Chromium process ancestry before matching namespace-local renderer PIDs, preventing collisions with independent desktop processes.

- Prevented command and desktop startup cancellation from orphaning Bubblewrap's namespace during its initial fork. A trusted permission gate under Tini delays monitor termination until namespace parent-death cleanup is armed; unknown startup/drain outcomes remain quarantined.
- Prevented browser shutdown from killing Chromium before its persistent cookies were flushed. Network access ends before bounded graceful shutdown; namespace exit remains required before releasing the profile.
- Accepted real browser viewport screenshots within the existing size limits and included their surface/dimensions in receipts. Desktop screenshots still require the fixed display size.
- Prevented viewer disconnect from ending a recovered private browser after readiness, rejected stale private grants before launch, and preserved unexpected private cleanup failures. Screen-open success now waits for physical readiness after receipt admission.
- Kept the service test constructor mock constructible after automatic formatting and ran final regression tests after repository checks.
- Approval cards (auto-review) carry the facts a member decides on, now that the card no longer shows the tool call: a Google Drive file's text (an excerpt) and its name, or its id when nothing names it; a GitHub issue's owner/repository (and `#number` for a comment), title, text and labels; who a group chat invites, by name, the member first (coworkers the context does not list are counted, never shown by id), and its purpose; when a routine runs, in plain words on its own clock (a time without one reads on the wake's clock: a routine run's own zone, else UTC), and a routine named by its key when paused, resumed, removed or ended; a new agent's role, charter and first assignment; and a worker's or coding job's title, goal or follow-up text (naming the worker) and repository (the card read `task`/`instructions`/`brief`, which the worker tool never sends). Quoted text (bodies, typed text, commands, intents, tasks) keeps its lines; any fact cut to its bound is marked `[...]`; C1 controls and bidi overrides read as spaces. Cutting text to its byte bound no longer re-splits the whole string per step, which blocked the run for seconds on a long email body.
- Pointed members to the rules at the bottom of Settings → General (web #360 removed the Rules tab): the `add_review_rule` tool description and the kernel refusal for a full rule list (migration `20260928013400`) no longer say "Settings, Rules".
- Fixed `read_event` skipping text the agent never saw. The database returns a piece of up to 16,000 characters (32 KiB of JSON), or 24 KiB of bytes as base64, but every tool result is cut to 8 KiB from its end; with sorted keys `next` and `done` survived while the tail of `text` or `data` was cut, so reading on from `next` silently skipped what was cut (most of each piece of a long email). The tool now cuts the piece itself to what one result shows whole, measured as the result fence measures it (the tool's JSON inside the fence's JSON, `< > & U+2028 U+2029` as six bytes, 3 KiB kept for the provenance, as `read_context` keeps), at a character for text or after whole three bytes for base64, with `next` right after it. The headers come with the first piece (offset 0) only, so a sender's long headers cannot shrink every piece; there, headers that would leave the body less than 1 KiB are cut first, every value to the same length (`headersCut`). A receipt whose `next` is not exactly after its piece (code points for text, decoded bytes for base64), or whose `done` contradicts `next`, is refused. A piece is now about 4,500 characters of plain text, fewer for quoted or HTML text, or about 3.4 KiB as base64. No SQL change.
